# TeaTree Blueprint

The product spec. Code is an artifact; this file is the product.

If the entire `src/` and `tests/` tree were deleted, this document — plus every architectural appendix rostered in § "Architectural Appendices" and the skills in `skills/` — should be enough to regenerate the project without ambiguity. That table is the sole roster; the count is stated nowhere else.

**Tone.** This blueprint is functional and architectural. It names classes and files where the structure carries the design, but it is deliberately language-agnostic — a competent engineer should be able to reimplement teatree in another language with this file alone. Prose-of-code (paragraph descriptions of method bodies, ticket-history rationale, line-by-line walkthroughs) does not belong here. The code itself is the source of truth for implementation; this file is the source of truth for architecture.

**Change policy.** Every code change to teatree must keep this file consistent with the architecture. Implementation details — new flags, new error messages, why a particular regex was tightened — belong in docstrings, commit messages, or the issue tracker, not here. Before modifying this file (or any appendix) please ask the user for approval — this is the source of truth and the user validates every change.

**Status:** current architecture under [#541](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/541). All phases (0–8) shipped.

- Statusline file is the always-on persistent UI surface; an on-demand HTML admin dashboard (`teatree.dash`, [#3162](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3162)) is served by `t3 admin` on a local gunicorn
- Code-host + messaging Protocols unified; backends are selectable per overlay
- Fat loop + scanners + dispatcher; the headless executor runs in-process via `claude-agent-sdk`
- No-overlay-leak grep gate keeps the platform tenant-agnostic

The §17.1 Invariants list is parsed by `scripts/hooks/check_blueprint_invariant_numbering.py` (gapless 1..N — do not renumber, do not delete). Its CI cross-PR mode (`blueprint-cross-pr` job) is **fail-closed**: when BLUEPRINT.md is in the PR diff but the merge-base §17.1 snapshot cannot be read (unresolvable merge-base, or the file introduced since the branch-point), the gate reds rather than delegating to the approximate common-prefix fallback, which would silently approve the exact `[1..N]`-on-both-sides cross-PR collision the gate exists to catch. The §5.6 Loop Topology summary is scanned by `tests/test_blueprint_loop_epic_alignment.py`.

---

## 1. What TeaTree Is

A personal code factory for multi-repo projects. It turns a ticket URL into a merged pull request by coordinating the full lifecycle — intake, coding, testing, review, shipping, delivery — across multiple repositories, worktrees, and agent sessions.

**Target:** service-oriented projects with databases and CI pipelines (any language). Not for docs-only repos or CLI tools.

**Operating mode.** Attended `t3 agent` sessions run in Claude Code or Codex, selected by project configuration and sharing the same lifecycle-skill contract and MCP server. The `t3 worker` runs every automated loop as worker task chains, with or without a session open: it sweeps PRs, auto-reviews PRs assigned to the user, intakes assigned issues, watches messaging mentions/DMs, drains the task queue headlessly, and renders a multi-line statusline that is the **always-on UI surface**. An on-demand HTML admin dashboard (`teatree.dash`) is served by `t3 admin` on a local gunicorn.

**Code-host neutrality.** Pull requests are the canonical concept. Both **GitHub** and **GitLab** are first-class in core; GitLab MRs map onto the PR abstraction at the Protocol layer.

**Messaging-backend pluggability.** Mentions, DMs, and outgoing posts go through a `MessagingBackend` Protocol declared per overlay. Slack (Socket Mode bot) is the first implementation. A `Noop` default lets overlays opt out.

**Core principle.** Infrastructure is deterministic code; development work is skill-guided. State management, port allocation, provisioning, task routing, code-host sync, and messaging integration are Python code with >90% branch coverage. The actual development — coding with TDD, debugging, reviewing, shipping — is driven by skills that encode methodology, guardrails, and domain knowledge.

**Core stays generic.** No customer-, tenant-, or product-specific names appear in the scanned roots (`src/teatree`, `docs`, `skills`, `agents`, `tests`, `scripts` + top-level `README.md`/`BLUEPRINT.md`/`AGENTS.md`); per-overlay specifics live in the overlay package and the DB `ConfigSetting` store. A gate (`scripts/hooks/check_no_overlay_leak.py`) enforces this — cited as `BLUEPRINT § 1` from `tests/test_no_overlay_leak_hook.py`. It runs two passes: the configured term list (whole-token via `teatree.hooks.term_match`) and an opaque-ID pass (`teatree.hooks.opaque_id`) flagging a real-shaped Slack/forge id (`C0…`/`U0…`/`T0…`) — a leak class with no dictionary word — while an obviously-synthetic placeholder and any `leak-scan:allow` line are exempt. An empty `overlay` term list refuses the scan. As a pre-commit hook it sees only staged files; the full-tree CI job `overlay-leak-tree` walks every root and requires the `TEATREE_TERM_REGISTRY` secret on every event.

---

## 2. Architecture Principle: Code-First, Not Skills-First

Infrastructure and orchestration are code; development methodology is skill-guided prose. The split is load-bearing:

1. **Skills are prose, not code.** Prose produces different results depending on model, context, and what else is loaded. Anything that must be deterministic — state transitions, port allocation, provisioning, sync, the `/loop` tick — is code.
2. **Coordination needs transactional guarantees.** An FSM + ORM provide atomic transitions and row-locked workers. Coordination through JSON files cannot.
3. **Code is testable; prose is not.** Core logic must reach >90% branch coverage.
4. **One ABC with a handful of methods beats thirty thin extension points.** Overlay customization goes through `OverlayBase` — typed methods with defaults, no priority system, no plugin registries.
5. **Terminal agents today, provider-neutral headless execution by design.** Attended `t3 agent` work can run through Claude Code or Codex, with plugin-hook automation on the Claude Code side only; project configuration selects the manual CLI runtime. That choice is separate from the Factory's in-process headless executor, which stays behind the provider-neutral `Harness` seam in `src/teatree/agents/runner.py`. Don't adopt a mechanism that only works in one terminal CLI when an SDK/API-portable equivalent exists. The terminal-driven flow keeps working; SDK/API-portability is the binding constraint when the two pull apart. Dispatch, state, and skill-loading therefore route through code rather than terminal-only affordances.

---

## 3. Package Structure

Package name: `teatree` (double-e). Repo/CLI: `teatree` / `t3`. Python: 3.13+. License: MIT. Build: `uv`. Entry point: `t3 = t3_bootstrap:main` — a top-level shim that bootstraps Django settings before importing `teatree.cli`.

Top-level layout under `src/teatree/`:

```
cli/         # Typer CLI — bootstrap commands (no Django needed); cohesive groups are subpackages (cli/eval/ cli/review/)
core/        # Django app — models, FSM, managers, sync, runners, management commands; backend_protocols hub at root + packages models/ gates/ merge/ runners/ selectors/ views/ modelkit/ and the clustered leaf packages cleanup/ worktree/ provision/ factory/ intake/ review/ evidence/
agents/      # Headless executor (in-process claude-agent-sdk)
loop/        # /loop topology — tick, scanners, dispatch, statusline
mcp/         # Structured-search + gate-preserving-write MCP server (serializers + search + command_catalogue + per-service groups + write_tools + MCPServer wiring); `t3 mcp serve`. 14 read tools: command_search (CLI discoverability) + ticket_get/ticket_list/ticket_search/worktree_status/pr_for_ticket/task_list/question_list/loop_stats/incoming_event_recent/config_setting_get/gate_status/factory_signals/factory_score — plus the per-service read groups and the gate-preserving write suite (#3076). `t3 mcp serve --read-only` (`build_server(read_only=True)`) registers tools annotated `read_only_hint` plus tools allowed by name: every core, per-service, mailbox and overlay tool passes the one registration choke point (`_NoShadowServer.add_tool`), an unannotated tool counts as a write, and the flag survives the delegation to the container that owns the control DB. A headless phase without the `mcp_write` capability launches that read-only server (both lanes), with one `--allow-write <tool>` per write tool its own deliverable goes through (`phase_tools.mcp_write_tools_for_phase`: `requesting_review` reaches `review_request_post`, while `planning`, `reviewing` and `critic_reviewing` reach exactly `agent_mailbox_send`, `agent_mailbox_inbox` and `agent_mailbox_wait`; other read-only phases retain only mailbox self and peers); `core.mcp_registration.serve_flags_for_phase` derives the launch flags for every lane. `config_setting_set` refuses a governed key by declared EFFECT rather than a name-glob, delegating to the ONE predicate `config.setting_taxonomy.owner_only_reason` — a cold-read key, a feature flag, a registry row, a safety-posture key whose write IS an authorization (`substrate_auto_merge_authorized_by`, `substrate_self_signoff`, `trusted_issue_authors`, `send_proxy_allowlist`, `autonomy`, …), or a gate switch — so a shell-denied MCP agent cannot self-grant a substrate-merge delegation or widen an intake allowlist; a conformance test fails closed if any delegation-shaped field is classified as neither refused nor explicitly MCP-settable. The same predicate guards the WRITE SEAM itself (`ConfigSetting.set_value`/`set_values`), so the CLI and any programmatic write are governed identically rather than one surface refusing what another allows; it refuses only what teatree can PROVE is unattended (the loop runner's declared principal, inherited by anything it spawns) and lifts when an authorizer is NAMED, which is how a ratified directive applies the key/value/scope its human ratified. `ConfigSetting.written_by` records the decider — the authorizer when one is named, else the acting principal. On a containerized install `deploy/t3` serves `t3 mcp serve` through `utils/mcp_stdio_proxy.py`, a host-side stdio proxy that respawns the container-side server across a container restart, replays the client's negotiation, completes an open listen so the client re-reads its lists, and otherwise passes frames through byte for byte
backends/    # Pluggable external service integrations; per-forge subpackages github/ gitlab/ slack/ msteams/ notion/ (+ flat sentry, figma, sharepoint)
config/      # Settings load + overlay discovery
utils/       # Pure utilities (git, git_remote remote-URL parsing [#2404], ports, db, secret facade, sealing, master-key source, compose contract, ...)
skill_support/  # Skill metadata, loading policy, deps resolution, schema/ref validation
overlay_init/, contrib/, docker/, templates/overlay/
```

Plus top-level: `agents/` (phase sub-agent definitions), `skills/*/` (workflow skills), `hooks/` (plugin hooks), `tests/` (pytest suite), `scripts/` (utility scripts), `.claude-plugin/`, `apm.yml`, `settings.json`.

**Headless Notion documents (`t3 notion`).** `teatree.backends.notion` is the full document surface an unattended run needs beyond the page-status sync above: agents reach it through `t3 notion` and, for the five writes the MCP surface offers, the approval-gated `notion_*` tools (§ Teatree-own write tools). Auth is a Notion internal integration token resolved through the `Credential` machinery, failing loud rather than degrading to an unauthenticated read, and the overlay a call runs as is one answer (`core.overlays.notion_identity.notion_overlay`). Three invariants shape the surface: an archived page is not a source, so every page-scoped read goes through `PageLivenessProbe` and refuses a dead page (`backends/notion/liveness.py`); every write is verified by re-reading what it wrote and never reports success otherwise; and an incomplete enumeration is a named refusal, never a plausible subset. Each failure condition has its own exit code (`backends/notion/errors.py`). Detail: [docs/blueprint/notion-surface.md](docs/blueprint/notion-surface.md).

Per-overlay Slack bot setup (`t3 setup slack-bot --overlay <name>`) — and the one-command `t3 setup slack-provision` that runs the whole lifecycle (manifest scopes, OAuth URL, channel join, tokens) idempotently ([#1686](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1686)) — are detailed in [docs/blueprint/configuration.md](docs/blueprint/configuration.md) §10.1 "Slack bot setup", cited from `src/teatree/backends/slack/bot.py`, `src/teatree/cli/slack/setup.py`, and `src/teatree/cli/slack/provision.py` as `BLUEPRINT §3.6`.

---

## 4. Domain Models

`ReviewVerdict` records `merge_safe`, `hold`, or typed `unverified` evidence gaps at one PR head. `ReviewHoldDisposition` stores an independent later review's cited answer to each held point, and a point whose finding carries an executed reproduction is answered only by a `counter_reproduction` (the command or test node id run at that head and the result it printed); `MergeClear` snapshots the disposition ids that released a same-head hold. An unanswered or appended point keeps the hold active (§17.4).

**Ticket DM identity (#5361).** `TicketDmThread` owns one outbound Slack DM root per ticket, with a generation incremented only after a deleted root is proven. `TicketDmPost` records each confirmed root card, note, chase, and answer reply in that generation; its channel and message timestamp pair is unique. `ThreadBinding` still maps inbound roots to tickets. A question card keeps its own mirror timestamp even when posted inside the ticket root, so button taps still address that card.

**One agent per checkout (#5238).** A factory task holds its ticket checkout throughout its run. The writer fence checks occupancy, process evidence, and the attested checkout state before dispatch. Normal exits settle occupancy and attestation together; interrupted, deferred, and released exits can be re-attested at the next writer fence only with the custody, process, and forge proof described in [the worktree occupancy appendix](docs/blueprint/worktree-occupancy.md). A failed proof parks the task without spending its repair budget.

A changed checkout is decided by the writer fence: it requires a clean tree, no live checkout process or Git operation, an exclusive claimant when host process evidence is blind, a verified owned pull request for a pushed head, and a durable patch bundle for unpushed commits. A hand-off between phases of one ticket (the claimant holds the open custody tenure and every earlier tenure is a released task of that ticket) proves the ticket's own unpushed commits, which are bundled without a pull request; a pushed head still needs its owned pull request. The proof and its source are recorded in `Worktree.extra.auto_attestation` and re-read before dispatch. Unsafe or unreadable state parks the task without an owner question; a checkout park older than one hour appears in factory health and clears when the claim succeeds. The direct owner terminal attestation command remains available.
Only an authenticated owner button tap can answer an action card; a typed reply leaves it pending.
When the live evidence is unavailable (a busy or occupied checkout, an unreadable forge), the tick retries the answered action every five minutes and tells the owner once; after a day of waiting it refuses with a notice so the owner can ask again.

Five core lifecycle models in `teatree.core.models/`: **Ticket**, **Worktree**, and **Task** are FSM-driven (`django-fsm`); **Session** is a plain per-phase quality-gate tracker and **TaskAttempt** an immutable execution-history row. Around them, supporting rows under the same package: `AttachmentManifest` (the intake attachment-fetch manifest, below), `BotPing`, `ConsolidatedMemory`/`DreamQaProbe`/`DreamRunMarker`/`InstructionComplianceSnapshot`/`InstructionComplianceRecord` (memory-consolidation, below), `DbApproval`, `DeferredQuestion`, `EvalRunRecord`/`EvalScenarioResult` (eval-run ledger), `SessionAuditRecord` (eval audit layer), `HonestyEscalation`, `IncomingEvent`, `IntentClassification`, `LandscapeArtifact` (the intake landscape survey the planner consumes, below), `LivePostApproval`, `LocalStackQueueItem`, `LocalStackReaperMarker`, `Loop` (DB-configured autonomous loop — name, exactly one of prompt-FK/script, run_in_sub_agent/description/overlay, nullable delay, enabled, §5.6), `LoopLease`, `LoopState` (durable per-loop control plane, §5.6), `WorkerGeneration` (the registry of immutable image generations, below), `MergeClear`, `MergeVoid` (the audited record that a forge merge never delivered its content, below), `OnBehalfApproval`, `OutboundClaim` (drift verifier), `PendingChatInjection`, `PendingPullRequest` (the durable obligation a pre-push `ensure-pr` deferral owes — drained by the `pending_pr_drain` dispatch scanner, discharged by whichever `ensure-pr` run settles the branch, aged into a doctor FAIL, §5.6; the invariant needs two terminal conditions or it accrues obligations nothing can discharge, which is how sixteen of them reached ~12,000 drains apiece and turned `t3 doctor check` into sixteen permanent FAILs that buried its five real findings, [#4577](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4577) — a deferral from a checkout under a temp root owes NOTHING, since a cold review's scratch clone is deleted when the review ends and the pre-push hook has no teatree caller to ask, leaving the path the only knowable signal (`utils/disposable_checkout.py`, roots overridable via `TEATREE_DISPOSABLE_CHECKOUT_ROOTS`); and an obligation whose checkout has since gone is RETIRED by the drain rather than retried, announced once as a `pending_pr.retired` signal, because `ensure-pr` cannot run in a directory that is not there. Neither weakens the guard the invariant exists for: a branch that was pushed stays covered by the orphan-branch guard on the remote, one that was not is already gone with its directory, and a LIVE checkout with unpushed work still owes and still nags — the load-bearing case, pinned explicitly rather than inferred from the filter's narrowness), `ProjectLearning` (durable per-repo knowledge store, below), `Prompt`/`PromptVersion` (first-class reusable prompt with templated `params` + edit-history snapshots, the `Loop.prompt` FK target and the `/prompts` trigger, §5.6), `PullRequest`, `RedCardSignal`, `ReplyDispatch`, `ReviewAssignment`, `ReviewEvidence` (per-ticket review-evidence artifact — cold-review + integration-review kinds — gating review-request and cross-repo close, below), `ReviewRequestPost` (one row per posted review request — the Slack channel + thread it landed in, plus `nag_count`, which widens the re-ask interval as `base_interval * fib(nag_count)` so a long-ignored MR is re-asked less and less often, and `resumed_at`, the single-use stamp claimed by a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE resumed_at IS NULL` before the in-thread "now ready for review" reply, so a paused request can never be resumed twice), `ReviewVerdict`, `ScannedBroadcast`, `SelfImproveFiring`, `SessionHandover`, `SweepSkipStreak` (one row per PR the sweep keeps skipping — the streak ages into a doctor WARN so a permanently-skipped PR is surfaced rather than silently re-skipped every tick; a row is discarded once the PR is settled or has left the swept open set, since `resolve` fires only on a live `pr_sweep.*` signal and so cannot retire the row of a PR that closed outside the sweep, [#4518](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4518); `SKIP_REASON_DISPOSITION` classifies every emittable reason as a deliberate park or a stall in ONE place, so a parked draft never pages the owner however long it runs, and the doctor reports ONE standing finding naming the count rather than a line per row, [#4523](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4523)), `TicketTransition`, `UnshippedWorkRecord` (one row per checkout a reaping pass found holding staged/unstaged/unpushed work, written BEFORE the disposition so a KEPT or force-deleted checkout's work is observable rather than silent — the on-disk salvage bundle it points at is captured with `git diff HEAD`, never a bare `git diff`, which sees only the working tree, and VERBATIM through `git.run_strict_verbatim` rather than the stripping `run_strict` — a patch missing its trailing bytes is one `git apply` rejects as `corrupt patch`, and a bundle exists only to be applied ([#4435](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4435)); untracked-only content is in scope via `full_worktree_diff`'s throwaway-index `add -N`, since a `force` delete would otherwise keep the filename and drop the file; a read this venue could not complete writes its cause to a distinct `.unreadable` key and leaves the last good capture and the row's delta intact, rather than overwriting real content with zero bytes; `t3 <overlay> workspace restore <checkout> --into <checkout>` (`core/cleanup/unshipped_restore.py`) reads a bundle back, so the round-trip is exercised by the suite instead of first by an operator recovering real work; `t3 doctor check` reads those rows back as an advisory WARN carrying each checkout's age via `cli/doctor/checks_unshipped_work.py`, since a recorded row nothing surfaces leaves the work exactly as unobserved as no capture at all), `WaitingItem` (the manual entries of the durable waiting-on-you lane — questions/merge-authorizations/review-requests are computed live, §17.1), `WorktreeEnvOverride`. Further supporting rows back the loops and gates that own them: `KnownIssue` (health-registry), `StandingGoal` (§17 verified-green stop-gate), `PendingTriageRecommendation` (needs-triage assessor), `AssessSweepRun`/`AssessFinding` (assess-sweep ledger), `TicketFacet` (per-ticket decomposition), `ScannedFailedE2E` (failed-E2E watcher dedup), `ModeSchedule`/`ModeScheduleSlot` (weekly preset schedule, §5.6), `PendingReinstall` (self-update reinstall queue), `SlackSelfAckReaction` (self-ack dedup), `Rubric`/`RubricCriterion` (§17.4.3 rubric-done gate), `TicketSweepRun` (the append-only ticket-hygiene sweep ledger whose deduplicated changed-URL set is the "tends to zero" metric, § Issue hygiene), and the audit rows `DbAudit`/`OnBehalfAudit`/`E2EBypassAudit`. This roster is representative, not exhaustive — the authoritative model set is the `teatree.core.models/` package; each name above is cited from §5.6, §17.1, §17.4, or §4 prose where it is load-bearing.

`UnshippedWorkRecord` also names unregenerable ignored files and captures their content in the salvage patch up to an 8 MiB aggregate limit; omitted content marks the bundle partial, never complete. Its commit list checks live remote heads while the removal proof refuses an unanswerable remote.

**Memory-consolidation data layer ([#1933](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1933)).** The idle-time "dreaming" pass clusters session feedback and distils each cluster into one imperative rule, backed by durable rows: `ConsolidatedMemory` (the cited-rule consolidation ledger with a `CANDIDATE → VERIFIED → PROMOTED` status ladder — every rung written by the pass itself, so the terminal rungs the decay rail reads are reachable — AND a `disposition` ladder that makes it a queue that DRAINS a detected core-gap to a merged fix rather than a pile that grows), `DreamQaProbe` (the retention/interference/monotonicity QA corpus the acceptance gates replay), `DreamRunMarker` (staleness marker), and the `InstructionComplianceSnapshot`/`InstructionComplianceRecord` accountant rows. The engine ranks a bounded extract → an injected `Distiller` clusters by root cause → upserts by `cluster_key` (rejecting uncited clusters, never overwriting a BINDING rule), and the LIVE file-side phases (cross-link / merge / re-index / decay, each fault-isolated behind its own kill-switch) maintain the on-disk `~/.claude` memory corpus under a set of acceptance gates (retention, interference, consolidation-happened, index-budget, monotonicity, no-loss, compliance-non-regression) — a failing gate marks the pass attempted-not-succeeded, never stamps success. A cold-tier `MEMORY_ARCHIVE.md` recall resurfaces an archived rule relevant to the owner's prompt as advisory `additionalContext`, on the first tool call after that prompt (`hooks/scripts/owner_turn_context.py`); `t3 teatree memory recall` looks one up by hand. The full phase mechanics, disposition/escalation ladder, decay budget tiers, and eval-derivation loop are in [docs/blueprint/memory-consolidation.md](docs/blueprint/memory-consolidation.md).

**Dream-gap drain.** The backlog sweep offers 12 pending gaps per pass in oldest-offer order and caps each host at three undispositioned gaps. A misfit returns to the umbrella with that host recorded as declined; after three distinct declines, the rule stays as memory. The dream pass releases an idle shell only after branch absence or redundancy is proved, while a pull request row or post-open state holds it. `t3 dream funnel` records daily counts and reports a recurring question class fixed only after seven complete UTC days without a new card.

`Ticket` also carries a `Role` enum (`AUTHOR` / `REVIEWER`) orthogonal to its state. The reviewer role drives the §5.6 `reviewer_prs` scanner (`Ticket(role="reviewer", issue_url=<pr_url>)`) and short-circuits to the dedicated reviewer terminal `review_delivered` once the review work completes; the author role is the default lifecycle. `delivered` means ONLY author work merged to main, so a reviewer ticket never reads as author-merged "Landed" work on the board.

**Repo-namespaced ticket key ([#2293](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2293)).** `Ticket.repo_namespaced_key` is a collision-free `<repo-slug>#<issue-number>` computed from `issue_url` on save (issue-only — GitLab issues and merge requests are separate numbering sequences in the same project, so a PR/MR URL never feeds this key), backed by a partial unique constraint mirroring `issue_url`'s. `TicketManager.resolve()` and `t3 <overlay> ticket context show|add|edit` (the durable per-ticket context store, [#627](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/627)) accept it alongside pk / bare issue number / full issue URL, so `owner/repo#42` never risks landing on a different repo's issue #42.

**Per-project learnings, DB-placed ([#2892](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2892), #550 item 3, epic #2557).** `ProjectLearning` is the DB-placed analogue of gstack's per-project `learnings.jsonl` — durable, intent-bearing knowledge scoped to a *repo* (`owner/repo`), not a ticket, so it survives across every ticket on that repo. Keyed on `repo_slug` (unique), computed via `teatree.utils.url_slug.project_slug_from_ref` — the SAME repo-slug extraction #2293 introduced for `repo_namespaced_key`, reused rather than duplicated (a literal slug or a full issue/PR/MR URL both resolve; unlike the issue-only `repo_namespaced_key`, project scope has no numbering-collision risk to guard against, so a PR/MR URL is a valid ref here). `append_learning` mirrors `Ticket.append_context` — append-only, timestamped, refuses a blank entry. `t3 <overlay> learnings show|add|edit <repo-ref>` is the read/write CLI — the round-trip affordance the DB-only cache-vs-intent rule ([docs/blueprint/configuration.md](docs/blueprint/configuration.md) §10.5) requires for a DB-only user-intent field. Skill-flow consumption: `workspace ticket`'s intake summary renders any recorded learnings for the ticket's repo as a collapsed `<details>` block (`render_project_learnings`, mirroring `render_ticket_context`), so a fresh session sees prior project-scoped lessons before doing work with no explicit lookup.

**State machines** (one row per `Ticket` is the unit of work):

| Model | States |
|---|---|
| `Ticket` | `not_started → scoped → work_started → plan_recorded → coded → tested → self_reviewed → pr_opened → review_requested → merged → retro_recorded → delivered` (plus terminal `ignored`, and the reviewer-role terminal `review_delivered` for external-review tickets) |
| `Worktree` | `created → provisioned → services_up → ready → torn_down` (plus the reversible `services_up`/`ready → provisioned` `stop_services` demotion, [#2190](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2190): docker down only, DB + worktree preserved) |
| `Session` | per-phase quality-gate tracker keyed `(ticket, phase, agent_id)` |
| `Task` | `pending → claimed → in_progress → completed / failed`; carries a human-readable `subject` (+ `created_at`) that `Task.display_subject()` resolves (stored subject → work-item title → `#N phase`) so the pending-tasks scanner and statusline show readable titles, never the bare phase token |
| `TaskAttempt` | execution history rows (immutable); each carries the [#2009](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2009) repair-loop budget fields `iteration` (1-based for work attempts per ticket + normalized-phase, 0 for scheduling and audit rows, spanning re-queued `Task` rows) and `error_fingerprint` (normalized hash of the terminal reason); each also carries `lane` ([#657](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/657), `TaskAttempt.Lane`: `subscription` \| `metered` \| unattributed `""`) — the Layer-2 lane (subscription OAuth vs metered API key/OrcaRouter BYOK, [#2887](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2887)) that dispatch authenticated through, resolved in `src/teatree/agents/runner.py`'s `_resolve_dispatch_lane()` (`PydanticAiHarness` always metered; `ClaudeSdkHarness` attributed only under an explicit `agent_harness_provider` pin) and always `subscription` for an interactive `record-attempt` (rides the user's own Max seat) |

**One repair iteration is executed work.** `task_repair_queries.repair_iterations` is the shared filter for the per-phase cap, stall detection, stuck-ticket re-dispatch budget, `ticket show`, and S5 terminal-iteration mean. It excludes parks, recoverable capacity exhaustion, anchored cancellation/supersede/stale-plan and lease-reap audit rows, and a route attempt superseded by a persisted work successor. Stuck-ticket candidate detection reads `task_repair_queries.ticket_phase_observations` instead: the same rows minus capacity exclusions, so an audit failure can still mark its phase failing. S5 failure fraction reads those observations and excludes operator cancel, supersede, and stale-plan rows, so lease reaps and route failures remain visible. The stuck sweep separately halts repeated lease reaps since the last work attempt at the phase cap; stale-plan recovery separately halts at that cap. Audit rows break identical-failure adjacency. A route refusal without a work successor, API-credit exhaustion, genuine failures, and completed work still count. Stored historical iteration stamps are not rewritten.

**A failed task records WHY ([#3957](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3957)).** `Task.fail(*, reason, by_holder)` takes the reason as a REQUIRED keyword, so no failure path can record nothing; it writes `failure_reason` plus a `failure_kind` drawn from `core/modelkit/task_failure_taxonomy.py`, and `TaskAttempt.failure_kind` is stamped on save beside `outcome`. The kind separates an ENVIRONMENTAL failure (a lost lease re-claimed by another worker, a harness crash) from a DETERMINISTIC one (invalid config, missing phase evidence). What to DO about it is read off the SAME row: `task_failure_taxonomy.RECOVERY` maps every kind to a `RecoveryStrategy` (`retry` \| `corrective_retry` \| `halt_and_escalate`) plus that environmental column, and a kind added without a row is a red test ([#4505](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4505)). Before that table the requeue decision was a SECOND hand-maintained list keyed on error TEXT that never consulted the classification, so `harness_crash` was named environmental and still never retried — eleven tasks dropped in one day, and a PR that reached the owner unreviewed because its reviewing task died that way. The columns stay distinct under a one-way invariant, `retry ⇒ environmental` and never the reverse, so a requeue decision cannot re-run a deterministic failure into the same wall; text markers now feed `classify_failure` only. The reason reaches the surfaces an operator reads: `tasks list` (JSON + a "Failed because" column) and the dash ticket card as a kind chip. Without it a card shows a cause-less error and a genuine defect is indistinguishable from a transient fault.

**A failing ticket is repaired, not only a frozen one ([#3958](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3958)).** The hourly `loop/stuck_ticket_redispatch.py` sweep ran, but its candidate set excluded the population that actually fails: it filtered `role=AUTHOR`, and the failures are dominated by `reviewing`, which lives on REVIEWER tickets. It also keyed on FROZEN — in a work-state, nothing in flight, idle past a threshold — which finds a ticket that stopped, never one that keeps failing and so is never idle. Both are widened on ONE path. A reviewer ticket has no state→phase map (minted at `NOT_STARTED`, held until `REVIEW_DELIVERED`), so `_implied_phase` reads the phase off its own most recent task, which also preserves the codex review variants a plain `reviewing` would collapse; the open-PR exclusion stays AUTHOR-only, because a reviewer ticket's PR is its SUBJECT and excluding on it would re-narrow the sweep to author-only. A FAILING class is OR-ed alongside the frozen one — latest attempt for the implied phase failed, nothing in flight — minus any failure whose output `phase_landing_evidence` proves landed, which is a dead artifact rather than work to redo. Both classes and both roles reduce to one `_Candidate` through `_redispatch_one`, so no re-dispatch reaches a scheduler without the [#2009](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2009) repair-loop verdict. Neither class reaches a ticket whose newest task an operator CANCELLED ([#4105](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4105)) — see the cancellation paragraph in the intake section. What makes the widening safe rather than a repair storm is a second circuit-breaker: `repair_loop.is_kind_stalled` applies the same two-consecutive-identical rule to the recorded [#3957](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3957) `FailureKind` names, because a fingerprint compares free text and one deterministic defect carrying a varying detail yields two fingerprints that never match. Environmental kinds are dropped rather than compared — an outage is the environment's fault, so it says nothing about the work and stays retryable to the cap — and `unclassified`/`unrecorded` are dropped too, being the absence of a name rather than a shared cause. CAUSELESS kinds — `no_result_envelope` and `runtime_ceiling`, where the run reported nothing at all ([#4075](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4075)) — are dropped from BOTH checks by that absence-of-a-cause test, not by whether the text repeats: `no_result_envelope` is a compile-time constant and so also collides on the fingerprint side, but `runtime_ceiling`'s reason interpolates the breach and so does not — for it the kind-level drop in `task_failure_taxonomy.stall_kinds` alone does the work, which is why the pinned corpus asserts that asymmetry directly ([#4276](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4276)); four real tickets halted on the `no_result_envelope` fingerprint and three later ran the same phase and succeeded. The frozen class's idle rules are public as `idle_started_author_tickets`: issue intake holds on them when `issue_intake_idle_hold_hours` is set, except for a ticket with a pending question or a cancelled newest task ([#5187](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5187)).

**One agent per checkout ([#3952](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3952), #5238).** The contended resource is the worktree, so the occupancy claim lives on the `Worktree` row: `core/worktree/occupancy.py` grants it by one compare-and-swap `UPDATE` keyed on `(holder, holder_session)`, and every factory task holds its ticket's dispatch checkout as `task:<pk>` for its whole run, in every phase. The claim is advisory toward a rival — a second requester is refused (a factory dispatch parks), the first is never evicted — and a writing task is further fenced on host process evidence and the checkout's attested state. The run's own exit attests the checkout in the transaction that releases occupancy; a run whose processes outlive it keeps the checkout reserved until its lease lapses, and the next writer's fence attests the recorded exit state once the checkout is clear and unchanged. A later writing claim can also re-attest a clean checkout after a released same-ticket tenure chain containing a writer when its ticket-owned GitHub pull request's exact live head and bounded custody anchor prove the move. Release rules for self and third-party fails, the 30-minute lease, custody re-attestation and cancel-time survivors are in [worktree-occupancy.md](docs/blueprint/worktree-occupancy.md).

**A review is minted only while one is owed ([#4847](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4847)).** Every reviewer mint — the FSM scheduler's `schedule_external_review`, the stuck-ticket sweep's `reviewing` re-dispatch and its codex-variant `create_phase_task` — passes ONE gate, `core/models/ticket_external_review.reviewer_dispatch_decline`, cheapest first: a state `Ticket.admits_review` rejects (one `mark_reviewed_externally` cannot leave) (including a state value the code does not know) is `NOTHING_OWED` without a forge call; otherwise the forge is read once, and a MERGED or CLOSED PR retires the ticket to `IGNORED` (reversible, no false "review posted" claim) and returns `RETIRED` — or `NOTHING_OWED` when the state has no `ignore` transition — while a PR the forge cannot confirm OPEN is `PR_STATE_UNKNOWN` and mints nothing — fail closed. The `ReviewDeclined` values are all a caller needs: the scanner path unrecords `reviewed_sha` on `PR_STATE_UNKNOWN` so `ReviewerPrsScanner` re-offers the head next tick, and the sweep simply re-admits the ticket on its next pass; no decline burns repair budget, and a budget-halted reviewer ticket whose PR settled is retired rather than escalated as stuck. The sweep admits reviewer tickets by an explicit list (`Ticket.pre_ship_states()`), never by excluding known-done states, so an unknown state value is never read as live, and it logs one warning per pass naming each unknown value and its count. A review whose target has no recorded head can only be refused, so after such a refusal the ticket requalifies only through the idle class, never the failing fast path. The reap side reads the same state test: the reviewer-PR sweep's local reap (`loop/scanners/reviewer_prs._orphaned_task_signals`) fires only on a settled state `Ticket.admits_review` rejects, so a `review_delivered` ticket's reviewing task is closed only once the forge reports its PR MERGED or CLOSED, and the sweep never closes a CLAIMED task — the claim sweeps own a run in flight ([#4901](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4901)).

**A lost lease is evidence about the LEASE, never about the WORK ([#3982](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3982)).** A shipping task that pushed its branch, opened its pull request and advanced its ticket to `review_requested` had completed the phase's entire purpose; recording it `failed` / `lease_lost` because the heartbeat's renewal found the claim generation moved on feeds the auto-repair sweep a "re-do this" signal for work that already landed, and inflates the environmental-failure rate capacity decisions read from. `core/models/phase_landing.py` answers "did this phase's output land?" from the DB alone — the ticket's position on the FULL author ladder (`task_phase_disposition.phase_output_reached`, which runs to `delivered`, unlike `Ticket.has_completed_phase`'s deliberate stop at `pr_opened`), plus the phase's own artifact: for `shipping` an attached non-closed `PullRequest`, and for a REVIEWER-role ticket's review phase a `ReviewVerdict` recorded at the head that task reviewed ([#4100](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4100) — reviewing carries most of the recorded lease losses, and a reviewer ticket has no author-ladder position at all, being minted at `not_started` and held until `review_delivered`, so the ladder half can never speak for it; the reviewed head comes from the `AutoReviewDispatch` contract the task was dispatched under, else the ticket's `reviewed_sha`). Reader and writer are keyed on the SAME head ([#4126](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4126)): `agents/attempt_recorder` records the returned envelope's verdict at the dispatch head, and REFUSES a reviewer's divergent self-asserted one — recording it there hid the verdict from this guard (which reads the dispatch head), while recording a divergence at the dispatch head would vouch for a tree nobody reviewed, so a reviewer that judged a different tree than it was dispatched for fails the task as the finding it is. An UNDISCLOSED head is refused on that same reasoning ([#4168](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4168)): reading an omitted `reviewed_sha` as agreement enforced the rule only against reviewers that disclose a head, so one that said nothing got `merge_safe` recorded at the dispatch head with no check performed at all — the shell sibling `t3 <overlay> review record` already refused an empty `--reviewed-sha`, and `build_review_contract` hands the reviewer the literal 40-char head, so disclosing it costs a compliant reviewer nothing. `RESULT_JSON_SCHEMA`'s `required` list for `review_verdict` and the recorder's refusals are ONE fact, pinned by a test that walks the declared list, so a field declared required but never enforced cannot read as a guard that guards nothing. The artifact half is trusted only when the caller can attribute the row's failure to the lease itself, not to the work — a REQUIRED `trust_phase_artifact` keyword (un-omittable, so no call site opts in by omission) — because a pull request can exist on a SELF_REVIEWED ticket independently of a successful `ship()` (the no-orphan pre-push gate and the `PendingPullRequest` drain both open one regardless of the FSM transition) and a verdict at a head can be another reviewer's, and trusting either for a genuinely deterministic failure would silently swallow a real defect. Three consumers read the one predicate: `src/teatree/agents/runner_interruption.py` records an exit-0 attempt carrying the evidence instead of the false failure (landing the row COMPLETED only via `UPDATE … WHERE status=pending`, so a live successor's claim is never stolen) and passes `True` unconditionally, since its call is already gated on `outcome.lease_lost`; `loop/transient_requeue.py` and `loop/stuck_ticket_redispatch.py` each evaluate an already-recorded row with no live outcome to consult, so they pass `True` only when that row's own `failure_kind` is `LEASE_LOST`, and retire (rather than reopen, escalate, or re-dispatch) only then. A watchdog runtime/turns breach is a real runaway and stays a failure regardless. The lost-lease reason itself is read back from the row (`task_claim.describe_lease_loss`) rather than assumed: a single worker driving every loop routinely loses leases to its own `reclaim_orphaned_claims` sweep, and reporting that as "re-claimed by another worker" sends the operator hunting for a second worker that does not exist.

**An interruption noticed after the row COMPLETED records nothing at all ([#4100](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4100)).** The heartbeat outlives the work: the task reaches COMPLETED, the next lease renewal finds the claim generation gone — because the row moved on by FINISHING — and the interrupted run wrote a `failed` / `lease_lost` outcome over a completed row. Roughly half of all recorded failures on the live box were work that had succeeded, which inflated `repair_burn` past its red threshold and had the auto-repair sweep re-dispatching reviews that were already done (three concurrent review tasks for one PR). `src/teatree/agents/runner_interruption.py::_record_stuck_outcome` therefore consults the LIVE row before recording anything: a COMPLETED row leaves the interruption as an exit-0 attempt naming it and touches the row itself not at all — the run had, in the diagnosis's own words, nothing left to hand over. What the run had already PRODUCED is a separate question ([#4464](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4464)): a reviewing agent that emitted its verdict envelope and only then lost its lease had that envelope dropped for a summary-only attempt, leaving a `completed` row with nothing persisted behind it — indistinguishable from a reviewer that silently declined to record, which is the shape that made [#4308](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4308) so hard to pin down. The produced envelope therefore rides onto the attempt beside the interruption, making the work durable and inspectable without re-driving an FSM the rival run owns.

**Bounded factory turns preserve their envelopes when cut short.** The one-shot turn, clean-room eval run, CI-eval heal fixer's write turn, two dream turns and spawn-payload doctor check use `src/teatree/agents/compaction_guard.py::with_compaction_off`, which sets `DISABLE_COMPACT=1` and registers a `PreCompact` tripwire. The ordinary `run_agent` Claude dispatch leaves that guard off so the CLI can compact near its context limit; `runner_preparation._native_compaction_env` sets the native window and reserve for known models and replaces inherited disable switches with `0`. The owner's attended sessions (`t3 agent`, `t3 loop start`) exec the CLI directly and carry neither, which `tests/teatree_agents/test_claude_sdk_options_pin.py` pins with planted-leak controls. The tripwire blocks every compaction, and an automatic one also interrupts the run, recorded under a `result_error: compaction_blocked` reason the requeue sweep re-dispatches as a fresh session. A CI-eval heal turn stopped that way keeps what it wrote: `src/teatree/loop/ci_eval_heal_fixer.py::_HeadlessFixer` pins the work to a `refs/ci-eval-heal-salvage/<sha>` ref before force-removing the turn's worktree, keeps the worktree when it cannot prove the pin, and the halt escalation names the ref — the same path covers the turn's watchdog timeout and any other error it raises. A dispatch cut short by the turn ceiling, the runtime ceiling (`src/teatree/agents/runner_stream.py::StreamCapture` holds the stream outside the cancelled await), a full context window (`runner_failure_taxonomy.is_context_exhaustion`, also a `result_error:` retry) or a blocked compaction keeps only an envelope that passes the phase's evidence gate, recorded through the normal recorder against the task's own claim (`runner_interruption.CeilingSalvage`). The ticket's state proves nothing about the run, so every other cut-short run is recorded as its failure, carrying the tail of the text it wrote.

**A factory run loads nothing the claude.ai account syncs; the owner's attended sessions keep it.** `src/teatree/agents/claude_cli_spawn.py::with_account_skills_off` merges `syncClaudeAiSkills: false` and `syncClaudeAiPlugins: false` into the child's `--settings`, which the CLI honours for that one invocation, beside whatever settings the run already carries. `ClaudeSdkHarness.open` applies it in `prepared_spawn` to every harness-opened run, the CI-eval heal fixer's write turn (`run_bounded_write_turn`) included; the SDK sessions opened outside the harness — the clean-room eval run and judge (`build_sdk_options`) and the two dream turns — compose it themselves. `tests/teatree_agents/test_claude_cli_spawn.py` renders the harness spawn, the write turn (`test_the_bounded_write_turn_spawn_turns_it_off`) and the dream turns through the pinned SDK transport; the eval run and judge are pinned by settings equality in `tests/teatree_eval/test_api_runner.py` and `tests/eval_harness/test_virgin_isolation.py`; and `tests/teatree_agents/test_compaction_guard.py` fails on a composer that opens a session directly without the helper.

**Repair-loop budget + stall detection ([#2009](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2009)).** The verify→diagnose→patch→re-verify retry loop has a *visible* per-ticket-phase iteration budget, not just the time-based 24h stale-task expiry. Each `TaskAttempt` is auto-stamped on insert (the single `TaskAttempt.save` chokepoint) with `iteration` (its 1-based attempt number for the `(ticket, normalized-phase)`, spanning re-queued `Task` rows) and `error_fingerprint` (a stable hash of its terminal reason, normalized so transient noise — timestamps, pids, hex ids, paths, bare numbers — does not defeat the identical-failure check). `t3 <overlay> ticket show` surfaces `attempt N/max` per phase against the configurable `MAX_PHASE_ITERATIONS` cap (default 5). The re-queue chokepoint `TaskQuerySet.reclaim_orphaned_claims` consults `Task.check_requeue_allowed` (pure policy in `teatree.core.repair_loop`, model orchestration in `teatree.core.models.task_repair`) before returning a failed/orphaned task to PENDING: at the cap it raises `MaxIterationsExceeded`; two consecutive identical fingerprints on the same phase raise `IterationStalled`, which halts the re-queue with a durable failure stamp and a self-clearing health signal — so a doomed phase neither re-runs forever nor burns another attempt on the identical failure. The CAP alone (never the stall check) is skipped for a synthetic loop-cadence-anchor ticket — `teatree.core.forge_url.is_synthetic_ticket_url` — at both call sites, `check_requeue_allowed` and `transient_requeue._budget_halt_reason` ([#4714](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4714)): `PhaseCadence` re-fires `architectural_review` and its siblings (`eval_local`, `scanning_news`, `backlog_sweep`, `triage_assessor`, `dogfood_smoke`) forever by design on its own cadence/backoff timing, so a LIFETIME attempt count against that anchor ticket is not a doom signal the way it is for a one-shot deliverable ticket — the un-exempted cap was tripping on ordinary healthy recurrence and staying tripped forever after. Every `last_two_fingerprints` builder passes through `task_failure_taxonomy.stall_fingerprints`, and every `last_two_deterministic_kinds` builder through its kind-side sibling `task_failure_taxonomy.stall_kinds` — the two places a CAUSELESS attempt is withheld from a stall comparison ([#4075](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4075)): `no_result_envelope` / `runtime_ceiling` are the ABSENCE of a cause — the run reported nothing about why the work is unfinished — so two of them are one silence repeated, not one defect recurring, and the corrective retry the loop itself schedules supplies the second strike, making the halt an artifact of the repair rather than an observation. `stall_fingerprints` also withholds `outage`, `head_superseded`, and `resume_refused`: repeated text for these records describes the same external interruption, not a defect in the work. That set is deliberately NARROWER than the operator-display `is_environmental` axis: `result_error` / `provision_failed` / `landing_unverified` / `lease_lost` carry defect-specific text, and `harness_crash` carries a traceback naming a code path, so a verbatim repeat of one is a defect recurring; `transient_requeue`'s reopen branch passes no `last_two_deterministic_kinds`, so the fingerprint check is its only stall check. The attempt still counts toward `iteration_count`, so a phase that keeps reporting nothing still halts at the cap with its cause recorded.

**Current failures and legacy gap return.** The Claude SDK can first acknowledge a stale task notification with a clean zero-turn result; the session holds that result while reading for an assistant turn or a real result, releasing it only at stream EOF if neither appears. The hold ends at EOF, a real turn, an error or answered result, or the existing runtime ceiling. A resumed stream that reaches EOF with only a zero-turn result records `FailureKind.RESUME_REFUSED` (retry, environmental, text-uninformative) and retains the exhaustion marker that starts a fresh conversation; generic `result_error` retains its defect-specific fingerprint. `LifecycleIncidentDetector` counts a failed task only while no later same-ticket task in the same non-empty canonical phase owns the work; a newer administrative failure (`cancelled`, `superseded`, or `plan_stale`) does not hide the earlier real failure, and a blank phase never recovers; it counts that failure only while its newest attempt started within the board's `STALE_ERROR_AFTER` window, or when the task has no attempt. A legacy dream-gap member with no live task, PR, or extant worktree can return its full batch to the configured umbrella's `dream_gap_pending` through `ticket return-gaps`; the command verifies the pending keys before ignoring the member in one transaction, requires per-ID confirmation above the bulk-close threshold, and requires `--advanced <id>` for a member past an early state. The release stamp copies the cleared batch and delivered claims with its proof reason so the move can be reversed; a second return is a no-op. ConsolidatedMemory remains untouched.

**Deterministically-correctable config breaches self-repair instead of paging ([#3665](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3665)).** A repair-halt whose failure has exactly one valid resolution carries no decision, so it is corrected rather than escalated to a person. The criterion is exact: a failure is *self-correctable* iff its error text is recognised by a registered resolver AND that resolver yields **exactly one** setting→value assignment that makes the configuration valid — zero candidates (nothing to correct) and two or more (a genuine choice) halt automatic retry and surface through factory health. For an invalid `agent_harness`/`agent_harness_provider` pair the candidate set is the harnesses under which the operator's EXPLICIT provider pin is valid; correcting the *provider* is deliberately not attempted (every harness admits more than one provider, so picking one would invent a credential decision the operator never made), and a future harness sharing a provider makes the set ambiguous so the condition pages again — by construction, not by a second rule. Over-suppression is foreclosed on three sides: `ConfigRepair.apply` logs at WARNING naming the setting, the value and why it was unambiguous; a durable `[self-repaired] <setting>=<value>` stamp on the task is rendered by the `/dash/settings/` *Self-repairs* readout; and that stamp is also the idempotency guard, so a breach surviving its own repair escalates normally. The split is by layer — `core.config_self_repair` owns the pure criterion and the correction it yields (over an injected valid-provider table), `loop.config_self_repair` resolves it against the live harness registry, because the agents-layer registry and the core-layer config store are domain siblings that cannot import each other.

**A phase wedge is repaired by the factory, never asked of the owner ([#5030](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5030)).** When a completed phase cannot advance its ticket and exactly one resolution exists, the factory applies it; otherwise it records the wedge for itself, never as an owner question.

- **One-resolution repairs.** A completed plan on a ticket that never formally started walks the ticket through the missing start steps, and a self-improve repair ticket starts in the state planning needs. A ticket with nothing to check out (no repo, and no forge issue to attach one from) is never provisioned, so it never raises a provisioning question.
- **Recorded wedges.** Any other wedge is an internal health item, recorded once per completed task (or per distinct refusal when no task is behind it). A dismissal sticks, the item clears itself once the ticket moves on or settles, and the lifecycle-incident detector turns open items into repair work. A completed task is not replayed after a later state edge moves its ticket backward over it; a wedge row tied to that task heals once the move back is recorded.
- **Moved targets.** A run whose target moved while it worked gets its recovery where the failure is parked, whatever the PR state: a coding run whose plan went stale gets a planning pass that reaffirms the plan against what moved or re-plans it, and an author's own review of a PR that moved is re-armed at the live head. Each recovery spends that phase's retry budget, then halts internally.
- **Owned work.** Red-PR debugging lands on the ticket that owns the PR, a merge-safe review records the root-cause record a fix needs, a delivery refusal is recorded instead of failing silently, and a review verdict that reaches no pull request is refused rather than reported as recorded.

**§4 invariant — worker enqueue pattern (load-bearing).** Transitions that own long I/O follow one rule:

- Transition body stays pure: state change + metadata only, then `transaction.on_commit(lambda: execute_X.enqueue(self.pk))`. The state change and the queued work land atomically.
- Workers take a row lock (`select_for_update()`), re-check the source state, run the runner, and on success call the next transition. On the production SQLite engine that clause is a **silent no-op** (`has_select_for_update` is `False`, so Django drops it and Django's own outside-a-transaction guard — itself gated on that feature — never fires): the exclusion actually comes from `transaction_mode: "IMMEDIATE"` (§4.3), which only a `transaction.atomic()` block takes. So the locked re-read must sit INSIDE `atomic()`, and `skip_locked`/`nowait` are unavailable — `IMMEDIATE` blocks where they promise to return. `tests/conformance/test_select_for_update_is_backed_by_immediate.py` re-derives the census of every call site on each run and fails on either shape ([#4226](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4226)).
- At-least-once delivery is safe because the state guard makes redelivery a no-op.
- Every `atomic()` is that write lock, not only a transition's: no call whose duration a remote party decides runs inside one (`long-io-holds-control-lock`), and a block meant as a savepoint is a full `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` when no transaction encloses it.
- `ControlDbJournal` chooses WAL with `synchronous=NORMAL` only for a containerized control DB on a proven local filesystem. Uncertain storage selects TRUNCATE for a new database, a deployed install, or a source tree without `.git`; an unshipped checkout opening an existing WAL database on uncertain storage raises before connecting. A checkout with `.git` is unshipped unless `TEATREE_CLONE_DIR` resolves to its own root: it preserves an existing journal header without an override, and refuses a mode-changing override at the literal default control-DB directory. An explicit `wal` override in another control-DB directory can change an existing rollback file to WAL if the storage is eligible. Other database paths remain TRUNCATE. The boundary engine reads the header on each writable connection: WAL needs a setter only for a new file or WAL/rollback family mismatch, while TRUNCATE needs one on every connection because the rollback header does not persist the choice; read-only connections retain `query_only` and deferred transactions. A same-family WAL connection reads a held file without the setter, and a rollback connection can select TRUNCATE while an ordinary reader or reserved writer holds it. A live WAL connection prevents another process from switching that file to TRUNCATE. Snapshots normalize to single rollback-mode files.
- `post_transition` signals are reserved for lossy cross-cutting side effects (audit log, Slack reactions) — never for the main work of the transition.

**Review states name WHOSE review they record.** `SELF_REVIEWED` is the maker's own pass (self-review) and `REVIEW_REQUESTED` is peer review in flight, so a ticket that has cleared its own review is not yet a ticket a colleague has looked at — the review-request gate reads `has_passed_review` for the first and the broadcast lifecycle for the second. The labels say so rather than leaving the two readable as one "reviewed".

**A self-review HOLD stops the ticket in TESTED ([#5076](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5076)).** An author's pre-PR self-review verdict stays on its reviewing attempt (`core.models.self_review.SelfReview` reads it back, scoped to the current delivery cycle). While the ticket is pre-ship, a HOLD never advances it: its findings go whole to ONE coding task parented on that review, and only that rework's completion fires `address_self_review` (TESTED or SELF_REVIEWED → CODED), so the fix is re-tested and re-reviewed. The findings are fixed before anything ships, never posted on the PR or DM'd; a PR opened early does not exempt them, and once the ticket ships the PR-keyed review governs. Past `max_phase_iterations()` HOLD self-reviews in the cycle (one per rework lap, whether or not the rework moved the head) the factory records one sticky INTERNAL question instead of more rework. The disposition, the bound, the shipping gate and the `ticket rework-hold` recovery verb are in [docs/blueprint/self-review-hold.md](docs/blueprint/self-review-hold.md).

Auto-scheduling chains the phases: `start → provision worker → schedule_planning`, `plan → schedule_coding` (gated on `PlanArtifact` DB record — no artifact raises `NoPlanArtifactError`; the only path from WORK_STARTED to CODED passes through PLAN_RECORDED), `code → schedule_testing`, `test → schedule_review`, `review → schedule_shipping` (gated on `has_shippable_diff()`; a self-review HOLD never fires `review`, see above). Each forward transition threads the completing task as the next phase task's `parent_task` (PR-12), so the phase-boundary brief is warm — `agents.prompt._parent_result_summary` reads the parent's last attempt (summary, files, next steps) instead of dispatching cold. The runner classes live in `core/runners/`; the workers in `core/tasks.py` and `core/worktree/worktree_tasks.py`. The PLAN_RECORDED `plan_text` also carries an `E2E test plan / Acceptance scenarios` section for UI-visible tickets (prose-gated on `frontend_repos`, mirroring `is_ui_visible`) so behaviour-level TDD is planned up front (#2240).

When review finds no recorded shippable diff, `dispose_unshippable_review` leaves the author ticket at `SELF_REVIEWED` with `extra.shipping_hold` instead of inferring that no work exists. The recovery sweep checks recorded worktrees, matching checkouts and forge branches outside the ticket lock, then re-reads under the claim window. It releases a hold and schedules shipping on recorded commits, retains it for unshipped or unreadable evidence, and ignores only a ticket with at least one worktree row when every source verifies empty. The final ignore check compares both the worktree rows and the recorded ticket branch against the evidence scan. A rowless ticket remains held, and held tickets are excluded from stuck-ticket shipping redispatch. Rework leaving `SELF_REVIEWED` clears the hold so a later review can schedule shipping.

**Intake landscape survey baked into the FSM ([#2541](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2541)).** The provision worker (`execute_provision`) is the ticket-intake (info-fetch) step: once the worktrees materialise and before `schedule_planning`, it surveys what is already in flight or already settled — open PRs/MRs, in-flight local worktrees (uncommitted/unpushed), a per-issue close/merge/supersede recommendation, and a bounded fix-first block of open defects and idle author tickets — and persists it as an append-only `LandscapeArtifact` (the git/forge landscape gathered django-free by `teatree.core.intake.landscape`, the Django-backed fix-first block added by `teatree.core.intake.landscape_gather`; mirrors `PlanArtifact`, latest governs). The planner CONSUMES that persisted survey rather than re-deriving it (a headless planner sees it inline in its `INTAKE LANDSCAPE SURVEY` system-context block; any planner reads `LandscapeArtifact.latest_for`). Provision persistence is best-effort and never a gate — a forge outage during provision degrades to a log line and never blocks provisioning or planning; the planner then falls back to the `workspace landscape` fetch. Manual planning enqueue first records a missing survey using the ticket's overlay credential and refuses task creation if a configured forge cannot be read. `info artifacts` surfaces the latest survey alongside a ticket's other artifacts.

**Where the token may write (`backends/notion/write_guard.py`).** Notion's capabilities cover the whole integration and its API cannot say which pages a customer sees, so the write boundary is configuration. Every mutating request goes through `NotionClient._write`, the single chokepoint, and a test fails when a client method sends a mutation outside it. The guard walks the target's parent chain (`GET /blocks/{id}`, cached per client for five minutes) and refuses unless a `notion_write_allowed_roots` id is among the ancestors; a `notion_write_denied_roots` id anywhere on the chain always wins. Both are per-overlay DB settings, empty by default, so a venue with nothing configured refuses every write, and a chain that cannot be read, loops, or runs past the depth cap refuses too (exit 17). The settings are read through `core.gates.notion_write_roots`, which keeps `backends` off `teatree.config`, and the root ids are overlay data, never core constants. The MCP write tools reach the same `_write`, and their dry runs the same `check_writable`, so a page outside the roots is refused at exit 17 before an approval is asked for. Reads are not gated.

**Pre-flight attachment-fetch gate (PR-15).** The same intake step (`execute_provision`, after the landscape survey and before `schedule_planning`) refuses to hand off to the planner while any attachment the ticket references is still un-fetched — a GitLab upload, a linked Notion file, a Slack-thread file. `teatree.core.intake.attachment_manifest` extracts every referenced attachment URL from the issue body + comments (deterministic regex, code-host text via an injected seam, fail-open), reconciles them against the on-disk cache under `<ticket_dir>/.attachments/` (ground truth: the file exists), and persists an append-only `AttachmentManifest` (mirrors `LandscapeArtifact`, latest governs). The gate is vacuous on a zero-attachment ticket; a hold is transient (re-running provision after a fetch hands off); a `--fetch` or manual placement releases it. CLI: `t3 <overlay> ticket attachments <ref> [--fetch]`; fetch transport is injected via a core registry backends populate at app-ready (Notion wired, other kinds report a manual-download hint). Companion intake-source check: `teatree.verification.url_check.check_url` (HEAD-first, ranged-GET fallback, typed `OK`/`UNRESOLVED`/`NETWORK_ERROR`) gates `PendingArticleSuggestion.record_candidate`, dropping a fabricated/404 candidate URL and recording-anyway on a transient network error.

**A repo-less provision retries before it asks, and a healed provision question is withdrawn ([#4904](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4904)).** `Ticket.begin_planning` starts a ticket before `workspace ticket` attaches its repos, so the intake step's "no repos on ticket" is usually transient: `execute_provision(ticket_id, attempt)` re-enqueues itself with `run_after` on `teatree.core.provision.failure_question.NO_REPOS_RETRY_DELAYS` and records the `provision-failure:<pk>` `DeferredQuestion` only once that budget is spent (or at once on a backend that cannot defer). That no-repos question offers the `workspace ticket` retry or ignoring the ticket; a ticket with repos is still asked at once, as before. `teatree.core.question_heal.HEAL_CHECKS` withdraws (dismisses as stale) a question whose trigger has provably healed — here, every repo on the ticket has a checkout `probe_checkout` proves live — before each Slack surfacing drain (mirror, resurface, re-ask) and, as positive-only resolvers, in the tick question sweep.

**Who drains the queue.** No always-on `db_worker` is assumed; a won-owner tick drains a bounded batch of `DBTaskResult` jobs in-process (`teatree.loop.queue_drain`), standing down while a live worker holds the `worker` (`WORKER_SINGLETON`) flock — the one singleton every worker entry point acquires — and first expires READY jobs older than `T3_QUEUE_STALE_HOURS` (default 24h) to the reversible `FAILED` state so a fresh drainer never fires stale heavy jobs.

**Clean-worktree preflight (#884).** `code()`, `test()`, `review()`, `ship()` refuse the transition if any worktree has tracked uncommitted changes. The refusal raises a `DirtyWorktreeError` (an `InvalidTransitionError` subclass, **not** `TransitionNotAllowed`) inside the caller's outer atomic block, so the FSM state change is rolled back and the lease reaper returns the CLAIMED task to PENDING on the next tick. No auto-stash — worktrees share one `.git`, so a stash is repo-global and could clobber an unrelated branch's work.

**DoD local-E2E preflight ([#88](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/88)).** `ship()` runs a second preflight (`core/gates/dod_gate.check_local_e2e_dod`) after the clean-worktree one: a UI-visible ticket (scoped `repos` intersect the overlay's `frontend_repos`) needs a green local-stack E2E artifact (`Ticket.extra['e2e_recipe'].last_run` with `result == "green"` and `env == "local"`) before leaving the implementation lifecycle. Refusal raises `DodLocalE2EError`; hatch `dod_e2e_override` with a non-empty reason; an overlay without `frontend_repos` is silent. `is_ui_visible` fails **closed** for an unregistered overlay ([#1426](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1426)), while a known **path-only** TOML overlay is answered from its `[overlays.<name>]` table ([#733](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/733)).

**Mandatory-E2E gate for customer-display-impacting changes ([#1967](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1967)).** `core/gates/e2e_mandatory_gate` runs at `pr create` and §17.4 `ticket clear`: a change the fail-closed classifier `overlay.review.classify_customer_display_impact` marks impacting needs a satisfier — a green AND **posted** SHA-bound `E2eMandatoryRun`, or a single-use **user** `E2EBypassApproval` (maker/agent/loop refused). A repo listed by `overlay.review.mandatory_e2e_exempt_repo_slugs` never reaches the path classifier at all: the repo ships no customer display surface, so no diff in it can. An overlay may also declare `mandatory_e2e_repo_slugs`; when nonempty, a repo outside that scope skips the classifier, while an included repo retains per-path classification unless explicitly exempt. Both declarations use the slug from `ticket.issue_url`; an unparsable URL yields no slug and keeps the classifier. Both hooks default empty, so the exemption ships inert and every other repo stays fail-closed. Deny names both remedies. A diff the gate cannot read (a HEAD git cannot resolve, no worktree, an ambiguous one, a failing `git diff`) is presumed impacting and, when no satisfier covers the tree, refuses as `[gate:e2e_mandatory] DID NOT RUN` (§17.6.5); a verified empty diff classifies non-impacting. Additive to #88 (which keys on frontend repos; this keys on diff content).

**Per-(repo, ticket) open-PR budget gate (north-star PR-2).** `core/gates/pr_budget_gate.check_pr_budget` runs at three pre-creation seams — `_run_ship_gates` (`pr create`, right after the shipping gate), the ship executor precheck, and the orphan-branch path (`_ensure_pr.create_or_defer_pr`) — refusing to open a PR when the ticket already has `max_open_prs_per_repo_per_ticket` live (neither merged nor closed) PRs in that repo. The knob is a durable `UserSettings` field (`OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE`, **not** a `FEATURE_FLAG`) read as data via `get_effective_settings(overlay)`; its **shipped default `1`** (D9) enforces at-most-one-open-PR-per-repo-per-ticket out of the box — the generic core shape a max-one-MR-per-repo directive resolves to, with no overlay-local branch in core (the N=2 litmus) — while a per-overlay `ConfigSetting` value of `0` is the explicit **unlimited opt-out** (the empty-table escape). The count unions the ticket's live `PullRequest` rows with the synchronously-written `extra["pr_url_by_branch"]` index (reconciler-lag safe); an indexed URL whose row is MERGED or CLOSED does not count. After the forge union, the shipping branch's own recorded URL or `extra["unverified_pr_by_branch"]` retry marker is excluded, while other branches remain counted. The refusal names the offending PR URLs and the `t3 <CLI overlay> config_setting set … 0 --overlay <stored overlay>` escape. **Forge-authoritative third source (fleet-safety Stage 3).** The local union only sees PRs THIS instance opened — a sibling fleet instance's PR is invisible until its own reconciler imports it a tick later, the window in which two instances each open a PR for one ticket (invariant I2). When a caller supplies the repo's code host (all three seams do), `check_pr_budget` adds a live forge read (`core/gates/pr_budget_forge.forge_open_pr_urls_for_ticket` over `host.list_my_prs` — the client the PR scanners already use, no new forge client), scoped to the ticket's repo and attributed by the same `<slug>#<n>` close-keyword join the tick reconciler uses, unioned with the local set and deduped by URL (so a PR both sources know is never double-counted). It **fails OPEN**: a forge/network/rate-limit error, an unresolvable identity, or a missing host degrade to the local-only count so a forge outage never blocks all shipping; the read is memoised per `(ticket, repo)` for a short window so the two ship seams in one flow share one `list_my_prs` call.

**Directive intake front-end (north-star PR-6).** A `Directive` (`core/models/directive.py`) is a first-class guarded-row FSM in the `OuterLoopExperiment`/`MergeClear` family that turns a plain-language directive about teatree's own behaviour into a ratified, typed `MechanismSketch` (`core/models/mechanism_sketch.py`: setting key + neutral default, core policy chokepoint, overlay activation, acceptance tests, and the recorded **rejected alternatives** — the N=2 litmus). PR-6 implements the intake arc `CAPTURED → CLARIFYING → INTERPRETED → RATIFY_PENDING → ADMITTED` (post-`ADMITTED` implement/configure/verify states are declared but a later PR's). Interpretation is a headless `directive_interpreting` phase (the `CriticDispatch` idiom — `DirectiveDispatch.enqueue`) carrying its own `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE`/`_TOOLS_BY_PHASE`/`PHASE_REQUIRED_EVIDENCE["directive_interpreting"] = ("directive_interpretation",)`; the server-side recorder (`core/gates/directive_interpret_gate`) validates the sketch (structural checks in the model, the overlay-registry scope check in the gate) and writes it, or records clarifying `DeferredQuestion`s — maker≠checker. **DEFAULT BEHAVIOUR FIRST ([#4181](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4181)).** The kind catalog is three: `default_behaviour` (the DEFAULT — the constraint ships as the unconditional behaviour at the core seam, `setting_key` and `activation_scope` both empty), `setting_policy_gate` (the EXCEPTION), and `activation_only` (the duplication-check branch). A setting is minted only when a SECOND overlay demonstrably needs a different value, and the recorder REFUSES a `setting_policy_gate` that names no such consumer — an empty `activation_scope`, or an `activation_value` equal to the `neutral_default` — naming `default_behaviour` as the reshape. The refusal is what closed the shipped-inert class: the previous "constraint as data with a NEUTRAL default (core inert)" doctrine had queued 31 sketches to mint 31 settings that would never run. Ratification is one human `DeferredQuestion`; `Directive.admit` is the ONLY writer of `ADMITTED` and **raises without a consumed ratify question answered on an owner channel** (no auto-admit, mirroring the outer loop). The owner channels are `DeferredQuestion.OWNER_CHANNELS` — provenance the server records, never read from the answering process: a Slack reply whose recorded author is the owner's Slack user id (`loop/question_binding.bind_reply` binds no other reply), and the owner's own graduated policy. The MCP `question_answer` tool and the `questions answer` command line refuse an owner question and touch no row, so neither can admit or reject, and the #119 dial's metrics never count a non-owner answer as an approval (a decline on any channel but the MCP tool still re-tightens a graduated class). **UNDECIDABLE NEVER TERMINATES ([#4184](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4184), [#4187](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4187)).** Every reader of a ratify answer — this loop, the outer loop, and the #119 dial's decline metric — shares ONE classifier (`core/models/ratification.py`, in core because core cannot import a loop), pinned by `tests/conformance/test_one_ratification_classifier.py` so a fourth copy cannot appear silently. It reserves `DENIAL` for a bare refusal standing alone or an unnegated denial verb stated up front; a NEGATED approval ("not approved yet", "cannot approve until …") and a self-contradicted bare denial ("Nope, actually approve it") re-ask instead, because the rejected state is terminal with no recovery transition and a re-ask costs one round trip. The directive loop processes a captured directive when its Loop row is admitted; what bounds the arc is structural rather than a flag — the `DIRECTIVE` intent is unrouteable at intake (ambient directive detection is deleted, #105), so the router DROPs a DIRECTIVE-classified event and nothing writes a row unless the explicit `t3 directive capture` CLI is used (`Directive.objects.capture` is the ONLY producer), and every effectful step sits behind the human ratify gate. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Net-new-tech-debt merge gate (north-star PR-3).** `core/gates/debt_delta_gate.check_debt_delta` runs at the same two core PR-creation seams as the budget gate — `_run_ship_gates` (`pr create`, right after the budget gate) and the orphan-branch path (`_ensure_pr.create_or_defer_pr`), both through one `evaluate_debt_delta` orchestration — refusing a ship whose diff introduces **net-new** tech-debt suppression: a new `noqa` / `type-ignore` / `pragma-no-cover` comment, an unreferenced `pytest.mark.skip`/`xfail`, a new `per-file-ignores` entry, or a lowered `fail_under` coverage floor. The pure scanner (`teatree.quality.debt_delta.scan_debt_delta`, reusing `gate_relaxation.parse_diff` + `blank_string_literals`) reads only diff-**added** lines of `git branch_diff` (merge-base..HEAD), so pre-existing legacy debt is exempt and removing a suppression is always allowed — **delta, not absolute**: a shrink-only ratchet like the deferred-import-peg / module-health ledgers. The escape is an **audited plan-manifest waiver**: an `approved_debt` entry (`pattern` + non-empty `reason`) on the ticket's latest `PlanArtifact.adequacy`, mechanizing "no tech debt without explicit approval" as a recorded artifact rather than a silent bypass. The gate runs for every ship; an unverifiable diff (no real repo / git error) is a no-op, never a confirmed block. Diff-computed — no migration.
