**Merge-quality critic gate (north-star PR-4).** The SEMANTIC quality judgment on top of the deterministic debt gate: "clean + tested-enough WITHOUT bloat" becomes a real merge gate, so a merely-green auto-change that is not well-engineered does not merge. Two LLM-advisory rubric items at `transition="merge"` on the existing transition-keyed critic registry (`critic_rubric`) — `test_value` (anti-vacuity AND anti-bloat, anchored to the ratified `test_strategy`: the `MechanismSketch.acceptance_tests` for a directive ticket, the plan's `test_strategy` adequacy section for an ordinary one) and `cleanliness` (the CLAUDE.md bar) — are judged by the headless critic on the shared `critic_reviewing` phase, recording a `CriticVerdict(transition="merge")` at the shipped head. Judging is split from gating in time (the critic-gate doctrine — no LLM in the blocking path): `core/gates/merge_quality_gate.assert_merge_quality_verdict` runs a DETERMINISTIC row-check at `core/merge/execution.execute_bound_merge` (the single chokepoint both merge paths cross), refusing unless a verdict covers the exact shipped head and affirmatively PASSes every unwaived merge item (a FAIL, an uncited pass, or an OMITTED item blocks unless the owner waived that LLM item). Fail-closed like "CI must be green", and satisfiable — the gate arms the async critic when no covering verdict exists. Every ticket is gated unconditionally, including ordinary and directive work. No migration. The contract requires every redundant or vacuous test in ONE `test_value` citation, one per line (`REDUNDANT <node> — pinned by <node>`, `VACUOUS <node> — <why>`), lists every earlier merge verdict of the ticket (head, slug, status, citation) and requires a new finding on an already judged test to say what changed; a caller can supply tests changed since the last passing head for the critic to inspect. The coding directive carries the same questions and rule as a pre-push self-check, both rendered from `critic_rubric.merge_item_questions` and `EXHAUSTIVE_TEST_VALUE_RULE`. A crashed critic task is decided by its `CriticDispatch`, not by the per-ticket-phase budget (`loop/transient_requeue_critic`): while `attempts` is below `MAX_DISPATCH_ATTEMPTS` it is reopened by a compare-and-set increment, at the cap it escalates once, and it is parked with no question when a verdict covers its head, its dispatch is resolved or it was re-armed onto a newer task. The owner can waive one LLM merge item for a ticket (`ticket merge-item-waive`, `core/gates/merge_item_waiver`): the waiver is recorded in `Ticket.extra['merge_item_waivers']` with its owner identity and reason, covers the ticket across heads, is refused for any slug outside `llm_items("merge")`; a recorded owner Slack button answer is required, and a direct terminal or caller-supplied alias alone grants nothing, and `unmet_items` counts a waived item as met and records no FAIL finding for it.

The merge-quality and push gates read plan limits against the whole PR diff and each coding/debugging task limit against changes since its first recorded dispatch head, retained across retries of that task. Quoted text and attributed requirements from another issue or PR are never task instructions. A legacy task with no dispatch-head record contributes no round limit; the plan still binds the whole diff.

**Design critic — mechanism_placement + transition=plan generic-vs-hack judgment (north-star PR-5).** The anti-tech-debt tooth at DESIGN time: for a directive-implementation ticket, the plan must declare its generic-shape decision and a coder is never dispatched against a hack. Two layers. **Deterministic teeth:** a fifth plan-adequacy section `mechanism_placement` (`core/models/plan_adequacy.py`: `MECHANISM_PLACEMENT_SECTION` + `mechanism_conforms`) that a directive ticket's plan must carry — a CORE-seam chokepoint (never an overlay package — reusing `mechanism_sketch.is_core_seam_chokepoint`, the no-overlay-leak core-seam predicate), the constraint as the ratified setting + its neutral default, the ratified per-overlay activation, the N=2-litmus `rejected_alternatives`, and every refactor the sketch names. `mechanism_conforms` (whose own presence check is the 5th-section requirement) is enforced inside `plan_currency_gate.check_plan_current` (at `code()` + `schedule_coding`), so a non-conforming plan raises `NoCurrentPlanError` and BLOCKS coder dispatch. These directive teeth are checked UNCONDITIONALLY (H3): the `mechanism_conforms` call sits ABOVE the trivial-skip early-return, so a directive-linked plan is held to its ratified sketch even when a skip marker would carry the ticket past the general teeth — consistent with `merge_quality_gate`'s "directive tickets unconditionally" doctrine. It is a no-op for ordinary work (no linked directive), so ordinary tickets are never over-blocked. Type-aware value comparison keeps a JSON `false`/`0` (bool-vs-int) neutral-default/activation-value a drift. Because the teeth are unconditional, the trivial-skip marker does not disable them; the never-lockout escape is the audited all-reasoned-negative plan-bypass manifest (`mechanism_conforms` waives it) — a section-level `none_reason` does NOT waive (it is only reached once a mechanism was ratified, so a "mechanism-less" waiver is contradictory). **Semantic net:** four LLM-advisory rubric items at `transition="plan"` on the transition-keyed `critic_rubric` — `generality` (the N=2 litmus), `sketch_conformance`, `convention_fit`, `refactor_honesty` — judged by the headless critic on the shared `critic_reviewing` phase (`core/gates/design_critic_gate`), armed at `Ticket.plan()` for directive tickets and recording FAIL items as `CriticFinding(transition="plan")`. ADVISORY-only (the deterministic `mechanism_conforms` holds the block); the advisory-only design critic carries no independent switch — it runs for directive-linked tickets and is a no-op for unrelated tickets. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Directive loop runtime (north-star PR-7).** `loops/directive_loop/` drives the post-`ADMITTED` arc `IMPLEMENTING → CONFIGURING → VERIFYING → FULFILLED | REVERT_PENDING → REVERTED`, using guarded FSM rows, single-step `run_tick`, and injectable `TickSeams`. **IMPLEMENTING** schedules a real coder against the ratified `MechanismSketch` via `Ticket.schedule_coding` (the maker≠checker pipeline: plan gate → design/quality critic → cold review → critic-gated merge keystone), linking the synthetic ticket back with `extra["directive_id"]`; a `default_behaviour` sketch builds code the same way, and only an `activation_only` sketch skips straight to configure. **CONFIGURING** applies the overlay `ConfigSetting.set_value(key, value, scope)` only when byte-identical to the ratified sketch (the drift guard), confirmed by a `get_effective_settings` read-back; a global (empty-scope) mechanism needs no per-overlay row, so configure is a no-op that advances to verify. **VERIFYING** judges the five evidence classes — activation live, acceptance node-ids green at the merged tree, behavior probe clean (`loops/directive_loop/probes`), `no_collateral_regression`, and zero open `CriticFinding` rows — reaching `FULFILLED` only when all pass; a setting-less `default_behaviour` sketch has no activation to read back, so evidence 1 is carried by the other four rather than failed against a setting deliberately never minted; anything less rolls the config back instantly and parks in `REVERT_PENDING` for a human-asked revert (`t3 directive resolve-revert`) to terminal `REVERTED`. The guard chain uses shared probes and is ARC-SCOPED (#3643): the pre-admission INTAKE arc checks the budget, while the post-admission EXECUTION arc checks signal trust and the budget before changing config. Interpreting owner intent still STOPS at the structural human ratify gate (`Directive.admit` raises without a consumed, answered ratify question). A guard refusal is logged at warning level and printed as `WARN` by `t3 directive tick`, so a refused tick is never indistinguishable from an idle one; The loop remains subject to its `Loop` row, off-live-tick scheduling, and its code guards. Ambient directive detection is DELETED (#105): the intake scanner (`incoming_events.IncomingEventsScanner._execute`) DROPs a `DIRECTIVE`-classified event as an unrouteable intent, so the ONLY `Directive` producer is the explicit `t3 directive capture` CLI (`Directive.objects.capture`, `source=CLI`); the #116 firewall substrate (provenance stamping, reader profile, taint floor) is retained for other untrusted inbound processing and for #119's dial. CLI: `t3 directive tick|resolve-revert|history`. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Untrusted-input controls (#116).** Incoming events carry stamped provenance; the approval policy forces non-owner taint to `ASK` before consulting the dial. Ambient directive intake is disabled: only explicit capture creates a directive. No returned-candidate recorder or automatic sanitization layer is on the intake path.

**Directive dogfood + eval (north-star PR-8).** The capability's capstone evidence — the proof that a natural-language directive drives a clean, verified self-modification end to end. `tests/integration/directive_dogfood/` runs the WHOLE arc `CAPTURED → FULFILLED` on REAL components (the delta vs the PR-6/PR-7 unit suites, which fake every seam): real settings resolution from `ConfigSetting` rows, the real `directive_interpret_gate` recorder, a real ratify `DeferredQuestion`, a real admission `FactoryScoreSnapshot`, a real byte-conformant `ConfigSetting` activation + `get_effective_settings` read-back, a real `pr_budget_violations` probe over real `PullRequest` rows, the real `no_collateral_regression` fold over two real snapshots, the real `CriticFinding` count, and one real `run_acceptance_tests` subprocess; only the two host-wide guard seams (G3 signal-trust, G4 budget) stay injected. Four journeys cover the full proof case, rollback on a probe violation, the unconditional merge-quality and debt-delta gates on a loop-created ticket, and the explicit-intake/ratification floor. The evals live on the existing metered catalog (`evals/scenarios/directive_interpretation.yaml`): a `clean_room` interpretation-quality scenario (duplication-first → `activation_only`, deterministic field-level matchers pinned to the checked-in exemplar) and an `under_load` ratify-conformance scenario (never hand-apply a value that drifts from the ratified sketch — the skill's byte-law section), each with passing, failing, and no-op replay fixtures and anti-vacuity/contract-drift/exemplar-conformance pins so a doctrine or exemplar edit reds for free on every PR. No new model, migration, flag, or eval-runner — the delta is test/eval evidence, one skill-prose section, and this paragraph. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Snapshot-baseline attestation gate.** The `snapshot-baseline-gate` prek hook (`scripts/hooks/check_snapshot_baseline` over the pure `teatree.quality.snapshot_baseline` engine) refuses a commit that stages a Playwright visual baseline (a file under `__snapshots__/` or `<spec>-snapshots/`) unless the commit's worktree ticket carries a recorded visual-verification attestation — a green + posted `E2eMandatoryRun` read via `E2eMandatoryRun.has_visual_verification` (a per-ticket, any-SHA variant, since the tree the baseline lands on has no SHA yet at commit time). It rides the existing mandatory-E2E attestation row rather than a second store. Sibling never-lockout shape: the `snapshot_baseline_gate_enabled` kill-switch, the `ALLOW_SNAPSHOT_BASELINE='<reason>'` per-commit marker, and fail-open on a Django/DB error or an unresolvable ticket (crash ≠ deny). The live-post approval matcher (`cli/review/live_approval._has_approval_phrase`) additionally masks quoted spans so a quoted approval phrase (reported speech) never authorizes a `--live` publish.

**Browser tool: `t3 browser` (Playwright, headless).** `teatree.browser` keeps one headless Chromium per worktree in a detached keeper process (`browser/keeper.py`) that exposes a DevTools endpoint; each `t3 browser open|act|inspect|close` step re-attaches over CDP (`browser/session.py`). The keeper records console messages, page errors, failed requests, responses and navigations to the session's event log (`browser/evidence.py`), so `inspect` reports what earlier steps caused next to an accessibility snapshot, the HTML and a screenshot. A session is keyed by the git toplevel of the invocation cwd and ends on `close`, after 30 idle minutes, or when worktree teardown removes its state directory; the keeper holds a kernel lock on `keeper.pid` for its lifetime, and a step signals only that lock's holder, never a pid the file merely names. There is no headed mode. The visual-QA gate records pages through the same `EvidenceRecorder`. `t3 doctor check` FAILs when the headless browser cannot launch (WARNs when the launch only timed out), and `--repair` installs Playwright's `chromium-headless-shell`.

**Cron-shells-a-t3-loop gate ([#2663](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2663)).** `core/gates/cron_loop_shell_gate` (pure prompt classifier) + `hooks/scripts/cron_loop_shell_gate.handle_block_cron_loop_shell` (the PreToolUse hook on the `CronCreate|ScheduleWakeup` matcher) refuse a scheduled prompt that shells a `t3 loop` command the worker already drives. Two shapes: `t3 loops tick --loop <name>` is refused unconditionally (PR-28 retired the native `/loop` cron mirror and left no fallback plane), and `t3 loop <slot> run` is refused only while a live worker holds the `worker` singleton — so a genuinely worker-down box can still register the reactive slots it is the sole driver for. The worker-liveness probe reads the kernel flock (`utils/singleton.flock_is_held`), the same reading `queue_drain.a_worker_is_running` and `t3 worker status` take, and an unprovable probe reports not-alive so it can never manufacture a block. A sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[gate-escape:block-cron-loop-shell: <reason>]` token, the `cron_loop_shell_gate_enabled` kill-switch (`t3 <overlay> gate cron-loop-shell disable`, a `self_rescue` allowlist entry), and the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain (crash-proof — fails OPEN when `teatree` is unimportable). The rule's three recorded recurrences are why this is a gate rather than prose; the behavioural pin is `evals/scenarios/no_session_crons_for_t3_loops.yaml`.

**Standing-grant sign-off ask gate ([#2663](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2663)).** `core/gates/standing_grant_ask_gate` (pure, Django-free phrasing classifier) + `hooks/scripts/standing_grant_ask_gate.handle_block_standing_grant_ask` (the PreToolUse hook on the `AskUserQuestion` matcher, registered before the question mirror) refuse a question asking the owner to sign off a substrate merge that the overlay's standing grant already authorizes (invariant 4's standing opt-ins). The classifier needs a sign-off cue AND a substrate-merge object, and passes any ask carrying a cue the grant does not cover (a floor waiver, a HOLD override, a close/revert, a change to the grant). Only then does the hook resolve the overlay by the question's repo identity (session overlay as fallback) and read `core/merge/substrate_standing.configured_substrate_grant` — the same `substrate_standing_authorization` policy both merge paths use — denying only when a grant is configured. Running before `handle_mirror_question_to_slack` means a loop-driven ask is refused rather than deferred to the owner's Slack. A sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[gate-escape:block-standing-grant-ask: <reason>]` token, the `standing_grant_ask_gate_enabled` kill-switch (`t3 <overlay> gate standing-grant-ask disable`, a `self_rescue` allowlist entry), the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain, and fail-OPEN when the grant cannot be read.

**Extract-first advisory on an over-cap module ([#2663](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2663)).** `src/teatree/hooks/portable/check_module_health.over_cap_growth` / `growth_from_loc` are the ONE predicate answering "will the shrink ratchet refuse this growth?", consumed by both the commit-stage ratchet and `hooks/scripts/over_cap_growth_advisory.handle_over_cap_growth_advisory` (the PostToolUse handler on the existing `Bash|Write|Edit|…` matcher), so the two can never disagree about which growth is refused. The refusal itself now QUANTIFIES the deficit (`net +N` plus the extract-first remedy and [`docs/module-health.md`](docs/module-health.md)), so the offsetting extraction can be sized before any code is written rather than discovered as rework at commit time. Unlike every sibling gate here it has NO deny path, no kill-switch and no per-call token — `PostToolUse` carries no deny, the handler returns `None` on every path, and a source-scan test pins that no deny helper is reachable from it; the enforcement stays with the ratchet and this is only its earlier voice. Its `additionalContext` cannot collide with a second stdout payload on the same call — the only other `PostToolUse` writer, `handle_read_dedup`, fires exclusively on `Read`, which the advisory never handles, and a test pins that exclusivity. Deduped once per session per file through the router's swept state dir, precision-biased on Bash targets (an unpinnable `$VAR` path is passed over, never guessed at), and crash-proof in-handler. The behavioural pin is `evals/scenarios/rules.yaml::over_cap_module_extract_first`.

**Read-before-overwrite gate for tracked config/dotfiles ([#2661](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/pull/2661)).** `core/gates/config_overwrite_guard` (decision core) + `hooks/scripts/config_overwrite_guard.handle_block_config_overwrite` (the PreToolUse hook) refuse a blind destructive write to a user config/dotfile (a `dotfiles`-repo file, `.zshrc`, any file under an XDG `.config` dir regardless of suffix): a `Write` or `Edit` that overwrites an existing one from content the agent assumed rather than read, or a `git checkout`/`git restore` that restores a tracked one from a committed version — when the path was NOT read this session (the predicate consumes the existing `<session>.reads` capture `handle_read_dedup` maintains). The on-disk content is authoritative even when it diverges from the committed version (uncommitted edits beat git), so reading it first clears the gate. A sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[gate-escape:config_overwrite: <reason>]` token, the `config_overwrite_gate_enabled` kill-switch (`t3 <overlay> gate config-overwrite disable`, a `self_rescue` allowlist entry), and the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain (crash-proof — fails OPEN when `teatree` is unimportable, never tracebacks).

**Main-clone working-tree guard ([#2836](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2836)).** `core/gates/main_clone_guard` (pure git-command classifier) + `hooks/scripts/main_clone_guard.handle_block_main_clone_mutation` (the PreToolUse hook) deny a working-tree mutation of any REGISTERED (teatree-managed) main clone — a primary `.git`-*dir* checkout, distinguished from a linked `.git`-*file* worktree by the shared `running_from_worktree` helper. Blocked: an `Edit`/`Write` to a path under it, or a `git checkout`/`switch` to a NON-default branch / `reset --hard` / `restore` / `stash pop` in a main-clone cwd. ALLOWED (never locked out): `git fetch`, `git pull --ff-only`, `git checkout <default>`, `git worktree add/remove/prune/list`, and all read-only git — so `t3 update` and worktree creation keep working. The deny is UNIVERSAL (even the owner branches a worktree) and FAIL-LOUD, pointing at branching a worktree off origin/main. The managed-repo / worktree-vs-clone path toolkit (`resolve_branch_and_root`, `repo_root_is_teatree_managed`, `is_agent_state_path`, `default_branch`, …) is shared with `handle_protect_default_branch` via the `hooks/scripts/managed_repo` sibling. Sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[gate-escape:main_clone: <reason>]` token, the `main_clone_guard_gate_enabled` kill-switch, and the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain. Its companion `core/worktree/stale_clone_notice` makes the housekeeping self-update scanner and `t3 update` emit a DURABLE user-facing notice (a `BotPing`-backed bot→user DM, idempotent per clone/reason/HEAD) when they must skip a dirty/detached clone — naming the repo path + manual remediation, never auto-stashing/auto-checking-out to recover.

**Worktree placement gate ([#5247](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5247)).** `config.checkout_roots` resolves the durable worktree base and safe stored `workspace_dir` roots, disposable temp roots, and the source clone's `.claude/worktrees` harness root. The t3 creation and move seams refuse disallowed destinations before writing; raw Bash worktree commands cannot target the harness root; live or adopted checkouts remain usable. `core/gates/worktree_containment` classifies `git worktree add|move` targets in Bash commands, and `hooks/scripts/checkout_placement_gate` denies a managed-source command outside those roots. It follows bash's scoping (a subshell or pipeline side keeps its `cd` and assignments, a per-command `NAME=value` prefix is not the shell value, `export`/`+=` rebind) and, where the outcome is not statically knowable, judges every candidate source and target. A `sudo -D` or `parallel --wd` directory is the command's working directory, a target an `xargs`/`parallel` replace-string or a brace word fills is unknown, and a heredoc or here-string a shell reads is scanned as its script. A command with no worktree verb and no shell or `eval` invocation skips the scan. An unresolved source or internal hook error passes, while an unresolved target from a managed source is refused. `[gate-escape:worktree_containment: <reason>]` waives one call; `t3 <overlay> gate worktree-containment disable` sets the default-on `worktree_containment_gate_enabled` switch. The read-only `t3 doctor` worktree-health check lists every linked checkout outside these roots with counts of uncommitted paths and commits on no remote; an unreadable clone is named UNVERIFIED, never reported clean. No gate or doctor check moves an existing checkout.

**Blank-sub-agent dispatch gate.** `hooks/scripts/general_purpose_agent_gate.handle_block_general_purpose_agent` (the PreToolUse hook, registered right after `handle_enforce_skill_loading`) refuses an `Agent`/`Task` dispatch whose `subagent_type` is `general-purpose` — or absent, which defaults to it — when the brief names a repo the harness manages. Such a sub-agent starts with ZERO context, so it rediscovers the stack from scratch and works outside the workflow; the rule shipped as an advisory `additionalContext` injection, read as a suggestion, and was overridden about a dozen times in one session before it became a gate. The refusal names the typed agents that carry the context (`t3:coder` / `t3:debugger` / `t3:tester` / `t3:e2e` / `t3:reviewer` / `t3:bughunter` / `t3:planner` / `t3:shipper`, or `Explore` for read-only fan-out) so the caller can re-dispatch immediately. WHICH repos count is overlay knowledge, read from `managed_repo.overlays_registry`'s repo slugs rather than named in core, so the module stays overlay-agnostic and an overlay declaring no repos disables the gate for itself; matching is word-boundary anchored on each slug and its path segments. Scoping is by EXACT tool name — `TaskCreate`/`TaskUpdate` carry a description and no `subagent_type`, so a prefix match would deny them and block task tracking. Sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[gate-escape:general_purpose_agent_gate: <reason>]` token, the `general_purpose_agent_gate_enabled` kill-switch (`t3 <overlay> gate general-purpose disable`), an unreadable registry (no tokens → no deny), the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain, and every internal error fails OPEN.

**Orchestrator delegation gate.** `hooks/scripts/orchestrator_delegation_gate.handle_block_undelegated_investigation` (the PreToolUse hook, registered between the two existing orchestrator-boundary handlers) refuses a main-agent `Bash`, `Grep` or `Glob` call whose SHAPE has no ceiling on what it returns. The boundary was uncovered: the heavy-Bash gate keys on DURATION and a sweep is fast, and the investigation nudge is structurally forbidden from denying — so one attended session ran dozens of inline `rg` sweeps, log trawls and `… --json | python3 -c` reductions with nothing refusing. THE LINE is *routing reads a fact; investigation builds understanding*, decided from the command alone because a PreToolUse hook cannot see an output that does not exist yet. Two shapes qualify: a SWEEP (`rg`, `grep -r`, `git grep`, `find <path>`) carrying neither a count bound (`-m`/`--max-count`) nor a `| head`/`| tail`, and a PARSE (output piped into `python`/`node`/`ruby`/`perl`), the shape of *this was too big to read, so I wrote a program to reduce it*. The sweep patterns are anchored to a command HEAD and never fire after a single `|`, so a `grep` used as a downstream FILTER over an already-bounded output still passes — as do `git status`, `docker ps`, `gh pr view`, `t3 … list --json`, `| jq '.field'`, `head -50` and `sed -n '1,80p'`; `jq`/`awk`/`sed` and a BARE `python3 -c` are deliberately absent, being field extractors and routing arithmetic. It is NOT an arm of the heavy-Bash gate, because the two guard different resources and so cannot share an escape: that one guards RESPONSIVENESS, which backgrounding discharges, while this guards CONTEXT, which a backgrounded sweep still spends the moment its output is collected — so `run_in_background: true` is deliberately not an escape here. The NATIVE spelling of the same sweep is gated too, or the gate is routed around by construction: a `Grep` is bounded by `head_limit` or by a `path` naming ONE file, a `Glob` only by a literal stem in its pattern's basename (`**/*_scanner.py` passes; `**/*.py` is the refused `find . -name '*.py'` walk). `Read` and every other tool are allowed BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by an allowlist that goes stale, so `Agent`/`Task`, `TaskCreate`/`TaskUpdate`, `SendMessage`, `AskUserQuestion` and every session-bound MCP connector always pass. Scope is the ORCHESTRATOR only, via the discriminators the sibling gates already share — `call_is_from_subagent`, `session_lane() == interactive_cli` and `_teatree_engaged`. Sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[gate-escape:orchestrator_delegation_gate: <reason>]` token, the `orchestrator_delegation_gate_enabled` kill-switch (`t3 <overlay> gate delegation disable`), the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain, and every internal error fails OPEN. A native call carries no free-text field the token can practically ride in, so its per-call escape is the BOUND itself — which satisfies the gate rather than bypassing it.

**Silent-no-op guard for `glab mr create`.** `hooks/scripts/glab_stale_base_remote_guard.handle_block_glab_stale_base_remote` (the PreToolUse hook, registered right after `handle_validate_mr_metadata`) refuses a `glab mr create -R <slug>` that `glab` would answer with exit 0, no output and no MR: `glab` reserves the remote name `glab-base` for its own `-R` override and persists one into the cwd repository, so a later create aimed at a DIFFERENT project hits the stale override and bails silently. A must-fail call and a should-succeed call become the same empty result, which reads as a swallowed hook rather than a tool that gave up. It fires only on a real create (verb detected on the quote/heredoc-stripped skeleton) with an explicit `-R`/`--repo` target, run in a directory whose `glab-base` points elsewhere; a matching or absent remote, no `-R`, and a non-git cwd all ALLOW. Sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[gate-escape:glab_stale_base_remote: <reason>]` token, the `glab_stale_base_remote_gate_enabled` kill-switch (`t3 <overlay> gate glab-base-remote disable`), the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain, and every internal error fails OPEN. Its sibling in posture is the MR-metadata gate's typed skip (`gate_result.GateSkipped` + `warn_gate_skipped`): a call the gate RECOGNISES but does not evaluate names itself on stderr, so only "not an MR mutation" and a clean PASS stay silent.

**Foreign-branch push gate.** `hooks/scripts/foreign_branch_push_gate.handle_block_foreign_branch_push` (the PreToolUse hook, registered right after `handle_block_unknown_repo_push`) refuses a `git push` onto a branch the agent has not established as ours. The failure it forecloses is not hypothetical: an orchestrator dispatched a coder with "commit and push your branch" without establishing ownership, a 28-file commit landed on a colleague's open draft MR, and the remediation force-pushed their branch — a destructive rewrite of a third party's history. Prose rules covering every step existed and were not followed. It reads a command in three outcomes, with the known gaps named at the end of this entry. A push it can READ (through `&&`/`;`/`|`/newlines, backslash-newline continuations, `#` comments, `{ }`/`( )` groups and control-flow leaders, redirections, `cd`, `git -C`, `bash -Xc` clusters, `timeout`, bare wrapper leaders, env-var prefixes, short-flag clusters, a leading `+` refspec, and the tail of a heredoc OPENER line (`cat <<'EOF' && git push …`), which is ordinary shell and was otherwise removed with the body; `--dry-run`/`-n` writes nothing and is skipped) is judged by the ladder below. Comments are stripped BEFORE lexing, because `shlex.commenters` discards one with `readline()` — taking the newline that separates two commands with it — and ends a word at a `#` the shell keeps inside one (`origin feature#123`). Material it can NOT read that still runs a push — an unresolved wrapper such as `sudo -u`/`nice -n`/`env -i`/`eval`, a quoted script handed to a shell (`xargs -I{} sh -c '…'`, `docker exec box bash -lc '…'`, `setsid /bin/sh -c '…'`), a push PRINTED into a payload-less shell (`echo '…' | sh`), a heredoc a shell executes — resolved THROUGH the wrapper that hands the body over, so `docker exec -i box sh <<'EOF'` is `sh` and not `docker`, while `bash -c 'cat > f' <<'EOF'` reads its `-c` script and writes the body to a file — wrappers nested past the recursion cutoff, unbalanced quoting — is REFUSED, naming the single-line re-issue path, because a gate that cannot read a command must not conclude 'no push here'. A wrapped script and a heredoc body are both PARSED rather than pattern-matched: over the recorded corpus a text-level scan called every `grep -E 'git push'` and every `--get push.default` inside one a push, and the parse leaves 25 refusals across 209,299 unique commands, every one a real push, next to 1,869 pushes read. A push issued from data the shell does not execute — a script file, a `$VAR`, a heredoc fed to a non-shell interpreter — is outside this gate's perimeter (the pre-push hook and the forge are the other layers). It reaches no forge at all when the command carries neither outcome, because the handler runs on every Bash call. The shell flattening lives in the stdlib-only leaf `hooks/scripts/foreign_branch_push_shell.py`, the command-to-pushes walk in `hooks/scripts/foreign_branch_push_read.py`, and what ONE push's argv writes in `hooks/scripts/foreign_branch_push_argv.py` — short-option CLUSTERS expanded git's own way, so `-oskip.notify` is `-o` carrying a value rather than a group of flag letters (reading it as flags made its `n` a dry run and blinded the gate on a push that LANDED) — leaving the gate the ownership decision alone; a grammar parser is deliberately not used, because the hook runs under an interpreter with no project dependencies and an unimportable parser there would fail the whole gate OPEN. Which branches a REFSPEC-LESS push writes is asked of the repository rather than assumed: `remote.<remote>.push` first, then `push.default` (`matching`/`nothing` name no branch, so they are refused like `--all`; `current` judges the LOCAL name, not `@{u}`), with the push's own `-c` pairs replayed into the probe. The ladder, first answer wins: branch ABSENT from the remote (ours to create) ALLOW; an OPEN MR/PR whose source branch is the target is ours ALLOW and anyone else's REFUSE; no MR but the remote branch carries only another author's commits, or NO commits beyond the default branch (nothing on it says whose it is), REFUSE — except when the pushed branch IS the remote's default, which is compared against itself and therefore empty for every push of it, and then only while the push neither FORCES nor DELETES: rewriting or deleting the branch every other is cut from is refused whoever owns it, checked BEFORE ownership so an owned MR sourced from the default branch cannot answer 'ours' first, and no other rung at this layer would catch one (`handle_protect_default_branch` is scoped to `Edit|Write|Read` with a `file_path` and never sees a Bash push); anything unobtainable REFUSE, naming the probe that did not answer. Ownership is asked through `teatree.hooks.foreign_mr_cli`, which already owns that question for the pre-push hook. **It INVERTS invariant 10 deliberately** — every sibling above fails OPEN because over-denying costs a wedged session; here an un-asked question resolves to REFUSE, and there is NO per-call `[…-ok: <reason>]` token, because a waivable gate is a documented way to switch the gate off and the incident was an agent doing exactly what such a token would have permitted. A FORCE push or a DELETE this gate refuses additionally bypasses the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain (self-rescue allowlist, master fail-open switch) — a force leaves the branch and its reflog on the remote, a delete removes the ref outright. That bypass is decided over EVERY refusing item a command yields, never the first one read, so a force cannot hide behind a plain refusal beside it; it is raised only by a force flag among the PUSH's own words, so a wrapper's `-f` (`docker compose -f <file>`, `ssh -f`) does not raise one, and a force this gate ALLOWS is not a refusing item and escalates nothing. The only escape is the owner's deliberate `foreign_branch_push_gate_enabled` kill-switch (`t3 <overlay> gate foreign-push disable`), the same shape as the delegation gate. Known gaps, stated rather than implied: five shapes yield neither a push nor a refusal, so the gate allows them — a shell carrying its own `-c` script is read as running THAT rather than stdin, so a payload which re-executes stdin (`sh -c 'sh'`, `bash -c 'source /dev/stdin'`) runs a heredoc body unseen; a backtick substitution and a `$()` inside double quotes each stay ONE token, so neither becomes a segment a push can be read from; and a redirection PRECEDING the command word (`<<'EOF' sh`), or a backslash-newline continuation between the command word and its `<<`, leaves the heredoc's PHYSICAL opener line empty of a consumer, so the executed body is classified as data. All five are PRE-EXISTING — `main` reads none of them either, so none is introduced here. Coverage limit, stated rather than implied: the `Agent` tool's dispatch reaches `PreToolUse`, but Task/Workflow-dispatched work may not, so a push issued from a Workflow run bypasses this entirely — one enforcement point, not the whole perimeter.

The Codex approval and post-tool router subprocesses run with an explicit environment allowlist that carries the task's routed GH_TOKEN and its route marker, plus a fixed `T3_DISPATCHED_CODEX_ROUTER` marker the launcher sets itself. The push ownership gate reads the remote's first configured URL (refusing one that starts with a dash, and passing `--` before it), then runs `git ls-remote` and the authorship `git fetch` from a neutral temporary directory, leaving system and global credential helpers available while excluding checkout-local program settings. Under that router marker, a dispatched task's push-ownership credential refusal is marked in its structured result, recorded as an existing route failure, and advances to the next route candidate without an owner question. For checkable worktrees, coding and debugging results are refused when tracked changes are uncommitted or no commit is ahead of the base.

**Schema-readiness admission gate ([#3901](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3901)).** `SelfUpdateScanner` advances a live worker's HEAD on a cadence while applying the schema is a separate boot-time `init` step, so a merge that lands a migration left the process running code whose models the control DB did not carry. `core/schema_readiness` answers that in three values — `CURRENT` / `BEHIND` (named unapplied migrations) / `UNKNOWN` (the probe itself failed) — over the same `pending_migrations` the #869 pre-flight reads, memoised per alias for 60 s because the claim chokepoint consumes it. Both non-`CURRENT` states refuse admission at `claim_next_pending` and `claimable()`, exactly where `worker_quiescing` already gates: `UNKNOWN` cannot be laundered into a green light, and an unknown schema state never becomes a green light. In-flight leases renew through a different path and are untouched, so the gate defers new work without killing a live agent. The paired write half is `loop/scanners/self_update_schema.reconcile_schema_after_pull`, run the moment a clone advances, with `retry_pending_reconcile` re-attempting it on the ticks where nothing advanced — HEAD has already moved, so without the retry a reconcile that failed once (a transient locked SQLite is enough) would park the claim gate until a human intervened. The retry is gated on the memoised verdict, so a `CURRENT` control DB never walks the graph for it.

**Process-freshness admission gate ([#4387](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4387)).** The MIRROR of the schema-readiness gate, and the destructive direction: a long-running role that imported its model classes before a migration landed keeps claiming, an agent runs to completion, and the INSERT that records the result omits the new `NOT NULL` column — so `Task.complete()`'s single `transaction.atomic()` rolls back and a run that finished is stored as `failed`. `core/process_freshness` freezes the `max_migration.txt` head of every installed app in `CoreConfig.ready()` (no DB access, no migration graph) and compares that FROZEN snapshot against the applied head now, memoised 60 s. It never re-reads disk: `MigrationLoader.load_disk()` reloads the migrations package, so a process that re-read it would see the files the reinstall drain fast-forwarded under it and report `CURRENT` precisely when it is broken. Only a proven skew refuses (`claim_admission_block_reason`, the one composition every work-starting path calls); an absent snapshot, a snapshot that froze no head, or an unreadable probe ADMITS and warns (never a green `CURRENT` record on a comparison that never happened, [#4411](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4411)), because a fail-closed UNKNOWN here has no self-heal path — unlike schema-readiness, which clears the moment `init` migrates. Each reading is PUBLISHED to `$DATA_DIR/process-freshness-<role>-<pid>.json`, and `gates/schema_guard.doctor_check_process_code_freshness` reads those records rather than measuring itself: the watchdog runs `t3 doctor check` in a fresh process whose own snapshot is always current, so a self-measuring check reports healthy exactly while the stale worker is destroying results. The paired schema-level backstop is `db_default=""` on `Task.owner_pid_namespace` / `LoopLease.owner_pid_namespace` ([#4379](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4379)) — the gate protects processes running the gate's own code, the DB default protects every writer including ones older than it.

**Stranded-quiescing self-repair ([#4359](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4359)).** The third term of the same admission composition is `worker_quiescing`, and unlike the two schema directions nothing self-clears it: `deploy/deploy.sh`'s stage-2 drain sets it and its stage-7 `resume_admission` clears it, so a convergence killed in between halts ALL admission while `t3 worker status` reports RUNNING, the loops tick green, and the outage is visible only by reading the flag. `cli/doctor/self_heal_quiescing` therefore CLEARS that gate rather than only printing about it — the same `set_worker_quiescing(value=False)` write the deploy would have done, confirmed by re-read so an env or file layer outranking the config store reports a FAIL instead of a claimed heal. Deadness is established from LIVENESS, never from the gate's age alone: `cli/doctor/deploy_liveness` answers `LIVE` / `GONE` / `UNKNOWN` from the deploy's own in-progress record (`<pid> <heartbeat> <deadline>` written into the lock FILE under the flock and refreshed every 60s — `/proc/locks` is pid-namespace-filtered, so the flock itself is invisible to the containerised doctor; a heartbeat over 180s old or a passed deadline retires it) corroborated by the host process table, and `GONE` requires BOTH to answer. That table is refused when the deploy host is not Linux (`TEATREE_HOST_OS`, exported by `deploy.sh`): under Docker Desktop `/host-proc` is the VM's table, where a host `deploy.sh` never appears. What the venue cannot establish it refuses, falling back to the pre-existing report-only FAIL. Clearing this gate cannot admit work onto a mismatched control DB — the schema-readiness and process-freshness gates above are separate terms of `claim_admission_block_reason` and are untouched.

**Deploy checkpoint ([#5089](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5089)).** A drain closes admission, so its length is the factory's zero-admission window; waiting on the slowest in-flight run would hold it for the whole grace and then SIGKILL what was left, with no attempt recorded and the conversation lost. So an in-flight run checkpoints instead. `managers_task_claim.drain_block_reason` names the subset of the admission refusals that means THIS worker is going away: `worker_quiescing`, its own generation draining, retired or failed, or a process-local non-zero exit reason set by the worker before shutdown. A schema, process-freshness or fleet refusal is not in it, so a preset-off hold still lets runs finish. The runner's heartbeat (`agents/runner_heartbeat`) reads it on every beat and, once it is non-empty, interrupts the run — deferring at most `CHECKPOINT_MAX_DEFER_BEATS` beats while a tool call is open (`StreamCapture.tool_in_flight`), so a push or merge is not cut in half. The outcome carries the typed `checkpointed` flag, and `_record_stuck_outcome` parks it through the one park chokepoint (`usage_window.record_park`, `limit_parked: checkpoint: …`): the attempt keeps its session id and billed usage, `Task.park` returns the row PENDING with `session_continuation=SELF`, and the fresh worker's dispatch resumes that conversation. A park is excluded from `phase_attempts`, so a checkpoint burns no iteration, and a park that billed spend is never folded into an earlier park row. The drain waits only on claims a run is driving (`owner_driving_since`, or a claim under a minute old that has not marked its drive yet): an operator's in-session claim is executed outside the worker, cannot checkpoint and survives the swap, so it never holds a deploy. The drain therefore ends in about one heartbeat; `DEFAULT_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (600) is only the backstop for a run that cannot checkpoint, which the deploy then contains as before. `t3 worker status` and a doctor WARN for a run still CLAIMED past the drain stage read the RESOLVED gate (`loop.drain.quiesce_status`, env included); the loop line's `deploy drain` chip reads the STORED gate row (`stored_quiesce_status`) straight from the ORM, so a failed read drops only the chip and never marks the config tier degraded. The two can differ when the worker's environment overrides the stored row (`T3_WORKER_QUIESCING=0`); the chip is display only and nothing decides on it. A checkpoint attempt carries the one scheduling-park marker, so `classify_failure` names it `usage_limit_parked` together with the usage-window parks; its reason text (`checkpoint: …`) is what tells them apart. The park is taken only while the run still holds its claim generation, so a claim a rival took in between is left alone, and the heartbeat keeps renewing the lease after its one interrupt request until the run ends. Deploys are debounced in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`: a push to `main` waits 300 s in a `debounce` job that a newer push cancels, so a merge burst costs one drain-and-swap rather than one per merge, while the `deploy` job stays serialized and is never cancelled mid-run. The schema-lag admission gates are unchanged.

**Executor lock tolerance ([#5218](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5218)).** A `database is locked` error that escapes `django_tasks_db.Worker.run` closes only that thread's DB connection, waits from 0.5 s up to 8 s in stop-aware slices, and re-enters `run()` in the same thread. A run lasting at least 60 s resets the delay; a continuing streak logs one ERROR every 10 minutes. After the first ERROR, the supervisor publishes degraded worker health until the lock streak quiets. Other errors still kill the executor, and the supervisor still respawns it up to its budget. A respawn alive for 10 minutes refunds that budget. On any remaining non-zero exit from supervision, the supervisor publishes a process-local drain reason before stopping the pool and waits at most 300 s for heartbeat checkpoints, refreshing the `t3-master` lease during that grace; a run that cannot park keeps the existing lease-lapse recovery. SIGTERM exits the checkpoint grace early and follows the stop path.

**Finished-run lock wait.** `core/modelkit/db_retry.wait_out_locked_writes` is a reentrant connection scope, also a decorator, around `run_agent` and `record_result_envelope`: a statement that fails on `database is locked` is re-issued only when no transaction is open and never as `executemany`, with backoff from 0.5 s doubling to 8 s and at most 60 s per statement. Any other error is raised at once, and a nested scope adds nothing, so the budget never doubles. `drive_claim` clears `owner_driving_since` by compare-and-set on the timestamp it wrote, inside the same scope, so an older drive's exit leaves a newer drive's marker and a clear that meets a lock still ends cleared.

**Headless admission reads the same gate, plus the `dispatch` loop ([#4834](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4834)).** The django-tasks path — the `post_save` auto-enqueue in `core/signals`, `drain_queue_body`, and `execute_task`'s own entry — never asked `claim_admission_block_reason`, so a frozen factory kept running headless agents minted by FSM transitions. All three now read `core/admission/dispatch_mask.headless_admission_block_reason`, which is that composition plus the `dispatch` loop's mode/hold verdict (`loops.enable_verdict.loop_admits`); the arm sits one layer above `managers_task_claim` because `enable_verdict` reaches back into it through `core.models`. So `t3 loop preset use off --hold` is the freeze lever — `worker_quiescing` stays the deploy drain gate — and any preset masking `dispatch` (`low-token` too) stops headless claims. A withheld task stays PENDING and the next admitted drain picks it up. A run the stuck-run reaper re-enqueues while frozen is skipped by `execute_task` and stays CLAIMED until the unfreeze; it recovers on its own once admission reopens. The interactive paths (`/loop` spawn-claim, `tasks work-next`) stay on `claim_admission_block_reason` alone: they are explicit operator acts, and a masked `dispatch` loop already never ticks them. The stuck-ticket sweep is not gated: an operator cancel now survives a later lease loss (`agents/runner_interruption` records the interruption over a CANCELLED row without re-failing it), so the #4105 guard still sees the cancel, and escalation to a human keeps firing while frozen.

**Immutable worker generations.** A process started from a `teatree-factory:<sha>` image carries `TEATREE_GENERATION=<sha>`; `""` is a legacy or dev process and keeps every behaviour above. `WorkerGeneration` rows (`starting → active → draining → retired`, plus `failed`) gate admission per generation, both claim paths stamp `Task.claimed_generation`, and `drain_worker(generation=<sha>)` drains one generation without touching `worker_quiescing`. `deploy/roll.sh <rev>` builds the generation and runs `teatree/deploy/roll.py` from the new image, under the deploy lock: drain the generation the worker container carries, stop it, run the new `init`, bring the new one up, verify, promote and retire, rolling back on any failure after the drain unless the new init moved the schema. The image layers, the lock and record contract, the rollback rules, the compose override and the one-off routing are in [docs/blueprint/worker-generations.md](docs/blueprint/worker-generations.md).

**Pre-merge migration-fork probe ([#995](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/995)).** Two branches each adding a migration off the same parent both pass per-branch CI (each graph is linear), then the post-merge `migrate` fails with `Conflicting migrations detected`. `core/migration_leaf_probe.sha_forks_migration_graph` predicts the merged tree via `git merge-tree --write-tree` (no worktree mutation), parses every migration's `dependencies` against each app's Django label (the installed `AppConfig`'s, else the tree's `apps.py` literal, else the folder name), and refuses at §17.4 `ticket clear` when any app's graph would have more than one leaf, with a `makemigrations --merge` hint. Fails open on an uncomputable merge-tree (the merge-time CI re-check backstops). **Commit-time layer ([#2277](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2277)):** `django-linear-migrations` records a per-app `max_migration.txt` sentinel; a fork rewrites it with a conflict marker surfaced by `manage.py check --tag models` and a pre-commit hook (`scripts/hooks/check_linear_migrations.py`), so the conflict shows at commit time.

**Destructive-reinstall headroom gate ([#4338](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4338)).** `uv tool install --editable … --reinstall` deletes the working tool venv before rebuilding it, so a filesystem that fills mid-build leaves neither install — measured: 391 MB free, `typer` present, `click` absent, every `t3` invocation dead at import and the worker crash-looping. `utils/install_headroom` answers "is there room?" for both venues that run it: `deploy/entrypoint.sh`'s boot install (`require_install_headroom`, the bash twin of the same 2048 MB default) and `self_update.reinstall_running_editable`, which returns `deferred=True` WITHOUT invoking the runner so the previous venv is untouched. `loop/self_update_reinstall` maps that to `DrainOutcome.DEFERRED` and leaves the `PendingReinstall` row PENDING — marking it failed would let one transient low-disk moment disarm self-update for good. Fail CLOSED on a measured shortfall (proceeding is irreversible), OPEN on an unmeasurable filesystem (an absent reading is not evidence of no room). The boot install is then VERIFIED: `t3 --help` runs the whole import chain, and a CLI that cannot start fails init where it happened instead of surfacing downstream as an unrelated traceback.

**Hook tool env re-sync ([#4988](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4988)).** The hooks run under `${UV_TOOL_DIR:-~/.local/share/uv/tools}/teatree/bin/python` (`hooks/scripts/run-hook.sh`), importing teatree from the checkout's `src/` and every dependency from that env, so a fast-forward that bumps a dependency leaves them on a stale closure. One measurement serves every venue: `utils/dep_skew.running_env_skew` (declared versus INSTALLED, so an out-of-range version counts, not only a missing name), with `cli/doctor/skew_repair.remedy` naming the fix. The doctor's `_check_version_skew` reads it; `t3 update` re-syncs on it, measures again after the re-sync, and an env still stale is one `FAIL` line and exit 1. On a docker host the container cannot reach the host's `uv/tools`, so `deploy/deploy.sh` runs `deploy/sync-hook-env.sh` once the stack has converged: when the env's `uv-receipt.toml` names this checkout (compared as physical paths) it runs `uv sync --frozen --no-default-groups --inexact --python <env>/bin/python` into it — never `uv tool install --reinstall`, which replaces the receipt's entrypoint, on a docker host the `t3` launcher — and verifies with `python -m teatree.utils.dep_skew`, where exit 1 with output is stale and any other failure is "could not verify". A failed sync or a stale or unverifiable env fails the deploy naming the exact command to run by hand, and an unreadable receipt or no usable uv fails it naming the cause, each with a line saying the stack itself converged. A manual `git pull` of the host checkout between deploys stays stale until the next deploy.

**Fix-ticket FixRecord DoD gate ([#1661](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1661)).** `Ticket.kind` is `feature` (default) or `fix`. For `kind=fix`, `mark_delivered` (RETRO_RECORDED → DELIVERED) runs `check_fix_record_dod`: refused (`FixRecordDodError`) unless `Ticket.extra['fix_record']` carries `root_cause`, `evidence`, `regression_test`, `observed_red`, and `recurrence_fingerprint` — done = root cause + verified, not a merged manifestation patch. When `forced_repro_gate_enabled` is off for the ticket overlay, `observed_red` is optional in the gate and both recorders; the stored field is blank if omitted. Hatch `fix_record_override` with a reason; `feature` tickets pass. **The positive path ([#4520](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4520)).** That artifact is written from the agent result envelope, not a self-attesting CLI (rejected on `repro_waiver`'s own reasoning): the fixing agent returns a `fix_record` object, and `agents/fix_record_recorder.record_returned_fix_record` — chained into `attempt_recorder._record_returned_envelopes` beside the review/critic/directive recorders — validates it and writes it through `ticket.merge_extra`. One definition feeds every side: `core/models/types.FixRecord` yields `FIX_RECORD_FIELDS`, from which storage and the envelope JSON schema derive; `fix_record_required_fields` selects the mode-specific gate and recorder requirements, and `phase_blocks` renders the matching brief on `coding`/`debugging` for `kind=fix`. Compatibility on a cross-overlay schema is deliberate and asymmetric: an **absent** record is a no-op — an overlay that never emits one keeps today's behaviour and still reaches DELIVERED via the override — while a **malformed** one is refused at attempt-record time with `envelope_refusal.MALFORMED_FIX_RECORD_PREFIX`, so it classifies as a recorder refusal and earns `transient_requeue`'s one-shot corrective retry rather than being silently dropped. `fix_record`/`fix_record_override` are declared in `TicketExtra` because `validated_ticket_extra` strips undeclared keys on every `_extra()` write-back, so an undeclared record would not survive `Ticket.test()` on the way to the gate. **That rule is enforced for every key, not just this one ([#2663](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2663)).** `tests/conformance/test_model_extra_keys_are_declared.py` walks the whole tree for undeclared keys in each shape a write can take — the `extra["k"]` subscript, `merge_extra(set_keys=…)`, a key held in a module constant, a `cast("TicketExtra", …)` wrapper, and a `Ticket.objects.get_or_create(defaults={"extra": …})` literal — plus undeclared READS, which are dead by construction since no write of such a key survives a transition. Reads carry a tighter receiver rule than writes, because `Directive`, `OutboundClaim` and `OuterLoopExperiment` own `extra` JSON that no TypedDict constrains. Measured when it landed: `slack_answer` sat undeclared on 50 live tickets, and `directive_id` — which `Directive.for_ticket` resolves BEFORE its reverse FK — was stripped on the first transition, silently demoting the primary resolver to its fallback. The one shape an AST walk cannot follow is a dict literal reaching `merge_extra` through another object's field; that is closed by TYPING the producer (`_BoardItem.extra: TicketExtra`) so `ty` rejects the unknown key at the literal — which is precisely what the `cast` it replaced suppressed. And the strip is no longer silent: `validated_*_extra` logs one WARNING per (model, key) per process, since a hot read path must not spam but a drop nobody hears about is how #4152's revival cap read zero for months. The exception beside that route is `t3 <overlay> ticket fix-record-override <id> --reason '<why>'` (`_close_commands`, writing through `Ticket.record_fix_record_override`) — the gate's refusal has prescribed that command since #1661 while nothing implemented it, so its documented exception was unreachable until now.

**Forced-repro gate ([#118](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/118)).** The *executed* upgrade of `fix_record_dod`'s self-attested `observed_red` field: for `kind=fix`, `ship()` runs `core/gates/repro_gate.check_forced_repro` (registered via `register_gate`, invoked right after `local_e2e_dod`). Refused (`ForcedReproGateError`) unless `ReproEvidence.objects.has_valid_repro(ticket)` — a harness-recorded RED→GREEN pair with `provenance_ok=True` — or a `ReproWaiver` exists. The anti-fabrication proof lives in the guarded `ReproEvidence.record_red`/`record_green` factories: the harness (`t3 <overlay> repro record-red|record-green`) stamps both SHAs from `git rev-parse HEAD` and runs the command itself, so a RED must actually fail (exit ≠ 0), a GREEN must actually pass (exit 0), and `record_green` refuses unless the RED tree is a **proper ancestor** of the GREEN tree (`merge-base --is-ancestor red green` with `red != green`) — a RED captured at/after the fix proves nothing. The ancestry result is frozen into `provenance_ok` at record time, so the gate needs no git read. It sits in `ship()` (not `mark_delivered`) because the record commands need the live worktree, which `mark_merged` tears down. The `ReproWaiver` escape is **human-authorized** (`ReproWaiver.record` refuses a maker/coding-agent/loop `approver_id`), deliberately stronger than `fix_record_override`'s self-authored reason — a self-waivable repro gate would defeat itself. The gate runs for fix tickets unless the owner turns `forced_repro_gate_enabled` off for the ticket overlay (default on); that also stops the recorder refusal and GREEN replay and removes the prompt’s record-red line and the seeded rubric criteria that demand a failing run. It also removes `observed_red` from the fix brief and required FixRecord fields. `feature` tickets never fire. When the off switch exempts a fix lacking evidence, it emits a WARNING and adds the disabling setting scope to a durable ticket marker once per scope; rendering a prompt does not mark the ticket. Turning the setting back on requires a valid RED→GREEN pair or a human `repro waive`.

**Acceptance criteria have ONE home: the plan.** Each criterion has the first 32 hex digits of SHA-256 of its stripped text as its identity: `ticket rubric-waive` records an owner waiver on the ticket and removes that row, future plans skip its hash, and reviewer grades carrying a different hash are refused atomically with their verdict. The plan's `acceptance_criteria` section — the fifth `adequacy` section, validated like the other four — is the sole producer, and `PlanArtifact.record` turns it into the ticket's `Rubric` rows in the same atomic, so arming the done-gate can never mean arming it against an empty table. The rule is "a PASS grade must cite its test": `RubricCriterion.record_grade` refuses an uncited PASS, and the rationale is free-form prose naming ANY test kind (unit, integration, functional, e2e). **The PRODUCER is the reviewer.** `agents/review_envelope_recorder` records a reviewing task's returned `review_verdict.rubric_grades` — the reviewer already IS the independent verifier the gate requires (grader != maker) and the one actor that read the tree, so nothing else needs dispatching. It resolves the graded ticket through `core/merge/ticket_resolution.gated_ticket_for_review_task`, the SAME `resolve_gated_ticket` the merge gate reads (a reviewing task's own `task.ticket` is a reviewer-role row keyed by the PR url, so the rubric is unreachable from it). Coverage is checked BEFORE any write and the verdict, the claim retirement, the lock release and the grades share ONE atomic: `ReviewVerdict.record` retires the per-head claim, so a verdict written over a half-graded rubric would leave nothing to re-arm review while the gate still refused the merge — the head unmergeable forever. An ungraded criterion therefore records nothing, refused as `envelope_refusal.MALFORMED_RUBRIC_GRADES_PREFIX` so it earns the corrective retry rather than a page. `ticket rubric-grade` remains the operator's manual seam beside it. The `mark_delivered` chokepoint runs `check_rubric_verified` beside the fix-DoD check — no rubric is a block (zero proven criteria is the partial-subset claim), as is a PENDING, FAIL or uncited-PASS criterion. One audited waiver is the human-authorized `ticket plan-bypass` — the all-negatives manifest only `record_bypass` writes, so a planner can never waive its own grading; it waives a missing or ungraded rubric (the phase-seeded process criteria the ticket never declared included). The delivery-only owner-merge exception also waives missing or ungraded criteria after forge evidence binds `merged_by` to an owner alias; neither path overrides a recorded FAIL.

**A review whose verdict could bind to no tree is refused BEFORE it is paid for.** `review_envelope_recorder` already refused a verdict whose pull request carries no recorded head — correctly, since such a verdict binds to nothing and no merge guard could read it — but it refused AFTER the review ran: 53 refusals over eight days each paid for a full review whose verdict was then discarded, 29.8% of one window's metered spend. The precondition is two pure DB reads on rows that exist before the task is claimed, so the answer at dispatch time IS the answer at record time. `core/gates/review_recordability_gate` asks it first, at the two seams that matter: `dispatch_refusal.pre_harness_refusal` (the chokepoint no headless dispatch avoids, so the refusal is recorded with zero turns billed) and `loop/persistence._handle_reviewer` (the producer seam, which re-emits every tick and carries no failure memory — which is why the recurrence was 53 freshly-minted tasks rather than one retried; a broadcast with no head has it read from the forge, and an unreadable or closed pull request mints nothing and logs a warning). It calls `review_target_for_task` rather than re-deriving the head, so the pre-check and the post-check cannot disagree about which PR and which head the review is answerable for; `review_target_for_ticket` is the ticket half of that same resolver. Scoped to `phase_tools.ENVELOPE_VERDICT_PHASES` — the phases whose brief RETURNS the envelope — because the `codex_*` and `e2e_reviewing` lanes record through the shell off the reviewer's own checkout and never read `ticket.extra`. FAIL FAST, not park: no amount of waiting stamps a head, so parking would invent a wake-up condition that does not exist; the refusal is stateless and the first tick after a producer supplies the head dispatches normally. `FailureKind.REVIEW_UNRECORDABLE` names it (HALT, non-environmental), which is what lets `stall_kinds` see two in a row instead of dropping them as unnamed. An own-PR review (an author-role ticket whose `issue_url` names its open pull request, with no `AutoReviewDispatch`) has one more precondition, asked first in `runner._preflight` by `agents/own_pr_review_head.prepare_own_pr_review_head`: the ticket's checkout and the forge must share one head, checked again before stamping. A clean checkout strictly behind the forge head is fast-forwarded to it and `Ticket.rearm_review_at` stamps that head, all in the same dispatch; an equal clean head with readable, nonfailed required checks is stamped without a fetch. Uncommitted or unpushed work, failed or unreadable required checks, or an unreadable forge, process table, checkout or fetch park the task instead (a `review_held:` attempt, `Task.park`, no turn billed), and only the owner's own work earns one deduped INTERNAL `DeferredQuestion` per pull request, forge head and checkout digest. `review_held:` is a park like the limit and checkout parks: outside repair iterations, and the cycle-time breakdown attributes it to review.

**Review-state FSM chain (PR-08).** Four gates around the review-request/close/review flow, with their evidence and thresholds so a normal reviewed-and-cleared PR still proceeds, backed by the new `ReviewEvidence` model (two kinds `cold_review`/`integration_review`; guarded factory with maker≠checker + full-SHA validation; recorded via `review record-evidence`). (a) **Review-request SELF_REVIEWED-state gate** (`core/gates/review_request_state_gate`): `review_request_post` refuses a broadcast for a ticket-owned merge request unless the ticket has passed the `SELF_REVIEWED` milestone (`ticket_review_state.has_passed_review` — `SELF_REVIEWED` or any later maker state; the broadcast fires at `request_review` time so a progressed ticket is `PR_OPENED`/`REVIEW_REQUESTED`, not the momentary `SELF_REVIEWED`, when it goes out — PR-08b) with a review-evidence artifact — a `ReviewEvidence` cold-review row OR an existing `ReviewVerdict` from the cold-review step (so the artifact stays recordable by that step). Pre-review states are still refused. This gate always runs. (b) **Cross-repo integration-review DoD gate** (`core/gates/integration_review_gate`): the same `mark_delivered` chokepoint runs `check_integration_review` — a ticket touching ≥ 2 repos cannot close without a `ReviewEvidence` integration review covering the combined changeset (`IntegrationReviewError`). The gate always runs for multi-repo tickets; single-repo tickets never fire; hatch `integration_review_override`. (c) **No-bulk-close gate** (`core/gates/bulk_close_gate` + `ticket bulk-close`): a single close action over `bulk_close_threshold` (default 5) tickets is refused without an explicit per-item confirmation token — the deterministic replacement for the backlog-sweep prose ask-gate. (d) **Review-shape gate** (`cli/review/inline_shape_gate`, in the pre-publish chain): refuse an MR-level draft note when inline drafts already exist on the same MR (stay inline once inline); `--force-general` escape.

**Merge-evidence FSM gate (self-catch, work-tracking-truth).** The terminal `MERGED` state is unreachable without real merged-SHA evidence. `Ticket.mark_merged()` and `reconcile_merged()` each run `core/gates/merge_evidence_gate.check_merge_evidence` (registered via `register_gate`, invoked exactly as `ship()` invokes `local_e2e_dod`): the transition is refused (`NoMergeEvidenceError`) unless a `MergeAudit` row with a real `merged_sha` exists for the ticket's PR, OR the overlay's live forge merge-state probe (`fetch_pr_merge_state`) confirms the PR merged. This kills the believe-done-not-done class — the ungated `_advance_ticket` walk (and the loop's `complete_ticket` action) could previously mark a committed-and-tested-but-unpushed ticket done. **Fail-closed:** an inconclusive/erroring forge probe yields no evidence, never a false pass. **Never over-blocks the keystone:** `merge.execution.record_merge_and_advance` writes the `MergeAudit` row atomically BEFORE calling `reconcile_merged()` in the same transaction, so an authorised merge always passes. **Forge fallback:** a genuinely merged PR whose `MergeAudit` row is absent (a manual merge) still passes when the live forge confirms the merge. An inconclusive forge read refuses until evidence is available. Evidence naming a PR whose merge was voided (below) does not count; a different PR still does.

**A merge that declares a remainder keeps its ticket out of `MERGED` ([#5670](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5670)).** Most merged pull request bodies link their issue with a non-closing phrase (`Part of #N`, `Relates to #N`; a merge never closes an issue), so the phrase alone says nothing about whether the work is finished and is never the trigger. `core/merge/partial_merge.py` reads the merged body once, before the keystone's row-locked block (`record_merge_and_advance` -> `read_merged_pr`; a forge that does not answer reads as no remainder, and a read never rolls back the consumed CLEAR or the audit). A closing keyword (`Closes`/`Fixes`/`Resolves`) naming the ticket's own issue keeps today's behaviour. A non-closing link to the ticket's own issue holds the ticket only when the body itself declares a remainder: an acceptance-status table row marked Unmet or Partly met, an `Unmet:` / `Remaining after this PR:` line, a `stays on #N` / `remains on #N` line naming this issue, or a `does not close` / `the issue remains open` / `keeps the issue open` sentence (a bare `open:` caveat line about the pull request itself declares nothing); when the body declares none, the issue's own unchecked boxes under an Acceptance heading that the body does not quote count. The markers live in one place in that module. A held ticket is not marked `MERGED` and its worktree is not torn down: `hold_for_remainder` returns it to `WORK_STARTED` through the transition it already has (`reopen()` from `PR_OPENED`/`REVIEW_REQUESTED`, `rework()` from `CODED`/`TESTED`/`SELF_REVIEWED`, a walk up from the early states), records the pull request with `stamp_reopened_over` so the board reconcile's rule A does not merge it again on the next tick, releases the merged pull request's branch binding so `ship` cannot adopt it for the remainder (`t3 doctor`'s PR-branch check skips URLs whose pull request row is merged), and queues one planning task whose reason names the unmet items (the planning task completes only from an early state, which is why the ticket cannot keep `PR_OPENED`). A later merge that declares the identical remainder is not held again, so a body that never changes cannot loop. `PLAN_RECORDED` has no transition back to planning and a ticket at `MERGED` or later is past the rule, so both keep today's behaviour. Several pull requests on one ticket: it reaches `MERGED` when a closing one merges or when the last merged one declares no remainder.

**Historical partial-merge inventory.** `t3 <overlay> ticket partial-merge-report` is read-only: it inspects author tickets still at `MERGED`, checks their issue is open on the forge, and applies the same remainder parser to the last merged pull request body. Merge audits order multiple pull requests; an unknown order or unreadable forge answer is reported explicitly, not counted as a clean ticket. The report identifies old tickets to reopen through the ordinary ticket lifecycle.

**Merge void (human-only correction).** A forge can report a pull request merged while its content never reached the default branch (a history rewrite force-pushes the head onto the base, and GitHub then records head == base == merge commit). `t3 <overlay> pr void-merge <pr-url> --reason <why> --by <operator> [--dry-run]` corrects the ledger for exactly that case. One read, `CodeHostBackend.fetch_pr_content_snapshot`, gathers the forge facts: when the head equals the base the content head is the newest `HeadRefForcePushedEvent.beforeCommit` (the merge base retried from the newest `BaseRefForcePushedEvent.beforeCommit` on "No common ancestor"), plus the merge commit, the default tip, the history position of the content head and of a merge commit that carried a diff, and four path-to-blob trees. The pure `core/evidence/forge_content.classify_content` permits a void only when the content head and that merge commit are both outside the default history and NO changed path matches the merge commit or the tip; an empty diff, a GraphQL `errors` payload, an HTTP 403/429, a truncated tree or a tip that moves mid-probe is UNKNOWN and refuses. GitLab answers UNKNOWN by name. The write is human-only: the command refuses the loop runner principal, any process carrying the `T3_DISPATCHED_AGENT` marker the Claude and Codex spawn seams set, and an agent-looking `--by`; it then needs a real terminal answering yes, re-plans against the forge and refuses if anything moved, and writes in one transaction. Those refusals, the terminal answer and the forge content check are what keep an agent from writing a void. As defence in depth, a PreToolUse guard on agent Bash calls and the shared hard-deny registry refuse a command whose text names `void-merge` (literally, with quotes and line continuations removed, or once the outer shell's quoting, `$'…'` included, is decoded, so a mention inside a `bash -c`, `script -c` or `ssh` string, a `$(…)` substitution or a heredoc counts), unless every mention is a standalone `t3 <overlay> pr void-merge … --dry-run` or `--help` call whose words up to that flag are literal and leave it a flag rather than an option's value. The guard does not see a word built at run time (a variable, `$(printf …)`, a Python call, a script file, a glob matching a planted file) or these shell spellings: `$"…"` quoting, a `${x:-void-merge}` default, a brace list, an escaped spelling inside a nested `bash -c`, `script -qec` or heredoc, `eval` of an escaped word, and a shell function named `t3`. The write records one `MergeVoid` (keyed by host, case-folded slug and PR number, with the merge commit, content head, merge base, default tip, full changed-path list and the per-row plan as evidence), closes each MERGED `PullRequest` row through its `void_merge` transition (conditioned on that row, MERGED -> CLOSED), and returns each merged-or-past ticket that rests solely on this PR to the from-state of its newest edge into merged through the self-guarding, non-FSM `Ticket.void_merge`, which writes its own `TicketTransition`. A ticket still evidenced by another, non-voided merged PR is kept and reported; a ticket with no recorded edge into merged refuses the whole void. Every ledger row and `extra` key is kept. Every reader asks one snapshot, `MergeVoid.objects.voided_refs()`, which matches a PR through `PrRef.same_pr` (a blank host on either side matches); a structural test refuses a module that queries the table or the snapshot's rows itself. The merge-evidence gate stops counting audits, recorded forge evidence and live probes that name a voided PR, `record_forge_merge` leaves its rows closed, and the board janitor skips a ticket whose own url is a voided PR, or that a void restored while no open or merged PR outside the voided set carries it (rules B/C, D and F); after the write the command reminds the operator to reopen the ticket's issue before re-delivery, because rule F retires a ticket whose issue stays closed. `ticket show`, MCP `pr_for_ticket` and the dashboard PR chip ("Merge voided") show it.

The messenger route starts with `pr request-void <pr-url> <reason>`, which records a card with the forge verdict, affected records and the requester's reason for the owner to approve. Only a Slack answer by the configured owner can authorize its action. The owner-action tick re-plans from live forge evidence before taking the write lock, compares the bound plan while allowing the default tip to move if the content verdict stays absent, then writes the existing merge correction and an outcome audit in one transaction. A decline records no ledger change. The terminal route remains available.

**Plan-adequacy + late-bound-plan gate (self-catch, SELFCATCH-3).** Forecloses the named root cause of the 26-bug integration campaign: thin-spec-as-plan and stale-base coding. `PlanArtifact` gains `base_sha` (the target HEAD the plan was authored against) and an `adequacy` five-section manifest (`design`, `integration_seams`, `edge_cases`, `test_strategy`, `acceptance_criteria`; each section substantive OR an explicit reasoned negative — the proven `no_new_tests` shape, so silence never passes; pure validators in the leaf `models/plan_adequacy`). Unconditionally, `PlanArtifact.record()` refuses a new row without a 40-char `base_sha` and a complete manifest — a scope+acceptance thin spec structurally fails (the audited `record_bypass()` sibling writes an all-negatives manifest so `plan()` still advances). `core/gates/plan_currency_gate.check_plan_current` (registered via `register_gate`) runs on `Ticket.code()` (PLAN_RECORDED→CODED) AND at the top of `schedule_coding()` — covering the **normal author PLAN_RECORDED→CODED flow** (not corrective/synthetic re-entry: the loop's `_handle_red_card` / `_handle_skill_drift` synthetic tickets and the review-loop author leg mint a `coding` task directly and are intentionally exempt, since those tickets carry no plan of their own). **Stale-is-absent** — a plan whose `base_sha` moved off the live target HEAD AND whose intervening commits touch a declared seam (`git log base_sha..HEAD -- <seam paths>` via `branch_currency`'s fetch machinery) is treated as ABSENT (`NoCurrentPlanError`). **Never-lockout:** the error names the exact remediation, distinct per case — a STALE-but-adequate plan reaffirms with `plan-reaffirm <id> --base-sha <new> --disposition <…>` (carries the adequate manifest forward, one disposition per intervening seam commit); an INADEQUATE/legacy plan supplies a fresh manifest with `plan-reaffirm <id> --base-sha <new> --adequacy-json '<…>'` (carrying-forward an inadequate manifest cannot make it adequate); the `trivial-skip` and `plan-bypass` escapes stay. **Fail-open** on any inconclusive git probe (no worktree, offline fetch, unresolvable range). Declared seams are plumbed into the coding-task prompt + review context (data only).

- **3 DETERMINISTIC blocking teeth** — `spec_not_plan`, `done_not_done`, `completeness`: a pure predicate over REAL artifacts that REUSES its sibling gate (`plan_currency`/`plan_adequacy`, `merge_evidence_gate.has_merge_evidence` + `ticket_worktree_checks`, `rubric_gate.check_rubric_verified` — no-rubric is a FAIL) and fires on ABSENCE. These are the ONLY items that can block. No LLM in the blocking path.
- **5 LLM advisory items** — `coherence`, `duplication`, `deferred`, `ignored_input`, `unenforced_guarantee`: determinism cannot read them, so (when live) the gate ENQUEUES a headless critic on its OWN `critic_reviewing` phase (`CriticDispatch.enqueue`, a clone of `auto_review_dispatch`) that judges them against the REAL delivered artifacts (plan text, the PR diff, the intake `AttachmentManifest`) and RETURNS a `critic_verdict` envelope. The dedicated phase is load-bearing: it carries its own `PHASE_REQUIRED_EVIDENCE["critic_reviewing"] = ("critic_verdict",)` + `_TOOLS_BY_PHASE` (read-only+web) + `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE` entry, so `attempt_recorder.record_result_envelope` measures the critic result against the critic evidence contract and reaches `record_returned_critic_verdict` — under the reviewing phase a critic result (only `critic_verdict`) would fail `check_evidence("reviewing")` and never record. It records the `CriticVerdict` server-side (maker≠checker — `grader_identity` refused if a maker/loop role, mirroring `ReviewVerdict`), then mirrors the verdict's FAIL items into `CriticFinding` — ADVISORY, never blocking. They do NOT read any self-declared `extra` key. Only a `mark_delivered` verdict re-runs the delivery rubric when it returns; a `merge` or `plan` verdict on the same phase is recorded and mirrored by its own gate, so `done_not_done` is never judged before the merge ([#5075](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5075)).
