**Always-on critic gate.** Every delivery records deterministic findings and dispatches the async LLM critic when no fresh verdict covers the head. The returned verdict is recorded and semantic findings are advisory. A blocking deterministic finding refuses delivery (`CriticGateError`, ticket stays RETRO_RECORDED). **Rollback safety:** `mark_delivered` runs inside `transaction.atomic()`, so the block's raise would roll back the findings — `CriticGateError` carries the specs and `execute_retrospect` re-records them on a fresh transaction after the atomic unwinds. **Anti-theater:** a predicate that raises OR an uncited LLM pass is recorded as `instrumentation_gap` (a FAIL, never a silent pass); a now-clean item's stale finding is deleted so the row set is the latest verdict. Deferred (tickets filed): the `plan()`/`review()` bindings, the dream-promotion of RedCardSignals into rubric candidates, and the `critic_catch_rate` rubber-stamp signal.

**Unreadable critic evidence.** The critic phase has no shell and reads the change with `github_pr_diff`, `read_file`, or `search_files`. An item explicitly marked `unverified`, or a return that says the diff could not be read, fails the task without writing a verdict or a failed item. The unresolved per-head claim re-arms immediately within its existing retry budget. An uncited claimed pass still becomes `instrumentation_gap` and never clears a merge item.

**Reviewing-phase review-skill evidence gate ([#1539](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1539)).** When `review_skill` is set, recording the `reviewing` attestation is refused (`ReviewSkillEvidenceError`) unless `Ticket.extra['review_skill_run']` attests the skill ran (`lifecycle record-review-skill-run`). Empty (default) is a NO-OP. Distinct from the `architectural_review_skill` cadence scanner. Both names must resolve to an installed skill for the gate/cadence to do anything, so `t3 doctor` (`_check_configured_review_skills`, [#3352](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3352)) resolves the effective `review_skill` / `architectural_review_skill` against the canonical skill set (`ref_validator.canonical_skill_names`) and hard-FAILs on any configured name that resolves to no `SKILL.md` — the `ac-reviewing-skills` → `ac-reviewing-codebase` incident class caught at the live config site, not just the `agents/*.md` sites `validate-skill-refs` covers.

**Declared-dependency provisioning gate ([#3652](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3652)).** A dependency the configuration declares REQUIRED but nothing provisioned must be a loud `t3 doctor` FAIL, never silence. `teatree.provisioning.declared` enumerates the mandate from `apm.yml`, `pyproject.toml`, and enabled plugin settings, so a checker-local list cannot drift behind the declarations. `teatree.provisioning.probes` answers provisioned-ness against the surface the consumer reads, and `_check_declared_dependencies_provisioned` names every gap and its remediation. `t3 setup` groups each enumerable APM skill by pinned package and asks the pinned skills CLI to install only those selected names for both Claude Code and Codex, idempotently. **The mandated skill set is `architecture-design`, the three `souliane/skills` companions, FastAPI's own `fastapi` framework skill, and the seven individually pinned Superpowers methodology skills TeaTree requires.** The individual Superpowers subpaths prevent setup from installing the whole upstream bundle. In-repo TeaTree skills install from the running checkout; external skills install from their reviewed package/ref. `ac-reviewing-codebase` is loaded by `t3 assess`; the `architectural_review_skill` default is the in-repo `architectural-review`, and no in-repo skill may share a bare name with a declared one: the resolver refuses a declared name satisfied first by a folder no installer manages (`skill_support.pin_shadow`), recorded as a dispatch refusal by both the headless lane and `claim-next` and named in the `SessionStart` context, and `t3 doctor` FAILs that, an install that resolves outside the install roots or into a git checkout (a link, a linked `references/`, a linked install root, a checkout in place of a copy), and any `dependencies.apm` entry, whole-repo bundles included, whose `#ref` is not a 40-hex commit. **Every install path is pinned.** `skill_source.pinned_commit` is the one full-commit test and returns the lowercase ref: `t3 setup` refuses any other spec before the skills CLI runs, hands the CLI that same lowercase string, and refuses a pin that is also a branch or tag name on its source (`skill_pin.refs_named`, one `git ls-remote`), because the CLI clones `--branch <ref>` before it fetches a commit. A source it cannot read (offline, or private: there is no `gh`/SSH fallback) is refused fail-closed and the installed copy is left unchanged. A refusal leaves the strict-setup ready marker unwritten, so the worker stays down until the cause is fixed. Overlay skill sources (`install_published_skills`) and `SkillLinker` never install or link over a declared pin. The skills CLI's install record (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/skills/.skill-lock.json`, else `~/.agents/.skill-lock.json`) stores the requested ref, so `t3 doctor` compares it with the pin as a consistency check, not as proof of the installed commit. Named gap: the ref check and the CLI's clone are two network reads, so a ref created between them is caught only by the next `t3 setup`. The checks read `repo/apm.yml`, which a `uv tool install --from git+…` wheel does not carry. **An `apm.yml` that exists but cannot be read is one refusal on every surface that resolves skills** (`SkillPinsUnreadableError`, a `SkillPinRefusalError` like the shadow refusal): the headless lane and `claim-next` park the task (PENDING, five minutes, under the `limit_parked:` marker, repeats folded into one attempt row) and never dispatch an empty bundle, so the queue-stall alarm still fires and the repair budget is untouched; `t3 setup` reports not-ready (it exits non-zero only under `--strict-agent-skills`), `skill-preamble` and `config write-skill-cache` exit non-zero, `SessionStart` names it, and `t3 doctor` FAILs a malformed manifest while an absent one stays a WARN.

**Skill-supply gates.** Two failures the readers above cannot see, because both live on surfaces they do not enumerate. `_check_dispatched_overlay_skills` covers ABSENCE at the dispatch site: an overlay's `stage_skills` / `companion_skills` / `pr_review_companion` name skills the lifecycle loads on every ticket reaching the phase, and one that resolves to no `SKILL.md` loads nothing and says nothing — the phase proceeds without the discipline the skill carried. Enumeration comes from the overlay config itself, so a skill wired in later is gated with no change to the check. `_check_skill_source_drift` covers STALENESS: skills install as dereferenced physical COPIES, and a copy cannot announce that its source moved on, so a merged fix reaches nobody until each person separately re-installs. `teatree.provisioning.skill_drift` compares installed bytes against the source clone's reviewed ref OFFLINE (`git ls-tree` / `git grep` read a ref directly — no fetch, no network). Both hard-FAIL inside `_check_enabled_but_unprovisioned`.

**Skill-pin freshness.** The third staleness question, and the only one the two gates above structurally cannot ask: they compare an install against the PIN, so a fresh install at a months-old pin is clean by both while work merged after that commit reaches no consumer at all. `teatree.provisioning.skill_pin` compares the PIN ITSELF against its source's current head — the pins read from `declared.skills_declared_in_apm_manifest` (never a checker-local list), one `git ls-remote --symref <url> HEAD` per source repo rather than a fetch or clone, and an entry carrying no `#<ref>` skipped because it declares no pin to bump. A source that cannot be reached — no network, no auth, rate-limited, no resolvable HEAD — is UNMEASURABLE, never current. The answer needs the network and `t3 doctor check` is the offline lane, so the work is split: `t3 setup`'s `SkillPinAuditor` takes the measurement, suggests each bump, and RECORDS the verdict with its timestamp, and `checks_skill_pins._check_skill_pin_freshness` reads that record offline. A recorded verdict ages, so past a 14-day horizon the finding becomes UNVERIFIED again rather than staying green — an absent, aged, or unmeasurable record all report themselves, and only a recent measurement finding every pin at head is silent. Unlike its two neighbours this **never gates**: a pin may be held deliberately, so a trailing pin is an INFO carrying the sha to move to and the pasteable `declared.skill_bump_remediation` line (the manifest entry first, then `apm install <spec>` at the new sha — fixing only the install would leave the mandate behind).

**Recommended (not mandated) skills ([#3668](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3668)).** The counterpart surface to the gate above: a skill teatree SUGGESTS but does not require. `teatree.provisioning.recommended` carries a curated `RecommendedSkill` list — each with a pinned `apm` source, a provider caveat, and a scope note — and `checks_recommendations._check_recommended_skills` surfaces every absent recommendation as a `t3 doctor` **INFO** that never gates the exit code (it is not installed by default and its absence is not a FAIL). The first entry is the agent-platform vendor's `claude-api` architecture skill: its guidance is load-bearing on Claude/Anthropic API primitives and its own frontmatter directs the reader to SKIP it for non-Claude providers, so it is offered — with an explicit Anthropic-specific caveat and the install command — only to operators running on Claude, rather than shipped by default onto a box that may run another provider. The overlap with the local `architecture-design` skill is resolved in that skill's `SKILL.md`: local is scoped to this repo's conventions, the vendor skill to provider-level agent architecture.

**Cold-hook settings readability gate ([#3499](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3499)).** The CLI and the cold hooks read the same `ConfigSetting` store through DIFFERENT interpreters: `t3` runs from a uv-tool venv that can import `teatree`, while the hooks run under whatever `hooks/scripts/run-hook.sh` selects off `PATH` (the first `python3.13|3.12|3.11|python3`), which on many hosts is a bare system interpreter with no teatree installed. When the hook-side reader cannot import `teatree`, every DB-home flag in `hooks/scripts/teatree_settings` degrades to its compiled-in default — so `t3 <overlay> gate <name> disable/enable` writes the DB and the gates never see it, and `autoload` reads OFF while the store says on. The failure is silent by construction: a value alone cannot distinguish "never opted in" from "cannot read the store", since both resolve to the setting's declared default. `read_cold_setting_status` therefore returns `(value, status)`, and two consumers branch on it — the SessionStart advisory (`hooks/scripts/engagement_advisory`) names the read failure and points at `t3 doctor check` instead of telling the operator to set a flag that may already be set, and `checks_cold_hooks._check_cold_hook_settings_readable` asks the HOOK's own interpreter (through `run-hook.sh`, since the interpreter SELECTION is half the fault) what it resolves, hard-FAILing on an unreadable store or on any CLI/hook disagreement. Fail-closed is unchanged throughout: an unreadable store still never auto-engages, and an unaskable probe WARNs rather than reddening the run. **The ORM-side twin of that fault records itself, surfaces where sessions look, and retires itself.** When the `ConfigSetting` override tier cannot be read, `override_read_health` writes a marker beside the primary control DB and every `SAFETY_FAIL_CLOSED_STORED_VALUES` key resolves to its most restrictive stored form — so the box runs on `autonomy=babysit` / `require_human_approval_to_merge=true` rather than on a shipped default it never chose. That state reaches a session through `statusline.config_tier_chip` (`config: UNREADABLE (fail-closed values in force)`, beside the health chip, fail-open and TTL-bounded) as well as the doctor, because the doctor's own check lives inside `t3 doctor check` — a command agents are told not to run. And the FIRST healthy read of any process clears the marker (`note_healthy_read`, once per process so the hot read path pays no filesystem work): a successful read is the evidence the fault is gone, so the 24h TTL is a backstop rather than the only thing that ever retires one.

**Review deep-retrieval constraint — path-independent.** Reviewing carries the same responsibility as implementing: a verdict from the diff alone is not a review. `Ticket.review_context_satisfied()` is a `django_fsm` condition on `review()` and `mark_reviewed_externally()`, so every verdict path is refused until `review_context_gate.is_complete` accepts `Ticket.extra['review_context']` (the work item fetched from source, links followed, referenced docs analyzed against the diff). Stamped via `lifecycle record-review-context`; `mark_review_no_action` (bot/no-diff) is exempt.

**Anti-vacuity attestation gate ([#1829](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1829)).** `core/gates/anti_vacuity_gate` extends the §17.4 CLEAR machinery so a maker cannot request-review/merge an MR whose new regression test is *vacuous* (green even with the bug present). **Merge** and **request review** always refuse unless `Ticket.extra['anti_vacuity_attestation']` maps the diff to the acceptance criteria and proves each new test anti-vacuous (revert fix → RED) or claims `no_new_tests`. SHA-bound like `MergeClear.reviewed_sha`; stamped via `lifecycle record-anti-vacuity`. `autonomy = full` does NOT collapse it.

**Rubric→verifier done-gate ([#2241](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2241)).** `core/gates/rubric_gate` adds a sibling §17.4.3 merge precondition: a ticket's `Rubric` must be graded PASS by an INDEPENDENT verifier (≠ maker). Unconditional and **fail-closed** — there is no setting that disables it, and it runs at `mark_delivered` too (`check_rubric_verified`, the SHA-free sibling). At delivery, a forge-evidenced owner merge whose recorded `merged_by` matches an owner alias, with no keystone audit or live PR, waives missing and ungraded criteria, but never a recorded FAIL; merge-time grading remains strict. **The checklist has content on day one.** `PHASE_CRITERIA` (`core/models/rubric_phase_criteria.py`) states the probe-first standard as one gradeable criterion per phase that has one — a measurement of the live system with its venue named, a claim naming the consumer that was read, an empty result paired with a control, a RED pin observed failing, a test that fails without the change — and `Session.visit_phase` seeds the entering phase's criterion, at the one boundary that owns the canonical phase token. The seed is ADDITIVE and idempotent on the criterion text, never `populate`'s replace: a replace resets every grade and would overwrite an operator's own checklist with a template. The done-gate stays unconditional, but the seed follows `forced_repro_gate_enabled`: switched off for the ticket's overlay, the two criteria that demand a failing run (`FAILING_RUN_CRITERIA`, matched by text, read through the `failing_run_required` resolver) are no longer added, and a row already on the rubric is never pruned or re-graded. The plan producer shares that same `Rubric.add_criteria` primitive, so a `plan-reaffirm` re-recording the manifest against a new base SHA never destroys a verifier's grades. Each criterion is positive by construction, so it can FAIL — the falsifiability guard the population seam already enforces. Full spec: [docs/blueprint/rubric-done-gate.md](docs/blueprint/rubric-done-gate.md).

**Falsifiable acceptance criteria ([#3762](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3762)).** The anti-vacuity rule raised from *tests* to *feature-level acceptance criteria*: a criterion with no state of the world that makes it FAIL is not a criterion. A silently-skipped implementation phase was certified by "the existing resolver test suite passes UNMODIFIED" — satisfied precisely by never touching the resolver, so the skip scored as a success. `quality/falsifiable_criteria` is the mechanical half of `skills/rules` § "An Acceptance Criterion That Cannot Fail Is Not a Criterion" ([#3759](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/pull/3759), the prose half): it detects the absence-satisfied shape ("X stays unchanged", "passes unmodified", "no changes to Y", "does not change") and `Rubric.populate` refuses a checklist whose criteria are ALL of that shape — the same reason it already refuses an empty one, since both pass the verifier vacuously. An absence criterion PAIRED with a positive one is accepted: the pairing is the rule, not a ban. Refusal is at population time, so it can never strand a merge.

**Review-verdict merge gate ([#2829](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2829)).** `merge.authorization.assert_review_verdict_gate` runs at the TOP of `execute_bound_merge` — the single chokepoint both autonomous merge paths (keystone CLEAR + solo-overlay bypass) cross before the forge squash PUT — so no merge reaches the forge without a non-stale, INDEPENDENT `merge_safe` `ReviewVerdict` at the live head. Always-on, **fail-closed**. The semantic is **standing hold wins** (`ReviewVerdict.objects.effective_state_at`): any non-stale HOLD blocks a `merge_safe` at the same head, regardless of identity or recording order. The head-SHA bind is free via `reviewed_sha` + `is_stale_at` (a force-push staleness-invalidates every prior verdict). The solo sweep reads the same rule through `head_review_state` and its `HeadReview.authorizing_verdict`. `ticket clear` refuses a standing hold before issuing a CLEAR or recording its sibling verdict. Resolve a hold by pushing a fix for a new-head review, or by recording an independent, cited disposition of every held point at the same head.

**Diff-scope gate on a blocking finding ([#4251](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4251)).** A cold reviewer can diff three-dot correctly and still take every RUNTIME measurement against the branch checkout, which reports what `main` did to a file since the branch was cut — a docs-only PR was held at high severity on `src/` code it does not touch, and every prescription was already on `main`, so applying them would have turned an `rc=0` merge into a conflict. `ReviewVerdict.record` therefore refuses a blocking-severity finding (`modelkit.diff_scope.BLOCKING_SEVERITIES`) citing a path outside the PR's own changed-file set, unless the reviewer attests a merge-result re-take. Both recording seams supply the set from `CodeHostQuery.pr_changed_paths` via `core.review.diff_scope_probe` — `t3 <overlay> review record --merge-result-retake` and the orchestrator's returned-envelope recorder (`review_verdict.merge_result_retake`) — and only when a finding is blocking AND cites a file, so a clean verdict pays no forge round-trip. The changed-file set is **three-valued**: an empty or sentinel-marked fetch is UNAVAILABLE, never a proven no-op diff (the rule `ci_rollup.attach_touched_paths` already reads), and an UNAVAILABLE set never fires the gate — "outside the diff", asserted without having read the diff, is the same unbacked claim the gate exists to stop. `t3 review merge-tree` is the one-step extract a re-take runs on: `git merge-tree --write-tree` + `git archive` into a PLAIN directory (never a git worktree — `resolve_data_dir` auto-isolates one onto a per-worktree DB), `git init`-ed with the source clone's real `origin` URL (`resolved_repo_slug` returns `""` for a local-path origin).
**A recorded verdict's findings are readable and reach its PR ([#4476](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4476)).** The read is strict: a stored row that is not an object or carries no summary is REFUSED rather than dropped, so a findings count and its content can never disagree, and `--findings-json` refuses a non-object element. Three surfaces read it — `review findings`, `review status --json`, and the PR itself, where the publish routes by PR author ([#4968](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4968)). The author is read on the host that serves the repo (`review record` takes it from the running clone's origin or the single host an overlay's `owned_repos` declares, `review publish-findings` from the URL it is given; no single host withholds rather than guesses), and logins compare case-insensitively. A self-authored PR gets nothing — no comment, no DM: its findings are fix work. An unreadable author or head withholds, as does any other author while our own forge login is unreadable or is a declared bot with no `user_identity_aliases` naming the owner. A colleague PR whose live head equals the reviewed SHA gets ONE submitted inline review: each anchored finding on its line, a finding without file and line as a summary bullet, and a line the forge cannot anchor refuses the whole review. A hidden per-verdict marker rides the last body posted and is the dedup key, so a re-record never duplicates and a post that dies partway is retried, not skipped. Every body first passes the same forge-neutral comment checks as `review post-comment`, with no per-call escapes and no pending-draft counterpart (the review posts no drafts); a diff the checks cannot read refuses rather than passes. One failing body withholds the whole review before the on-behalf pre-gate, the public-repo leak scan and its send audit run, so it writes no audit row and spends no approval. The review carries the findings and the marker only — `Nit:` is the one severity it names; no verdict word, reviewer identity or operator command. After the submit the marker is re-read: a forge refusal, a partial post (what landed is audited, the approval survives) or a review that does not read back fails loud rather than reading as published. The publish action `post_review_findings` is deliberately NOT in the shipped `on_behalf_auto_actions` default — on a customer overlay a review critique under the user's name is a colleague voice — so the shipped mode withholds it, reports the block, and DMs the owner the findings naming both exits (enable per overlay, or approve once). A comment-check withhold is reported with no DM, an own PR reports `self-review`, and `review publish-findings` reports a leak or send-proxy block as an `error` instead of raising. All five review leaves are declared capabilities, so a front end reads the findings through the same `--json` contract as the rest of the machine seam. A hold remains active at its reviewed head. Push a fix and get the new head reviewed to resolve it.

**Per-MR review-dispatch lock ([#1405](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1405), `MRReviewLock`).** Keyed on `(slug, pr_id)` with an explicit state machine `idle -> review_dispatched -> resolved`, acquired atomically (get-or-create + CAS on state). Both review-dispatch paths — the manual `Agent()` spawn (`t3 <overlay> review lock-acquire <mr-url>`, run before spawning `t3:reviewer`) and the loop's `AutoReviewDispatch.enqueue` — acquire the lock before dispatching; a second dispatch attempt on an already-held lock is a deterministic no-op naming the holder. The self-PR path is the third dispatcher and takes NO lock — its `CodexReviewMarker` claim dedups per head in a ledger this one cannot see, so a green head was reliably reviewed twice. `arm_cold_review` therefore asks `review_in_flight_for_pr` (a non-terminal `reviewing` Task on the PR's own ticket) and defers to the reviewer already running, rather than admitting a second writer to the lock; a reviewer that died leaves the head armable on the next sweep. `merge.authorization.assert_no_active_review_lock` runs alongside `assert_review_verdict_gate` at the SAME `execute_bound_merge` chokepoint: a merge is refused while the lock is actively held (`review_dispatched`, not yet stale) even when a recorded `merge_safe` verdict already exists at the head — a concurrently-dispatched review could still be about to record a HOLD. `ReviewVerdict.record` resolves the lock as a by-product (merge_safe or hold, either way the review concluded) — refused only for a MISMATCHED holder (`resolve(..., holder=)`). Five code paths produce a `Task(phase="reviewing")` and only these two acquire the lock, so an unqualified release let a verdict from a lockless path (codex / self-PR review, the two ticket schedulers) free a lock a DIFFERENT, still-running reviewer held — reintroducing the very race #1405 exists to prevent. The holder check is anti-theft, not proof-of-ownership: a caller that NAMES a lock identity is taken at its word and cannot release another identity's lock, while a caller that names none releases. The asymmetry is load-bearing — the recorded holder is a DISPATCHER identity (`LOOP_SCANNER_HOLDER`, or a manual `lock-acquire --holder`) whereas the releaser is the REVIEWER that concluded, and a reviewer shelling `t3 <overlay> review record` cannot learn the dispatcher's; demanding a match would hold the lock for its full `deadline` on every CLI-recorded verdict, and a stranded lock is what leaves a PR unmergeable and escalating ([#3920](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3920)). A `deadline` set at acquire time makes a crashed reviewer's lock self-healing: it is treated as unlocked once its deadline passes, both by a fresh `acquire` and by the merge gate's consult; `MRReviewLock.reconcile_stale` is the explicit sweep that resets an expired row back to `idle`.

**Sync writers honor the same gate ([#1426](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1426)).** The DoD decision is factored into `dod_gate.sync_gate_allows` so every sync writer — not just `ship()` — respects it, closing the bypass where sync wrote a post-ship state directly. A **post-ship** state is at or past `PR_OPENED`. A *workflow* state inferred from a live open PR is *capped* — a gate-refused state demotes to `WORK_STARTED`; a *terminal* state reflecting an external fact (merged PR → `MERGED`, board "Done" → `DELIVERED`) is **never demoted** but records a durable `dod_e2e_violation` audit marker and logs loudly, so the gap is auditable not silent.

**Concurrent-stack cap (#1397).** `max_concurrent_local_stacks` caps the distinct tickets whose worktrees can be in `services_up`/`ready` at once per overlay. The gate (`local_stack_gate.check_local_stack_limit`) runs before `Worktree.start_services()` and raises `LocalStackLimitExceededError` naming the blockers. Default `1` (a single in-flight stack, the headless-safe cap; `0` restores unbounded); sibling worktrees of one ticket count as one stack; each blocker is reconciled against docker first (a zero-container row is a phantom and excluded; a mid-restart row stays counted, fail-safe).

**Provisioning time-box + loud alert ([#2220](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2220)).** A long-blocking provisioning subprocess (DSLR restore, `migrate`, `--create-db`) must fail loud, never grind silently. `provision_timebox.run_timeboxed_step` bounds each step by a *fast* (`provision_fast_step_timeout_seconds`, default 120) or *heavy* (`provision_step_timeout_seconds`, default 1800) ceiling — `ProvisionStep.heavy` opts a step (a DB import, a frontend build) into the long one; every other step (symlinks, settings, a compose override) defaults fast, so a hang on an ordinary step surfaces in seconds instead of burning half an hour. On the ceiling it aborts and DMs the user, while a heartbeat distinguishes slow-but-moving from a hang. `detect_migration_conflict` matches a running `migrate`'s conflict output and surfaces the `makemigrations --merge` remedy. Best-effort.

**Parallel provisioning + resource-aware admission ([#2949](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2949)).** `workspace provision` runs a ticket's worktrees under a bounded subprocess pool (`_workspace/provision_parallel.run_worktree_provisions_in_parallel`) instead of one serial `for` loop — each worktree's provision runs as its OWN `python -m teatree worktree provision` subprocess, never in-process threads, because `WorktreeProvisionRunner`'s DB-import path mutates process-wide `os.environ`. The pool size defaults to an nCPU-derived cap (`teatree.utils.ram_probe.default_provision_concurrency`, halved core count) unless `provision_max_concurrency` pins one. Before each new subprocess is admitted, `provision_admission_gate.check_provision_admission` HOLDS it when host RAM is at/above `provision_ram_ceiling_percent` (default 75); a held request re-checks on the next poll and drains automatically once RAM frees, bounded by `max_hold_seconds` (default 600s) past which admission is overridden (logged loud) rather than blocking the CLI invocation forever. Within one worktree, `ProvisionStep` is a typed DAG node (PR-27): `requires`/`produces` tokens order the steps and steps with no dependency path between them run concurrently (the `subprocess_only` ones on a bounded thread pool, ORM steps serially in-process) via `run_provision_steps`; a `requires` token no step `produces` fails the run loud (fail-closed). `ProvisionStep.post_condition` is a truth check evaluated after the callable — a step that "ran" but did not produce its resource is recorded FAILED — and its aggregate (`teatree.core.provision.provision_postconditions.aggregate_provision_post_conditions`) is what `worktree status` evaluates to prove a worktree is really provisioned. `ProvisionStep.skip_probe` lets a step short-circuit to a near-zero `StepResult(skipped=True)` when its precondition is already satisfied. Every provision persists a `ProvisionReport` (steps + total duration + success) to `Worktree.extra['provision_report']` — no schema change — rendered by `workspace provision --report` and `worktree status`; a total exceeding `provision_slow_threshold_seconds` (default 600) fires the same best-effort user alert as a single step timeout.

**Wrong-repo worktree guard ([#2276](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2276)).** `find_clone_path` resolves a ticket's repo by basename, so a sibling clone of the same name (different `origin`) could be cut silently into the worktree. The provisioner's preflight (`WorktreeProvisioner._plan_cut`) calls `git_guard.guard_repo_remote_slug` before any worktree is cut whenever `repo_name` is an `owner/repo` slug: a mismatch between the clone's `origin` slug and the expected slug raises (a missing `origin` fails closed). A bare basename carries no canonical slug, so the legitimate `--repos <basename>` flow is untouched.

**A worktree is cut from a freshly fetched base, or not at all ([#4967](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4967)).** `WorktreeProvisioner.run` has two phases. The preflight checks every repo of the ticket before any directory, `Worktree` row or git worktree exists: a recorded checkout git resolves here is reused with no network read; one only the clone vouches for (`probe_checkout` says CHECKOUT through the clone's admin entry, yet every git command inside it fails) is refused with `checkout_liveness.wrong_venue_reason`, never re-created and never removed; then come the single-branch rule, the clone, the #2276 guard and `utils/git_worktree.cut_start_point`. That reads the remotes live (`ls-remote`, exact `refs/heads/<name>` match) and fetches the ticket branch when origin holds it, else the target `resolve_target_branch` names (on that target's own remote). A read that fails raises `git_sync.RemoteReadError` with git's redacted stderr — never an "absent" answer — so one unreadable remote refuses the whole ticket and nothing is cut. That refusal is `RunnerResult.retryable`, so `execute_provision` retries it on the provision budget before asking the owner, and the `workspace ticket` CLI keeps the ticket for that retry rather than discarding it. A clone lacking the remote, or a remote holding neither ref, raises `git_worktree.NoStartPointError` instead: a retry reads the same answer, so that refusal asks the owner at once. `fetch_branch` retries one failed fetch, because a concurrent fetch of the same ref (the CLI racing the worker) fails one side with "cannot lock ref". Phase two cuts each new branch at that start point, never at the main clone's HEAD, and never pulls or moves the main clone: `worktree_add` refuses to create a branch without a start point. Reconcile adopts a leftover at the slot only when git can read it here. The `workspace ticket` summary prints the branch git reports for each checkout, with the ticket's recorded branch alongside when they differ.

**Ticket repository scope ([#5551](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5551)).** `workspace ticket` resolves explicit `--repos` first, then `overlay.metadata.get_issue_repos(issue_url)`, then the dev repo, then the declared workspace entry that owns the issue slug. An unresolved issue keeps an empty repo set for the worker retry and owner question; it never inherits all overlay repos. The first repo set scopes a ticket with no repos without a reason. Adding repos to a ticket that already has repos requires `--reason`; each later addition records its names and reason in `Ticket.extra["repo_expansions"]`.

**Per-repo branch provisioning ([#33](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/33)).** A ticket whose repos live on DIFFERENT branches (an unmerged multi-repo change — one repo on a feature branch, a sibling on another) maps each repo to its own branch in `Ticket.extra['branches']` (repo → branch); the `WorktreeProvisioner` (#2589) checks each repo out on its mapped branch while the ticket DIR stays the single `extra['branch']`, so the repos provision as SIBLINGS in one dir. The CLI surface is the `--repos` token: a `repo:branch` form (e.g. `--repos 'backend:fix/x,frontend:main'`) supplies the override — `resolve_repo_names` strips the suffix to the bare name and `parse_repo_branch_map` extracts the map from the same string; a bare `repo` token (no `:branch`) falls back to the shared ticket branch. The map merges across `workspace ticket` reruns so adding a sibling repo's branch never drops the others.

**One ticket, one workspace dir.** `core/worktree/ticket_workspace.ticket_workspace_dir` is the single predicate for "where do this ticket's worktrees live": the parent shared by every materialised `Worktree`, or `None` when the ticket has none yet or its existing ones already disagree. The provisioner reads it to co-locate a repo added later as a sibling, through `ticket_workspace_dir_or_refuse` — the two states behind that `None` need opposite handling, so "nothing materialised yet" falls back to the root while an existing SPLIT refuses rather than adding the next repo to one arbitrary side. `assert_joins_ticket_workspace` then guards every registration: `adopt_worktree_for_ticket`'s, and `WorktreeProvisioner._provision_repo`'s, which is the seam that registers most rows (`TicketWorkspaceDivergenceError`). A split ticket cannot resolve its own siblings, so overlay code scanning the worktree's parent dir generates a compose override with the sibling service missing — and provisioning reports success.

**The worktree ROOT comes from the ticket, not the process.** `config.worktree_root(overlay=…)` takes the overlay whose root is wanted; the provisioner and the `workspace ticket` summary both pass `ticket.overlay`. Resolved ambiently instead, the segment is present under the CLI (`T3_OVERLAY_NAME` is set) and absent in the container (no env var, no `manage.py` ancestor, two registered overlays), so two provision runs of one ticket disagree about where its worktrees live. `overlay=""` is DISTINCT from omitting the argument — a caller declaring itself genuinely overlay-less — because collapsing them puts the split back at the parameter. Adoption also records the repo from the clone's leaf (`canonical_repo_root`), not the directory's name, so a hand-made `git worktree add` on a branch-named dir no longer stores the branch slug as `repo_path`.

**Idle-stack reaper + acquisition queue ([#2190](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2190), [#44](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/44)).** Four composed parts replace the hard `SystemExit` at the cap; all are global, mechanical-only, and individually kill-switched.

- **Reaper** (`IdleStackReaperScanner` → `reap_idle_stack`): demotes an IDLE running stack to `provisioned` via `stop_services` (reversible — DB + worktree preserved). Idle (`core.gates.idle_stack.preserve_reason`) = no live `Session`/`Task`, `last_used_at` past `idle_stack_idle_minutes`, not the CWD, no live external-delivery lease, no recent E2E run, not `reaper_pinned`; fail-safe ⇒ KEEP. `classify_running_worktrees` logs preserved-vs-reaped so a reap is never silent (#2227).
- **Queue**: at the cap `acquire_or_enqueue` reaps → retries → enqueues a `LocalStackQueueItem`; `LocalStackQueueDrainerScanner` re-checks the cap each tick with Fibonacci-minute backoff and re-fires `start` once a slot frees — never tearing down another ticket's stack.
- **Stale-stack reaper** ([#2207](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2207)): teatree-owned compose projects older than `stale_stack_min_age_minutes` (default 240 — a zero floor calls everything stale, which makes the manual and pre-start paths useless without a loop) are downed via `workspace reap-stale`; unknown age ⇒ KEEP. Ownership and liveness are both gated in the shared candidate seam (`teatree.docker.reap._reapable_candidates`), so neither reaping flavour can select the deploy stack, an unrelated user project, or a stack that is still up. Ownership needs positive proof: the self-identifying `<repo>-wt<ticket-pk>` name teatree mints, or the `<checkout-dir>-test` sibling a `Worktree` row vouches for — compose gives that second name to ANY host directory called the same thing, so the suffix identifies the test harness and never the owner. Liveness is the container STATE, because age cannot see it: a container that never stopped carries a days-old `StartedAt` and the docker zero `FinishedAt`, so an age-only test reads a live stack as abandoned. A docker that could not answer reaps nothing.
- **Safe disk reclaim** ([#2246](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2246)): `workspace reclaim-disk` runs only three zero-data-loss prunes (build cache + dangling-only images + unreferenced-only volumes) via a fixed argv, so `-a`/`system prune` can never enter the set — the sanctioned path so the agent stops hand-rolling raw `docker image prune -af`. The daemon is probed BEFORE anything runs ([#4585](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4585)): a venue that cannot reach it attempts no prune, prints no `Total reclaimed:` line and exits non-zero naming the route that works (`teatree-worker` is the one service `deploy/docker-compose.yml` grants the socket to AND the one that reads the control DB), so a reclaim that never happened can never read as one that found nothing.
- **Checkout liveness is venue-relative; UNKNOWN never reaps** ([#3583](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3583), [#3912](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3912), [#3853](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3853)): a linked checkout records its admin dir as an ABSOLUTE path written by whatever execution context created it, so read from a context that reaches the clone elsewhere `git rev-parse` fails with the SAME wording it uses for a directory that never held a repository. `core/worktree/checkout_liveness.py` supplies the discriminator, and `probe_checkout` (`core/worktree/worktree_roots.py`) is the one probe the reapers, the setup-time warning and the `t3 doctor` worktree-health check share, so they can never disagree. It is three-valued: `NOT_A_CHECKOUT` demands positive proof — the dir carries no `.git` at all AND git agrees there is no repository, so no pointer exists that another context might resolve; `CHECKOUT` covers a checkout git resolved here and one the caller-supplied source clone still holds an admin entry for (proof of life resolved by NAME, which is context-free, rather than by an unusable absolute path) — that upgrade applies only when the CALLER can resolve the clone, and clone resolution is a separate pre-existing seam (`clone_paths.resolve_clone_path`) that answers `None` for a checkout whose stored clone path was written elsewhere, in which case the verdict stays UNKNOWN; everything else is `INCONCLUSIVE`, the UNKNOWN a fail-open pass keeps. The three values are verdicts on what a caller may DO, never claims about what it can RUN: a clone-vouched `CHECKOUT` still fails every git command run inside it, and `NOT_A_CHECKOUT` authorises releasing a registry ROW and never removing a directory. `_workspace/broken_worktrees.py` (run by `workspace clean-all`) therefore REPORTS unresolvable dirs and removes none — it needs no dry-run branch, since a preview and a live run emit the same lines. `core/worktree/worktree_roots.py` is also the single answer to "which roots hold teatree worktrees?" — the canonical per-overlay `worktree_root()` is the only root ever written to, while the SCANNED set additionally covers every root existing registered worktrees actually live in, so an alternate root stays visible rather than accumulating unseen (the scanned set is sampled BEFORE the row reaper runs, so a row it releases cannot take its root out of the sweep). Mount aliases are resolved through `teatree.mount_identity.MountTable` and are not reported as a namespace split; an obscuring mount on another device remains outside. The doctor check FAILs on a registered worktree PROVED never to have been a checkout, WARNs UNVERIFIED on an unresolvable one — naming no destructive remedy, because the same evidence is produced by live in-flight work — and WARNs on a namespace split across roots. The same evidence bar governs CREATION, not just reaping ([#3967](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3967)): provisioning's reconcile step used to clear its worktree slot on an INFERENCE — a directory the acting clone held no registration for was a partial leftover, so it was removed before `git worktree add` — and a checkout created in another context is unregistered here for exactly the reason its gitdir is unresolvable here, so a container-view actor re-created a live host worktree underneath its writer. `core/worktree/checkout_disposal.py` is the one seam every removal in that path asks first: it authorises disposal only on positive proof — the directory carries no `.git` of its own, or the acting clone vouches for it by admin-entry NAME — and refuses everything else with the operator's next action named, a gitdir naming a root absent here being reported as a VIEW MISMATCH rather than a dead checkout. Occupancy is the independent second signal, read from the same `Ticket.has_active_work` the reapers use, so a checkout the acting clone DOES vouch for is still never torn down out from under a live writer (the requesting ticket is excluded, so its own re-provision is never wedged by its own liveness). `tests/conformance/test_checkout_disposal_deciders_refuse_a_foreign_view.py` is the forward ratchet: every decider that can authorise removing or re-creating a checkout is driven against one real foreign-view checkout, so a new path that reads an unresolvable pointer as proof of death goes red there.
- **One owner for a dead registered checkout** ([#3583](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3583) follow-up): `core/worktree/broken_checkout.py`, consulted by `reap_done_worktree`, owns a registered `Worktree` whose dir is provably not a repo. It closes the deadlock where the row reaper kept the row (its dirt probe runs git INSIDE the dead dir, so it could only fail closed) while the broken-DIR pass skipped the same dir as row-tracked — making the doctor's prescribed `workspace clean-all` a structural no-op for 100% of what the doctor flagged. For a dead checkout the question moves off the unreadable dir onto the BRANCH in the source clone, where any recoverable git work actually lives. The source clone is resolved BEFORE the checkout is probed, since it is what separates a checkout that is live in another context from one that is dead everywhere. Release needs proof on both halves: `NOT_A_CHECKOUT` (never a probe that merely errored, and never a pointer this venue simply could not follow) AND the #706 standard on the branch — nothing that exists on no remote whose content is also not upstream. An unresolvable clone, an unreadable push state, or genuinely unrecoverable commits all KEEP the row and name why. The DIRECTORY is nobody's to remove: no evidence available to a single venue proves a checkout dead, so disposing of its files stays an explicit operator decision reached through `workspace salvage`.
- **A branch tracks its OWN remote ref, or nothing** ([#4225](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4225)): `git worktree add -b <branch> <path> origin/main` — the recipe this repo published for an ad-hoc worktree — makes git configure the new branch to track `origin/main`, so `branch.<branch>.merge` reads `refs/heads/main` (measured: 28 branches on one box, 20 of them at `main`). `push.default = upstream` would then aim a routine `git push` on any of them straight at `main`, leaving forge-side branch protection as the only remaining barrier; under git's unchosen `simple` default the same config instead makes `git push` refuse over the mismatched names, which is the live friction each agent has to diagnose. Conformance is deliberately two-valued — `utils/git_upstream.py` accepts the branch's own merge ref or NO upstream, and repairs a mistracked branch to whichever applies (its own ref when the remote holds one, otherwise untracked). Always-set is NOT the third option it looks like: pointing a never-pushed branch at its own absent ref makes git render it `[gone]` — a deleted-upstream signal that says nothing about whether the work landed, which is why `_workspace/cleanup.prune_branches` deletes a `[gone]` branch only behind `branch_landed_for_teardown` and why the repair must not manufacture one. `utils/git_worktree.worktree_add` normalises the branch it CREATES (a branch cut from `origin/<target>` would otherwise track the target); an existing branch's config is the repair command's business, never a side effect of checking it out. `core/worktree/branch_upstream.py` sweeps every clone `clone_paths.known_clone_paths` names — the same enumeration `candidate_clones` uses — behind `t3 <overlay> workspace repair-branch-upstreams`, and `t3 doctor check` hard-FAILs on any remaining mismatch with the per-branch remedy named. FAIL rather than WARN on purpose: a box already carrying a score of benign WARNs cannot carry a single-layer push hazard as one more. Neither seam reaches the recipe an agent COPIES out of a skill, so the published text is its own surface: `quality/worktree_recipe_tracking.py` reds on any `git worktree add` under `skills/`/`docs/` that creates a branch from a remote start point without `--no-track` — line-scoped to the invocation, so a `-b` belonging to another command quoted in the same sentence cannot leak, with a `worktree-tracking: allow` pragma for prose quoting the defective shape deliberately.
- **Published checkout recipes stay in bounds ([#5247](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5247)).** `quality/worktree_recipe_tracking.py` also scans worktree add and move examples in skills and docs for targets outside `~/workspace/t3-workspaces/`, `/tmp/` and `/var/tmp/`, the defaults every reader has, so the verdict never depends on the runner's configured roots. An intentional quoted example carries `worktree-placement: allow`; the existing branch-tracking rule and its `worktree-tracking: allow` escape remain independent. The main-clone deny text sends ad-hoc worktrees under `~/workspace/t3-workspaces/`.

---

## 5. Agent Execution

**Native Anthropic prompt cache.** The pydantic-ai factory's native Anthropic binding and the `anthropic_api` eval runner share `agents.pydantic_ai_config.anthropic_cache_settings`: `cache="5m"` and `anthropic_cache_diagnostics=True`. In pydantic-ai 2.56.0 the unified setting places five-minute breakpoints on the last tool definition and static instructions, plus an automatic breakpoint that advances through message history; a typical request uses three of Anthropic's four slots. The OpenAI-compatible router receives neither setting. The provider ignores a cacheable prefix below the model's minimum length. `TaskAttempt` records cache reads and writes via `RunUsage` and `runner_usage`; compare read share as `cache_read_tokens / (input_tokens + cache_read_tokens + cache_write_tokens)` for comparable, known-usage attempts. Diagnostics explain misses but do not supply the share numerator.

The native Anthropic Lane-B shell measures each command against that five-minute cache lifetime and appends a cache-expiry note after a long foreground command. The router binding has no Anthropic cache lifetime and receives no note. The interactive Claude Code PreToolUse hook advises a main agent with at least 50,000 conversation tokens before a declared long foreground wait; it never denies the call, and existing orchestration denials keep their precedence.

**Cost profile (`t3 cost-profile`).** The `cost_profile` management command reads real dispatches only — non-park, `output_tokens` recorded, `usage_unknown` false (`TaskAttemptQuerySet.real_dispatches`) — and reports the cache read share overall and per lane, phase and tier, the `context_tokens` percentiles with the share of attempts and cost above 120K, 200K and 300K, a per-(phase, tier, reasoning_effort) baseline with failure rate and mean iteration, and three addressable-spend upper bounds (resume context above the 200K cap, balanced-tier output tokens, cache re-writes after tool calls of 240s or more) with their union and pairwise overlaps. `TokenTotals.read_share` divides by `UsageGroup.cacheable_input_tokens`, the denominator `t3 cost` uses for its cache-hit ratio; an item with no measurable attempt prints `unmeasured`, never 0%, and an empty sample, all-NULL `context_tokens` or an unreadable store exits non-zero naming the cause.

The agent layer is `teatree.agents` (headless executor + prompt + skill bundle + structured result schema) and `teatree.loop` (the /loop topology). The orchestrator-as-keystone contract is §17.8 — every implementation, review, test, debug, and ship action is dispatched to a sub-agent; the orchestrator's job is synthesis and dispatch, not execution.

**Headless executor (`src/teatree/agents/runner.py`).** Drives the agent in-process behind the `Harness` seam (`src/teatree/agents/harness.py`) — a real-environment run with the resolved phase model and the worktree `cwd`. A task typed as a continuation resumes its lineage's session; a task that begins a new phase starts fresh and reads its predecessor's result from a per-dispatch handoff file. `resolve_harness()` reads the DB-home `agent_harness` setting and returns the backend behind the narrow `Harness`/`HarnessSession` protocols (`claude_sdk` by default) from an open harness registry. The global `agent_advisor_model` setting adds `advisorModel` to the settings JSON only for headless harnesses that spawn a Claude CLI child; an empty setting leaves the options unchanged. Sub-agent fan-out, rounds and turns each have a ceiling, and the envelope Stop gate refuses a turn-end that carries no result envelope. The mechanics — the transport switch, the Lane-B tool layer, cached-resume parity, the regulated-path model allowlist, the dark OrcaRouter wiring and the metered spend states — live in [docs/blueprint/headless-executor.md](docs/blueprint/headless-executor.md).

**Phase dispatch.** A task whose `(role, phase)` has a registered agent (`Task.loop_dispatched`) is worked by that agent; a pair with none is free-form work the generic runner takes. Either way the run goes through `core.tasks.execute_task` → the `src/teatree/agents/runner.py` executor behind the `Harness` seam. WHICH transport and credential drive it is the two-layer pair `agent_harness`/`agent_harness_provider` ([#2887](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2887), see the executor paragraph above). `Task.dispatchable_q()` is the ONE filter every dispatch consumer builds on — the `orchestrate` planner's target + admit sweep and its in-flight budget count, and the live `claim-next` in `loop_dispatch` — so the #2104 external-delivery exclusion and the role/phase set can never diverge across them. The `agents/attempt_recorder` applies one schema+evidence gate to every recorded attempt, whichever entry point produced it. Evals split the same way: `t3 eval run` defaults to `--backend transcript` (REUSE a recorded run by grading its on-disk transcript, $0 extra); CI passes `--backend api` to RUN the model fresh in-process via the Agent SDK, on the credential `agent_harness_provider` selects — DEFAULT the subscription OAuth token, with the metered `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` selectable per run via `t3 eval run --credential api_key` — and `--trials`/`--models` force the fresh-run runner. **Credential layer ([#2707](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2707)).** `teatree.llm.credentials` is THE canonical way to authenticate any Claude SDK / bundled-CLI invocation in teatree. A `Credential` resolves one secret from injected `CredentialSource`s (`EnvSource` → `PassSource`; env wins, else `pass`, else a loud `CredentialError` naming the fix), and `child_env(base)` returns a child process env with its own `env_var` set and every `conflicting_vars` entry STRIPPED — so an invocation can never silently fall back to a different credential. The concrete `AnthropicApiKeyCredential` (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, strips `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`) and `AnthropicSubscriptionCredential` (the inverse) are the two Claude credentials. **Forbidden vars (base-URL containment).** `child_env` has a second, security-bearing rule: `CredentialSpec.forbidden_vars` are RAISED on, not stripped. A stripped conflict is a fallback to remove; a forbidden var is an operator misconfiguration to surface. The concrete case is `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` on the subscription credential — both the bundled CLI and the Anthropic SDK read it natively off the inherited env, so an ambient value silently redirects a plan-authenticated child at a third-party endpoint, where plan auth is not valid. It stays legal on the metered key (a gateway, Bedrock/Vertex, an Anthropic-compatible provider on its OWN key), which is why it is a forbidden var of one credential rather than a conflict of the pair. Seams that pin no credential get the same refusal from the shared `reject_ambient_base_url_redirect()` chokepoint, wired into every AUTONOMOUS unpinned spawn (the headless dispatch's ambient default, the clean-room one-shot turn, the maker pane) and into the eval lane's `isolated_claude_env`, whose env is a copy of the parent's. `t3 agent` on its `-p` branch is also inside it: that exec replaces the process, but the child runs headless with nobody watching its auth, which is the condition the guard exists for. The `exec` spawns that leave a human in front of the child (bare interactive `t3 agent`, `t3 loop start`, the ttyd debug session) are deliberately outside it — the operator sees the child's own auth behaviour directly. **Unattended permission mode.** `src/teatree/agents/permission_modes.py` holds the modes teatree pins: headless dispatch, the `t3 loop start` session, and `t3 agent` on its `-p` branch all pin `UNATTENDED` (`bypassPermissions`), and the #116 quarantined reader pins `READER_DEFAULT_DENY` (`dontAsk`). The pins matter because `permissions.defaultMode` in `~/.claude/settings.json` is a single global key that every lane would otherwise inherit — `t3 doctor check` advises `auto` there for the session the operator drives, and without the pins that advice would classifier-gate the autonomous loop and every headless `t3 agent` run. Bare `t3 agent` with no task argument pins nothing on purpose: it execs an interactive `claude`, and that session's mode is the operator's to choose.

**Live control of a running session ([#5063](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5063)).** While `run_agent` drives a task, `runner_heartbeat` publishes it in the process-local `LiveSessionRegistry` (`src/teatree/agents/live_registry.py`) under its task pk, and the worker's live socket (§8, live agent mailbox) answers five operator verbs: `t3 <overlay> live list` and `live inspect` are PASSIVE — they read what the driver's `StreamCapture` already holds (state, phase, harness, model, elapsed time, tool calls, open tool, pending inputs) and send the agent nothing — and `live steer`, `live follow-up` and `live pause` are ACTIVE (`live pause` interrupts the running turn and parks the task for an operator `resume`). A harness opts in by implementing `SteerableSession.steer` (`src/teatree/agents/live_control.py`), which takes input into the CURRENT turn at the harness's next safe boundary or refuses it, and never starts a turn; a harness without it lists as `steerable: false`. `claude_sdk` delivers at TeaTree-owned hook boundaries (`src/teatree/agents/claude_sdk_session.py`): a main-thread `PostToolUse` `additionalContext`, or a `Stop` block when the turn reaches its end first — never a sub-agent's hook (the CLI 2.1.284 contract is recorded in `tests/fixtures/claude_cli/2.1.284-live-control.json`). `codex_app_server` delivers through App Server `turn/steer` with the open expected turn id; only an answer naming that turn proves admission, and a timeout, wrong-turn answer or turn-end race is `unknown_delivery`. `pydantic_ai` admits text through `AgentRun.enqueue` in the current turn; the observed `EnqueuedMessagesEvent` confirms delivery, and a final-response race redirects input into the continuing run. `ProcessHistory` compacts old messages near the model window while preserving recent tool call/result pairs, and native cancellation snapshots completed turn history for restart. `LiveSessionController` is the session's one command owner: it frames the text as operator input, admits at most 4 pending inputs of at most 16 KiB, keys receipts on `(task, command_id)` with a text digest — an identical retry returns the first receipt, a changed text is refused `duplicate_mismatch` — and hands every command to the session's own event loop. A receipt is `accepted_current_turn`, `rejected` with a code (`turn_ended`; `not_accepted_in_time`, whose input is withdrawn and never delivered later; `not_steerable`; `too_large`; `duplicate_mismatch`; `backpressure`; `session_closed`; `unknown_session`; `permission_denied`; `stale_command` for a mismatched attempt or worker generation), or `unknown_delivery` when delivery cannot be confirmed — the answer was lost, or the harness failed mid-delivery — which is never resent automatically and stays the answer to every retry. A receipt means the session accepted the input, never that the model obeyed it. The live controller persists no receipts: a finished session keeps them in a bounded in-memory tombstone and a restarted worker answers `offline`; Slack thread routing separately persists `SessionCommand` outcomes, so the task list stays the durable scheduler, and a live session is still its checkout's one writer — it never admits a second dispatch. A steer delivered at a `Stop` boundary makes the next `Stop` carry `stop_hook_active`, so the envelope Stop gate stands down for that one `Stop`; the framed input tells the agent its final message must still carry the envelope, and the recorder's refusal still applies. Every stop request the heartbeat makes (`src/teatree/agents/runner_heartbeat.py`: a lost lease, a deploy-drain checkpoint, a watchdog breach, the runtime ceiling) goes through the controller, which closes admission before it asks the harness to stop: a steer that lands after the request is refused `session_closed` and never delivered, while one already taken stays delivered. The same controller pushes a peer's mailbox message (§8) into the current turn through the same `steer` boundary, framed as peer information; those pushes keep no controller receipt (the sender's broker keeps it) and have their own 4 pending slots, so a peer can never spend an operator's receipt budget or crowd out an operator steer.

After a clean result, an eligible `claude_sdk` session remains open for a 60-second idle window while the heartbeat renews its task and checkout leases. `live follow-up` claims that window on the session owner loop, starts one new turn through `ClaudeSdkSession.follow_up`, and returns `accepted_new_turn` when the SDK accepts the prompt; a duplicate command id returns the same receipt and a busy session returns `not_idle`. Operator requests and peer pushes carry the selected session's attempt token and worker generation; the registry rejects a stale fence before delivery, and new-turn admission rechecks the task claim and checkout before starting. A peer send to that idle session uses the same one-turn claim. The driver owns all session I/O, and a session starts at most four follow-up turns under the run's hard runtime ceiling. A conversation larger than `RESUME_CONTEXT_CAP_TOKENS` (200K) opens no idle window, and a follow-up turn that ends over it closes admission after the turn finishes. A drain closes an idle window cleanly; a lost lease still ends it. After expiry, a stopped worker or a restart, live commands report offline and name the durable task-list route. An answer lost mid-command is `unknown_delivery` and is never replayed automatically; a parked task resumes only through the task list. No live session or receipt table is created.

**Cached-prefix stability on the dispatch lane.** The `claude_sdk` lane keeps per-dispatch churn (cwd, git status, auto-memory) out of the cached system prefix, and teatree's own append is ordered stable-first and bounded by `MAX_APPEND_BYTES`, so consecutive dispatches in one phase share a warm prefix; context over that budget sheds whole skill sections and names them. Per-account routing, metered-lane auth and the eval lane's isolation guards are in [docs/blueprint/headless-executor.md](docs/blueprint/headless-executor.md).

**The served model is the recorded model, and a downgrade the CLI forced fails loud ([#4874](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4874)).** `TaskAttempt.model` is the `model_usage` key with the most tokens, never the first key, which Claude Code's auxiliary Haiku call often holds. A `model_fallback` event whose content carries `claude_code_version_too_old` means the CLI build cannot serve the requested model and `fallback_model` quietly took over, so a run that would otherwise record success is recorded FAILED as `cli_too_old_for_model` (HALT, non-environmental: only a redeploy clears it); genuine failures, parks and ceiling salvage keep their own recording. Each attempt also records its conversation's size at the last main-thread turn (`context_tokens`), the served model's reported window (`context_window_tokens`), and whether any `model_fallback` event served it (`model_fell_back`). `Task.continuation_on_requeue` and the needs-input resume (`schedule_resume`, whose FRESH child carries the owner's answer and the original question) answer FRESH when the last attempt fell back, grew past `RESUME_CONTEXT_CAP_TOKENS` (200K, on any window, because every resumed turn re-reads the whole conversation), or left less than `RESUME_HEADROOM_TOKENS` of its window, because resuming any of these replays a conversation the retry cannot afford or hold. The balanced tier runs at `high` effort and the frontier tier at `xhigh` (`agent_tier_effort`). The fallback flag comes from the stream, not from comparing model ids, because the metered lane legitimately records the router's pick rather than the handle it requested. A resumed run the CLI ends cleanly without a turn is stamped `context_exhausted` for the same reason.

**Structured result schema (`src/teatree/agents/result_schema.py`).** Agents return JSON: `summary`, `files_modified`, `tests_run`, `tests_passed`, `tests_failed`, `decisions`, `needs_user_input`, `user_input_reason`, `next_steps`, `commands_executed`. `additionalProperties: false`. Validated without the `jsonschema` library to keep the dep tree small.

**Skill bundle (`src/teatree/agents/skill_bundle.py`) + delegation map (`skill_support/map.py`).** A phase → companion-skills map (e.g. `coding → test-driven-development`), topo-sorted `requires:` resolution (a dispatched phase's bundle never carries the attended engagement skill, `interactive`, even when a lifecycle skill requires it), per-overlay `companion_skills`. Reviewer-dispatch review skills come from `OverlayConfig.get_review_companion_skills()` (default `code-review`); on the `reviewing` phase `build_system_context` embeds them in full, and an orchestrator-built reviewer dispatch goes through the single shared `build_reviewer_dispatch_prompt()`, which prepends a REQUIRED Skill-tool load block (`t3:review` + overlay review skills) so conventions reach the reviewer structurally, not via orchestrator memory. The **coding phase** force-loads a symmetric contract: a REQUIRED load block (`/t3:architecture-design`+`/t3:code`), `architecture-design` embedded in full, and behavior-preservation + no-AI-signature clauses, so the discipline reaches a skill-stripped builder. It also injects an explicit stack+overlay load block computed from the resolved bundle (framework skill `/ac-django`/`/ac-python`/`fastapi` plus the active overlay skill), so a code-touching dispatch never relies on auto-detect; an unresolved stack falls back to a "load your stack's coding skill yourself" instruction ([#1368](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1368)). **Stack skills are a REQUIRED load in every phase ([#5073](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5073)).** A detected framework skill is never a "Read <path> when it applies" companion line (agents followed that line 0 of 36 times) and never embedded unless it is primary or a stage skill: `skill_injection._stack_load_block` renders each as `REQUIRED: Load /<skill> via the Skill tool before you start; if this lane has no Skill tool, Read <path> in full.`, first after the reach line so a budget cut cannot drop it, and `prompt.required_skill_delivery` lists it as an explicit load so `assess_skill_dispatch` refuses a dispatch whose body is missing or whose directive did not render. A Django repo names both `ac-django` and `ac-python`, so the bundle never leans on the `requires:` closure. A dispatch from the last 6 h whose recorded bundle (`TaskAttempt.skills_loaded`) lacks a stack skill its ticket's checkout or clone calls for raises the `stack-skills-missing` health WARNING (`core/factory/stack_skill_coverage.py`). **Per-stage overlay skills (`OverlayConfig.stage_skills`).** A per-phase map (keyed by canonical phase, DB-overridable like `required_third_party_services`, an unknown-phase key rejected loud by `phases.canonicalize_phase_keys`) of ADDITIONAL skills appended LAST in `select_for_runtime_phase` (after base/overlay/review, before the `requires:` chain; first-wins dedupe keeps the base authoritative — additive, never replacing). `active_overlay_stage_skills(phase)` sources them from the ACTIVE overlay only, so a public-repo dispatch under the base overlay never sees a team overlay's map; for a no-Skill-tool maker they and their direct `requires` embed IN FULL (`src/teatree/agents/stage_skill_prompt.py`) with an ADDITIVE precedence line, and a stage-driven phase renders scoped even without a lifecycle skill; the embed/preamble search roots are the index's (`skill_support.index.harness_skills_dirs`) so a team skill body resolves for headless dispatch. The SKILL.md reading + rendering primitives (`_read_skill_contents{,_scoped}`) live in the Django-free `src/teatree/agents/skill_injection.py`, shared by `build_system_context` and a **raw-Agent-tool spawn** preamble: an orchestrator that spawns an e2e/coder/reviewer sub-agent with a bare prompt string bypasses every skill-injection path, so `skill_injection.build_subagent_skill_preamble` renders framework stack skills as required load directives and embeds other requested skills' `SKILL.md` bodies (resolved across the framework AND active-overlay skills dirs) and `t3 <overlay> skill-preamble --skills t3:rules,t3:e2e[,<overlay-skill>]` emits it for the orchestrator to prepend to every brief (fails loud, non-zero, on an unresolved skill). `skills/rules/SKILL.md` § Sub-Agent Limitations makes the carry mandatory, pinned by `evals/scenarios/orchestrator_embeds_skills_in_subagent_brief.yaml`.

**Headless and interactive resolve the SAME skills, from the agent file ([#3667](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3667)).** A sub-agent's skills are declared once, in its `agents/*.md` frontmatter `skills:` list, and both dispatch lanes read that one declaration — so a phase can no longer load a different set headless than interactive. Three pieces by layer: `skill_support/agent_declarations.py` (platform, pure) reads the frontmatter list and knows nothing about phases or roles; `src/teatree/agents/phase_agent_skills.py` (domain) maps phase → dispatched agent file → declared skills through `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE`, role-agnostically (no phase dispatches two different `t3:` agents across roles, and that is pinned rather than assumed; a `codex:` row names a slash-command agent with no agent file and resolves to nothing); and `SkillLoadingPolicy.select_for_runtime_phase` takes `agent_declared_skills`, authoritative when present. The `_PHASE_TO_SKILL` map is KEPT as the fallback for a phase with no agent file (`ticket-intake`, `scoping`, `retrospecting`) and still resolves `lifecycle_skill`, which drives the review-companion branch and the scoped embed: every agent-file phase that has a lifecycle skill (including `answering`, `bughunt`, `critic_reviewing`, `directive_interpreting`, `e2e_reviewing`, `scanning_news`, `triage_assessing`) embeds only `rules` and that skill in full, the stack skills as REQUIRED loads, and the rest as followable companion lines. The deliverable is the equivalence test (`test_phase_agent_skills.TestResolutionPathEquivalence`), parametrised over every `t3:` row in `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE` with two assertions per phase — the headless bundle carries every declared skill, and the fallback map never names a skill the agent file omits — so either half turns red the moment the map and the frontmatter drift.

**Deterministic phases are non-agentic on BOTH headless lanes ([#3570](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3570)).** A few headless phases are fixed data transformations scheduled as `Task` rows only so a scanner need not run an LLM inline; dispatching one through the agentic path hands it the generic "work on ticket N, check git log, code, test" brief, which contradicts its least-privilege toolset — the brief demands a shell the phase is correctly denied, so the agent parks with `needs_user_input` (or narrates a result it has no path to persist) and the scanner's dedup then suppresses the ticket. `core/deterministic_phases.py` is the one registry (`PhaseRunner`, `deterministic_phase_runner`, `run_deterministic_phase`) and BOTH entry points consult it — the `tasks work-next` CLI lane and the django-tasks worker `core.tasks.execute_task` that the post_save auto-enqueue actually fires. Registration is INVERTED, mirroring `core/agent_runner.py`: a runner that drives a model turn lives in `teatree.agents` and registers itself at app-ready (`src/teatree/agents/ticket_short_description.py` for `short_describe`, the single writer of `Ticket.short_description`), so `core` never imports the higher layer.

**Tier model declarations — abstract tiers.** Every concrete Claude model id in teatree's PRODUCTION dispatch lives in exactly ONE place: `src/teatree/config/registries.py` (abstract tier → the current concrete id, one entry per tier). Production phase dispatch, eval scenarios, the benchmark, the eval TRANSPORT lane, and the tests reference an abstract TIER (`frontier`/`balanced`/`cheap`) or DERIVE from `tier_models()`, never a concrete id: the eval lane's family aliases (`opus`/`sonnet`/`haiku` → concrete id) come from `src/teatree/agents/model_aliases.family_alias_models()`, and its judge / fallback defaults index `tier_models()[DEFAULT_TIER]`, so a tier bump can never desync the eval lane from the models it measures. The remaining allowlisted concrete-id pins are NON-dispatch capability pins (`llm/rate_limits.py`'s probe model, `core/autocompact_advisory.py`'s harness 1M-native set) plus historical usage-record parsing prose (`core/cost.py`), enforced both directions — across Python string literals AND the doc/workflow surfaces — by `tests/quality/test_no_hardcoded_model_ids.py`. `resolve_tier(tier)` reads `tier_models()`, overridable per tier via a `tier_models` config setting (merged OVER the default, mirroring `skill_models`); the sibling DB-home key `agent_phase_harness` pins a per-phase harness override (default = the registry's verification-phase map). Adopting a new model is one edit to the `agent_tier_models` declaration or one config line — zero scenario, test, or dispatch edits. This is a pure in-memory constant lookup (no I/O, no DB) so the agent-spawn hot path stays trivially safe.

**Model capability order.** `core/cost.tier_rank(model)` ranks a model id / tier name along a *capability* axis `cheap < balanced < frontier` (the abstract tiers). An old short-name (`opus`) or a concrete dated id ranks identically to the abstract tier it belongs to (`frontier`). An unknown full id ranks *above* every known tier (most-capable, so a lower floor never silently downgrades an unknown spawn target), the inverse of the conservative pricing fallback. This order is the comparator for the most-capable-wins floor merge below.

**Cache-multiplier SSOT ([#2401](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2401)).** The Anthropic cache-pricing multipliers — a cache *read* bills 0.1x and a 5-minute cache *write* 1.25x the model's base input rate — are a fixed property of the API, not of any model, so they live in **one** foundation-layer leaf `src/teatree/pricing.py` (`CACHE_READ_MULTIPLIER` / `CACHE_WRITE_MULTIPLIER`). Every cost path imports them from there — `core/cost` (per-attempt SDK cost), `eval/cost_fit` (the warm-equivalent benchmark fit), and `eval/models` (the billed-input regressor) — rather than each keeping a private copy that could silently drift (the single source of truth). `teatree.pricing` is a `tach` `foundation` module with no dependencies; `tests/test_pricing_ssot.py` greps `src/teatree` and fails RED if the multipliers are ever defined in more than one place.

**Per-skill model floor + spawn-model merge.** `model_tiering.resolve_spawn_model(phase, *, skills)` is what both spawn paths call (headless SDK `model=` and interactive Agent-tool dispatch). It starts from the per-phase tier (resolved to a concrete model id) and raises capability for each loaded companion skill carrying a `[agent.skill_models]` MODEL floor (most-capable-wins via `tier_rank`, order-independent; a winning tier-name floor is resolved through `resolve_tier`). The floor is MODEL only — no per-skill effort axis — and returns `None` (inherit) only for a sentinel `phase_models` override with no floor; every other phase resolves to a concrete model id.

**Session-level pins (interactive main agent).** `[agent] session_effort` (`low|medium|high|xhigh|max`) and `[agent] session_model` are the interactive main-agent pins: injected as `--effort`/`--model` into the interactive `claude` spawn argv, so the user never runs `/model`. This is a SEPARATE axis from the per-tier `tier_effort()`/`resolve_spawn_effort` spawn effort above — the interactive `session_effort` pin never leaks into a headless SDK sub-agent (whose effort comes from its phase tier), and the in-session Agent tool still has no effort param. `t3 doctor` validates the values (a bad effort FAILs, an unknown model WARNs) and `_check_stale_path_t3` ([#2281](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2281)) FAILs when a `t3` binary precedes `$UV_TOOL_BIN_DIR` on `PATH`, masking `uv tool install` updates.

**Selective per-phase fan-out ([#2229](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2229)).** The `core.phases.FANOUT_BY_PHASE` registry maps `(role, phase) → PhaseFanout`, parallel to `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE`, and is the single source of which phases fan out (adversarial-verify on review, judge-panel on planning, find-then-verify on bughunt). Every registered pair receives its **plain-text** directive (§2 SDK/API-portable, [#2236](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2236)) in the `fanout_directive` dispatch field; unregistered pairs receive `""`. Ships **route (b)** only (rigor inside the one in-session sub-agent); real top-level parallel fan-out is deferred.

**Situational honesty-critical escalation ([#2263](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2263)).** Routes a *verification* spawn to the most-honest configured model (`[agent] honesty_model`, default Opus) when the agent's honesty is in question — situational, NOT a phase floor. Four triggers write a session/task-scoped `HonestyEscalation` row (mostly agent-fired via the `skills/rules` directive `t3 <overlay> honesty escalate`; `shipped_incomplete` also has a deterministic backstop in the rubric done-gate). `resolve_spawn_model` raises the winner to `honesty_model` when a verification phase has an active escalation — the LAST step of resolution, so nothing downgrades it afterward. `is_active` auto-clears at read-time (`cleared_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now()`, 6h TTL backstop); the primary clear is a fully-passed `rubric-grade`.

### 5.6 Loop Topology

**Disk headroom and reclaim.** The resource-pressure loop compares ordinary-user available blocks against the greater of the configured critical floor and 17 GiB, the measured build scratch need. Its disk ladder records each completed step and a cursor in one marker write; an interrupted pass resumes at that cursor, and only a completed pass stamps the freeing cadence. Each Docker prune gets the smaller of 300 seconds and what is left of the pass budget; a prune that times out or never starts defers the docker step, which keeps its cursor so the next pass retries it. The artifact sweep runs every 30 minutes, or every five minutes below the floor. It keeps every artifact of a checkout that holds a review-evidence receipt (resolved with `git rev-parse --git-path`, so a linked worktree's receipt counts) or is on an evidence, rescue or backup branch, with or without a ticket row, and re-reads that before each deletion. It keeps a registered ticket's `.venv` while its task or session is active, its ticket was active within two hours, or a file in the environment was written within two hours; unreadable activity or file evidence keeps it. The same scheduled pass removes three-hour-idle isolated SQLite copies from the host and Codex home roots, leaving logs and stamps; its process, owner, ticket, review-evidence, git and tree-write guards fail closed, and its cursor survives an interrupted pass. The worker also sweeps its own `/tmp` and `/var/tmp` with the existing scratch guard at a fixed four-hour idle window; each root has a cursor so protected entries cannot starve later scratch under a time budget. The configurable manual scratch retention remains independent. Each artifact substep records its result before the next.
