A failed reclaim below the floor records a one-line free-space and last-yield status, and sends at most one informing owner notice per UTC day (free space, the build need and what the cleanup reclaimed, with no process ids or names) through the audited notify egress; the marker allows three delivery attempts a day, a delivered or pulled-surface notice settles the day, and a recovery followed by a new dip the same day sends nothing more. Both deploy build paths measure the least available space across the root and Docker filesystems immediately before each build; a temporary directory on a memory-backed filesystem is not counted, and both paths report reserved space and refuse a build below 17 GiB. The full deploy path measures first and attempts `t3 worker reclaim` only when the least available space is below 17 GiB, falling back to safe Docker prunes if that route is unavailable, so a box with room keeps its build cache; under 1 GiB it streams its own log to tmpfs.

**Started pull request priority.** `PullRequest.objects.live()` identifies started, unfinished work for admission ordering and the doctor's merge-brake check. Task claims require pending status and a due `not_before` deadline; lane ceilings and machine pressure bound admission. Scanner queues follow their cadence and intake budgets. The merge brake gates new issue intake.

**Loop restructuring lives on the lead session — there are NO panes ([#1838](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1838)).** `orchestrate_phase` runs on every tick as the read-only planner that writes the admit budget ([#2315](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2315)), and per-loop ownership ([#2318](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2318)) holds for every loop. The original `dedicated_loops` toggle ([#2319](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2319) — split one fat `/loop` into N dedicated scoped-tick slots) is **retired**: [#2650](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2650) made the per-loop split unconditional — the singleton `t3 worker` drives one self-rescheduling `loop_timer` chain per verdict-admitted `Loop` row (below) — so the fat-`/loop`-vs-dedicated-slots toggle is gone, not a default-off setting. The work-execution fan-out is the classic in-session `Agent(run_in_background)` sub-agent path; the pane-backed teammate layer that once sat beside it is **retired** ([#3734](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3734)) — it never had a production spawn caller and was bound to the Claude SDK by construction, so `teatree.teams`, the `team:<role>` claim namespace, the `pane_reaper` loop, and the four `teams_*` settings are gone.

TeaTree drives the day from the singleton `t3 worker`, which drains one self-rescheduling `loop_timer` chain per verdict-admitted `Loop` row (#2650; the local/read-only operational core ships enabled while colleague-facing, externally-visible, destructive-capable, and token-costly loops stay opt-in, so a fresh install registers the sound operational set — seeded by migration `0001_squashed`), each firing on its own DB-configured cadence (default 12 min). The tick body `teatree.loop.tick.run_tick` is code — tested, typed, version-controlled — composing named single-responsibility phases ([#1796](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1796)): `scan_phase` (parallel world-scan), `sweep_phase` (maintenance scanners split out of the scan), `act_phase` (dispatch + mechanical + persist), and `orchestrate_phase` (the wip-driven autonomous fan-out — a no-op at `medium`; `slow` targets one live worker, `full` targets the summed per-overlay `max_concurrent_auto_starts` budget, and `boost` targets `boost_concurrency = N` live workers via a **pool-refill driver** (clamped by the PR-01 resource concurrency ceiling `provision_max_concurrency`; `boost_concurrency = 0` keeps `full`'s summed-overlay target), admitting a queued TODO/followup before a new-ticket auto-start — the admission-priority ordering lives in `teatree.loop.queue_drain`). `orchestrate_phase` is wired as a **read-only budget PLANNER** — it computes the standing live-worker *target* (and the marginal `cap = target − in_flight` it would admit this pass) but never claims a `Task` (the single claim point stays the live `claim_next` CAS, so the tick can never orphan a claim). The tick persists its admission target for the live claimer, so claimed ≡ spawned — and, the pool-refill invariant, when a worker exits below the target the next tick admits the shortfall back to N. At `medium` or with the toggle OFF no budget key is written (absence = no SIDECAR clamp = byte-identical throughput as far as the sidecar goes; the admission governor below still supplies its own ceiling, [#4097](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4097)), and the reader fails open on an absent/stale/corrupt budget so a dead loop never throttles live dispatch ([#1838](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1838), [#1917](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1917)).

**Adaptive admission governor — static concurrency settings are CEILINGS, not targets ([#3644](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3644)).** `core/admission_governor.py` is the pure, model-free decision every dispatcher consults before admitting work: the loop lane through `loop_dispatch._admit_budget_exhausted`, and the interactive `Agent`/`Task` gate through `core/dispatch_admission.py`, whose seats count toward the same live-agent ceiling. `loop/admission.py` assembles the signals and persists the brake state. Token budget is the primary signal and machine pressure — load and memory, read on the host in the scope each was taken in — the secondary one; `core/admission_pressure.py` folds the dimensions into one normalised scalar in which 1.0 is each dimension's own watermark, and the verdict is per phase cost class. The mechanics — the interactive seat ledger, merge back-pressure, restore-side admission, the metered-lane budget, host-side pressure reads and leaderless process groups — live in [docs/blueprint/admission-governor.md](docs/blueprint/admission-governor.md).

**#786 epic — the immortal-singleton roster model is fully retired (WS1–WS5 + #54, all merged).** The original model — a coordinator spawning a fixed roster of long-lived loop sub-agents it had to keep alive and re-spawn on death/compaction — was the root cause of the recurring "loop died on compaction / re-spawned" toil and the duplicate-on-restart hazard. It is **fully retired**: no roster, no `spawn_brief`, no takeover-respawn, no resume-by-agentId. The replacement satisfies three acceptance-contract invariants, each delivered by a specific workstream and detailed in the appendix:

- **Invariant 1 — 0 sessions ⇒ the loops still run.** The `t3 worker` runs every loop and drains the task queue with no session open; 0 workers ⇒ nothing drains, and `t3 doctor check` FAILs the stalled queue (`_check_queue_stall`) and WARNs the missing worker. `account_switch` ([#1916](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1916)) rides `SessionStart` / `t3 doctor check` / `t3 setup recover-account-switch`: it compares `oauthAccount.accountUuid` and, on a `/login`, invalidates the backend cache and expires the cached per-account token health, then records the new account. teatree depends on no MCP server but its own: `t3 doctor check` verifies the plugin-bundled registration, exercises `t3 mcp serve`, and FAILs any service in an overlay's `required_third_party_services` that teatree holds no credentials for (`mcp.service_resolver.SERVICE_CLIENTS`, the same builders the MCP service tools resolve). A separate in-process `scope_cache` (PR-19) records known-missing `(token_id, scope)` pairs so a backend call short-circuits pre-HTTP (`ScopeMissingError(cached=True)`, one banner per pair) instead of re-failing every call site — consulted by the Slack transport, reset per tick, cleared by `t3 doctor authorizations`.
- **Invariant 2 — at most one tick per admitted loop.** WS1 atomically claims each unit at its spawn boundary; WS2 uses a per-loop `LoopLease` to keep concurrent drivers from ticking the same loop twice while other loops run in parallel. The self-rescheduling timer chains and cadence claim described below preserve this bound after a worker restart.
- **Invariant 3 — retired by [#4961](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4961).** The WS4 per-agent consolidation loop on the Stop hook is gone; the `t3 worker` is the only task-queue drain.

**Standing directives — the rules are re-delivered, not merely written down ([#4166](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4166)).** The golden rule (PLAN → IMPLEMENT → COLD REVIEW), the todo-consolidation pass and the PR-board drive are written in the skills and were skipped anyway: the failure is context decay, so the repetition is automated. `teatree.loop.standing_directives` is the harness-neutral model — three slots, each carrying a compiled default text overridable by a `Prompt` row named `standing-directive:<slot_id>` (an empty body switches that slot off; an over-cap one falls back to the default, so the per-session context cost stays bounded), a cadence, a `scope`, and a `drives_work` boolean. Every resolution failure degrades to the compiled defaults, so the directives resolve with no DB at all.

**Every directive rides a turn that already exists, so the mechanism costs 0 self-woken turns.** `standing-golden-rule` (300s, floor 60, `T3_GOLDEN_RULE_CADENCE`), `standing-todo-consolidate` (1800s, floor 600, `T3_TODO_CONSOLIDATE_CADENCE`) and `standing-pr-board` (600s, floor 300, `T3_PR_BOARD_CADENCE`) all reach an engaged session as context at `SessionStart` and again on the first tool call after an owner prompt once their own cadence has passed; nothing wakes a session and nothing asks it to register a `/loop` or a cron, because teatree's worker runs the loops. The PR board's scope is `attended-singleton`: one board per host, so it reaches only the session that owns the host's loop slot in `loop-registry.json` — N sessions each driving it would mean N cold reviews per PR per pass and two sub-agents on one branch, not faster merges. The bounds are the cadences, their floors, and the dispatch brake: no slot that drives work resolves while the resolved mode masks the `dispatch` loop OFF, failing OPEN to delivering if the mode cannot be resolved or holds no opinion, and the golden rule is never braked because a rule that costs nothing has no reason to be rationed. The brake reads the MERGED `resolve_active_mode`, never the L3/L2 preset layer ([#4196](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4196)): that layer is blind to the L0 default mode and to the live-presence upgrade. It is read only once a work-driving slot has survived text resolution.

**The off switch is reachable and versioned.** `t3 loop directives disable <slot> | --all` (and `enable`) writes an empty override body through `Prompt.revise`, so the disable is snapshotted as a `PromptVersion` and `enable` restores the owner's own text; `--all` is the whole-feature kill, which is why no `standing_directives_enabled` setting exists. `Prompt.body` allows blank for exactly this, narrowed on both write surfaces — `Prompt.clean` (the admin form and every `full_clean` caller) and `Prompt.revise` (the versioned content-change path) — each refusing an empty body on a prompt a `Loop` runs. A directive override is referenced by no `Loop`, so the disable is untouched by either.

**The hooks read a publication, never the store.** `standing_directives.publish()` writes the resolution to `teatree.standing_directives_cache` (`<primary data dir>/standing-directives.json`, `{schema, written_at, directives}`, replaced atomically and only when the list changed) — from `t3 loop directives disable|enable` at once, and from the worker's self-rescheduling 60 s `teatree.loops.standing_directives_publish` chain (seeded by `ensure_maintenance_chains`, successor queued before the publish), so an admin edit or a mode change reaches the hooks within a minute; every publisher resolves and writes under one `flock` on `.standing-directives.lock` beside the file, so the chain's resolution read before a `disable` never lands after the disable's own publication; there is no age expiry, which would resurrect a disabled slot, and with no worker the brake does not apply. `t3 loop directives show --json` prints the `{slot_id, cadence_seconds, text, scope}` contract, and that contract is the harness seam: teatree's Claude plugin maps it onto its own context channel in `hooks/scripts/standing_directives_delivery.py` (the `SessionStart` process `standing_rules_start.py` delivers every slot, `owner_turn_context.py` the slots now due; an `attended-singleton` slot only to the loop-slot owner, read from the registry and never claimed; a per-session `<session>.directives-injected` marker written only after the context was; the Agent-SDK lane excluded; compiled defaults while nothing readable is published), while another harness gets the same behaviour by reading that JSON and writing only its own adapter. No directive text and no cadence value may appear in the adapter — pinned by a layer-purity test whose denylist is DERIVED from the layer-1 texts, so a new policy word is caught by default.

**Self-owned worker — `t3 worker` + self-rescheduling timer chains ([#1796](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1796)).** The singleton `t3 worker` owns the tick cadence: `SessionStart` re-spawns a detached worker when no remote factory target is active and the `worker` flock is free (the SessionStart resurrector `hooks/scripts/worker_supervisor.py`; worker admission remains the worker's question), and the worker is the sole cadence owner. The **stop condition is the active preset admitting zero loops** — there is no second kill-switch surface, because one that the reactive queue drain ran straight past was never a stop at all. A stopping posture halts every `loop_timer` chain at its source, quiesces the worker's executor pool while leaving the PROCESS alive (the schedule boundary that re-admits work is driven from it), and halts the reactive drain too — which strands the `default` queue, so the drain reports the depth it stranded rather than implying a clean cycle. The cadence is durable, crash-surviving state — exactly one pending `loop_timer(name)` django-tasks row per verdict-admitted `Loop` row on the dedicated `loops` queue is a scheduled `run_after` timer; the DB row IS the fire, so a crash never loses a beat. When a worker executor drains a timer it runs a five-step body that re-schedules its own successor BEFORE doing the tick work (self-dedup → successor-first re-enqueue → live `admitted_loop_names` admission check → a deadlined subprocess tick in its own process group, deadline `max(300s, 3×cadence)`, whole-group kill on expiry → post-tick cadence refinement from the fresh `mark_run_if_unchanged` anchor), so a crash mid-tick always leaves a queued successor and the chain never stalls. The `mark_run_if_unchanged` CAS stays the sole idempotency guard (an at-least-once double delivery is a no-op). A deterministic zero-token reconciler `ensure_loop_timers()` keeps the "exactly one pending timer per verdict-admitted loop" invariant true — adding a missing chain head, pruning a surplus timer, repairing a chain stuck RUNNING past its deadline, deleting an un-admitted/unknown loop's timers — and runs at worker startup, on its own ~5-minute self-rescheduling chain, from the loop enable/disable chokepoint, and from the preset-transition chokepoint ([#4185](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4185)). Chain MEMBERSHIP is `teatree.loops.chain_membership.timer_chain_loop_names` — the unified enable verdict (hold > manual override > preset) resolved through the ONE `teatree.loops.enable_verdict.EnablePlanes` seam the live tick's per-fire admission also asks ([#4196](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4196)), intersected with the registered live-tick set, NEVER the raw `Loop.enabled` column, which is the manual-override layer and empty on a fleet nobody has intervened on: reading the column alone left eight preset-admitted loops with no timer row of any status, ever, and actively pruned any they had. One object, not two call sites kept in step by convention — resolving the mask separately (membership through the L3/L2 preset layer, the tick through `resolve_active_mode`, which continues to the L0 `default_mode` row and the live-presence upgrade) made the two contradict each other at every scheduled away slot the owner typed through, and the reconciler deleted the timers of the loops the tick was about to fire. **One object, two NAMED readings, and membership is deliberately the WIDER one.** Membership is persisted as timer rows and admission is evaluated per fire, so the two questions are asked at different instants and the costs are asymmetric: a member the tick refuses costs one `skipped` no-op (the successor is queued before the gate, so it self-corrects), while an admitted loop that is not a member does not run AND has its timer DELETED. Pruning is the destructive direction, and every arm of the verdict now moves only on a durable write (a `LoopState` hold, a manual override, a `ModeOverride`, `default_mode`) or a schedule boundary, each with its own chokepoint — so membership needs no wider closure and a decision taken now stays answerable later. The presence upgrade was the one arm that flipped with no observable event, raised by a keystroke and lowered by the mere absence of one; it no longer decides which loops run. `admitted_loop_names` narrows the same object further with the `is_due` and colleague-facing arms; those stay the per-FIRE admission. `EnablePlanes.admits` is what every other surface reports — reporting membership would render a masked-off loop as running — and `tests/conformance/test_enable_verdict_seam.py` is the AST guard that no fourth variant of the verdict appears. `chain_membership.starved_loop_names()` derives the admitted-but-driverless set (membership minus the live timer rows) that `t3 loop list`, `t3 loops list` and the dash live panel render as `starved` — no field, no migration, so a persistent reading is a canary that the reconcile chain itself has stopped; an hourly `prune_task_results` chain runs the control-DB retention pass (`teatree.core.retention.prune.apply_retention`: short batches under a shared budget of `SCHEDULED_MAX_BATCHES`, spent in lane order — stale limit-park attempts, then the failed and then the completed task history of quiescent tickets, then old notification payloads, settled inbound events and transition non-edges, with `DBTaskResult` outside the budget — returning per-lane counts), and an hourly `expire_stale_jobs` chain keeps the `default`-queue backlog swept for a long-lived worker. Worker startup ALSO expires the stale `default`-queue backlog once BEFORE spawning any executor (`expire_stale_default_jobs`, scoped to `default` so the reconciler stays the sole owner of `loops`-queue timer staleness), so a box that queued days-old provision/ship jobs while no worker ran never blind-fires them the instant the default-ON worker spawns (the flip's load-jam class). The worker runs K=4 programmatic `django_tasks_db` executor threads — 2 pinned to `loops`, 2 to `default` — so a heavy headless job can never starve a reactive timer; a supervisor thread re-reads the fleet verdict every ~5s, quiescing the pool when the active preset admits nothing (the process stays alive, so the next schedule boundary still lands) and stopping every executor on SIGTERM. At-most-one comes from the `worker` flock singleton; at-least-one from the worker supervisor + reconciler (NO OS scheduler — no cron/launchd/systemd) plus the `SessionStart` resurrector (`hooks/scripts/worker_supervisor.py`), which re-launches the worker when no remote factory target is active and the flock is free; a fully-headless box starts `t3 worker` once from a login profile. This is DRIVER-ONLY: the runtime transport (the Pydantic-AI harness + OpenAI-compatible router) and cached-resume are owned by epic [#2565](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2565), and the existing Claude-SDK dispatch path is unchanged. **Availability reconciliation ([#2544](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2544)).** Both drivers of a per-loop tick — the worker's deadlined subprocess timer tick and the legacy native `/loop` cron — converge on the SAME command (`t3 loops tick --loop <name>` / `python -m teatree loops_tick --loop <name>`), so the active mode's mask is applied in ONE place for both. **A posture holds colleague-facing work off per ACTION, not per loop ([#2904](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2904), [#4202](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4202)).** Masking the loop cannot express it: `followup` is the sole `Loop.colleague_facing` row and `afk` RUNS it — the sync half is the daily job, only the review-request nag is a colleague-facing voice — so a posture that means "the owner cannot weigh in" declares `Mode.egress = "forbid"` instead. That is read at SELECTION time, so a loop whose output the posture forbids is never dispatched to produce it, and the on-behalf chokepoint stays a backstop whose firing is a bug report about a loop that was not split. #3569 likewise unmasked `review`, which always runs so self-review keeps going when away, with colleague admission gated upstream by `admit_colleague_prs_to_board`. A masked loop's cadence anchor is preserved untouched, same as a `LoopState`-held row. `Loop.colleague_facing` survives as a REPORTING axis — `t3 doctor`'s stale-override finding names the colleague-facing loops a live override masks off. **A colleague-facing posting change is verified by PREVIEW, not by firing it.** `t3 loops tick --loop followup --dry-run` runs the real built-in followup scanners against a disposable copy of the database and replaces ONLY the final Slack transport (`core/egress_transport.py`), so the same `collect_scan` fan-out, the built-in scanners, the same `_is_self_authored` and the same on-behalf gates decide — there is no shadow implementation to drift. Overridden overlay hooks and their scanners are omitted and reported as `not_previewed` because hook construction may write to the forge. Only the preview's pool carries the caller's context into its workers, and it joins every scanner before switching back to the real database, so one past the live deadline finishes on the copy. It reports one row per candidate (target, author, the self/foreign/unreadable verdict, the suppressed action, channel/thread, live outcome, refusal reason) and writes nothing. Selection goes through `loops/loop_table.preview_loop_jobs`, which deliberately skips the admission gate and the cadence CAS: the loop is previewed precisely BECAUSE the manual override masks it off, so a gated preview would answer "0 candidates" and read as a clean bill of health for a scan that never happened; the live verdict is READ from `loop_block_reasons` and printed in the header instead. The `Mode.egress` posture stays on the shared selection path — it is reported, never bypassed — and a run that selected no scanner or omitted an overridden overlay hook exits 3 (INCOMPLETE) rather than 0, so a gate run can never pass with missing coverage. Exit 1 is the breach gate: a candidate that would be acted on whose authorship the guard did not clear as the owner's; a foreign candidate the guard REFUSED is the guard working and exits 0.

**A loop killed at its deadline backs off and is named.** `Loop.consecutive_deadline_kills` counts ticks killed at their deadline in a row and a tick that completes resets it; the successor is pushed out `deadline × 2^(kills-1)` (capped at an hour, `timer_chains.deadline_backoff_seconds`) instead of re-firing on its cadence, and `t3 worker status` names every admitted loop with a non-zero count as a FAIL — and as the blocker of any loop stale behind it.

**A loop's idle cost is measured, not guessed ([#4999](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4999)).** Each worker tick subprocess reads its own `getrusage` (self plus waited children, including interpreter startup). `LoopManager.record_tick_cost` blends each sample at alpha 0.2 into `idle_cpu_seconds` when the tick found no signal or work, or `busy_cpu_seconds` when it did. A live tick with at least one signal, a non-statusline action, or a stall repair is busy. This single `UPDATE` is the sole writer and does not bump `updated_at`. SKIPs, waits, refusals, parks, and crashes before reporting progress count as idle; a deadline kill cannot write its sample. A failed telemetry write logs and never fails the tick. The cost recorder runs before the tick subprocess's hard exit. Off-live commands also carry the runner principal and cost marker; manual runs cannot skew the sample.

`Loop.idle_core_share` divides idle CPU by the *effective subprocess wake interval*: 600 seconds for the worker's `dream`, `outer_loop`, and `directive_loop` driver, including their cadence SKIPs, and the declared cadence for admitted timer-chain loops. It is `None` before a sample or for an every-tick loop. `t3 loops list` shows per-tick CPU and core share, sums enabled loops in its idle footer, and ranks by share with `--by-cost`. Compare CPU figures back-to-back on one box.

The fixed tick floor is trimmed where profiling named the frame: the cold tick import path keeps agent SDKs out, and overlay entry-point discovery scans installed distributions once per tick. The tick enters that scope after the reinstall drain; scan-pool jobs inherit its shared, locked snapshot. Other threads and later ticks scan afresh, and settings or preset writes stay visible.

**A recent anchor is not evidence of a schedule — scheduledness is its own reading ([#4140](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4140)).** `loop_staleness` asks whether `Loop.last_run_at` moved recently; `loops/schedule_liveness.py` asks the question that one cannot — is a chain still carrying the loop at all. The two come apart exactly where a worker dies mid-tick: the corpse RUNNING row wins the `_outranked_by_running` id tiebreak, the losing fire returns BEFORE the successor-first enqueue, and the chain is handed to something that will never re-chain. A manual `t3 loops tick` then bumps the anchor without restoring the chain, so every cadence surface reads healthy while the loop never fires again. `unscheduled_loops(now)` names every enabled, timer-chained loop with neither a READY timer row nor a RUNNING one inside its tick deadline, over the same `is_stranded` corpse predicate `ensure_loop_timers` reaps with, so the alarm and the reaper can never disagree about which rows are dead. `off_live_tick` loops are excluded by construction — the enumeration is `timer_chain_loop_names`, and `off_live_tick_driver` fires their own tick command, so having no timer row is their correct steady state. A preset admitting zero loops is the reading's PRECONDITION, not a breach of it: `loop_timer` step 0 halts without a successor precisely to terminate every chain at its source, so a stopping posture drains the whole fleet into the state this reading calls stopped — `unscheduled_loops` is empty there, gated the way `_check_worker_running` and `self_heal._Probe` gate theirs, and the stopped posture is reported once by the surface that owns it rather than once per enabled loop. The corpse predicate bounds detection latency, disclosed: a chain dropped mid-window is named once `compute_tick_deadline + STUCK_GRACE_SECONDS` expires, not at the drop. `t3 doctor check` hard-FAILs on a stopped chain, and `tests/conformance/test_scheduling_liveness.py` is anti-vacuous by construction: it drives the REAL `loop_timer` body against a stranded row plus a higher-uuid live fire rather than hand-building the end state, with the uuid ordering flipped as its control.

**Shipped definitions are guarded by detecting INERTNESS, not by forbidding deletion ([#3842](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3842)).** Nothing shipped has ever been deleted, and every failure that cost time was present-but-inert (a janitor wired to no loop, a colleague loop held dark by an override, a tick reporting 58 actions while no ticket advanced). Deletion is also self-healing, since the seed is `get_or_create` by name and `t3 setup` recreates a removed shipped row, so a deleted definition is the *recoverable* failure. `teatree.loops.seed_inertness.shipped_inertness()` therefore names every shipped loop/preset/schedule that is missing, disabled, or not ticking, and sources its EXPECTED set from the `[loops.*]` / `[modes.*]` / `[schedules.*]` seed tables of `config/defaults.toml` — never from the DB, because a check that reads the DB for both "expected" and "actual" cannot detect a missing row at all (the same self-referential defect as a golden compared against its own renderer, [#3836](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3836)). Presence alone was not enough ([#4096](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4096)): with every row present, every mask non-empty and every slot naming a real preset, a live `standard` calendar carrying an extra `Mon-Fri 19:00 → maintenance` slot against a `maintenance` mask that stopped delivery without stopping intake stalled the merge lane 13h a night and the audit still read OK. So each live VALUE is now compared against the shipped one too — `Mode.entries` and `ModeSchedule.slots`/`timezone`, through `loops.seed_drift` (whose `classification_drift` half already did this for one `Loop` field) — and reported as a NOTE naming both sides, never rewritten, because a per-box override is a legitimate operator decision and the gap was that nobody could see it. What IS a fault is a mask that can only hurt, shipped or hand-written: one admitting `db_backup` while both reclaim loops are quiet (`core.models.mode_shape.backup_without_reclaim`), one leaving a loop unnamed so it reads OFF by fail-safe rather than by choice, and an empty mask where the seed ships opinions. The intake-without-delivery rule this began with existed only because an absent entry inherited `Loop.enabled`; under totality there are no absent entries. Severity keys on the shipped mode and schedule admission: a loop expected to run but found inert is a fault; a deliberately masked loop is a note. Staleness reuses `loop_staleness.stale_loops` (the 3× cadence reading and its suppressed/unexplained split), so there is no second copy of the cadence arithmetic. Surfaced by `t3 loops audit [--json]` (non-zero exit on any fault) and a crash-proof `t3 doctor check` check that echoes faults only. Deleting a shipped definition is soft-protected rather than forbidden: `loops.shipped_guard.require_shipped_delete_confirm` demands a typed `stop-<name>` and the refusal quotes the shipped `description`, because the operator deleting a shipped loop is usually unclear on what it does — a confirm that names the consequence teaches more than a refusal, and refusal invites the hand-edited DB, which is worse than an audited delete. It is enforced at `delete_preset` plus the new `delete_loop` / `delete_schedule` seams (`t3 loops delete`, `t3 loop preset delete`, `t3 loop schedule delete`, all via the `shipped_seed` management command), and the Django admin ROUTES a shipped row to those seams while leaving an operator-created row's ordinary delete alone. The confirm never overrides an integrity refusal — a preset a slot still names, and the active calendar, are refused whatever phrase is typed.

**A gated FEATURE is guarded the same way — a declared evidence observable, not the flag's own value ([#4189](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4189)).** #3842 covers loops, presets and schedules; features had no equivalent, and that is the larger class. Measured: twelve quality gates once shipped default-OFF across 562 recorded `MergeClear` rows, with their evidence tables empty while every surface reported success. Those gates have since become unconditional. `teatree.config.gate_evidence` is the declaration half: for each governed gate declared inactive it records the observable being live would generate — a model, a `Ticket.extra` key, or an explicit NONE for a refusal-only gate that writes no artifact and therefore can never be proven live at all. The current declaration set is empty. `teatree.core.factory.feature_inertness.feature_inertness()` measures against that declaration, never against the flag, because reading the flag to decide whether the flag matters is the same self-referential defect [#3836](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3836) names. Two quiet directions keep it honest: a populated observable clears a STAGED gate (the decision exists and the evidence proves the behaviour runs), and a gate merged under `INERT_AFTER_DAYS` ago is too young to judge. Evidence never clears an UNDECIDED one — a gate whose producer demonstrably runs while the gate stays off is the loudest line the report has, and clearing it is how the two oldest undecided gates dropped out of the report the day their producers first wrote a row; a gate on in ANY `ConfigSetting` scope is doing its job, so the check reads every scope carrying a row rather than the global one alone. Severity is #3842's doctrine translated: a gate the owner deliberately staged (with a decision reference the declaration REFUSES to accept as prose) is a NOTE, a gate nobody ever decided to leave off is a FAULT. Prevention rather than reporting is `tests/conformance/test_gate_evidence_declared.py`: every default-OFF member of `FEATURE_FLAGS | DURABLE_GATE_SETTINGS` must carry a declaration, so a NEW default-OFF gate fails the PR that mints it — the walk fires only on an undeclared key, so the existing surface needed no retro-fit. Surfaced by `t3 <overlay> config_setting inert` (the full report, notes included) and a crash-proof `t3 doctor check` advisory that echoes faults only. Detection and refusal-of-new only: what to do about an inert gate stays an owner decision.

**A declaration also names what would SATISFY the gate, because "has it fired?" is not the question an arming decision asks ([#4375](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4375)).** Ten of the twelve were still UNDECIDED up to 67 days later against a report that named no next action: `_finding` assembled the never-fired line from the intent clause alone, so the producer six declarations already recorded in `rationale` prose was dropped on exactly the loudest lines. Every `GateEvidence` therefore carries a required `satisfier` — the command that writes the observable (`t3 <overlay> review record-evidence`), or, for a refusal-only gate, the state that satisfies the refusal (a `MergeAudit` row the keystone writes either way; a work group whose every open member is review-ready) — rendered on both the never-fired and the unobservable line and refused when blank by `declaration_faults`. It is a declared field rather than prose because prose drifts unseen: the first version sent operators at a `repro record` subcommand that has never existed, only `record-red` and `record-green`. `tests/conformance/test_gate_evidence_declared.py` resolves every `t3 …` citation in a satisfier against the live #550 command registry, so a satisfier naming a dead command fails CI instead of stranding the arming decision it exists to inform. Naming the satisfier still leaves the operator without the OTHER half — how a gate LEAVES `UNDECIDED` — so `render_inertness_report` closes the faults with `DECISION_TRAILER`, naming both exits: arm it (`t3 <overlay> config_setting set`), or move the declaration to `intent=STAGED` with the citation `declaration_faults` already demands. The staging exit is a source edit nothing advertised, so an operator reading the report had only the arming one — which is the friction that made "nobody decided" durable. It follows the faults only: a report of staged notes alone is a report with no decision to prompt for. Arming itself stays an owner decision, unchanged from #4189 — the report now carries every input that decision needs, and makes none of it.

**Idle usage-window auto-recovery — park, detect-reset, re-arm (Directive #3, permanently armed).** When a Claude usage window empties (the ~5h rolling session limit or the 7-day weekly limit) the headless plane would fold the hit into a terminal FAILED attempt (`_outcome_failure`) and go idle until a human pokes it — a measured 7.8h loss. A limit hit is instead PARKED, not failed: `teatree.agents.usage_window.park_task_on_limit` records a `UsageWindowState` row per credential lane (`teatree.llm.anthropic_limits.window_horizon` fills the re-arm instant from the SDK's structured `RateLimitInfo.resets_at`, else the cause's known window length; API-credit exhaustion has no time-based recovery so it still terminal-FAILs — the operator must add credits) and returns the `Task` to the queue PENDING with `not_before` at that instant (a new `Task.not_before` claim gate, null on every never-parked task so the claim path is byte-identical). A metered router's spend stop (`LimitCause.PROVIDER_BUDGET`: an HTTP 402, or a 403 naming a spend limit such as OrcaRouter's `token cycle spend limit reached`; a 429 with no wording is a `RATE_LIMIT`, both decided in `runner_failure_taxonomy.limit_match`) parks the metered lane until the reset its body states (`anthropic_limits.parse_stated_reset`), else re-probes after 6h, and DMs the owner once per window. A leak block (`LimitCause.LEAK_BLOCKED`: OrcaRouter's `400 guardrail_blocked`, or teatree's own `anthropic_limits.EgressBlockedError`, both outranking every other phrase) has no window: the task records FAILED, the owner is DMed once per task, and the context is never parked, retried or re-sent. An admission guard (`maybe_park_for_active_window`) parks further LLM dispatches on the exhausted lane the same way rather than burning attempts that will 429, **A hard provider refusal reaches that guard too ([#4816](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4816)).** `limit_match` classified by the SDK's typed window and then by PROSE, and the phrase table is Anthropic vocabulary — so an OpenAI-compatible router's `403 access_denied: token cycle spend limit reached` matched nothing, fell through to `error_result_reason`, and failed 307 tasks one at a time at 224/hour. The park machinery was already lane-keyed and already fed by `RateLimitEvent`; only the CLASSIFICATION was missing. `LimitCause.PROVIDER_ACCESS_DENIED` is read from `api_error_status in {401, 403}` on a rung ahead of both the typed and prose ones — the provider refused the credential, which is a fact about the transport rather than about any Anthropic window — and the pydantic_ai session yields the rejected `RateLimitInfo` alongside its error result, its `resets_at` taken from `Retry-After` then from an ISO-8601 instant in the body — both bounded by `believable_refusal_reset` to `[+5 min, +24 h]`, because a refusal names no window teatree can verify and nothing downstream bounds a park at all: `effective_resets_at` returns the reported instant verbatim and `_future_park_instant` clamps only the lower end, so a body's key `expires_at` parked the metered lane for YEARS with the low-power preset engaged, while the body scan's FIRST instant — as often the request's own `created` stamp — landed on the five-minute floor at 12 re-probes an hour. Outside the band the reset reads unknown and the lane takes the one-hour horizon below. Its horizon is a one-hour CONSTANT, not a setting: the horizonless `API_CREDIT` shape would wedge the lane on a transient 403, and one re-probe an hour is the safe direction to be wrong in. The cause is in `RECOVERABLE_EXHAUSTION_CAUSES`, so a task that landed FAILED under the old shape is auto-requeued once the window clears rather than escalated. while non-LLM loop work — scanners, and the keystone merge of already-green PRs — keeps running (a reviewed-and-green PR still merges during the outage). The re-arm is the self-rescheduling `usage_window_recovery` loop-timer chain (`teatree.loops.usage_window_recovery`, on `LOOPS_QUEUE`, seeded by `ensure_maintenance_chains`, the same #1796 machinery — NO OS cron): once the reset instant passes it CLEARS the window, RELEASES the parked tasks (`not_before` nulled), PUMPS the loops, and posts ONE bot→user Slack line. The re-arm decision is deterministic and time-based (`resets_at` has passed), so no fragile "is the model reachable yet" inference probe is needed — a released task that re-hits the limit simply re-parks. **Harness-limited (NOT teatree-fixable, stated plainly):** this re-arms the WORKER/DB loop plane; resuming the interactive orchestrator SESSION's conversation, or auto-resuming the Claude Code harness's own session, at reset is outside teatree (no supported prompt-injection API) — the mitigation is architectural, keep moving loop ownership to this worker/DB plane so the interactive session is optional. The parked `TaskAttempt` carries the `limit_parked:` marker and is excluded from the repair-loop iteration budget (`task_repair_queries.phase_attempts`) — a limit-park is a scheduling event, not a work iteration. Because it is a scheduling event on an UNCHANGED condition, a repeat records STATE rather than an append: `TaskAttempt.objects.create` folds a park whose reason repeats the task's most recent attempt verbatim into that row, bumping `park_repeats` and refreshing `ended_at` (a changed reason or an intervening real attempt always opens a new row, so every genuine transition still shows). Un-coalesced, the pollers that re-derive the park wrote one audit row each: 338,741 of 339,944 `TaskAttempt` rows against 1,203 real dispatches, in a 1.2 GB control DB — and, because the park rows inflated the denominator, they were what made cost coverage on real dispatches read as 0.35% when it is 1,203/1,203. The reconciliation ledger's park-spin detector (`_check_park_spin`) therefore counts park OBSERVATIONS — rows plus `park_repeats` — so coalescing cannot blind the alarm it exists to raise. Default ON after its soak (the whole point is unattended recovery); survives as a per-overlay escape hatch. While the flag is OFF the park path, the admission guard, and the recovery chain body are all inert — a limit records a terminal FAILED exactly as before.

A routed subscription session or weekly limit enters the same account-aware rotate-or-park path as an unrouted result. A different account is selected for the next dispatch without holding the route; a reselect of the signing account parks the task to the reported reset. A timed usage-limit route hold is never spent from the two-park route-refusal budget. Park attempts have their own `PARKED` outcome, and attempts record the signing account path for audit.

**Modes — a read-time mask over the admission predicate ([#3159](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3159), reshaped by [#61](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/61) and [#4202](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4202)).** Loop control is modal, so each loop's verdict is `loop_state_admits(*, held, manual, preset_state)` (`teatree.loop.loop_state_db`), first opinion wins: a durable `LoopState` PAUSE/DISABLE hold, else the manual per-loop override (`Loop.enabled` when a human set one), else the active `Mode`'s opinion. Every input is REQUIRED, so the type checker catches an admission or observability site that resolves a loop without the mode. The mode is resolved once per tick by `teatree.core.mode_resolution.resolve_active_mode` (manual `ModeOverride` → the `active_loop_schedule` calendar's governing slot → `default_mode`), and every surface reads the same verdict through `loops.enable_verdict.effective_verdicts`. A `Mode` (`teatree_loop_preset`) is a TOTAL per-loop on/off map plus an `egress` posture: there is no absent tier to inherit, a loop born later is written `False` into every mode, and a masked loop is skipped with its cadence anchor preserved, exactly like a held row. **A mask may not consume disk with nothing left to free it ([#4188](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4188)).** No mask may admit `db_backup` while both `teatree.core.models.mode_shape.DISK_RECLAIM_LOOPS` (`idle_stack_reaper`, `resource_pressure`) are quiet: the `preset_editing` edit seam refuses it, `loop_preset.mask_refusal` refuses it together with a partial map wherever a whole mask is stored as given (`Mode.clean()` and the config import), and `seed_inertness` reports it as a fault on any live mask. The `t3 setup` seed installs the five modes and the `standard` / `always-afk` calendars idempotently and pins `active_loop_schedule` to `standard` through the provenance-aware `ConfigSetting.seed`. A self-rescheduling `preset_transitions` chain (`LOOPS_QUEUE`, 0 tokens idle) handles side-effects only — one Slack line per switch, naming any standing override and what it now masks — so resolution never depends on it. A usage-window park engages the `token-outage` mode (re-pointable via `token_outage_preset_name`), cleared on re-arm and never over a live user override. The modes, the precedence and its fail-open posture are specified in §5.6.3 ([docs/blueprint/loop-topology.md](docs/blueprint/loop-topology.md)). **No owner-intent silently rots.** A masked/idle loop is indistinguishable from "nothing to do" — the directive loop once sat masked for days while owner directives piled up at `CAPTURED`, never interpreted, producing zero signal. `t3 doctor` (`_check_intent_freshness`, `cli/doctor/checks_intent.py`) closes that gap: it HARD-FAILs (gates the exit code, like the self-heal silent-freeze detectors) when a consumable intent queue is non-empty while its consumer is not live, and WARNs (surfacing-only, like the dream/marker-jam advisories) when a live consumer lets an item age past the 24h `INTENT_FRESHNESS_THRESHOLD` (one directive-loop cadence). Each queue is scoped to exactly the rows its named consumer still has to drain, and "live" means every gate that consumer actually passes: the directive intake arc is consumed by `directive_loop` only when the loop row is unmasked AND its fail-closed INTAKE guard chain allows (`evaluate_intake_guards`), so an unmask alone can never silence the finding while directives still never advance, and a `CLARIFYING` directive counts only once every clarify question of its generation is answered (`_advance_clarifying` otherwise waits on the owner, exactly like `RATIFY_PENDING`); the owner deferred-question arc is the `dispatch` poster's `unmirrored_pending()` rows only — a DELIVERED question stays pending until the human answers, so away-mode masking `dispatch` never reds the box. `_gather_intent_queues` is the seam for more queues. Each finding names the stuck items (capped, with an "and N more" tail, and age-free — the refs are the queue's identity and the WARN text already carries the threshold) and the remediation for what actually has to change.

**Modes and schedules are the handle; the manual override is the per-loop exception ([#3248](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3248)).** `t3 loop resume/disable/pause` refuse without `--emergency`, guiding the operator to `t3 loop preset use` / `t3 loop schedule set-active`. `t3 loop override <name> on|off|clear --reason '<why>' [--lift-by 2h]` sets the manual per-loop override, the one handle that beats the mode: it lives on `Loop.enabled` with its reason, never expires, and `--lift-by` is advisory — what the override watcher reminds against. The hold and manual planes are bulk-read once per tick (`loops.enable_verdict.EnablePlanes`), so the #2584 N+1 guard stands. The statusline loop line carries `schedule:` and `mode:` segments and a `forced ON:` / `forced OFF:` section for each override that diverges from what the mode would decide (`loops.preset_status.manual_override_entries`).

**Session-start auto-load is opt-in ([#256](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/256)).** The loop-*arming* machinery — the tick-owner claim (`handle_session_start_bootstrap`) — fires only when the session both opted into teatree (the `<session>.teatree-active` marker) AND the operator enabled autoload. The single arming gate is `hook_router._loop_auto_load_active` = `_teatree_active AND _autoload_enabled AND NOT remote_factory_target_active`. `autoload` is the ONE owner flag — env `T3_AUTOLOAD` first, then the DB store read cold via `_cold_db_bool`; defaults **OFF** and fails CLOSED on absence — and it both *engages* the session and *arms* its loops. The loop **statusline** (`hooks/scripts/statusline.sh`) is a display-*visibility* surface, not loop-arming, so it renders when `autoload` is on, or in a session carrying the `.t3-engaged` marker — never on `.teatree-active` alone. The two markers come from different loads: `hook_router._maybe_engage_t3` writes `.t3-engaged` on any `t3:` skill load, while `engagement.engage` writes `.teatree-active` at SessionStart only when `autoload` is on, and otherwise only on a skill load whose `requires:` closure reaches `t3:interactive`. The harness runs the loop in a background `bg-spare` daemon session (which gets `.teatree-active` and owns the tick) while the owner's foreground TUI sessions frequently never get it, so ANDing that marker with autoload blanked the statusline in exactly the sessions the owner looks at; `autoload` on renders it in every one of the owner's sessions. `autoload` defaults **OFF** and fails CLOSED, so a colleague cloning the repo never claims the loop tick. With `autoload` off, a session that loaded a `t3:` skill shows the full bar, and a session holding only `.teatree-active` (a non-`t3:` skill requiring `t3:interactive`) or no marker shows the one-line `statusline off (autoload disabled)` hint; an unreadable `autoload` shows an `autoload UNKNOWN` line instead. The loop owner opts in once and keeps the prior behaviour intact.

**Engagement is default-OFF ([#256](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/256)).** Installing the plugin must not force teatree onto every Claude session. A fresh session is *not engaged*: the SessionStart suggester writes no `<session>.pending`, so the PreToolUse skill-loading gate has nothing to demand (never-lockout), and `handle_session_start_bootstrap` emits the one-line how-to advisory `_TEATREE_NOT_ACTIVE_ADVISORY` ("teatree is installed but not active … run /teatree … or run `t3 <overlay> config_setting set autoload true`") instead of arming the loop. The engagement seam is `hook_router._teatree_engaged` = `_autoload_enabled() OR _teatree_active(session) OR <session>.t3-engaged`. The DB-home setting `autoload` (`UserSettings.autoload`, default `False`, resolved cold by `hooks/scripts/teatree_settings.autoload_enabled` — env `T3_AUTOLOAD` first, then the DB store via `_cold_db_bool`, fail-CLOSED; a `[teatree] autoload` TOML value is ignored on read) is the owner's flip to auto-engage every session: when ON, `handle_session_start_bootstrap` touches `.teatree-active` BEFORE the `_loop_auto_load_active` check so today's loop bootstrap fires unchanged. The lighter `<session>.t3-engaged` marker (set by `handle_track_skill_usage` when the ORIGINAL Skill-tool token is `t3:`-namespaced) engages only the suggester — loop scheduling still consults exclusively `.teatree-active`, preserving downstream-overlay loop isolation. A not-engaged compact/resume skips the advisory but still runs `_merge_session_start_context`, so snapshot-recovery / hand-off context is never dropped. `autoload` is the single owner flag: it both *engages* a session and *arms* its loops — the former separate `[loops] auto_load` arming flag is subsumed, so one flip does both.

**Subsumed issues (WS5 — documented, not closed here).** [#789](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/789) (a non-owner session still arming the tick cron) is **subsumed**: under the WS1 claim/lease a non-owner tick finds nothing to claim, so the concern dissolves — #789 was closed-as-completed when WS3 landed and is **not** reopened. Board task #50 (the per-agent TODO-consolidation loop) was **subsumed by invariant 3 / WS4**, itself retired by #4961; #50 is a project-board card, **not** a repository issue, so it is documented as subsumed here and tracked on the board — no repo issue to close. WS5 itself carries no GitHub closing keyword on the #786 umbrella; only an explicitly-authorized epic-completion step does.

**Deep mechanics live in [docs/blueprint/loop-topology.md](docs/blueprint/loop-topology.md).** The DB-lease singleton, the t3-master claim and `SessionStart` loop-slot owner record, the Stop-gate family, post-compaction snapshot recovery, the always-on 5-minute continuous stop-snapshotter (PR-20 — `teatree.core.stop_snapshot.prepare_stop`, called by the Stop slot / `PreCompact` / `t3 <overlay> session prepare-stop`, availability-independent so it refreshes recovery artifacts even while loops are paused, at-risk-worktree capture git-object-native under `refs/t3-resume/`), the three-stage tick (scan → dispatch → render), the full scanner set, multi-overlay/host/identity scanning, the auto-start/dispositions/completion phases, and §5.6.1 Statusline rendering / §5.6.2 Mode + training-wheel / §5.6.3 Mode posture all live there. CORE always-on or cross-referenced scanners worth naming here: `architectural_review` (cadence-and-merge-count; the `arch_review` Loop row is its switch); `scanning_news` ([#1191](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1191), with each article decision settled in `PendingArticleSuggestion` and only scoped, checked ideas filed through the issue facade); `dogfood_smoke`/`eval_local` ([#1308](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1308)); `backlog_sweep` ([#2419](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2419), [#4344](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4344) — DAILY backlog GROUPING that closes nothing for real: every reduction folds the member's body into an existing host (`ticket fold`) and proves it landed (`ticket fold-check`) before the row retires, gated by `ask_before_backlog_sweep_closes`; the `Loop` row plus the active preset are the single switch); `review_request_merge_react` (reacts `:merge:` once an MR merges); the closure-reverify Stop WARN (non-blocking so it cannot deadlock the loop) and its hard-blocking sibling the completion-claim Stop gate ([#2665](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2665), `handle_completion_claim_gate` — refuses turn-end on a multi-deliverable "done" lacking an on-target deliverable→evidence map; loop-driven turns only, kill-switch + `[gate-escape:completion_claim: <reason>]` token); the `SessionHandover` hand-off (`t3 <overlay> handover`, injected on `SessionStart`; [#4194](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4194) — the DB row is the delivery surface and the XDG mirror is read ONLY when the DB is unreachable, --park deliberately targets the next session even with a live owner, an author holds at most one unclaimed row that later hand-offs are absorbed into behind a fence, and no row the DB accepts is claimable by nobody: a partial unique index plus two check constraints on `SessionHandover` refuse a self-addressed target and the `loop-runner` slot alias; the sub-agent wrap-up is ROW STATE — the `SessionHandover.subagent_wrapup` union of every agent its barriers ever enumerated, plus `last_barrier_at` and `barrier_ran_at_latest_handoff` — RENDERED onto the delivery surface (mirror + claimed payload) at delivery and never spliced into the payload, so an authored body can never be edited by the barrier, an agent whose worktree has since vanished is still named, and a hand-off that ran no barrier says so instead of inheriting an earlier barrier's freshness; and a payload that resolves EMPTY writes NO row at all, since a hand-off that transfers nothing is not durable state — ONE resolution, taken after the sub-agent barrier and immediately before the write, decides both the refusal and what is persisted, because state settling across that slow barrier otherwise let the gate pass on one payload while a second resolve wrote another); the open-PR statusline anchor ([#271](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/271)) read from a per-tick `open-prs.json` cache so the display costs no API call; the manual-MR reconciler ([#1912](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1912), `loop/manual_pr_reconcile.py`, invoked from `render_phase` alongside the open-PR snapshot writer) that upserts `PullRequest` rows from the same per-tick `my_pr.*` signals for manually-opened MRs resolving to a ticket (close-keyword footer + `Ticket.extra['prs']` fallbacks, unique on URL, footerless MRs stay statusline-only, a live-merged PR transitions via `mark_merged`) so a PR opened outside the ship pipeline still enters review-request tracking and the FSM; and the typed `jobs_for_domain` seam ([#1482](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1482)) partitioning the per-overlay scanner fan-out.

The overlay provision smoke (`t3 dogfood overlay-provision-smoke`) binds an explicit `--fixture-ticket-url`, then the overlay's `OverlayConfig.dogfood_smoke_fixture_url`, then the core fixture. It qualifies a CLI short overlay name to the registered name before checkout lookup, gives `workspace ticket` 180 seconds for its orphan scan, and ends its plan at `worktree teardown --path` for the fixture checkout. After a run, fixture cleanup removes only checkouts the run created, including sibling checkouts; pre-existing checkouts remain protected. It never sweeps unrelated workspaces.

The [#1554](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1554) `issue_implementer` mini-loop closes the auto-implement-intake gate: each tick its per-overlay scanner claims open issues via the TOCTOU-safe `ImplementedIssueMarker.claim`. It is **behind a double gate** — an `in_flight_count < limit` budget and per-issue `claim()` idempotency; whether the loop runs at all is the active preset's opinion, not a second per-loop scalar. That `limit` is not a constant: `resolve_intake_concurrency` ([#3992](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3992)) hands back the resource loop's headroom-derived number, falling back to `issue_implementer_max_concurrent` verbatim whenever it is missing, stale, or switched off. Each claim emits `issue_intake.admitted` routing to `t3:orchestrator` as a **maker-side kickoff** — it starts the normal pipeline, issues no `MergeClear`, gains no merge authority (the §17.4 maker≠checker boundary holds).

**A held budget slot is released on evidence, and a jammed budget is visible ([#3978](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3978)).** The budget gate above is only as good as what frees a slot: with a small budget and a 24h stall grace, two claims that stop progressing close intake for a day, and nothing surfaces it — a full budget makes the scanner factory return `None`, so the tick legitimately does nothing and reports success. `ImplementedIssueMarker.find_stale` therefore releases a slot on the FACT that ends the attempt rather than on a grace the ticket will never reach: a MERGED PR completes the marker immediately (PR_OPENED is deliberately not a `marker_release_states()` member, so a ticket frozen there after its PR landed satisfied no release condition at all), and a ticket with nothing queued and no open PR is held to a short dead-claim grace rather than the mid-flight one. Visibility is one reading — `core.intake.budget.read_intake_budget` — shared by the tick, which logs `IntakeBudget.report()` naming every held slot instead of declining silently, and by `t3 doctor check`, which HARD-FAILs (`_check_intake_budget_deadlock`) when a full budget is held entirely by claims with no active task and no open PR.

**A cancellation is a DECISION, and a decision survives the next tick ([#4105](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4105)).** The release rule above knew four ways an attempt ends, all of them accidents — terminal, landed, gone, stalled — so an operator cancelling a claim landed in the one bucket that undoes itself: a cancelled attempt has nothing queued and no PR, so the dead-claim grace released it as ABANDONED, the single state `claim()` re-claims from, and intake handed the same issue back within two hours (observed on [#4048](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4048), twice). `Ticket.newest_task_was_cancelled()` is the ONE reading of "an operator said stop" — a `FailureKind.CANCELLED` newest task — and `find_stale` releases such a marker as `DECLINED`: terminal, so the budget still self-heals, and outside the re-claim path, so the decline holds. It needs no grace, because a grace buys time for an attempt that might still be alive and a human has already said this one is over; `SUPERSEDED` is excluded, being rework the factory initiated. Something changing is what puts the issue back in play: a PENDING/CLAIMED task on the ticket holds the slot ahead of both branches, whatever the last outcome was. The `stuck_ticket_redispatch` drain reads the SAME predicate and skips such a ticket, because one actor honouring the decision while the other undoes it is no decision at all — a cancel leaves exactly the shape both of that sweep's admission predicates match, so it re-queued the phase on the next tick and two cancels were needed to stop it, by exhausting a repair budget that then pages a human about a doomed phase rather than an honoured one. `State.relinquished()` (ABANDONED + DECLINED) names the states in which the attempt was LET GO rather than finished — the fleet-claim heartbeat skips them, so no refreshed ref keeps a sibling instance out of work nobody is doing, and the completion receiver leaves them alone rather than erasing why the attempt ended.

**A landed merge is recorded where merges LAND, not where one route records them ([#3984](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3984)).** Both readings above are drawn from `PullRequest.state`, so the release rule and its alarm are only as good as what writes that field. It was written solely by the keystone post hook, and the sweep — the path most merges actually take — reaches the forge through `PrApiClient.merge_pr_bound` instead: its merges left the row reading `open` forever (measured: 32 of 33 rows), which held the slot *and* silenced the alarm, because a stale `open` reads to the alarm as proof of a live attempt. The write therefore sits at `core/merge/execution.execute_bound_merge` — the same single chokepoint every merge route already crosses for the gates above — so no route can land a merge without recording it, and record-and-proceed there (log, never raise) because the forge merge is already irreversible. `PullRequestQuerySet.reconcile_forge_states` is the convergence pass for rows merged out of band entirely: the intake tick runs it over the budget HOLDERS' rows before every budget read (bounded by the slot count, not the ledger size), and `t3 loop reclaim-markers` runs it over the whole overlay, so the operator lever no longer needs a forced-zero grace to free a slot whose PR demonstrably merged. The hourly `board_reconcile` scan runs it too, over one rotating page of the live rows bounded by its probe budget (`settle_live_pr_rows`).

**The keystone squashes unless told not to.** `t3 <overlay> ticket merge <clear_id> --no-squash` threads `squash=False` through `execute_bound_merge` to the backend's `merge_pr_squash_bound`, which asks GitLab for `squash: false` and GitHub for `merge_method=merge`, so a merge that must keep its second parent (an upstream ancestry link) lands as a merge commit. The consumed CLEAR records it (`MergeClear.merged_without_squash`); every other caller keeps the squash default.

**ONE intake scanner, ONE decision function ([#3634](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3634)).** The two former issue-intake scanners (`assigned_issues`, `issue_implementer`) are folded into a single `issue_intake` scanner so the factory cannot hold two divergent opinions about which issue becomes work. Every candidate runs through `core.intake.factory_admission.decide_intake`, evaluated top-down, first match wins: (1) `needs-triage` present → IGNORE (maintainer hold); (2) one of the overlay's `exclude_labels` present → IGNORE (operator hold, [#4134](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4134)); (3) the row is an umbrella/epic tracking parent → IGNORE ([#4105](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4105)); (4) an active ticket / claim / forge read-back already exists → IGNORE; (5) author trusted → ACT immediately, with no admit label, no assignment, and no grace window; (6) author untrusted **and** the owner-applied admit label present → ACT; (7) author untrusted, no label → IGNORE (fail-closed). The admit label is the `issue_implementer_label` setting, defaulting to the shipped `t3-auto` convention (`factory_admission.DEFAULT_ADMIT_LABEL`) — a forge-only convention with no other code coupling. This retires the per-overlay `admission_policy` tiers and the `issue_implementer_require_label` narrowing gate, both superseded by the table.

**Issue intake repo scope ([#5059](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5059)).** The per-overlay `issue_intake_repos` list replaces the owned scope for both trusted-author and admit-label discovery and may name repos the overlay does not declare. Valid `owner/repo` or nested group slugs survive malformed neighbours; malformed entries are dropped with a warning naming them. Empty or all-malformed lists use `owned_repo_slugs(overlay)`. Normalization rejects common branch prefixes such as `fix/`. Disposition, triage, external outcomes, and work-item filing continue to use the full owned scope.

**The exclude rule is the operator's reservation surface ([#4134](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4134)).** It reads `OverlayConfig.exclude_labels` — already plumbed to the scanner and, until now, never consulted there — and shares ONE predicate (`core.intake.label_admission.excluded`) with the GitLab sync path, so the two intakes cannot drift on what "excluded" means. It defaults to EMPTY, which excludes nothing, so an overlay that configured no denylist keeps its pre-#4134 verdicts. It sits directly under `needs-triage` because the two are the same kind of thing — a label the human applied to withhold an issue — and ABOVE the umbrella rule because an explicit per-issue operator act outranks a structural classification: a row that is both an epic and excluded logs the exclusion its operator wrote, not the shape the detector inferred. The two label rules keep separate verdicts because `needs-triage` is a shipped convention and the denylist is per-overlay data, and the log line has to say which one held the issue.

**An umbrella row is not an implementable unit ([#4105](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4105)).** The umbrella rule sits above "work exists" because umbrella-ness is a property of the ROW, not of what has happened to it. An epic carries no single acceptance criterion and no bounded diff, so an agent handed one holds a bounded `issue_implementer_max_concurrent` slot for an unbounded scope and displaces a real, implementable ticket for the whole run. `core.intake.umbrella.umbrella_reason` recognises one by three INDEPENDENT signals, any one sufficient: a LABEL in the operator-maintained `umbrella_issue_labels` set (the explicit exclusion list, per-overlay overridable, and an EMPTY set is the honest "none configured" rather than a silent fallback to the shipped one); a DECLARATION that the row is never closed ([#2663](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2663)); or the STRUCTURAL shape of a tracking parent — a checklist whose items are bare child issue references, and no acceptance / definition-of-done heading anywhere. The `Epic:` title prefix is deliberately NOT a signal: a prefix convention stops working the first time someone titles an epic differently, and admitting it as a disjunct would make the weakest signal sufficient on its own. An emphasised `DO NOT CLOSE` is admitted on the opposite grounds — it is not a way of naming a category but an explicit instruction that no state of the world ends the claim, which IS the unbounded-scope property the umbrella rule refuses. [#2663](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2663), the standing dream-pass ledger, carries no label and lists prose checklist items, so it defeated both other signals and a coding agent was dispatched at a row with nothing to implement. A false positive STARVES a genuinely implementable issue, so all three branches are narrow and the decline logs which signal fired: the declaration counts only where the directive both LEADS an emphasised line (a heading, or bold-opened text), sits outside a fenced code block (a quoted example documents the detector rather than invoking it), and ENDS there at line's end, sentence-final punctuation, or a dash introducing an aside — a colon, comma, or attached hyphen instead means the phrase runs on to describe some other object, so a heading naming another object is prose and an unemphasised "please don't close this yet" is a temporary hold on work that stays implementable. The two EXPLICIT signals are tested ahead of the inferred structural one, so a row carrying several reports the reason its author actually wrote; an acceptance heading defeats only the structural branch, because it withdraws an inference and there is none to withdraw once the row has stated the answer itself.

Discovery is scoped so the factory never fetches work it may not do: one author-scoped query per handle in the trusted union (`CodeHostBackend.list_authored_issues`) and one label-scoped query for the admit label (`CodeHostBackend.list_labeled_issues`), both bound to the selected intake repo slugs. Selection narrows; the decision function decides — every candidate is RE-CHECKED at claim time through the shared author-trust decision, so an over-returning forge query cannot launder an untrusted author past the fail-closed last rule, and the classifier's private-repo bypass cannot hand an unlisted collaborator the keys to the factory. An unresolvable author, an unparsable issue URL, and an unset admit label all resolve to REFUSE.

**ONE author-trust decision function, two gates ([#3577](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3577)).** `core/review/author_trust.decide_author_trust(subject, *, gate, extra_trusted)` is the single home for "may the factory act on this author's work?", over the existing `classify_author` / `classify_pr_provenance` resolvers. Its types are `AuthorSubject` (slug / author / host kind / same-repo), `AutonomyGate` (`INTAKE` | `MERGE`) and `TrustVerdict` (`AUTONOMOUS` | `HUMAN_REVIEW`) — `TrustVerdict` rather than `Autonomy`, because `teatree.config.Autonomy` already names the operator's babysit/notify/full autonomy TIER, a different axis. All four call sites consume it, so intake and merge cannot disagree about who is trusted: `loop/scanners/issue_intake.author_is_trusted` and `core/review/stranger_pr.pr_is_admitted` at the `INTAKE` gate, `loop/scanners/pr_sweep_decision.untrusted_merge_provenance` and the §17.4.3 keystone `core/merge/authorization` at the `MERGE` gate. The two gates' asymmetry now lives INSIDE the decision rather than as a hand-rolled conjunction at each site: `INTAKE` additionally requires EXPLICIT trusted-set membership (closing the private-repo bypass), while `MERGE` applies the strict fork model. `classify_pr_provenance` takes `extra_trusted` so both gates draw on the same trust union.

**Intake is by TRUSTED AUTHOR, not by a hand-applied label ([#3235](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3235)).** The owner does not tag tickets, so the issue's author *is* the authority. The trusted set is an explicit **UNION** of three named sources — the owner's `user_identity_aliases`, the `trusted_issue_authors` allowlist (other humans whose issues the factory may act on), and the canonical `TrustedIdentity` rows; `config.effective_trusted_issue_authors` resolves the two config tiers and `core.review.author_trust` unions in the DB tier. This governs **intake only**: merge authority is untouched, and a substrate PR still needs a recorded human approver whoever filed the issue that spawned it.

**Stranger-PR policy — ignore until admitted ([#3634](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3634) §4).** An untrusted author's PR is invisible to the factory until the owner applies the admit label: `core.review.stranger_pr.pr_is_admitted` runs the SAME `decide_intake` table over the PR payload, and `ReviewerPrsScanner` skips an unadmitted PR before any review dispatch. That closes the prompt-injection surface an unattended reviewer would otherwise expose to arbitrary PR text and conserves review cycles. Once admitted the PR IS reviewed — and it is still never auto-merged, because merge authority is a separate axis the label does not touch. The gate is disarmed when no trusted set is configured (no admission context), so an ad-hoc caller keeps the pre-#3634 behaviour.

**MR triage is a pure ladder, and it has no silent no-op.** `core/review/mr_triage.triage(facts, *, thresholds)` is the single decision core for "what does this open MR need next?", over frozen `MrFacts` (draft flag, `CiState`, tri-state `ReviewRequestState`, repo owner, ages, stacked-base and escalation inputs) and returning one `TriageVerdict` (`TriageAction` + `TriageReason` + a rung-owned `detail`). The module does NO I/O: the caller reads the forge and does the arithmetic, so the policy is a table under test rather than a scanner's control flow. `_LADDER` is first-match-wins and its order is load-bearing — `FIX_CI` outranks EVERY other rung, a draft and a review-exempt repo included, because every MR owes green CI whatever else is true of it, so a red draft asks for the fix rather than sitting broken in silence. Below red CI a draft leaves triage entirely, review exemption answers for everything social, and the stacked-on-unreviewed-base flag, the repo-fit `ESCALATE_MODEL` rung and the `WORK_GROUP_NOT_READY` hold (`WAIT`, while any sibling in the same unit of work is not review-ready) outrank both the review-request and the nag rungs, so an MR whose disposition needs a judgement call — or whose group would be reviewed as a fragment — is never auto-pinged. Anything the ladder does not match falls to `ASK_OWNER`: a gap in the policy surfaces as a question, never as a dropped MR. `TriageThresholds` carries the four durations — `engineering_nag` defaults to the same 2 days `ReviewNagScanner` uses, `devops_nag` defaults to 5 days and `stale_no_review` defaults to 7 days; these are named, overridable settings rather than ladder constants, and `nag_backoff_cap` uses the operator's `review_nag_max_interval_days` (default 30), resolved by the transport and passed in; `review_nag_max_interval_days` is a shared communication setting (default 30) read by both the followup scanner and the PR board, while `review_nag_reask_mention` remains followup-owned; `RepoOwner` picks between the two nag intervals and every unknown-owner path resolves to the PATIENT (DevOps) one.

**A work group is connected components over shared signals, never one group key.** `core/review/work_group.group_members(items, *, generic_scopes)` answers "which open merge requests are ONE unit of work?" over `(url, title)` pairs, returning each url's whole group, so the batch gate can hold an individually-ready merge request until every sibling in its group is ready too. Like `mr_triage` it is pure — stdlib only, no forge read, no Django, no clock — so the policy is a table under test and the caller owns every read. Sharing a signal is TRANSITIVE, so the pass is union-find rather than a single group key: A and B on a ticket reference plus B and C on a feature flag is one group of three, and a single-key grouper would split that trio and release A while C is still unfinished. `signals_for` extracts three `GroupSignal` kinds — a `.../-/(issues|work_items)/<n>` url or a `<repo>#<n>` reference (`ticket`), a bracketed tag (`flag`), and the conventional-commit `(scope)` — as forge-agnostic supersets of the shapes a release-notes validator enforces, so core carries no forge- or project-specific prefix. Extraction is deliberately NARROW because the two errors are not symmetric: an over-grouped merge request waits on a sibling it has nothing to do with, silently and forever, while an under-grouped one is merely released on its own. Hence the `generic_scopes` filter (a scope every housekeeping change shares says nothing about being one unit of work), hence the placeholder and security-scanner bracket literals that are not feature flags, and hence a signal-less merge request being a group of exactly itself — which satisfies the batch gate trivially. `core/review/work_group.GENERIC_SCOPES` is that fixed list, passed IN to the grouper rather than read by it so the grouper stays pure — stdlib only, and testable as a table.

**R1 — a merge request is broadcast for review with its whole work group or not at all.** `core/gates/review_request_batch_gate.work_group_ready(mr_url=…, overlay_name=…)` answers whether every open member of the subject's group is review-ready, over the operator's GLOBAL cross-project open-merge-request listing rather than a url-prefixed subset: a filtered list makes a cross-repo group look smaller than it is, and a group that looks complete fires early — the exact premature broadcast the gate exists to prevent. A member is ready when its draft state is confirmed NOT_DRAFT, its `CiState` is GREEN, and it is not held by a live pause reaction (`resumed_at` lifts that permanently). **Every axis fails CLOSED** — `DraftState.UNKNOWN`, a `CiState` the allowlist cannot name, `PauseState.UNKNOWN`, an unresolvable host, an erroring listing, and a merge request absent from the listing all answer not-ready — because `review_request_draft_gate` already shipped the opposite shape once and a probe that answered the benign value on a failed read made the owner's hold mechanism inert exactly when it was needed. The one axis that does NOT hold silently is an oversize group: past `WORK_GROUP_MAX_MEMBERS` (12) the shared signal is likelier a coincidence than one unit of work, so `ask_mr_state` surfaces the merge request to the owner instead of holding it where nobody can see. A group of one satisfies the gate trivially, so R9's standalone merge request is never stalled. `review_exempt_repos_count_toward_group_readiness` decides whether an exempt member still gates its group (default `true`, the conservative reading — an unready infrastructure change keeps holding the batch it supports); an exempt member is always LISTED and never offered a broadcast line either way. Both review-request chokepoints (`review_request_post`, `review_request_check`) consult `work_group_batch_refusal` AFTER the draft gate and BEFORE `should_post_review_request`, so a held batch leaves no orphan `ReviewRequestPost` claim to wedge every later post. `t3 review-request group-status [--mr-url …]` (`review_request_groups`) is the read-only half — every group, every member's blockers, and the exact ordered `t3 review-request post` lines for a ready one — posting nothing and raising no question, and exiting non-zero on an unreadable forge so a failed listing can never render as a reassuringly empty queue.

**One payload→`CiState` reading, shared by every review surface.** `core/review/mr_ci_state` holds `PIPELINE_FIELDS`, `GREEN_STATUSES` and the `CI_BY_STATUS` table, so the batch gate, `MrTriageScanner` and `MyPrsScanner` cannot drift on what "green" means. The table is an ALLOWLIST: a cancelled, skipped or manual pipeline — and any status nobody has seen — resolves to `UNKNOWN`, which never satisfies a green and never names a failure, because a status this code does not understand is exactly the one it must not guess about.
