An unenriched payload (none of `PIPELINE_FIELDS` present) is likewise `UNKNOWN` rather than red.

**The followup flow sends the review request the ladder finds owed.** `loop/scanners/review_request_send.ReviewRequestSendScanner` runs in `Domain.FOLLOWUP`, so it is selected only with a messaging backend and a posture that lets the owner's voice out. It walks `MrTriageScanner.triaged()` and acts on each `REQUEST_REVIEW` verdict through the sanctioned `review_request_post` command (`call_command`), so the dedup claim, the on-behalf gate, the #117 send-proxy (allowlist, redaction, `SendAudit`), the post and the `ReviewRequestPost` record stay in that one chokepoint, and a later pass finds the request in both the ledger and the channel. What the command trusts a human to have checked is the autonomous caller's own, in this order: the owner's answer at the CURRENT head (`owner_answer_at_head` — any answer but "Post the review request" defers the send at that head), no standing hold at that head (`pr_sweep_decision.head_review_state`, the #4380 sweep's reading, so a hold beside another reviewer's later `merge_safe` still defers), a `merge_safe` verdict bound to the head or the owner's "Post" answer in its place, the owning ticket when one exists (`resolve_gated_ticket`; a merge request no ticket owns reaches the command with no ticket id, which then skips its anti-vacuity and review-state gates, the same rule as the merge-time ticket gates), a title and body the overlay's `metadata.validate_pr` accepts, and a live head (`fetch_live_head_sha`) that still matches the listing. A refusal becomes one capped, deduped `ask_mr_state` question tagged `[head <sha12>]`, the same tag SHIP's surveyor puts on its own question, so an answer given while nothing could be sent is honoured when the sender takes over. A question about an older head is superseded, as is one about a blocker that head has not seen (a blocker that alternates is asked about once), the replacement inherits the superseded row's slot under the cap, another caller's untagged question is left alone and counts as asked, a hold landing retires the head-bound question, and `ask_mr_state` asks nothing more at a head the owner has answered about. Only `read_failed_failsafe`, `already_claimed`, `already_posted`, `authorship_unreadable` and `draft_state_unknown` retry quietly as `review_request.send_deferred`; every other outcome, a reason added later included, is a `review_request.send_refused` the owner is asked about. A pass stops after `max_posts_per_tick` (3) LANDED posts, so refusals never starve a later MR, and one MR raising is logged and deferred without ending the pass. The command routes the text through the send-proxy ahead of the approval's transaction (so a refused destination keeps its `SendAudit` row), rolls its dedup claim back on every exit that posted nothing, a transport raise or a missing backend included, and finalizes the claim of a landed post before anything else can raise; `MrTriageScanner` reads an unposted claim as no request at all, so a claim a killed process left behind is reclaimed by the guard instead of stranding the MR. SHIP's triage still asks the owner whenever the sender will not run: no messaging backend, a forbidding posture, or a `followup` loop `loop_admits` does not admit. The command's post runs through `on_behalf_egress.observed_channel_post`, so the followup preview (`t3 loops tick --loop followup --dry-run`) suppresses and reports it, and skips the permalink read and the on-disk review-message record.

**A cross-project MR list carries no CI, so the red-MR lane reads it live.** GitLab's `/merge_requests?scope=created_by_me` payload has no `head_pipeline`, no `status_check_rollup` and no `mergeable_state`, so every MR in it resolved to the empty status — which `MyPrsScanner` classifies as legitimately in-progress. `my_pr.failed` was structurally unreachable for those MRs however red they were. `loop/scanners/my_prs_ci.BoundedCiEnricher` fills the gap per MR through `CodeHostQuery.required_checks_status` — never `classify_required_rollup` directly, because on GitLab the rollup fetch returns PIPELINES rather than check-runs and an empty required set classifies `green`, manufacturing a false green for an MR nobody read. A failed verdict still routes the MR to the debug agent; an unreadable verdict remains unknown and is retried. Bounded on four axes: URL-allowed PRs only, payload-carried-nothing only, the per-tick ceiling `MAX_READS_PER_TICK` shared by every MR needing a read so the budget follows their count up to that ceiling, and a memo keyed on the head SHA so an MR nobody pushed to is read once and a new push is a new key.

**The re-ping interval follows the repo review-cadence class.** `ReviewNagScanner` resolved one 2-day window for every MR, so a repo reviewed on a slower schedule was re-pinged on the engineering cadence. The interval comes from `TriageThresholds.nag_interval(owner)` — the base the per-attempt backoff below multiplies — with the owner resolved per MR through the `OverlayReview.repo_owner_for_slug` hook. Core's default answers ENGINEERING for every repo — deliberately NOT the ladder's fail-safe, because `MrFacts` defaults to the PATIENT owner so a verdict built from missing facts does not act, whereas an overlay that declares no ownership model must keep the cadence it shipped with. An overlay with a model owns its own unknown-repo answer. The mention and the text are unchanged: the interval is the only thing ownership varies.

**Each unanswered re-ask waits longer, and the nag train ends in a question rather than forever.** A flat interval re-pings the same group at the same rate whether it is the first ask or the twentieth, which is how a reminder becomes noise people learn to ignore. `TriageThresholds.nag_interval_for_attempt(owner, attempt)` returns `min(nag_interval(owner) * fibonacci_step(attempt), nag_backoff_cap)`, so the Fibonacci step MULTIPLIES the per-owner base rather than replacing it and the engineering/DevOps patience distinction survives the backoff: engineering widens 2, 2, 4, 6, 10, 16, 26 days, DevOps 5, 5, 10, 15, 25. `core/modelkit/fibonacci.fibonacci_step` is the shared unitless sequence — the local-stack queue multiplies it by a minute, the nag by a day — so the two schedules cannot drift apart. `ReviewNagScanner` shares the latest `ReviewRequestPost.nag_count` and one backoff across every open request in a channel/thread: one transaction claims `last_nag_at` and `nag_count` for all siblings, a confirmed reply records its timestamp for all, and a gated or failed post restores the claim. A re-ask that never reached Slack must not advance the backoff — otherwise a permanently-blocked thread walks itself to the ceiling having delivered nothing. `nag_backoff_at_cap` compares the UNCLIPPED product (the `min` has already erased the overshoot), and at the ceiling the scanner records an owner question through `ask_mr_state` instead of nagging again: the group has been asked enough, so the remaining question is for the person who owns the merge request.

**The nag fails CLOSED on every axis, because its harmful direction is POSTING.** A re-ping is colleague-visible: it mentions people who replied in the thread, commented, approved, or were requested as reviewers when each forge identity maps to exactly one active Slack user. The requester's own reactions and identity are excluded; when no colleague maps safely, `review_nag_reask_mention` supplies a group or user fallback, and a re-ask with no safe mention is held. `ApprovalReadState` (`APPROVED` / `NOT_APPROVED` / `UNKNOWN`, the sibling of `DraftState` over the `ApprovalState` payload) makes the unreadable case its own answer, and every unreadable answer SKIPS: no code-host backend, a failed open-state lookup, an `UNKNOWN` open state, an unparsable URL, `DraftState.UNKNOWN`, `ApprovalReadState.UNKNOWN`, and `PauseState.UNKNOWN`. A merge request whose review request the owner PAUSED (`review_pause.read_pause_state`) is likewise never pinged — escalating past the owner's own hold would @-mention colleagues about work its author has said is not ready. None of these close the row, so a later merge-react still fires and the next tick retries once the read works.

**Review exemption is a declared axis, never a reading of repo ownership.** On some repos the owner asks for review in person, so a posted review request is noise a colleague has to dismiss. `core/review/repo_exemption` answers "is this repo exempt?" from two explicit declarations — the `review_exempt_repos` setting and the `OverlayReview.review_exempt_repo_slugs` hook — matched on the host-free `slug_namespace_matches` segment grammar, so a repo pattern means one thing everywhere. The two are a TIER and a PIN, not a union: the hook is the table an overlay ships, and the setting is the operator's opinion over it, winning in BOTH directions because a `!`-prefixed entry SUBTRACTS an exemption rather than only adding one — a union could only widen, so an overlay-declared exemption outlived every operator opinion and a changed policy needed a code change and a merge. Both feed one flat list where the DEEPEST match decides (`slug_segment_depth`, the same host-stripped segment axis the match runs on), so `!org/one-repo` carves a single repo out of an `org` namespace exemption and the source that wrote a pattern carries no weight of its own — a pin beats the overlay by being written at its target's own depth, never by being read second. A TIE at equal depth resolves to NOT exempt, the same direction every unresolvable input already fails toward, so a contradiction is never read as an exemption. It is deliberately NOT derived from `repo_owner_for_slug`, whose fail-safe answers DEVOPS for every repo it does not recognise: an exemption read off that would silently cover every unrecognised repo, and a review request that never goes out is the one failure nobody sees. Both `review_request_post` and `review_request_check` refuse an exempt repo with `reason=review_exempt_repo` and exit 2, BEFORE the dedup claim (an orphan `ReviewRequestPost` would wedge every later post on `already_claimed`) — the same position, and the same rationale, as the draft gate. The ladder carries the matching rung `MrFacts.review_exempt` → `TriageAction.NONE`, placed directly BELOW the failed-CI rung because an exempt merge request owes no review request but still owes green CI. Both sources default empty, so the whole path ships inert.

**The MR-triage surveyor surfaces verdicts and asks the owner when needed.** `loop/scanners/mr_triage_scan.MrTriageScanner` is the other half of the pure ladder — it reads the operator's own open MRs, does the arithmetic `triage()` refuses to do, and emits one `mr_triage.verdict` signal per MR into the `action_needed` statusline zone, bounded to `MrTriageScanner.max_mrs_per_tick` (20) per pass so one survey stays a reviewable batch. It has NO colleague-post path and NO dispatch. `MrTriageScanner.triaged()` yields each in-scope MR with its verdict, which the followup sender above acts on; where no sender will run (a forbidding posture, no messaging backend, or a `followup` loop `loop_admits` does not admit), a green, ready MR with no review request becomes a durable owner question through `ask_mr_state` instead (`ask_owner_on_missing_review`), tagged with the MR's head so the sender reads the owner's answer once it takes over. It lives in `Domain.SHIP` and is unconditional. Facts it cannot read honestly stay UNKNOWN rather than assumed: absence of a `ReviewRequestPost` row is NOT proof nobody was asked, and an unreadable approval probe takes the MR out of the requested lane, so both surface as `ASK_OWNER` instead of a confident wrong verdict. The scan is TWO-PASS: `group_members` runs first over the UNFILTERED global listing to resolve every work group and each member's readiness (not-draft AND `CiState.GREEN`, both failing CLOSED), and only then is each MR triaged with `work_group` / `work_group_unready_members` populated — grouping a url-prefix-filtered subset would make a cross-repo group look smaller than it is, and a group that looks settled surfaces a fragment as review-ready. Readiness is resolved only for MRs that HAVE siblings, and each MR's `CiState` is read at most once per tick, so the pass costs no enrichment round trip a group of one would not need. The nag-patience inputs come from the same `OverlayReview.repo_owner_for_slug` hook the review nag uses, so the two can never disagree about how long a repo waits.

**The lease covers raw branch/PR work, not only the ticket ([#3561](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3561)).** The ticket lease excludes two lifecycle actors from one ticket but has no visibility into work done at the raw git/PR level — a hand-opened PR, an ad-hoc `git worktree add`, a direct branch push — so work that never entered the lifecycle was unlocked by construction. `core/work_lease.py` registers a claim at the two seams where branch/PR work becomes real (`loop/manual_pr_reconcile.py` and `core/provision/worktree_adopt.py`) under every identity the work is known by: the branch, the PR, and the ISSUE it serves. The issue identity is the join that makes raw work visible to the lifecycle — `foreign_work_holder` reads it, and `IssueIntakeScanner._claim` (covering both the plain marker claim and the fleet-ref claim) DEFERS to a live foreign holder rather than blocking, the least invasive resolution and the one that never stands in a human's way. The rows are `LoopLease` records under a `work:` prefix, so this reuses the backend-agnostic conditional-`UPDATE` CAS with no new table, and the TTL (`DEFAULT_WORK_TTL_SECONDS`, 4h — long enough that a human mid-PR is not preempted between pushes, short enough that an abandoned claim frees the loop within a working period) is the fallback release.

**WRITE parallel, MERGE serial ([#3634](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3634) §1).** The `wip` fan-out is split into two independently-budgeted lanes by phase. Implementation (coding / testing / reviewing) runs `write_wip` wide; the merge lane (shipping) is single-flight, so the next PR always rebases against what just landed — that serialization IS the conflict-safety guarantee, and `merge_wip` is clamped to 1 rather than honoured above it. Each lane counts its own in-flight rows, so a saturated merge lane never starves implementation. Within a lane, candidates are listed in admission-priority order and then spread round-robin across a CHEAP area key (`loop.phases.conflict_area.area_key` — the ticket's declared repos, else the issue URL's repo slug), so a fan-out prefers tickets in different repos. It is deliberately a heuristic, not conflict prediction: no diff fetch, no merge-base probe, and it only re-orders — nothing is dropped, so an unknowable area can never starve. Set with `t3 <overlay> wip split N`.

**An owner question is a short card, checked before it is stored and again before it is sent ([#4990](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4990)).** `core/modelkit/question_card.QuestionCard` is the one shape an owner question takes: the question (one sentence ending in `?`), one explanatory sentence, an optional quoted line, and 2 to 4 options with the recommended one first and a one-line consequence each. An optional subject URL binds a card to the pull request or post it concerns and survives the evidence round trip. Its decision kind, checked facts and blocker are required STORED fields (`DeferredQuestion.evidence`, read with `questions list --json`) and are never part of the text the owner reads. One pure renderer (`plain_text`) and one checker (`shown_problems`) serve record time and send time, so the text that was checked is the text that is sent: pattern rules, with no list of names, refuse internal shorthand (ids and id lists, `snake_case` names, hashes, paths, backticks, flags, `key=value`) in every shown field, quoted text included, and the card is capped at 120 words counted as the owner reads them. `DeferredQuestion.record(card=)` is the only way to make an owner row and raises with every problem at once; `questions record` takes it as `--why`, `--blocker` and `--options` (each `{label, description, recommended}`, the recommended one first), and a headless stop carries the same card in `user_input_card`. Every phase's brief states each limit, built at call time from the same `question_card` constants, and a card the recorder refuses is a `result_schema_invalid` fault that earns one corrective resume on any phase: the agent decides it itself unless only the owner can, and a second refusal escalates. A producer never interpolates internal names into a card: fixed prose and counts only, and any free text (a drafted reply, a pull request title, the owner's own reason) goes in through `QuestionCard.with_quote`, which leaves the quote off when the whole card would stop passing. `core/owner_question_message` renders a row as plain text and as Block Kit from the one layout: the first post carries both, with one button per option (the recommended one `primary`), and reaches Slack exactly as rendered — no kind prefix, no sentence split, no 90-character wrap of the text (a post carrying blocks keeps its fallback text) and no link rewriting; a row recorded before cards existed goes through the same renderer. A re-ask is a short plain line in the question's own thread, due 1, 2, 4, 7, 12 … hours after the first post (Fibonacci hours, read from the post's own Slack `ts`; colleague review nags keep their days). No owner-question DM is sent between 22:00 and 08:00 in the owner's zone — the active schedule's, else Europe/Paris, never the project's UTC (`loop/preset_resolution.owner_zone`): a first post held overnight goes out at 08:00 and a bump due at night is sent once then, at the step then current. Every owner send re-checks the row (`shown_problems_for`) in all three drains, so an old row that does not read plainly is WITHHELD instead (`core/question_heal.withhold`: it moves to the internal queue under an audit row, its posted root message is replaced by a neutral line, and it is never posted, bumped or counted again); the lifecycle-incident detector reports it as `withheld_owner_question` — to be asked again as a card under the same dedupe marker, never dismissed — and the waiting task moves to the new card. An answer draft that fails the checks is not asked at all: `core/question_heal.record_withheld` parks it, audited, on its task, and `core/models/question_withheld.carried_wait` hands that task to the card that later asks the same marker again. An answer, tapped or typed, only resolves the row, resumes a parked task and closes the card: carrying out what the chosen option promises is the producer's job, read from its own answered row on its next pass (directive ratify and clarify, the review-request send, the override lift, the owner-action pass that voids a merge), so a card whose producer reads no answer must not promise an action. A Slack answer also writes an `answered` `DeferredQuestionAudit` naming `slack:<user id>`, the identity an owner action is carried out under.

**Fleet-safety Stage 1 — idempotent read-back as the safety net.** Running two teatree instances (a laptop + a headless box) against one GitHub repo double-claims work, because coordination state (`ImplementedIssueMarker`, the outward-post ledgers) is per-instance SQLite while the work domain (issues, branches, PRs) is global on the forge. Stage 1 does NOT introduce shared state — the durable claim-ref mutex is Stage 2. It makes the local coordination safe against double-claims via idempotency, on three fronts. **(1) Stable instance identity:** `teatree.instance_id.instance_id()` returns a durable UUID persisted once in the machine data dir (`instance_id.machine_data_dir`, the non-isolated dir a worktree and the main clone both resolve), stable across restarts and distinct per machine; it is stamped into `ImplementedIssueMarker.claimed_by_instance` at claim time (the id a Stage 2 claim record names) and surfaced by `t3 info`. **(2) Pre-dispatch forge read-back:** before `ImplementedIssueMarker.claim`, `teatree.loop.scanners.forge_readback` reads the forge's open and merged PRs (reusing `list_my_prs`, asking for the UN-enriched hits since the read-back never looks at CI state) for an existing `<ticket_number>-*` branch or a referencing PR, scoped to the issue's repo, and skips the claim when found. The corpus is bucketed once per tick into a `ReadbackIndex` keyed by the numbers each PR cites, so a candidate reads only the PRs that could name it: re-deciding an already-decided issue was a scan of every PR, which cost the product of two growing sets and consumed the whole scan budget before the newest issues were reached ([#4466](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4466)). The scanner also stops itself at `issue_intake_pass_budget_seconds` (shipped 45.0, below the scan phase's 60s pool deadline, past which a scanner is ABANDONED rather than stopped) and records where it stopped in `IntakeScanCursor`. A resume point is left only by an INCOMPLETE pass, so the next tick continues at the frontier instead of dropping the same tail forever, while a finished pass still starts at the oldest candidate and the first freed slot goes to the longest-waiting issue. Three consecutive incomplete passes are a `t3 doctor check` FAILURE (`_check_intake_pass_incomplete`), not just a worker-log WARN — a **net, not a mutex**: it closes most of the cross-instance window the local ledger cannot see, but two instances can still both read clean in the same tick before either opens a PR. **(3) Outward idempotency keys from work-domain identity:** an outward-post idempotency key derived from a local autoincrement pk shifts between instances, defeating any future cross-instance dedup; the colleague-facing ledgers (`ScannedBroadcast` on `(channel, slack_ts)`, `ReviewRequestPost` on `mr_url`, `MRReviewLock` on `(slug, pr_id)`, `OutboundClaim`/`BotPing` on caller keys built from commit SHA / repo-slug) are already work-domain-derived; the two genuinely pk-derived keys — `DeferredQuestion`'s bot→user resurface/mirror keys — now derive from the stable `tool_use_id` (`DeferredQuestion.stable_notify_ref`, falling back to an instance-qualified pk).

**Fleet-safety Stage 2 — the durable claim-ref mutex.** Stage 1's read-back is a net; Stage 2 is the actual MUTEX, so N instances can never double-claim one work item. The primitive is a **git ref update as a server-side compare-and-swap**, enforced by the same forge that owns the work domain — no new database. A claim lives at `refs/teatree/claims/<slug>` (`teatree.core.fleet.claim`, Django-free): `acquire` creates it with a plain push — the receive-pack ref transaction rejects a create when the ref already exists, and a random nonce per claim commit makes an existing ref never a fast-forward of a rival's fresh commit, so exactly one create wins; `heartbeat`/`steal_if_expired` re-point it with `git push --force-with-lease=<ref>:<observed-sha>` (CAS against the observed value), so exactly one steal wins and a stealer that lost the race is rejected server-side. Every claim commit's only parent is the root of the default branch `origin` advertises (`ls-remote --symref origin HEAD`), read from the tip `origin` holds and never from a local tracking ref, which can name a history `origin` no longer holds, nor through the clone's replace refs (every claim git call runs with `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`, push hooks included); its tree is that root's tree, so the push adds nothing `origin` lacks beyond its record (the message). A clone lacking that tip fetches it into the claim's throwaway object dir, and a default branch `origin` does not advertise, or a tip that cannot be fetched, fails closed. The commit sha the ref points at is the CAS token, and it moves on every heartbeat as well as every steal. Each claim tenure carries a random `lineage` that `acquire` or `steal_if_expired` mints and every heartbeat writes forward. WHO holds the claim is the (`instance_id`, `lineage`) pair in the tip's claim record (`read_tip` → `ClaimTip.held_by`), so a steal always starts a new lineage, and an installation that shares this instance's id but not its ledger never writes this tenure's lineage; a copy of the ledger carries the lineage with it. `FleetClaimUnavailableError` distinguishes an unreachable forge from a normal contended loss (a local `commit-tree` failure, or an unreadable default-branch root, is classified the same way, per the module contract). The issue-implementer dispatch (`IssueIntakeScanner._claim`) acquires the mutex before work; the model manager keeps no dependency on coordination modules. The mutex governs the whole in-flight lifecycle, so exactly-once holds end to end (not just at claim time):

- **Claim.** The scanner wins the ref FIRST and the local `ImplementedIssueMarker` becomes a CACHE of it (`cache_from_fleet_claim` stamps the fencing sha onto `claim_ref_sha` and the claim's lineage onto `claim_lineage`, on a re-steal too). `resolve_claim_repo` pushes the claim only to a clone whose `origin` slug MATCHES the issue's `owner/repo` — a name-only/`T3_REPO` fallback that resolves the wrong remote fails SAFE (does not claim) rather than split the mutex across remotes.
- **Heartbeat.** A per-tick sweep (`heartbeat_inflight_claims`, on the issue-intake scanner — emitted even at full budget so it never stalls) re-affirms every in-flight claim (every marker outside `ImplementedIssueMarker.State.terminal()`, so a completed, abandoned or declined claim is left to lapse) with a `--force-with-lease` re-point at a cadence comfortably shorter than the TTL (`DEFAULT_TTL_SECONDS` = 4h vs the ~1h tick), so a claim can never expire and be stolen mid-dispatch. A failed CAS reads the tip back: a tip still on the marker's sha (the push was refused) raises `FleetClaimUnavailableError` and the beat retries next tick; a tip carrying this claim's (`instance_id`, `lineage`) whose sha the marker never recorded (a beat that landed but was never persisted) is ADOPTED and re-stamped, while a pre-lineage marker adopts nothing; any other tip, or a deleted ref, is a `ClaimLost`, which ABANDONS the marker so the in-flight work aborts rather than push under a lost claim.
- **Fence.** Every outward write is re-verified fail-closed via one shared `ticket_claim_loss` helper: the sync pre-ship gate (`run_fleet_claim_fence_gate`), the async `execute_ship` (immediately before BOTH the branch push AND the PR-open — the sync gate ran back at `pr create`, and the claim can be stolen in the async gap), and the orphan-branch PR-create (`_ensure_pr`). The write proceeds when the ref tip is still the sha the marker recorded, or when its claim record carries this instance's id and the marker's `claim_lineage`. Otherwise each refusal names its `ClaimLoss` cause: another instance holds the ref (`STOLEN`), the record names this instance's id without this tenure's lineage (`UNPROVEN`), the tip carries no readable claim record (`UNREADABLE`), the ref is gone (`MISSING`), or the ref infra is unreachable (`UNREACHABLE`, logged loudly). Each applies in every marker state, so a claim the heartbeat abandoned on a deleted ref stays refused.

- **Zero object footprint.** A claim op writes only its commit, plus any of `origin`'s default branch the clone lacks. Those objects, and the rival commit an expiry probe fetches to read its metadata, are routed into an EPHEMERAL object directory (`claim._ephemeral_odb`: `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` at a temp dir, with the clone's real object DB attached as the READ-ONLY alternate push/fetch negotiation needs) that is deleted when the op ends; both fetches name no destination refspec and pass `--no-write-fetch-head`, `--no-tags` and an empty `--refmap`, so whatever the clone's fetch configuration (a `tagOpt`, a mirror refspec) they leave the ref store untouched too. The remote ref is the claim's only durable home, so nothing local is needed once the push lands. The bug class this forecloses: an op writing into the shared clone leaves an unreachable loose object, nothing on the claim path triggers git's auto-gc, and at the defaults the footprint then grows by one object per in-flight claim per ~1h heartbeat tick, without bound, for as long as claims are in flight. Isolation rather than pruning is deliberate: there is no pass that could ever drop a live claim. Pinned by `tests/teatree_core/fleet/test_claim_objects.py` (footprint probe with a plant-the-old-write control, plus the fail-closed half proving a live claim is still enforceable and its metadata still readable after the cycle).

- **Credential and push gates.** Every claim git call (`ls-remote`, `fetch`, `push`) runs non-interactive with the routed forge credential `wire` passes as `extra_env` (`forge_push.forge_write_env`: `GH_TOKEN` or `GITLAB_TOKEN` by the remote's forge, the route `t3 push` uses), because the long-running roles carry no ambient env token. Claim commits are authored under a GitHub noreply identity, so the public-repo privacy gate (`scripts/hooks/refuse-public-push-with-leak.sh`), which runs on every claim push, passes them; a claim commit's patch is empty, so the gate scans only its record. The branch-scoped push gates run through `scripts/hooks/branch-push-only.sh`, which skips a push whose remote ref (`PRE_COMMIT_REMOTE_BRANCH`) is under `refs/teatree/claims/`; no claim push uses `--no-verify`. A refused push raises `FleetClaimUnavailableError` carrying git's stderr with the credential redacted. Every claim git call, the local `commit-tree` included, runs through one runner under a 300 s process-group deadline (`claim._GIT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, above the privacy gate's own 120 s remote listing), so a stuck hook or a stalled remote ends in `FleetClaimUnavailableError` with its hook processes killed rather than holding the intake tick. Intake has no local-claim fallback: an unavailable claim admits nothing and raises a CRITICAL health issue, since a local-only claim would reopen the cross-instance double-claim the mutex exists to close.

A limitation bounds what the mutex covers:

- **Claim-repo resolution is single-root and forge-host-blind.** `resolve_claim_repo` finds the clone by BARE repo name under one `clone_root()` (`find_clone_path` warns and takes the alphabetically-first match on a basename collision), then checks that clone's `origin` — but both sides of that check drop the forge host (`git.remote_slug` and `owner_repo_from_issue_url` both yield `owner/repo`). For an overlay spanning two forges: a same-basename, different-slug collision makes the guard refuse and the acquire fail safe, so that issue is **never claimed** and the operator sees `resolved clone … does not host … (origin mismatch)` repeat every tick with the issue never picked up; and the same `owner/repo` slug on two forges **passes** the guard against the wrong-forge clone, pushing the claim ref to the wrong remote and splitting the mutex across forges — the exact outcome the guard's contract claims to prevent. A strict host match is not the drop-in fix: an SSH host alias, a mirror hostname, or a `file://` origin each read as a different host than the issue URL's, turning working single-forge claims into fail-safe skips.

**Fleet-safety Stage 3 — forge-authoritative PR budget.** The same per-instance-SQLite blind spot that Stage 1's read-back nets on the CLAIM path applies on the PR-CREATE path: the per-(repo, ticket) budget gate (north-star PR-2, above) counted only local `PullRequest` rows + `extra["pr_url_by_branch"]`, so a sibling instance's just-opened PR was invisible until the local reconciler imported it — a one-tick window in which two instances could each open a PR for one ticket, breaking invariant I2 (at most one open PR per `(ticket, repo)`). Deterministic branch names + GitHub's one-PR-per-head-branch rule foreclose the identical-branch case, not two different branches for one ticket. Stage 3 makes `check_pr_budget` forge-authoritative by unioning a live `host.list_my_prs` read (`core/gates/pr_budget_forge`) with the local set at all three create seams (`_ensure_pr`, the ship fail-fast gate, and the ship chokepoint the loop's task-driven ship converges on) — repo-scoped, ticket-attributed by the shared `<slug>#<n>` close-keyword join, deduped by URL, and fail-open on any forge error (see the north-star PR-2 paragraph for the full mechanism). Like Stage 1's read-back it is a **net, not a mutex** — the durable claim-ref mutex remains Stage 2 — but it closes the PR-create half of the double-open window the local ledger cannot see. The shared close-keyword parser lives in `teatree.utils.close_keywords` because `teatree.core` (domain) may not import `teatree.loop` (orchestration).

**A disabled loop is never overdue ([#4066](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4066)).** The statusline's `overdue:` section judged lateness from `next_fire_at` alone, so a loop with no next-fire instant read as late — and a DISABLED loop has none BECAUSE nothing scheduled it, not because it slipped. `snapshot_warmer` and `triage_assessor` were therefore reported overdue permanently, with no state that could ever clear the alarm; an alarm that cannot clear is indistinguishable from no alarm, and it had already spent the banner a genuinely-late loop needs. The schedule row is now `MiniLoopSchedule(name, next_fire_at, cadence_seconds, enabled)` — a typed record rather than a tuple that could not express the difference — and `enabled` (the row's own `Loop.enabled`, what `t3 loop list` prints) gates the overdue predicate. It is deliberately NOT the same question as `admitted`: a base-disabled loop a preset forces on stays admitted and keeps its due-soon countdown, because the tick may fire it, but it is still nothing anyone is waiting for and so can never be reported late. "Enabled but never fired" remains genuinely overdue.

**Every board-walking scanner reads the board in bulk, not per ticket ([#4809](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4809)).** The dashboard's per-page pins have no counterpart on the tick, so a read that costs one query per ticket is paid at the tick rate across the whole backlog and shows up nowhere. Measured with `assertNumQueries` at two populations, two of them did: `StaleTicketsScanner` ran a `Max` aggregate per candidate ticket (1+N), and `reconcile_work_state_all` — the SELFCATCH-1 sweep, once per tick over every ticket — paid four per ticket, one of them a FULL scan of `Worktree` from the duplicate-scope probe. Both now resolve their repeated reads once for the sweep: the scanner groups both activity aggregates, and `WorkStateScope` carries the two workspace roots plus, for a sweep only, every `Worktree` row and the merge-audit evidence that `_done_but_unmerged_for_ticket` consults. Those two are what a sweep AMORTISES, so they are optional on the scope: `whole_board()` pre-reads them, `per_ticket()` leaves them unread and the accessors fall back to a read scoped to the asking ticket. Paid unconditionally they pessimise the opposite path — `reconcile_ticket` answers about ONE ticket and `reconcile_all` loops it, so `workspace doctor` would run one unfiltered `MergeAudit`-`MergeClear` join per ticket where a targeted `EXISTS` for done-claiming tickets suffices. `find_foreign_issue_worktrees` keeps its signature and delegates to a `foreign_issue_worktrees` that takes the rows already read, so the provisioning guard is untouched. `tests/test_hot_path_query_pins.py` pins each at two populations and asserts the path had work to do, because a count taken over an empty result proves nothing.

### 5.7 Self-Improving Monitor

Repairable incidents that reach the Slack or ticket rung open a repair ticket at first observation; the firing carries a repair-start marker. When ticket creation fails, the ticket is deferred and the firing is marked at the statusline rung; the later pulled status reads `Repair: ticket deferred`. A repair-of-repair kind is marked and held at the statusline rung to avoid recursive tickets. Resource-probe-inert kinds still alert immediately without a ticket. At the end of each cheap-tier cycle, a marked incident older than `self_improve_owner_wait_minutes` earns one pulled-status record only when a detector report confirms it remains open; covered-and-unreported firings resolve before the sweep. Quiet hours defer that record, and the firing generation gives retries a stable idempotency key. The cheap detector also records loop-health categories and recent open scanner faults as ticket-rung incidents; a failed loop-health read protects existing loop firings from false resolution. The answering brief can append `OverlayMetadata.work_item_return_lines()` for the ticket's overlay.

The cheap detector tier also runs unattended from a self-rescheduling `loops`-queue
maintenance chain (`loops/self_improve_cycle.py`). The worker seeds an immediate first scan on startup;
it re-arms before scanning and invokes the same lease-guarded `loop_self_improve`
command as an interactive `/loop`, so a missing Claude session cannot leave pressure,
task-failure, or unanswered-message incidents unobserved. A brake or a pressure ceiling
bounds AGENTS, never timers: the worker keeps its whole host-scaled `loops` control pool
(never below two) under every brake and ceiling, so with at most one live timer per loop a
tick that cannot finish holds one control executor, never the lane every other loop waits
in. Under a load, memory or swap brake it also keeps the `cheap` review, draining and owner-answering executors while
retiring the `default` coding pool; critical kernel memory pressure, token/quota and yield brakes
retain only the control pool;
a preset that admits zero loops stops all queues deliberately. On a four-core host,
the two-agent ceiling has one coding seat and one protected draining seat.
The admission stamp also checks the combined ceiling against claimed agents and recent
pending seats from both classes in one conditional write. A paced ceiling of one cannot
admit review beside coding, and review-first bursts cannot overfill a larger ceiling.
When an inherited all-coding fleet already holds a reserved seat, exactly one extra
review may enter to drain it; that exception is unavailable at a one-seat ceiling.
Setting the cheap-class ceiling to zero collapses its verdict onto coding but retains
the shared governor cap over pending seats.

Per-attempt skill assurance has its own OTel span and bounded local JSONL reader. It
correlates by ticket, task, and attempt IDs and records only the assurance status,
counts, and at most 16 allowlisted skill names per requested/missing set. Neither
skill bodies nor application evidence, transcript text, paths, or identities enter
the telemetry sink. The runner emits the span only after the attempt is saved;
the durable receipt records delivery, observed explicit loads, and the agent's
bounded declaration, but cannot prove that a skill guided the work. The local
stream follows the same seven-day retention and fixed-size tail limits as other
factory observations. A first missing or undelivered required skill opens a
deduplicated repair ticket; the owner is told only if it remains open after the
wait. Three unverified application receipts also open a repair ticket. None is
presented as proof of use.

A detector swarm that rides the worker's loop ticks. It watches for smells the rest of the loop cannot self-report — dispatcher silently skipping a phase, a `MergeClear` issued but never reconciled, a statusline entry whose evidence has gone stale — and converts each into a `SelfImproveFiring` row plus a graduated action (`log → statusline → slack → ticket → auto_fix`, monotonic ladder). It is the legibility substrate §§17.4–17.8 relies on. Auto-fix is whitelisted: today only `StaleStatuslineEntryDetector` carries `auto_fix = True`, and its self-heal is wired for real — the prior `_default_rerender` no-op is retired (#2625) in favour of the idle render seam `teatree.loop.phases.render.rerender_statusline`, which re-renders the zones file so a merged-PR / terminal-ticket URL drops out. Because the heal is idempotent and side-effect-free, the detector declares `max_rung = auto_fix` and the action ladder sends an `auto_fix` report straight to its ceiling on the FIRST observation — a graduated climb would be pointless (surfacing a stale statusline on the statusline it is about to re-render) and would in fact never reach `auto_fix`, since a persistent smell keeps the same `state_hash` and the cool-down would pin it at rung 1 forever (#2625 Part B). The detector stays in the domain layer and never reaches up into the orchestration render seam (tach-enforced): instead the orchestration entry point that drives the cheap self-improve tier — the dedicated `loop_self_improve` slot — injects `teatree.loop.phases.render.self_improve_rerender` as the ladder's `auto_fix_callable`; the schedule's `_detector_auto_fix` adapter (routing a detector's own `rerender`) is the fallback for a directly-constructed detector with no injected seam. This covers stale *entries*; whole-render *age* (a dead loop, when no tick runs at all) is caught instead by the read-path freshness banner since the detector cannot fire when the loop is not ticking. The shipped detector set is registered in `detectors/registry.py`; additional `auto_fix` slots land with their own structural whitelist test. **The cheap tier is the only tier with detectors.** `--tier medium|expensive` (the sets sketched but never built in the § 5.7 plan) and any unknown tier name are REFUSED: `detectors_for_tier` raises `UnimplementedTierError`, and both surfaces — the Typer `t3 loop self-improve run` and the `loop_self_improve` management command it delegates to — exit 2 with that message before the `loop-self-improve` lease is taken. An empty detector list would let the cycle report a clean success having scanned nothing; the loud refusal is the honest contract. `--tier all` is the union of implemented tiers (today: cheap).

The statusline summarizes active statusline-rung firings in one line, grouped by validated kind and cause with counts and the oldest active age; missing kinds use the detector and absent, invalid, or placeholder "unknown" causes say "cause unreported". It folds overflow as "+K more kinds" within the statusline hook's 200-column fallback width.

When a memory or swap pressure incident opens a repair ticket, its context captures the judged RAM scope, host and cgroup readings, cgroup memory split, and largest processes at firing time. The process scan runs before the ticket's database transaction; unreadable memory sources name their error class, an unavailable process table is labeled, and unreadable per-process metrics are skipped without blocking the ticket; a repeat firing keeps the first snapshot.

Sibling loop scanners (under `loop/scanners/`, not `SelfImprove` detectors) close the gaps the detectors only surface:

| Scanner | Closes | Contract |
|---|---|---|
| `PrSweepScanner` ([#1248](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1248), wired [#1257](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1257)) | forgotten / conflicted / un-reviewed merges | Invokes the §17.4 keystone merge for any open PR whose `MergeClear` is actionable, head SHA matches, and required checks are green (when no clearance matches the live head it first asks `merge.head_carry_over.carry_over_head` whether the records followed an unchanged patch to it — [head carry-over by patch identity](docs/blueprint/factory-architecture.md)) (`--fallback-uv-audit` escalation when the only red check is `uv-audit` and `main` is red too). A conflicted PR emits `pr_sweep.flag_conflict` ([#78](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/78)), flag only — never an auto-rebase; a solo bypass lacking a recorded cold-review emits `pr_sweep.flag_no_review` ([#68](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/68)) (below). A solo bypass whose live head carries an UNRECONCILED HOLD emits `pr_sweep.flag_held` and arms no further reviewer ([#4380](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4380)): `ReviewVerdict.objects.unreconciled_holds_at` finds every HOLD active at the reviewed head, which the merge keystone also honors: a later `merge_safe` at the same head answers it only when an independent, cited disposition covers every held point. Two reviewers running concurrently on one unchanged tree disagreed, and the autonomous no-CLEAR merge resolved the contradiction by timestamp in the direction of merging. `pr_sweep_decision.head_review_state` names the two shapes apart (on `HeadReview.hold_reason`) because they are different events to the owner they escalate to, selected by whether the one head read finds a non-stale `merge_safe` standing beside the hold: with one, `reason=contested_hold_at_head` — a disagreement between recorded verdicts, possibly under one identity because hold and pass have distinct idempotency keys; without one, `reason=hold_at_head` — the ordinary outcome of every cold review that holds, where reporting a disagreement would name a second reviewer who does not exist. That selector is deliberately `HeadReview.standing_merge_safe`, not `HeadReview.authorizing_verdict`: a standing hold leaves no authorizing verdict, while a pass beside it still explains the disagreement. Both refuse, both escalate, and the flag carries the `(verdict id, reviewer)` refs so the DM names who stands where. A MERGED attempt names the unheld `HeadReview.authorizing_verdict` instead — what the merge actually rested on — so a no-CLEAR merge records WHICH review authorised it rather than only that no CLEAR existed. Both the no-CLEAR bypass and keystone path refuse a standing hold. A CLEAR can consume a same-head disposition, but cannot itself answer a hold; otherwise push a fix and get the new head reviewed. A PR with any failing REQUIRED check whose branch is BEHIND `main` judged a base it has since fallen behind, so its verdict is UNKNOWN rather than red — a `gh run rerun` re-tests the run's pinned OLD base, so a fix that landed on `main` is unreachable to it. BEHIND-ness is a property of two commits and is read as one — `loop/scanners/pr_behind_base` aliases the whole open-PR set into ONE GraphQL `Ref.compare(headRef:).behindBy` call per base ref ([#4526](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4526)), where the REST `compare` endpoint costs a ~75 KB response per PR. It is NOT read off `mergeStateStatus`, which carries a single highest-precedence blocker rather than a set of flags: a PR that is behind AND red reports `BLOCKED`, behind AND conflicted reports `DIRTY`, and — absent the "require branches to be up to date" protection — a behind PR reports `CLEAN`, so `BEHIND` appears only when nothing else is wrong, which is mutually exclusive with the red this remedy repairs. That predicate was structurally unreachable and `BranchUpdateAttempt` had never recorded a single row. A read that fails degrades to the old `mergeStateStatus` signal with a WARNING naming the PRs — a strict subset of the truth, never a silent "nothing is behind" — and a CONFLICTED branch is refused by `red_required_at_stale_base` itself, since a conflict needs human resolution rather than a merge-update. The sweep APPLIES the remedy ([#4063](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4063), generalising [#2045](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2045)'s repo-state-only rule): the SHA-bound `PUT .../update-branch` merges the base in, emitting `pr_sweep.branch_updated`. Four bounds keep a broken PR from update-looping — one attempt per head (the `BranchUpdateAttempt` ledger, claimed BEFORE the call), a per-tick cap, the operator's own PRs only, and one update per patch (a head the sweep's own update produced from a head with the same patch is judged by its red, not re-updated; the `HeadCarryOver` ledger records it). Every refusal degrades to the flag-only `pr_sweep.needs_branch_update`. On a COLLABORATIVE overlay a self-authored PR that is green, not-draft, not-conflicted, and not-behind but has no actionable `MergeClear` emits `pr_sweep.flag_mergeable` (`reason=mergeable_awaiting_review`): the sweep cannot auto-merge (colleague review is the gate, and sending the review request is the followup flow's `review_request_send`, never the sweep's), so it DMs the user the MR link + "mergeable, ready to request review" — exactly once per head via the `MergeableNotified` ledger, re-firing only on a new commit. Notify-only: the sweep never requests review and never merges. **One question about the SET, not about any one PR ([#4090](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4090)).** Every rung above decides per PR, so a mutually-blocking red set — each PR red only because another's unmerged fix is missing — is invisible by construction: no re-run, no waiting and no merge ordering clears it, and the sweep correctly merges nothing while logging a sound per-PR skip forever. The sweep therefore carries the CI facts it already computed (`failing_required`, `base_current`) out to every emitted signal, and `loop/red_set_surface` folds them in the tick's `act` phase — one PAGINATED `main` check-runs read per repo (`--paginate --slurp`, `per_page=100`; the unpaginated read saw only the first 30 of main's 40+ runs, so which required contexts it reported depended on API ordering), no second PR listing and no second classifier — into the pure `loop/red_set_report` verdict: `main-red` (main fails a check the set fails, so the whole board inherits it), `shared-cause` (the failing sets overlap — one cause, one fix), `disjoint-reds` (no failing check in common, every run judged the CURRENT base), else `independent`. `disjoint-reds` asserts NOTHING about dependency: a mutual block and wholly unrelated failures reach that rung through identical records, so the note states the observation and names both readings without picking one — telling them apart needs evidence (a "this PR fixes check X" signal, failing-job-log content, or the merge-and-re-run experiment) no surface records, which is also why `independent` gains no new rung. Both indeterminate rungs refuse to read an inherited red as a property of the set: a red judged at a stale base is UNKNOWN, and an unreadable `main` is never assumed green. `main-red` over two or more PRs is the one verdict the data EARNS, so it alone is DM'd — keyed on the set's own signature, whose main term is the INHERITED set (a fixed red set took 4 distinct signatures across 8 main commits on the unscoped set, 1 on this one), so a permanently stalled board is announced once and an incidental main flip is not a new claim. Every other verdict, and a lone PR inheriting main's red, is log-only. Report only: no mutation, no merge decision. |
| `SlackBroadcastsScanner` ([#1131](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1131), wired [#1255](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1255)) | inbound review-request | Polls the review channel for MR-link broadcasts → `slack.review_intent` dispatch without an explicit reaction. |
| `SelfUpdateScanner` ([#1249](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1249)) | editable-install drift | Ff-only updates the editable teatree clone (`T3_REPO`) + every overlay clone on the hourly `housekeeping` `Loop` row, whose cadence is the only clock; `SelfUpdateMarker` records each clone's outcome. A clone that ADVANCES then reconciles the schema (`loop/scanners/self_update_schema.reconcile_schema_after_pull`, [#3901](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3901)): pending control-DB migrations are applied in place, and a migrate that fails — or a schema the probe cannot verify — pages the owner and emits `self_update.schema_behind` (exempted from the `self_update.` statusline drop, so the outage renders in `action_needed`) rather than resuming. A tick on which nothing advanced re-attempts an unresolved reconcile, so the park self-heals instead of waiting on a human. |
| `PullMainCloneScanner` | stale work-repo main clones | Same ff-only contract for the `$T3_WORKSPACE_DIR` main clones a worktree is created from, so a clone parked behind never poisons `git show` / `grep`; `pull_main_clone_cadence_hours` (1h), `PullMainCloneMarker`. |
| `ClaudeSelfPrReviewScanner` ([#1254](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1254), [#3569](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3569)) | self-review vigilance gap | Reviews the owner's OWN open PRs through the SAME `reviewing` → `t3:reviewer` (Claude) gate colleague PRs get — emits `self_pr_review.dispatch` → `_handle_reviewer`'s self-PR branch, which claims one per-SHA `CodexReviewMarker` (keyed `(slug, pr_id, head_sha)`, "since last review") before creating the `reviewing` Task. That claim expires ([#3921](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3921)), so a self-PR review whose task died is re-dispatched on a later tick instead of leaving the head un-reviewable until a force-push. Codex is RETIRED as the self-review mechanism (#3569): `t3:reviewer` is the sole gate for both self and colleague PRs; the codex agent/CLI/marker stay in the tree but are no longer wired into the review loop. Lives in the single `review` intake (`Domain.REVIEW`, alongside `ReviewerPrsScanner`), NOT a separate loop — self PRs are ALWAYS admitted, colleague PRs gated upstream by `admit_colleague_prs_to_board`. So a codex-less box reviews its own PRs out of the box instead of failing every dispatch. Dispatched once per SETTLED head, not once per push: a head whose branch-protection-REQUIRED checks are pending or red is one the author is about to replace, so `head_checks_unsettled` (the same `CodeHostQuery.required_checks_status` verdict the §17.4 keystone and the PR sweep read) defers it and the adapter stamps the answer onto `PrSummary` so neither self-PR scanner re-probes. A DEFERRAL, never a skip — the head that merges always carries a review, because the merge gate demands a `merge_safe` verdict bound to the live head — and it fails OPEN: an unreadable rollup dispatches the review. Before it, a PR recorded ~10 cold reviews per merge (976 verdicts / 97 merged PRs over 29 days), one per intermediate push. |
| `ResourcePressureScanner` ([#128](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/128)) | host OOM / full disk | Measures disk and RAM headroom, reports warn/critical pressure, and runs the safe reclamation ladder: configured caches, unreferenced Docker storage, rebuildable artifacts, completed-ticket worktrees, and idle stacks. The heuristic worktree GC and renderer process kill paths are removed; unreadable probes emit an inert signal. A reclaim that keeps returning nothing is itself reported: `ResourcePressureMarker.zero_yield_passes` counts consecutive empty passes below the critical floor and three raise the `reclaim-stalled:disk` CRITICAL health signal, keyed on the live free-space reading so it clears when the disk recovers — 23 such passes once reached 100% full behind a green chip. |
| `IntakeConcurrencyScanner` ([#3992](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3992)) | intake concurrency chosen once, in advance | The resource loop's second job — sets the issue-intake in-flight limit from cgroup-aware observed headroom instead of a hand-set constant, so it neither wastes an idle box nor over-admits when several agents run full suites. Target is the SMALLER of two capacity estimates — memory's `factory_in_flight + floor((available_gb - intake_ram_reserve_gb) / intake_ram_per_agent_gb)` and the whole-box `floor(hard_cap * box_load_headroom)` ([#4407](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4407), so load the factory did not start still lowers the number) — clamped to a core-derived hard cap: the reserve makes the number fall BEFORE the box reaches its memory ceiling, and the ramp is asymmetric (a drop adopted whole, a rise one step per measurement window) so it errs toward the cheaper mistake. Persisted on `ResourcePressureMarker` and read by `resolve_intake_concurrency`, which falls back to the static setting on an absent, stale, or unreadable answer; the adaptive path is always enabled. |
| `ArtifactEvictionScanner` ([#4244](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4244)) | rebuildable artifacts accumulate across every checkout ever worked | The resource loop's THIRD job, and the one deliberately NOT gated on the pressure bands: reclaiming a `.venv` / `.venv-hook*` / `node_modules` / `.nx` / `.angular` / `mutants` loses nothing at any fullness, so a threshold could only ever delay it (`mutants` — mutmut's output — only when git lists it as one ignored, untracked directory, since the plain name may be tracked source). Emits `resource.artifacts_reclaimable` once per `cadence_minutes` → `mechanical.sweep_artifacts` → `core/cleanup/artifact_eviction`. The same pass is the factory's unattended housekeeping caller ([#4923](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4923)): after eviction it runs `TeardownDispatch.drain_terminal_backlog()` (terminal tickets still holding worktrees get a deduplicated teardown job) and `core/cleanup/isolated_roots.reap_orphan_isolated_worktree_roots` under a 60 s walk deadline, each best-effort and recorded in `last_artifact_plan`. Hanging it off `resource.cleanup_needed` instead would drag the destructive ladder along and inherit its anti-thrash debounce, which silently downgrades a throttled CRITICAL to a WARN that frees nothing — so the pass stamps `last_artifact_sweep_at` (its own cadence field, and the mechanical action is its only writer) rather than `last_freed_at`, and persists to its own `last_artifact_plan` rather than the ladder's `last_plan`. It runs UNCONDITIONALLY — there is no enable flag, and that is the design rather than an omission: a candidate is deleted only once it is PROVED reconstructible, and a switch in front of a proof adds no safety while an off-by-default one guarantees the reclaim never happens. Four conditions must ALL hold and are re-established immediately before each deletion: the checkout still carries the rebuild inputs the documented recovery needs (`_REBUILD_INPUTS`), the checkout and artifact still have the identities recorded at planning, no live process holds or imports from the checkout or artifact (`_in_use_reason`, fail-CLOSED when any PID's held paths or executable cannot be inspected), and no artifact symlink in the current checkout population resolves at it (`_shared_target_reason`). The current population is enumerated again for every candidate; `.git` files count as linked worktrees only when their administrative directory carries `commondir`, so nested submodules remain under the parent checkout's liveness guard, and any registry or symlink-resolution gap refuses the plan or stops the batch because a hidden link cannot be ruled out. Destruction opens the verified checkout and artifact without following symlinks, empties the artifact relative to those file descriptors, and rechecks the root directory identity before removing it, so a path replacement between authorisation and deletion is retained. Anything unprovable is KEPT and named in the plan: a kept artifact costs disk, a wrong deletion costs work, and the asymmetry is the whole design. `t3 <overlay> retention artifacts` is the same pass with no deletes (dry-run unless `--apply`) — an operator inspection surface that gates nothing, where every symlinked artifact, shared target and unprovable candidate reads under KEEP, and a part-way-failed removal reads as STOPPED rather than as an untouched keep. A mid-batch refusal reports the artifacts already evicted and marks every untouched remainder STOPPED. |
| `IdleStackReaperScanner` ([#2190](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2190), [#2227](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2227)) | idle stack holding a slot + RAM | Emits `local_stack.reap_idle` for each idle running worktree (no live session/task, `last_used_at` past `idle_stack_idle_minutes`, not the CWD, no live external-delivery lease, no E2E run within `idle_stack_e2e_recent_minutes`, not `reaper_pinned`; fail-safe ⇒ KEEP). Reads `classify_running_worktrees`/`preserve_reason` and LOGS preserved-vs-reaped so a reap is never silent (#2227). The `reap_idle_stack` handler re-verifies live then `stop_services` (reversible demote to `provisioned`, whole compose project down). The scanner's own 5-minute debounce via `LocalStackReaperMarker`; the active preset admits or refuses the loop; mechanical-only, never an agent. |
| `LocalStackQueueDrainerScanner` ([#2190](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2190), [#44](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/44)) | queued stack acquisition | Emits `local_stack.queue_acquire` for each due `LocalStackQueueItem`; the `drain_stack_queue_item` handler re-checks the cap and re-fires `start` (slot freed) or reschedules a Fibonacci-minute backoff (`fibonacci_minutes`, capped at `MAX_QUEUE_ATTEMPTS` = 13). Never tears down another ticket's stack; per-item backoff IS the cadence (no marker); the active preset admits or refuses the loop; mechanical-only. |
| `TaskSweepScanner` ([#129](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/129)) | open teatree `Task` rows (not the harness TODO list) | Verifies each open `Task`'s artifact via `is_issue_done`; terminal → `task.completion_detected` re-checks live (fail-CLOSED) before `Task.complete`; unverifiable → `task.orphaned` (fail-OPEN). Per-item, idempotent via `Task.last_sweep_check_ts`; the task sweep runs whenever its Loop row is admitted. The TODO-vs-task glossary it follows is canonical in `skills/checking/SKILL.md`. |
| `IssueDispositionScanner` ([#2122](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2122)) | `needs-triage` dead noise | Runs unconditionally over the configured repository scope. It lists `needs-triage` open issues and auto-closes ONLY high-confidence DEAD ones via three deterministic buckets — `already_shipped` (a delivered ticket exists, falsified by a live in-flight ticket), `exact_duplicate` (title fingerprint matches another open issue, falsified by a unique fingerprint), `obsolete` (every body-referenced path is gone, falsified by one still-existing path). Conservative bar: ANY uncertainty emits nothing. Emits `issue_disposition.close_candidate` → the idempotent `close_dead_issue` mechanical handler closes with an audit-trail comment; bounded by `IssueDispositionScanner.max_closes_per_tick` (5). It can CLOSE noise but is physically unable to enqueue work — no `Task`, no claim, never an agent route — so the burn-down doctrine (done-signal = open-bug count, never an empty queue) is structurally preserved. |
| `SnapshotWarmerScanner` ([#2949](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2949)) | reference-DB DSLR snapshot rot | Checks every overlay-declared `DjangoDbImportConfig` (`overlay.provisioning.snapshot_warmer_configs`, default empty) for staleness — a DSLR snapshot's own `YYYYMMDD_<tenant>`-embedded date doubles as its "last refreshed" timestamp, no separate marker. A stale/missing snapshot emits `snapshot_warmer.refresh_needed`; the `refresh_snapshot` mechanical handler restores the newest snapshot, migrates, and re-snapshots only when migrations were applied (`teatree.utils.django_db.snapshot_warmer.refresh_reference_snapshot`) — absorbing the slow restore+migrate path out-of-band so a ticket-critical-path provision never pays it. Daily cadence (its own mini-loop, `snapshot_warmer_max_age_days`); the active preset admits or refuses the loop; mechanical-only. |
| `DbBackupScanner` (directive #2) | teatree's own control DB is unbacked-up | The `[loops.db_backup]` row IS the cadence — a 02:00 `daily_at` anchor. The newest artifact's OWN embedded `db-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.sqlite3` timestamp (`teatree.utils.django_db.backup.hours_since_last_backup`) only NAMES the trigger: no prior backup ⇒ `bootstrap`, otherwise `cadence`. It is not a second clock — a daily row chained to a 24h-since-last-backup gate pushed the backup later every day. A due backup emits `db_backup.due`; the `run_db_backup` mechanical handler drives the shared engine (`teatree.utils.django_db.backup.run_backup`) — a consistent `sqlite_snapshot` of the LIVE control DB into `<data_dir>/backups/` plus a keep-last-N-days prune, off the tick. A snapshot failing `PRAGMA quick_check` is refused before the prune runs; index-level inconsistencies that `quick_check` does not detect can still pass and be pruned normally. Backs up the control DB (resolved from the live Django connection), so unlike the snapshot warmer it carries no overlay anchor. Daily cadence lives in the row alone; `db_backup_retention_days` is the only setting; the active preset admits or refuses the loop; mechanical-only. The `db_backup` management command drives the same engine for a manual run. |
| `RatchetStalenessScanner` ([#4451](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4451)) | a reference ratchet left loose on the core clone | Reads the editable teatree clone's own `known_unresolved_refs.yaml` against its own source and reports pins the tree no longer resolves, naming `t3 tool ratchet-prune --write`. Detection is from the TREE, not from CI, so the answer needs no forge round-trip and arrives without waiting for a pipeline. READ-ONLY by construction: writing to the clone would leave it dirty and make `PullMainCloneScanner` skip its fast-forward, trading one stale artifact for another. Observe-only like `CiEvalHealScanner` — opening the repair PR is the sibling half that loop keeps behind its own dark flag, and is deliberately not built. 30m cadence (its own mini-loop); admitted by `present`, `afk` and `token-outage` (it calls no model), masked by `maintenance` and `off`. |
| `MemorySkimScanner` (directive 32) | factory knowledge accumulating in personal Claude memory | Runs `teatree.memory_audit.scan_all` weekly — the classifier behind `t3 tool audit-memory`, which nothing called on a cadence — and records ONE `DeferredQuestion` per ISO week naming every memory that reads as factory behaviour, asking which to promote into the repo and which to drop. The promote step is the owner's call by the directive's own terms, so the scanner asks rather than editing memories or skills; the question rides the ordinary ask-gate, so the backlog nag resurfaces it until answered. Dedupe is the ISO-week marker over EVERY row, not just pending ones, so an answered week is never re-asked. Weekly cadence (its own mini-loop, `cadence_is_floor`); ships `enabled=False` and masked off by the `low-token`/`off` presets. |

A stale-base PR with a claimed coding or debugging task on its owning ticket keeps the `needs_branch_update` signal while that task owns the branch; the sweep performs its one-per-head update only after the task leaves CLAIMED.

`PrSweepScanner`'s auto-merge dispatch is gated on the fleet doctrine (`mode = "auto"` + `require_human_approval_to_merge = false`); every other overlay stays manual. Self-PR REVIEW is not fleet-gated — it runs through the ordinary `review` intake like colleague review. All scanner knobs are per-overlay overridable.

The monitor never auto-merges substrate unless a standing owner opt-in authorizes it (the default-off `substrate_self_signoff` at `autonomy = full`, or the `substrate_auto_merge_authorized_by` delegation). ONE policy decision — `merge.authorization.substrate_standing_authorization` — governs BOTH merge paths, the keystone CLEAR path and the solo-overlay sweep bypass ([#3648](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3648)), so the two can never disagree about the same PR under the same config; the substrate hold is a blast-radius sign-off, so lifting it never touches the cold-review floor below. The monitor never auto-edits memory / skills / `BLUEPRINT.md`, and never bypasses the §17.4 `MergeClear` reviewer-attestation **except** on a solo overlay the user declared end-to-end-trusted (`mode = "auto"` + `require_human_approval_to_merge = false`) — where the per-diff CLEAR cannot be issued because maker and reviewer are the same human identity, so an unrelaxed gate would silently no-op every green PR ([#1309](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1309)). The carve-out is *minimal*: every precondition gate (draft, changes-requested, CI, uv-audit escalation) stays in force, the per-diff CLEAR is replaced by the SHA-bound `PrApiClient.merge_pr_bound` fallback (#1985, re-checking live SHA-bind/draft/CI), and the **cold-review floor still holds** — the bypass requires a recorded INDEPENDENT `merge_safe` `ReviewVerdict` at the live head (`reviewer != maker`) ([#68](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/68)). So does the **ownership floor**: every ticket-scoped merge gate (critic verdict, rubric, anti-vacuity) resolves its ticket through `resolve_gated_ticket` (ledger row, else CLEAR) and no-ops without one, so the sweep refuses an own PR that resolves none (`no_owning_ticket`, one owner DM per PR) instead of merging it ungated; `manual_pr_reconcile` heals the missing row from the head branch's live worktree (`branch_owner.ticket_owning_pr_branch`), and a foreign or bot PR, which has no ticket by design, is untouched. Non-opted-in overlays keep the CLEAR requirement.

**Auto-review dispatch closes the no-review loop ([#68](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/68)).** On `flag_no_review` for a green+clean own PR the scanner enqueues ONE claimable `Task(phase=reviewing)` (gated on `auto_review_dispatch = solo_overlay and not require_human_approval_to_merge`); the reviewer's recorded `merge_safe` verdict is the artifact the sweep merges on. Because a reviewing-phase headless dispatch is denied the shell (PR-11), the reviewer cannot run `review record` — it **RETURNS a typed `review_verdict` in its result envelope**, and the orchestrator (`attempt_recorder.record_result_envelope` — a DIFFERENT actor, so maker≠checker holds by construction) records the `ReviewVerdict` server-side from it, resolving the per-MR `MRReviewLock` ([#1405](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1405)) so the chain never stalls with the lock held; the interactive/CLI path still uses `review record`, whose `slug` positional must be the repo slug `owner/repo` — the command refuses a branch-shaped slug at the CLI boundary (`_looks_like_owner_repo`), because `ReviewVerdict.objects.latest_for_pr` and the sweep trigger key by the PR-URL-derived repo slug, so a verdict recorded under a branch name is unfindable by every consumer. The merge is **event-driven, not cadence-bound** ([#2026](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2026)): the CLI `review record` calls `trigger_sweep_for_verdict` the moment a `merge_safe` verdict lands, rebuilding the overlay sweep scanner and running its single-PR `evaluate_one` — the same decision ladder `scan` runs, so the two paths cannot drift; the periodic sweep is the backstop that merges the server-recorded verdict on the headless path. `AutoReviewDispatch`, its `CriticDispatch` twin and the `CodexReviewMarker` that dedups the codex / self-PR path are expiry-acquirable claims, not permanent ones ([#3920](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3920), [#3921](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3921)): each carries a `state`, a `deadline` and a bounded `attempts` count, and shares `MRReviewLock`'s compare-and-set shape through the one `core/modelkit/expiring_claim.acquirable_q` predicate — so "when may a review be dispatched?" has one answer across the four claim tables rather than four. A dispatched review that ends without recording a verdict expires and the next sweep re-arms that head, instead of the claim dedup'ing the PR out of ever being reviewed again (the pre-#3920 behaviour, where only a force-push escaped). A recorded `ReviewVerdict` is the terminal for both per-head claims — whichever path armed the review, the other's `mark_resolved` is a no-op; exhausting `MAX_DISPATCH_ATTEMPTS` leaves the claim saturated, which `t3 doctor check` surfaces through the reconciliation ledger rather than failing silently. A spent retry budget gets a NAMED cause rather than a bare count ([#4522](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4522), [#4530](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4530)). When a reviewer returns `merge_safe` over required checks it reports RED, `ReviewVerdict.record` refuses it (§17.8 clause 3) and that refusal is right, but nothing lands — so the head keeps no verdict and the claim is re-acquired once `DEFAULT_DISPATCH_TTL` lapses. That burn was always **bounded** by the `MAX_DISPATCH_ATTEMPTS` above, and the retries are **not** waste: `gh_verify_result` is the reviewer's own self-report, so a refusal decided on it alone describes that reviewer, not the tree. `record` therefore verifies it against a LIVE workflow-run read at the reviewed SHA ([#4554](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4554)) — `actions/runs?head_sha=`, not the `check-runs` tally, which has served false `0 pending` reads here — supplied as an injected, lazily-invoked probe (`core/models/checks_admission.py`) so the read fires only on the FAILED branch, before the recording transaction, and no model owns a network call. It has THREE outcomes, and the third is not folded into either neighbour: a live-confirmed RED raises `ChecksContradictionError` (terminal-eligible); a live GREEN/PENDING raises a plain `ReviewVerdictError` (the envelope misreported itself — a defect in one run); and a read that could not establish the state raises a plain one too, because latching an UNVERIFIED report resurrects the false refusal and admitting it would vouch for a tree nobody could vote on. A caller supplying no probe reads unreadable, so the refusal never weakens — only its terminal does. Measured on this deploy, 6 of the 9 heads that ever hit the refusal recorded a verdict at that SAME head afterwards, three of them a `hold` over checks that genuinely were red — the recordable outcome `VERDICT_CHECKS_RULE` asks for. A red head does not make a verdict impossible; it makes `merge_safe` impossible. So `mark_refused` fires ONLY at `MAX_DISPATCH_ATTEMPTS`, where the budget is gone either way and the latch costs nothing: it renames a claim that would otherwise stop `saturated` — "three attempts ran out", indistinguishable from three crashed reviewers — to `refused`, which says the last reviewer's `merge_safe` stood over checks the live read confirms were red, and records ONE `DeferredQuestion` deduped per claimed head. The latch and the page key the head the claim was ARMED at (`ReviewTarget.claim_head_sha`), never the live head the verdict bound to when the branch advanced mid-review — the verdict and its live checks read belong to the new tree, the spent claim to the old one. On the ticket path that claim head is the reviewer ticket's `extra["reviewed_sha"]`, and one ticket serves every head its PR is re-armed at, so `Ticket.rearm_review_at` restamps it once the new head's claim is taken and drops the old head's `last_review_state` and `discharged_sha` in the same write — a review of one tree never reads as covering the next ([#959](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/959)). The row leaves the doctor's saturation ledger as it gains that page, so exactly one surface owns it. Two scopes govern which claims a terminal may touch, and they are not the same: a recorded `ReviewVerdict` is a fact about the **tree**, so `resolve_head_claims` retires every per-head claim on it (`review_verdict._PER_HEAD_REVIEW_CLAIMS`); a refusal is a fact about one **run**, so it retires only the claim that armed that run (`ReviewTarget.armed_by`) and frees no `MRReviewLock` on either path. Reaching across was not a corner case — 328 of 444 `AutoReviewDispatch` rows share a head with a `CodexReviewMarker` row — and it let a codex-path refusal consume a #68 claim whose reviewer had not run and release the review lock it held, which is the #1405 race the lock exists to prevent. Both tables still share the one `expiring_claim.retire_head_claim` write, the write half of the `acquirable_q` predicate they already share. The safety valve is the per-head key: a push mints a new `head_sha`, which has no claim, so it arms a fresh review normally and the latch binds one tree rather than the pull request. The discrimination is the EXCEPTION TYPE (`ChecksContradictionError`), never the message text: every other `ReviewVerdictError` — an unknown choice value, a maker identity, an out-of-scope finding — names something the next reviewer could get right, so it keeps the ordinary retry. Reviewer briefs carry the recordable shape (`modelkit.review_contract.VERDICT_CHECKS_RULE`: red checks are a `hold` carrying the CI finding) so the contradiction is cut where it is written, not only caught after. Dedup/contract mechanics live in `AutoReviewDispatch`'s docstring. A dispatch PINS the head it arms, and the factory pushes to its own PRs while the review runs — so the recorder measures a returned `reviewed_sha` against BOTH the pinned head and the PR's LIVE head, records at whichever it names, and restamps whichever key `review_target_for_task` re-reads on that path — the claim's `recorded_head_sha`, or the reviewer ticket's `extra["reviewed_sha"]` on the 1454 of 2152 verdict-review tasks that hold no claim row at all — so the writer's key and the landed-work guard's key stay one fact ([#4737](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4737)). Stamping only the claim left the majority path recording a row at the live head while the resolver still re-read the pinned one, which is a verdict the system's own lookup cannot find. A SHA that is neither head is still refused. When the branch moved past both, any claim goes `superseded` (releasing the per-MR lock, unlike `refused`) so the sweep re-arms at the new head, and the `head_superseded` failure kind — read off the row's own error, never off a claim only a quarter of these tasks have — parks the row out of the requeue sweep and keeps that legitimate push out of both stall detectors; previously two of them parked the phase and the PR stopped being re-dispatched at all.

**Delivery-ownership predicate stops double-scheduling externally-delivered units ([#2104](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2104), [#2217](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2217)).** A unit delivered out-of-band by a hand-dispatched delivery agent (per `/t3:wip`) is implemented directly with no planning phase and no loop-armed reviewer, so the loop must not re-derive that work each tick. `external_delivery.under_external_delivery(ticket)` is true while a TTL'd `external_delivery` lease on `Ticket.extra` is live; it is claimed at the `workspace ticket` entry the delivery agent runs (never by the loop's own FSM) and refreshed on each external-owner seam (`ticket plan`/`transition`), so a hand delivery cannot lapse mid-flight while a crashed owner still self-reaps on the 1h TTL. The lease is consulted at **three** chokepoints: `execute_provision` skips `schedule_planning`; the `pr_sweep` review-arm no-ops (resolving the PR back to its author/delivery ticket via `resolve_author_ticket`, never by `issue_url == pr_url`); and the global dispatch chokepoint `orchestrate._dispatchable_filter` excludes live-lease tickets at the DB layer via `not_under_external_delivery_q` (held in lockstep with the Python predicate by a parity test). Without the third, hand-advancing WORK_STARTED → PLAN_RECORDED made any non-planning phase dispatchable and a second coder was claimed (the #2217 incident).
