**Filesystem-evidence double-dispatch guard ([#2217](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2217)).** The lease above only protects a unit the DB knows about; the #2217 incident produced a worktree on disk with **no `Ticket` row**. `worktree_collision.find_foreign_issue_worktrees` keys on the issue number instead — the dir for issue `N` is deterministically `<N>-<slug>`, so globbing `<N>-*` finds any existing worktree regardless of DB state. The single chokepoint is `workspace ticket`: inside the same `atomic()` that creates the ticket, a foreign `<N>-*` dir raises `ForeignIssueWorktreeRefusedError`, rolling the ticket back so a refusal leaves zero DB trace; the CLI prints the colliding path with a `--take-over` opt-out (the ticket's own dir is excluded, re-provision is idempotent). It is the filesystem-keyed complement to the DB lease.

**Adopt outside-originated work ([#2275](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2275)).** `workspace ticket --adopt` / `--adopt-branch <branch>` registers a Ticket + Worktree against an EXISTING branch instead of deriving `<number>-<slug>` — for work that originated outside the derive-a-branch flow. Run from inside the checkout, the branch auto-detects (`resolve_adopt_context` reads the branch, the worktree root, and the repo slug from git; `--adopt-branch` names it explicitly, a detached HEAD is refused). The provisioner records the existing `worktree_path` verbatim (`Ticket.extra['adopt']` maps repo → path, the backing clone derived from the checkout's own shared git dir when it lives outside the clone root) instead of `git worktree add`, so no second worktree dir is created; the `create_branch=False` fallback still covers checking a pre-existing branch out into a fresh dir. The foreign-dir guard is skipped like `--take-over` (adopt is an explicit "use this checkout" intent).

**Adopt an existing checkout — `worktree adopt`.** The sibling of `workspace ticket --adopt` for a checkout that already exists with no `Worktree` row: cut by a bare `git worktree add`, by another session, or left behind when its row was reaped. Registration was otherwise reachable only as a SIDE EFFECT of resolving something else (`intake.resolve._auto_register_from_git`), so a checkout that chain could not classify had no verb at all and every operation on it refused — `provision` being the only creating verb, and it runs the tenant-DB provisioning an overlay may forbid for unit-test work. `t3 <overlay> worktree adopt <path> [--ticket N]` records the row and nothing else: no provisioning, no DSLR, no database work. Repo and branch come from the checkout (`provision.worktree_adopt`, the same guarded seam `pr create --adopt-worktree` uses), and a path any row already records is refused BEFORE attribution runs, so a refusal can never leave a stray `auto:<branch>` ticket behind. `worktree.checkout_gitdir` classifies a checkout from the filesystem alone, which is what separates "not a checkout" from a linked worktree whose `gitdir:` pointer names a path THIS venue cannot see — a host-cut tree whose clone the container never mounts. Every git query about it fails identically, so the two were indistinguishable and the second was refused by the wrong cause; it is now `UnreachableGitdirError` (a `WorktreeAdoptError` subclass, so existing handlers are unchanged) naming the pointer, because the fix is to mount that clone rather than to stand somewhere else. The resolver's terminal `WorktreeNotFoundError` names the same cause and the reachable verb (`intake.worktree_refusal`) instead of telling an operator standing inside a worktree to go stand inside a worktree.

**Lightweight trivial-work plan-skip carve-out.** Some AUTHOR tickets are trivial mechanical edits (a typo, a one-line constant) where planning is pure overhead. `trivial_plan_skip.mark_trivial_plan_skip(ticket, reason=…)` stamps a durable marker (no TTL) carrying a MANDATORY reason (a malformed/empty marker is treated as absent, fail-safe to "planning required"). Consumed at three seams — `execute_provision` skips `schedule_planning`, `check_plan_artifact` accepts the marker so the ticket advances WORK_STARTED → PLAN_RECORDED with no `PlanArtifact`, and `plan_decision.has_plan_decision` accepts it so the implementing dispatch — and the fresh implementing mint — proceeds ([#4409](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4409)). Recorded via `t3 <overlay> ticket skip-planning <id> --reason`. The lightweight sibling of the heavyweight `plan-bypass` (`--human-authorize`, which fabricates a `PlanArtifact`); both stay intact and scoped to a ticket that explicitly carries the marker.

**Closed-issue dispatch gate ([#2663](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2663)).** The pre-harness seam's second refusal, beside the plan gate above. `core/gates/closed_issue_dispatch_gate.closed_issue_dispatch_refusal` reads the ticket's LIVE issue through the backend-registry seam (`core` never imports `teatree.backends`, and `teatree.agents` declares no such edge either, which is what puts the read on this side) and refuses an implementing dispatch whose issue the forge reports CLOSED — recorded as `FailureKind.ISSUE_CLOSED`, HALT and non-environmental, so the repair sweep escalates to a human instead of re-offering it. It closes the window the tick cannot: `TicketDispositionScanner` auto-ignores a closed-issue ticket on its own cadence, but an issue closed AFTER a `Task` was queued still reaches its agent, and measured that cost ten agent cycles across nine tickets and ~800 uncommitted lines that could never land. It **fails OPEN** on an unresolvable host, a fetch failure, or any unclassifiable payload — the opposite direction from the `IssueReopenState` reader, deliberately: there, acting wrongly revives delivered work; here, acting wrongly blocks live work, and the disposition scan retries every tick regardless. Scoped to `IMPLEMENTING_PHASES` on AUTHOR tickets with a real forge URL, so a planner (the phase that decides what to DO with a closed issue), a reviewer ticket's PR URL, and a loop-cadence anchor are never refused and never pay a round trip. The gate and the scanner share one `CLOSED_ISSUE_STATES` constant, so they cannot disagree about which payloads are closed.

### 5.8 Reactive Slack-Answer Loop

An owner reply in the current ticket root selects a pending question only when that question's card or chase is the newest confirmed ticket post before the reply; later progress posts leave the reply for ordinary ticket routing. A status question in a bound ticket thread reads that ticket's state, active tasks, and open pull requests directly, before a pending session can consume it. Top-level status questions retain the global statusline path. Confirmed loop replies join the ticket post ledger, including a reply found on retry.

A token-cheap reactive `/loop` slot that answers user DMs out-of-band so a quick ack / status question gets a reply in seconds, not at the next slower per-loop tick. Coalesces consecutive same-user messages into one logical turn, classifies (pure Python) into `ACK_ONLY` / `SIMPLE` / `NEEDS_WORK`, and either reacts, posts a threaded reply, or delegates to the `t3:answerer` sub-agent. A message that is only a control word (`pause` or `resume`, see `control_verbs.control_verb`) is recognised per message BEFORE coalescing: it is never continued onto an earlier message and never continued by a later one, so a lone `pause` sent 39 seconds after another message still reaches the live control. The inbound wake (`wake_slack_answer`) is enqueued at the highest task priority, because the queue orders READY rows by priority and then `run_after`, and an immediate enqueue carries the maximum `run_after`: at the default priority it sorted behind every due loop timer. A wake that finds the `loop-slack-answer` lease held re-arms ten seconds out while unreplied rows remain, instead of leaving them to the five-minute cadence. The owner's acceptance bound is the first reply to his Slack question in under 60 seconds, message to first reply in the thread; the answer itself is admitted in its own bounded class (see the admission governor page), so it never waits behind coding.

An unambiguous priority directive from the configured owner is resolved before live-session routing, after queued-question precedence; priority messages form separate turns. An explicit forge issue URL, qualified `repo#N`, or unique unsettled bare `#N` selects the ticket; otherwise the parked question, thread binding, or root message supplies context. Only unsettled author tickets with real forge issues qualify. A pure directive locks and updates `Ticket.expedited`, preserves newer instructions from the same Slack conversation in `Ticket.extra`, re-reads the flag, and confirms its threaded reply before the done reaction. A mixed request updates the flag, reacts lightning or sleep, and continues through ordinary routing. Ambiguous readings continue unchanged. Expedite affects admission order after reviews; it relaxes no merge gate.

**A bound thread follows the filed work ticket.** `ThreadBinding` maps `(overlay, channel, root thread ts)` to the answering ticket during triage, then `work_item_filing._attach` rebinds it to the admissible ticket created or reused for the work. A later message from the configured owner is offered to that ticket's live session before ordinary answer routing: a progress question uses read-only `inspect`, while a direction uses `steer`. `SessionCommand` stores the message's stable command ID, task and typed outcome; a settled command replays its receipt, while an unsettled command retries delivery to that same task. A stale unconfirmed session claim re-enters the loop after two minutes so a sender crash cannot strand it. The reply is confirmed by exact text and timestamp under the thread root before the inbound row is marked answered by the loop. If the bound task is pending behind a gate, the message queues for that task; once its work has ended, the ordinary answering path creates a follow-up on the bound ticket and names that path in the thread. Another author in a bound thread cannot reach either the live session or the answering task.

An idle live session takes the direction through `follow_up` as a new turn. `pause` reaches the running session through operator-only live control, interrupts it, and parks the same task with its conversation behind a durable gate: `Task.park` with an attempt error starting `operator_paused:` and a `not_before` ten years out, a prefix usage-window recovery never releases and cycle-time reports as a human wait. `resume` is `release_operator_pause` (`src/teatree/core/models/task_handoff.py`), a compare-and-set that clears the gate on the same pending task only while its newest attempt carries that prefix; messages sent while it is parked are queued into that task's next prompt and recorded as operator input after a successful run. When several tasks could receive a thread message, the owner selects one with a leading `#task-id`. A mirrored question card also binds its own thread to the parked task's ticket, so its reply follows the same route. The `live pause` CLI exposes the same typed receipt. A partial or unreachable worker list leaves the inbound row pending rather than assuming the ticket has no session; a command known to be unsent is retried. The command key includes overlay, channel and message timestamp.

The `pydantic_ai` history processor (`LateHistoryCompactor`) stubs older reads of a file superseded by a later read before summarization, pins operator inputs and idle follow-ups verbatim across the summary boundary (both prefixes come from `live_control`, so a framer change cannot drift from the pin), and uses a forced compaction after an overflow even when the model window is unknown. It aims to reduce a compacted history below half the model window less its reserve while preserving the latest turn and complete tool call/result pairs. The tool-result stub depth is the per-phase compaction setting; history reduction follows the model window. For the Claude child, a known window (an explicit `agent_model_context_windows` entry, else the model's native one) sets `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` and `CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE` so the CLI compacts 16,000 tokens before the window ends, and replaces any inherited `DISABLE_COMPACT` / `DISABLE_AUTO_COMPACT` with `0`; the SDK merges the child's env over the inherited one, so an inherited `DISABLE_COMPACT=1` would otherwise turn compaction off. Each `compact_boundary` frame the CLI streams is counted by `StreamCapture.compactions`; as that frame is consumed `LiveSessionController.restate_after_compaction` steers the directions it accepted back into the current turn (command id `compact:<attempt>:<n>`), once per compaction, and defers while the session is idle. `tests/fixtures/claude_cli/2.1.300-compact-boundary.json` records the frame the bundled CLI 2.1.300 streams; that run emitted it under the override and emitted none with either switch set.

**A dispatched lane is TWO rows, and only one of them is work ([#4527](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4527)).** `dispatch_work` mints the CONVERSATION row — a `Ticket` anchored on `SYNTHETIC_LOOP_UMBRELLA_URL` under a `#slack=<channel>/<ts>/dm` fragment (unique per message, so the dispatch is idempotent under `unique_nonempty_issue_url`; the non-numeric suffix keeps `derive_issue_number` from stamping the Slack ts as a forge issue number) and described from the interpreted request. It is deliberately never `Ticket.is_admissible()`: at dispatch time nobody has decided WHAT the work is. The WORK row is the real forge issue the shell-denied `answering` phase names through the result envelope's `work_item` channel — attach to an existing backlog issue, file a new one, or state a `no_work_reason` — which `teatree.core.answering.work_item_filing` files through the `route_forge_write` leak gate using the selected code host backend's forge identity and turns into an admissible `Ticket`; an unknown backend forge is stated in the thread before any outbound send. Attaching reuses a ticket by `repo_namespaced_key`, so GitLab's `issues` and `work_items` URLs for one issue resolve to one row. Every issue it opens carries `needs-triage` alongside the admit label: the title and body are the agent's paraphrase of a message a deliberately fail-open reader called work-implying, and the factory files as the maintainer's own account, so the author-keyed auto-triage Action cannot supply that gate and `IssueIntakeScanner` would otherwise claim it unreviewed. The repo it files in is the one the overlay's `metadata.choose_filing_repo` picks from the item's title and body among the overlay's owned repos (default: the first), chosen only once a new issue is actually being filed, so an attach or a declared no-work never asks it; the same repo carries the dedupe read, both gated writes and the create. A pick outside the owned repos, a hook that raises, or a refusal from the hook is STATED in the thread and files nothing. A forge refusal on the create — a 403, a rate limit, the posting gate — is raised as a `WorkItemFilingError` and STATED in the owner's thread, never allowed to escape and destroy the drafted reply along with it. A work-implying run that returns no `work_item` is REFUSED (the reply and a dropped request are otherwise indistinguishable) and earns the `transient_requeue` corrective retry. Only the admissible row is ever announced to the owner, as a clickable link. Rows that fail `is_admissible` are enumerated by `Ticket.objects.unfindable()` behind the doctor WARN and `t3 <overlay> ticket dead-rows`.

**Event-driven wake is the primary drain; the timer is a fallback (default 5m).** The Socket Mode receiver (`t3 slack listen`) records the configured owner's arriving DM as a `PendingChatInjection` row and then enqueues a one-shot `wake_slack_answer` task when it appends the event to the durable JSONL queue; the callback wakes for non-DM events, unresolved backends, and duplicate owner DMs, but not for a DM identified as coming from someone other than the configured owner, so the answer cycle runs on the worker's next ~1s loops-queue poll (~1s latency) instead of lagging up to a full cadence behind the arrival. For an owner's DM, recording first makes the wake useful: the cycle reads `PendingChatInjection.loop_unreplied()`, so a wake that arrived before the row would find an empty queue and stop without re-arming. Every writer of those rows — the live receiver, the listener-downtime catch-up (§ 7 "Inbound events") and the `slack_mentions` drain of the JSONL queue — records through the one `SlackDmRecorder.record_events` seam, so the write-side checks (`filter_self_messages`, `is_on_behalf_posted`, the fail-closed identity probe, and `owner_identity.sent_by_owner`) apply to every path structurally. Only the overlay's configured owner can send owner work: when the owner id resolves, a colleague's DM is queued as transport but records no row, emits no `slack.dm` signal and wakes nothing, and every reader of stored rows (this cycle, `AskUserQuestionReplyScanner`, `RedCardScanner`, the `inbound_unanswered` incident detector) reads them through `owner_identity.owner_rows`, which treats an unreadable owner id as nobody, so a row recorded before the filter stays stored and inert. Each catch-up slice that recorded at least one new row enqueues ONE wake, however many rows it recorded. The receiver posts nothing to Slack; every outbound reply stays in the answer cycle, which is what leaves exactly one `:eyes:` source (the `mark_eyes_reacted` CAS). Because the wake carries the primary path, the `run_slack_answer` timer chain is only a missed-wake / retry / startup catch-up safety net — its `T3_SLACK_ANSWER_CADENCE` default is therefore **300s (5m)**, not a tight spin (a deliberate low override is still honoured down to the 15s floor). The wake shares the cadence chain's lease-guarded body (`_run_slack_answer_cycle_under_lease` under the `loop-slack-answer` `LoopLease`) so it can never double-post with the chain or an owner session, self-dedups so an event burst collapses to one cycle, and does not re-arm. The receiver's `backends` layer cannot reach the orchestration layer, so the enqueue is injected as an `on_event` callback wired at the CLI composition root (best-effort — a failed signal only costs latency; the JSONL write is already durable).

**The wake is filtered at the trigger, and debounced (#4707).** The receiver refuses the wake for an event this app itself produced (`is_self_originated` — `api_app_id`, `bot_id`, or a match against the identity `auth.test` resolved once per connection). Without that the cycle fed on its own output: the answer cycle posts, Slack returns the post as a plain `message` event, and the wake fires again — loop gain ≥ 1, measured at 2,391 `chat.postMessage` in 24h. `filter_self_messages` already refused to *answer* such a row, but it runs in the scanner, downstream of the wake, so the guard had to move to where the gain is introduced. The wake additionally dedups against a wake that finished under `WAKE_MIN_INTERVAL_SECONDS` ago, re-arming once at the far edge — a trailing-edge debounce, so a burst costs O(1) cycles and its last event is still answered.

---

## 6. Overlay System

An overlay is a downstream Django project that customizes teatree for a specific project/organization.

**§6.0 Overlay Thinness Principle (Non-Negotiable).** Generic workflow logic belongs in core, not in overlays. Before adding logic to an overlay, ask: "Would a different project using the same framework need the same logic?" If yes, it belongs in core — parameterized and configurable. Overlays provide only: (1) configuration values, (2) project-specific glue, (3) truly unique workflows. Everything else — DB provisioning strategies, migration runners, symlink management, service orchestration — is a configurable engine in core; the overlay configures it, never reimplements it. An overlay method exceeding ~30 lines of non-configuration code likely contains generic logic to extract.

**OverlayBase ABC (`teatree.core.overlay`).** Composition over inheritance (PR-27b). `OverlayBase` itself carries only the **two required abstract hooks** plus a thin **identity/reference** surface (~10 methods total); every optional extension hook regroups by concern onto a **composed facet** exposed as an attribute — mirroring the pre-existing `overlay.config` / `overlay.metadata` split. An overlay overrides a concern by subclassing the one facet and assigning it (e.g. `provisioning = MyProvisioning()`), so the hooks stay lazy instance methods and behaviour is preserved exactly (no eager spec computation).

| Composed facet | Attribute | Concern (hooks) |
|---|---|---|
| `OverlayConfig` | `config` | credentials, URLs, labels, scope — now a **Pydantic model**, fail-closed (typed fields validate on assignment; a wrong-typed setting-module/TOML override raises instead of silently corrupting) |
| `OverlayMetadata` | `metadata` | PR title/validation, required description sections, CI/E2E config, skill metadata, `get_issue_repos` (optional per-issue repo hint), `choose_filing_repo` (the repo a filed work item goes to) |
| `OverlayProvisioning` | `provisioning` | `env_extra`, `symlinks`, `services_config`, `compose_file`, `base_images`, `docker_services`, `db_import_strategy`, `db_import`, `post_db_steps`, `reset_passwords_command`, `envrc_lines`, `cleanup_steps`, `health_checks`, `snapshot_warmer_configs`, `reap_external_resources`, `resolve_variant`, `declared_env_keys`/`declared_secret_env_keys` |
| `OverlayRuntime` | `runtime` | `run_commands`, `pre_run_steps`, `visual_qa_server` (optional checkout server), `test_command` (→ `run tests`), `lint_command` (→ `run lint`), `verify_endpoints`, `readiness_probes` |
| `OverlayE2E` | `e2e` | `env_extras`, `run_provenance`, `playwright_args`, `scenarios`, `preflight` |
| `OverlayReview` | `review` | `visual_qa_targets`, `classify_customer_display_impact`, `can_auto_merge` (→ `MergeGuard`), `merge_candidate_repo_slugs` (§17.4 cross-repo merge probe, [#2323](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2323)), `repo_project_paths` (ticket review identity), `repo_owner_for_slug`, `review_exempt_repo_slugs`, `mandatory_e2e_exempt_repo_slugs`, `mandatory_e2e_repo_slugs`, `get_followup_scanners` |
| `OverlayConnectors` | `connectors` | `preflight`, `mcp_tool_group` |

| `OverlayBase` direct | Returns | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `get_repos()` *(abstract)* | `list[str]` | Repositories to provision |
| `get_provision_steps(worktree)` *(abstract)* | `list[ProvisionStep]` | Ordered setup steps |
| `get_workspace_repos()` | `list[str]` | Repos for the multi-repo workspace |
| `get_issue_title(url)` / `is_issue_done(data)` / `resolve_mr_token(iid)` / `resolve_issue_token(iid)` | — | Forge issue/reference resolution |
| `get_timeouts()` / `get_health_signals()` / `get_checking_sources()` / `get_eval_scenarios_dir()` | — | Loop / factory operational hooks |

Every facet hook defaults to empty / permissive so existing overlays keep working — core enforcement only activates for overlays that opt in (the one exception is `review.classify_customer_display_impact`, which defaults **fail-closed** to `True` so the mandatory-E2E gate is never silently skipped).

**Load-time signature conformance ([#3526](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3526)).** The framework calls each facet hook with the parameters the base declares, so an override that cannot receive one — a dropped keyword-only parameter, or a dropped positional slot with no `*args`/`**kwargs` — is call-incompatible and crashes only on the path that forwards the missing argument. `teatree.core.overlay_conformance.overlay_signature_violations` compares every override against its base signature at discovery (`_discover_overlays`): an entry-point overlay fails loud with `ImproperlyConfigured`, a registry overlay is skipped with a warning, both naming the hook and the missing parameter. A renamed positional parameter stays legal (leading arguments forward positionally); only a dropped slot or dropped keyword-only name is a break. This retires the conditional-forward idiom (`kwargs = {} if … else {…}`) that hid such a mismatch until the non-default path ran.

**Typed provisioning substrate — `ProvisionStep` DAG + `Variant` (PR-27, [#787](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/787)/[#1385](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1385)).** `ProvisionStep` is a typed DAG node: `requires`/`produces` tokens order the steps (a `requires` token no step `produces` fails the run loud, fail-closed) and independent steps run concurrently, while `post_condition` is a truth check evaluated after the callable so a step that "ran" without producing its resource is recorded FAILED. The aggregate of every step's `post_condition` plus the core invariants (worktree dir, env cache, DB present) is `teatree.core.provision.provision_postconditions.aggregate_provision_post_conditions` — the truth test `worktree status` runs on a `provisioned`/`services_up`/`ready` worktree, so deleting `.t3-cache/.t3-env.cache` or the DB out from under it makes `status` refuse green with a non-zero exit. **Variant** is a first-class frozen dataclass (`teatree.core.provision.variant.Variant`: `name`, `canonical_tenant`, `default_language`, `dslr_snapshot_name`, `e2e_credentials_key`) resolved through `overlay.provisioning.resolve_variant(name) -> Variant` — no overlay hook takes a raw `variant: str`, so a tenant/snapshot/credentials name is never reconstructed from a bare string at a call site.

**Repo SCOPE axis (`owned_repos`).** `OverlayConfig.owned_repos` is a forge-host-keyed dict of the namespace patterns the overlay legitimately works on — the **scope** axis, orthogonal to **visibility** (`private_repos`/leak-prevention, fails open) and **collaboration** (`author_is_self`). Classification (`teatree.core.intake.repo_scope`) gates host equality before the namespace half, so a `gitlab.com` repo can never match a `github.com` scope. The unknown-repo gate (`owned_repo_guard`) holds a push or keystone merge to a repo no opted-in overlay owns — fails **CLOSED** on a clean unknown verdict (opposite the visibility gate) and **OPEN** on a resolver exception (never-lockout). It ships **INERT** (`require_owned_repo_approval` default `false`); enabling it requires first declaring the FULL owned host/namespace list in the private DB `ConfigSetting` store, since a partial list would hold the operator's own private merges as unknown. A path-only overlay cannot opt itself in (its repos are declared under an instantiable overlay). The PreToolUse hook bootstraps Django before resolving overlays so the gate actually fires in production. Owned does NOT imply auto-merge: a shared product repo is in scope yet still needs colleague review — and the declaration never reaches merge authorization, which resolves an overlay from the repo ENUMERATION alone. Two narrow readers sit outside the gate: `merge.host_kind` recovers the forge a bare `owner/repo` dropped, and `cli.review.forge_target` attributes a review target no overlay's repo table lists (GitLab-hosted declarations only, since `t3 review` posts nowhere else).

**Codex worker execution phases (#5543).** `bughunt`, `dogfood_smoke`, and `eval_local` keep their original phase capabilities on other harnesses. A Codex route uses the current worker with its existing write approval policy; only `planning`, `reviewing`, `critic_reviewing`, `scoping`, `triage_assessing`, `directive_interpreting`, `answering`, `requesting_review`, and `shipping` use the read-only executor. Route choice remains in skill-keyed data rows.

**Ordered harness candidates (`factory_phase_harness_candidates`).** `OverlayConfig.factory_phase_harness_candidates` maps a canonical phase to an ordered list of harness registry names for that overlay's factory dispatches. `resolve_dispatch_harness` reads it for the dispatched task's ticket overlay through the path-only-symmetric `OverlayConfigResolver` (never `get_overlay`, which cannot instantiate a path-only overlay — merely resolving the field must not change which dispatches work; a blank or unknown overlay declares none), pass each candidate through the same `resolve_phase_harness` pin as `agent_harness`, and take the first registered backend whose side-effect-free `HarnessSpec.unavailable_reason` probe passes (`harness_registry.select_harness`). Fallback happens only before a harness opens: a failure after `open` is recorded and never tries the next candidate, and a list with no available candidate records one `NoAvailableHarnessError` refusal naming every rejection. A phase with no list resolves exactly as it did without the field — no probe runs. One dispatch performs exactly ONE selection: `resolve_dispatch_harness` returns the built harness, the name it was selected under and the Layer-2 provider valid for that name (`provider_under`), so a probe answering differently on a second pass can never split the transport from its credential or exhaust the candidates after one has opened. A later phase candidate whose resolved lane is metered requires `agent_route_paid_fallback`; absent settings refuse a metered fallback closed. Core ships the field empty for every overlay.

**Ordered skill routes (`agent_skill_models`): progress, capture, holds.** `TaskAttempt.objects.failed_route_candidates` names the route candidates that already ran on a task and failed through their own fault (`harness_crash`, `landing_unverified`, `result_error`) since the task's last usage-window park. `harness_dispatch._skill_route_selection` walks the candidates in order through every eligibility rejection, skips one that already failed (the skipped failure is the recorded `fallback_reason`) and retries the last eligible candidate once all of them failed, so a one-candidate route is unchanged; a candidate after the first whose resolved lane is metered (`harness_dispatch._is_metered_route`, after credential policy) is rejected unless `agent_route_paid_fallback` is on, with a retry instant of its own only when no earlier candidate has one, so a long usage window parks the task until it resets and a metered candidate alone fails it after the two-park cap with a reason naming the setting (a metered attempt shows in the `Metered` group of the check-in report, and the first one of a UTC day sends one owner notification); outage, evidence and recording failures keep their retry on the same candidate. A routed run that raises records one FAILED attempt carrying its route index (`runner_route_recording.fail_routed_crash`) and the transient sweep reopens it; an unrouted crash still propagates. Route capture (rule R): a route reached through a skill the phase merely loads never captures a phase that bars write tools (reviews load `code`), while a route keyed by the phase's own lifecycle skill still does. A route candidate on an `agent_phase_harness`-pinned phase (as `agent_phase_harness` resolves it; other phases ignore that setting) is rejected with a reason naming the pin; `select_harness` rejects every harness but `claude_sdk` for a ticket with `extra["claude_only"]`, and a managed-lane harness (Codex) runs a phase that bars write tools only when its spec's `read_only_executor_refusal` hook answers `None` on that very selection: the hook is a live probe of a separate read-only executor (`codex_read_only_executor.attest`, judged by `codex_read_only_verdict.evaluate`), never cached and never read from a setting or environment flag, and a spec without the hook keeps the outright refusal. That executor mounts the deploy checkout's `.git` and, only when its clone exists, the worker Codex source clone's `.git`, runs as `nobody`, which owns none of the trees it reads, and is refused whenever it can read a non-empty env or credential file or a private key under its mounts, owns a mounted tree, or cannot read a run root's git files. Codex binds that executor only for a write-barring phase in the worker container (planning, reviewing, critic_reviewing, scoping, triage_assessing, directive_interpreting, answering, requesting_review and shipping; a host keeps Codex's own read-only sandbox): `open` attests again before anything starts, then runs `codex --disable plugins app-server` in `teatree-ro-executor` over `docker exec` (`codex_read_only_server`) with the executor's own login (`teatree/codex/ro-executor-auth-json-b64`) under its own lease, a fresh owner-only tmpfs home per server created only after a sweep has ended every process outside PID 1's session but the worker's own Codex-free `docker exec` programs (an identity no process can shed by clearing its environment or detaching), both tmpfs mounts emptied at every start and at stop after a verified persist, and a re-attestation per thread that retires a server whose home holds rules, config or plugins. Only an `ExecutorAttestation` grants such a phase the unsandboxed translation, and the worker's own transports refuse attested options. Such a run's brief (`read_only_venue_brief`, `prompt.build_read_only_task_prompt`) carries its facts in the task message, read in the worker under fixed caps (issue body and comments 64 KiB each, ticket context, handoff and survey 32 KiB each) and never truncated: an unreadable issue or PR body, an unrecoverable missing planning survey, a failed fetch of the target ref or a part over its cap refuses the run before it opens: the task parks for two minutes (twice), then fails with the refusal's reason, and a later free route can run, while a later metered route parks unless `agent_route_paid_fallback` is on (`read_only_venue_brief.ReadOnlyBriefRefusedError`, `runner._retry_route_exception`, and the ordered route gate). Its system context leads with the read-only directive, delivers every required skill across the system context and task message, with venue-only sections removed (the executor has no Skill tool and no skill folder; `reviewing` and `critic_reviewing` embed `rules`, their lifecycle skill, `code-review` and loaded overlay review companions, the lifecycle skill without the sections that only drive the `t3` CLI, and carry remote-review and author self-review diffs in the task message), moving whole skill blocks to the task message until the system context fits 96 KiB; it points at no skill path and rewrites command instructions on both surfaces while preserving quoted issue text, comments, handoffs and diffs byte-for-byte; one whose fixed system context still names a `t3` command or exceeds the budget refuses the run. Rubric criteria and declared seams for read-only reviews are carried in the task message with their commands rewritten. A triage assessment's brief inlines the body and comments of every issue its task lists, each under its URL, read in the worker under the issue body and comment caps (a task that lists no forge issue, or a listed issue that cannot be read, refuses the run); `triage_assessing` and `directive_interpreting` with no worktree start in the overlay's main clone, and the recorders for triage recommendations, directive sketches and scoping summaries are the ones the worker lane already uses. A brief refusal does not persist a route availability row: `TaskAttempt.objects.refused_brief_candidates` (since the task's last park) is what skips the refused candidate. Worker briefs are unchanged. `answering`, `requesting_review` and `shipping` return a draft or readiness verdict the worker acts on through its own gates (`reactive_envelope_recorders` for the answer, `review_request_recorder` for `review_request_post`, `ship_readiness_recorder` for `pr create` and the queued `execute_ship`); the executor posts, pushes and merges nothing, and a run on it that returns neither field is refused. A typed quota or authentication rejection holds the candidate for an hour; a classified time-bounded limit with a reported reset holds it until that reset. A failed agent narrative alone creates no hold. Short transient failures retain the two-minute hold. A routed or non-`claude_sdk` coding/debugging attempt that reports `needs_user_input` with no files and no new commit is recorded `landing_unverified: blocked before edits`, also for a ticket with no registered worktree. A Codex candidate whose model its `models_cache.json` catalog does not list is rejected before dispatch. When every candidate is rejected and at least one rejection carries a retry instant (a usage window with a reset, or a quota/auth hold), `usage_window.park_on_timed_rejections` parks the task until the earliest one instead of failing it; the park reason lists every rejection, and the third consecutive park on a route hold fails the task. A park engages the token-outage preset only when no other lane can dispatch (`harness_dispatch.another_lane_can_dispatch`: the configured harness or a global route candidate that is registered, off the parked lane, unwindowed, available, and authorized for paid fallback when it is a later metered candidate).

Route fallback is based on typed harness errors, classified limits, and HTTP status. A model’s crash narrative cannot mark its candidate quota-blocked. Content guardrail blocks remain terminal on skill routes. Subscription limits rotate accounts or park the task without writing a route-availability hold; other lanes retain route fallback and use a reported reset for a timed hold.

**Overlay API version pin + `teatree.overlay_sdk` (PR-27 / PR-27b).** `teatree.__overlay_api_version__` is held at `"1"` through the pre-stable window — pre-1.0 the overlay-facing API is explicitly unstable and every registered overlay migrates IN LOCKSTEP with each breaking core change (the PR-27b facet regrouping + Pydantic `OverlayConfig` is one such change, and every overlay loads and passes conformance against the reshaped base at `"1"`), so bumping the counter would manufacture a teatree-vs-overlay mismatch with nothing to detect. From the first stable release it becomes a genuine compatibility counter, bumped on any breaking change to the overlay-facing API. Overlays assert it at import to fail loudly when teatree diverges from what they were built against. `teatree.overlay_sdk` is the surface-frozen namespace an overlay imports from — a single documented facade (`OverlayBase`, `OverlayConfig`, the facet base classes `OverlayProvisioning`/`OverlayRuntime`/`OverlayE2E`/`OverlayReview`/`OverlayConnectors`, `ProvisionStep`, `Probe`, `Variant`, the config/util helpers) whose `__all__` and whose extension-hook signatures — on `OverlayBase` **and** each facet — are snapshotted by `tests/teatree_overlay_sdk/test_overlay_sdk_surface.py` in **core's** CI, so an internal rename or a signature change fails core *locally* instead of breaking an overlay's own CI asynchronously.

**Docker base-image sharing (§6.2a).** Teatree builds each `BaseImageConfig` once from the master clone's lockfile and tags it with the single master tag `{image_name}:base` (NOT per-lockfile) — shared across every worktree. `ensure_base_image` (re)builds only when the image is **absent** (`docker image inspect` rc≠0) or **broken** (the tag resolves but its config Id does not — a corrupt/interrupted build → `rmi -f` then rebuild); a worktree provision otherwise never triggers a build. Code isolation is volume-mount level (`.:/app:rw`); dependency drift between the branch's lockfile and the baked master deps is reconciled at container start by the overlay's entrypoint (`uv sync`), so no per-lockfile image tag is needed.

**`t3 startoverlay <name> <dest>`** scaffolds a lightweight overlay package: `src/<name>/{__init__,overlay,apps}.py`, `skills/t3:<base>/SKILL.md` (where `<base>` is the overlay app name with the `t3_` prefix and `_overlay` suffix stripped **and underscores mapped to dashes** — `t3_my_thing_overlay` → `skills/t3:my-thing/` — else `overlay`), `pyproject.toml`. No `manage.py`/`settings.py`/`urls.py` — teatree is the Django project.

**Discovery:** the DB `overlays` registry row (its `[overlays.<name>]` entries) wins over `teatree.overlays` entry points on name conflicts. `discover_active_overlay()` returns the unique overlay if one exists, else the one whose `manage.py` is in cwd ancestors.

---

## 7. Backend Protocols and ABCs

Ticket DM delivery reads each existing root outside the SQLite write transaction; an unreadable reply never blocks the note. The Slack implementation raises the core `MessagingReadRefusedError` with its refusal code: only `thread_not_found`, `message_not_found`, or a root tombstone proves deletion and permits a new generation; rate limits and unreadable replies preserve the current root. An unnamed notification posts at the DM root unless its invoking run resolves a ticket through `T3_TICKET_ID`; a named thread keeps explicit precedence.

Every external API concern is a `@runtime_checkable Protocol` in `teatree.core.backend_protocols` (the domain owns the abstractions it consumes; `backends` implements them). PR is the canonical term in core; GitLab MRs are translated at the API edge.

| Protocol | Implementations |
|---|---|
| `CodeHostBackend` — PR/issue/comment (incl. `list`/`update_issue_comment`, and `list_repo_open_issues` — the paginated open-backlog read the § Issue hygiene create-dedupe needs)/upload/review-state + the §17.4.3 merge-RPC surface (`fetch_live_head_sha`, `fetch_pr_merge_state`, `fetch_pr_draft_state`, `fetch_required_checks_rollup`, `fetch_required_status_check_contexts`, `fetch_workflow_runs_at_head` (the GitHub-Free plan-restriction Actions-API fallback, a GitLab no-op stub — [#4844](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4844)), `merge_pr_squash_bound` — raw forge I/O resolved via `core.backend_registry`; `merge.execution`/`merge.ci_rollup` keep the verdict/error classification) | `GitHubCodeHost`, `GitLabCodeHost` |
| `CIService` — pipeline cancel/trigger/quality-check | `GitLabCIService` |
| `MessagingBackend` — mentions/DMs/post/reply/update (in place, always with blocks)/react | `SlackBotBackend`, `NoopMessagingBackend` |

Forge API transport shares one process-wide HTTPX client per complete origin (scheme, host and port), capped at `forge_pool_max_connections` live and idle connections (default 4, range 1 to 16, box-global). A GET or HEAD is replayed once when a reused keep-alive connection went stale, a connect failure is retried once after a jittered pause, and a once-a-minute log line reports requests against reused connections. GitLab REST, GraphQL, upload and trace reads and GitHub `gh api` REST/GraphQL calls use the same per-host pool; credentials remain on individual requests. Non-API `gh` commands retain their CLI path.

Multi-field request parameters are grouped into frozen `slots=True` dataclasses (`PullRequestSpec`). `repo + pr_iid` is the natural unit on both code hosts — protocol methods never accept free-form PR URLs.

Review tracking reads inline discussion threads through `list_review_threads` and resolves a confirmed thread through `resolve_review_thread`; an unreadable thread list fails loudly. A GitLab follow-up issue can atomically resolve discussions with `create_issue_resolving_threads(IssueResolutionSpec)`, while GitHub refuses that operation and uses its GraphQL thread-resolution method after the issue is verified. The ordinary `create_issue` contract stays unchanged.

NIT follow-up issues keep one canonical numbered body and additive `nit` / `t3-auto` labels through `add_issue_labels`; the issue and resolved inline threads are verified by re-read. A later merge critic receives its prior verdict and the exact prior-to-current changed-line diff; unavailable evidence stays unverified instead of closing a dispatch with fabricated failures.

The NIT lane publishes each advisory finding as an inline review comment, collects its permalink in one labelled follow-up issue per pull request, and resolves only threads it reads back. The worker stamps a receipt for each point at the reviewed head after the issue and thread state read back; the merge-quality gate refuses an unreceipted point without changing the blocking-item verdict. Local comment and public-forge privacy refusals occur before verdict recording; publication failures retain the verdict and remain visible to the merge gate until retried. The own-PR scanner reads unmet merge-critic items at the live head and sends them through the existing review-findings fix slot, even when the cold review is merge-safe.
**Forge read interface.** `teatree.backends.github.reads` defines nine reads: `list_prs`, `list_pr_commits`, `list_pr_reviews`, `list_pr_comments`, `list_issue_comments`, `fetch_required_checks_rollup`, `fetch_workflow_runs_at_head`, `get_issue`, and `list_repo_open_issues`. The other `GitHubCodeHost` reads are `current_user`, `is_assignable`, `list_my_prs`, `list_my_merged_prs`, `list_review_requested_prs`, `list_merged_prs_since`, `get_pr_diff`, `get_pr_file_diffs`, `get_repo`, `list_pr_discussions`, `list_assigned_issues`, `list_authored_issues`, `list_labeled_issues`, `search_open_issues`, `repo_for_issue_url`, `verify_upload`, `find_pr_review`, `get_mr_approvals`, `get_review_state`, `get_pr_open_state`, `get_pr_author`, `fetch_open_pr_url_for_branch`, `fetch_live_head_sha`, `fetch_pr_content_snapshot`, `fetch_pr_merge_state`, `fetch_pr_draft_state`, `fetch_pr_author`, `fetch_pr_same_repo`, `fetch_required_status_check_contexts`, `fetch_pr_changed_paths`, `fetch_pr_message`, and `list_unshipped_branches`. `teatree.backends.gitlab.reads` defines the commit read; GitLab's other reads are in `backends/gitlab/pr_reads.py` and its client. Each reads module binds no write-capable name, so binding its module object in any form reaches no write; tach and `tests/conformance/test_forge_read_interface.py` additionally reject imports of names outside `[[interfaces]]`. The AST scan also flags supported module-binding forms. For same-layer or unlayered consumers, tach enforces the declared reads-only dependency; higher-layer consumers may still import lower-layer clients directly under tach's layered rules. Writes stay on `GitHubCodeHost` / `GitLabCodeHost`, which delegate the reads above to those modules. The `gh` transport, `teatree.backends.github.api`, is its own tach module with explicit public read helpers so the GitHub reads use it without a cycle through the client.

**Selection.** Per-overlay configuration (the DB `overlays` registry row) declares `code_host = "github" | "gitlab"` and `messaging_backend = "slack" | "noop"`. The loader (`backends/loader.py`) resolves the overlay's selected backend with no platform branches in caller code, cached `lru_cache(maxsize=1)` per overlay identity. Each `CodeHostBackend` reports its own `forge` (`github` or `gitlab`); `work_item_filing._file_new` routes its outbound write under `CodeHostBackend.forge`, the forge of the host that writes, rather than the overlay-level `code_host`.

**Per-remote GitLab credential.** `get_code_host_for_repo` already resolves the repo's `origin` remote to pick the forge; it hands that same remote to `OverlayConfig.get_gitlab_token_for_remote(remote)`, which defaults to `get_gitlab_token()` so an overlay that does not override it is unchanged everywhere. An overlay overrides it where ONE repo must be written under a different identity than the rest — the case being a repo whose PRs the sole human reviewer has to stay eligible to approve, which a forge refuses for a PR's own author. The scope belongs to the overlay and is expected to be an exact slug match: a prefix match would hand the second identity to every repo whose name merely contains the first one's.

**Project GitLab read credential.** `gitlab_read_token_pass_paths` maps a project path or numeric ID to a `pass` entry. The GitLab HTTP client uses that entry only for GETs under the matching `projects/<id>` endpoint, with URL-decoded, case-insensitive project matching. An absent entry keeps the client credential; an unreadable mapping or entry logs a warning and falls back to it. POST, PUT, DELETE, GraphQL and uploads keep the client credential.

**GitLab merge and write policy.** Merge transport resolves a GitLab credential through the overlay owning the repository; an optional one-merge bound token is scoped to the same repository and reset after the merge. `gitlab_write_guard` is off by default. When configured, the GitLab REST, GraphQL mutation and glab write paths apply per-item caps recorded in `SendAudit`, label and authorship checks, and line-start quick-action escaping. A configured receipt channel uses the `BotPing` delivery claim and falls back to the owner's DM when the channel post fails.

**Inbound events.** `t3 slack listen` runs a Socket Mode receiver that writes events to append-only JSONL queues (`slack-events.jsonl`, `slack-reactions.jsonl`) so scanners can drain atomically without racing. Every event, live or replayed, takes one path: `receiver.deliver_event` (type/subtype filter, JSONL enqueue, self-originated check, guarded `on_event`); only the owner's DM leaves it as owner work (§ 5.8), and `t3 slack check` drops any other DM before printing or acknowledging it. Each connected overlay loop stamps its own entry in `slack-listener-heartbeat.json` (`backends/slack/listener_heartbeat.py`; the top-level `updated_at` stays for an un-restarted reader), which `t3 doctor` reads from another container (`self_heal_slack_listener.check_slack_listener_alive`, per configured Slack overlay; an absent file or stamp fails). A stamp also means "everything before this instant is ingested": `run_listener` reads the heartbeat once before any overlay thread starts, the first `hello` envelope owes that pre-restart stamp when one exists and a later `hello` the last stamp, and the serve loop runs the owed catch-up (`on_connect`) with a 5/15/45/120 s backoff and stamps only once nothing is owed and the current session has greeted. A catch-up that keeps failing therefore shows as a stale heartbeat, and a restart seeds from that old stamp. The fenced `teatree.slack_catchup` package reads the owner's bot DM and, with a user token, member-channel mentions and owner reactions from the last-alive instant minus a margin; the receiver's hello hook and one stale-heartbeat inbox job replay each item through `deliver_event`, while the recorder deduplicates on `(overlay, slack_ts)`. A tap on an owner-question button arrives as an `interactive` envelope: the receiver acknowledges it first, then hands the `block_actions` payload to `t3 slack listen`'s click handler (`question_binding.answer_from_click`), which records the tapped option on the card at its own message `ts`, falling back to the thread root — only for the owner's own tap (another user's tap is refused and logged with the message `ts` alone), only while the card still offers that option — and edits the card in place (`chat.update`, always with blocks, buttons removed, owner-DM-guarded like every send). A tap on a closed card shows why instead; a typed or digit reply closes the same card. The answer cycle owns replies inside a card thread and the `askuserquestion_reply` scanner takes top-level replies, so each threaded row gets one binding decision. Typed replies join a pending card by its own message timestamp within the Slack thread root, and a reply that equals an option label (any case) records that option like a tap; ambiguous card threads ask for a `#<id>` choice (before any live-session steer in a bound ticket thread), while a reply under a closed or tap-only card, naming a `#<id>` that is not waiting, or giving text outside a card’s options receives an explicit thread note without closing a card. An addressed follow-up starts its own coalesced unit, and each threaded row logs its decision. An API-posted owner reply reaches the binder only in a pending owner-card thread; the recorder rejects non-owner, bot-authored, self-authored, and other app posts. A refused recorder thread read retains that event for the next JSONL drain while unrelated DMs persist; a refused binder read leaves the reply unclaimed. The app manifest turns Interactivity on, and the socket doctor says "reinstall" only when scopes, events or Socket Mode change; where Interactivity is off the thread reply keeps working.

**Bounded Slack recovery.** The `teatree.slack_catchup` reader walks forward in 24-hour slices with a 7-day cap and a 24-hour root lookback; each slice is read in full before any event is delivered. It reads the owner's bot DM and, when a user token is available, searches member-channel mentions and owner reactions. Without that token it warns and still recovers the bot DM. A refused page or page-cap hit raises, so a partial read never advances the cursor. The inbox job persists each completed slice and the per-overlay delivery memo atomically under `DATA_DIR/loop/slack-catchup-<overlay>.json`, then stops after a 20-second soft budget. A loop tick starts a new process, so the file carries the cursor and memo into the next tick; process globals cannot. The listener reads that cursor on `hello` without advancing it and saves only the delivered-event memo in the sibling `.seen.json` file, including live events after the hello pass. With no prior heartbeat, the first hello owes no earlier interval and may stamp after greeting. Both callers share `DeliveredEvents` with the live receiver so overlap does not enqueue a duplicate event; DM rows still deduplicate at `(overlay, slack_ts)`. The hourly retention pass removes replied `PendingChatInjection` rows and rows from known non-owner senders after 30 days, while preserving unresolved owner work; it blanks sent `BotPing` text after 7 days while retaining its dedup metadata. The bot remains on the DM-only scope profile; other integrations can supply a user token for channel recovery.

**Reaction surface (#1281).** FSM `reactions.add` failures (`missing_scope`, `not_in_channel`, `mcp_externally_shared_channel_restricted`, …) raise `SlackReactionError` from `backends/slack/react_errors.py` — never silently return `False` — so callers cannot fall back to a `chat.postMessage(text=":emoji:")` thread reply. `SlackBotBackend.post_message` / `post_reply` reject bodies matching `^:[a-z0-9_+\-]+:$` with `SingleEmojiBodyRefusedError`, foreclosing the failure-mode shape at the backend boundary. FSM-side wrappers (`add_reactions_for_transition`, `add_approval_reaction`) catch the raise locally so Slack auth gaps cannot roll back FSM transitions. The publisher peeks for a parseable review-message target before either on-behalf approval gate, so a targetless transition consumes no approval and writes no post audit.

**Destination-routed post/react ([#1750](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1750)).** Token picked by *destination* via `SlackBotBackend._route_token`: user's own DM → bot; colleague/channel → personal `xoxp`. Every colleague-surface post/react under the user's identity routes through the one gate+route+audit chokepoint `core/on_behalf_egress.OnBehalfSlackEgress` (self-DM acks ungated); the call-site authorization is pinned by the `on-behalf-routed-egress` / `on-behalf-colleague-primitives` entries in the chokepoint registry (`quality/chokepoints.yaml`, enforced by `scripts/hooks/check_chokepoints.py`); see [`skills/rules/SKILL.md`](skills/rules/SKILL.md).

**Deterministic reference linkifier.** The clickable-references rule (a bare `#N` / `!N` on a user-facing surface must become a clickable link) is enforced *in code* by `core/reference_linkifier.py`, not by asking the model. `ReferenceResolver` resolves DB-first (`PullRequest`, `Ticket.issue_url`) then constructs from the active repo context; `linkify` (idempotent; skips linked refs, inline code, fenced blocks) runs at the Slack chokepoint `SlackReplier._deliver`. Forge posts are NOT linkified (forge auto-links bare ids). It is the sole mechanism for the rule — the former bare-reference *blocking* gate was removed (it over-blocked and asked the model to rewrite refs non-deterministically); the linkifier rewrites in code with no block and no model round-trip.

**Sync ABC (`teatree.types`).** `SyncBackend` is an ABC — defined alongside `SyncResult` in `teatree/types.py` (the foundation-layer contract) with `is_configured(overlay)` and `sync(overlay) → SyncResult`. `core/sync.py` wires and drives the implementations `GitHubSyncBackend`, `GitLabSyncBackend`. Both consume `CodeHostBackend` — platform-specific code lives only in the Protocol implementation, not in sync logic.

---

## 8. Command Tiers

| Tier | Tool | Needs Django? | Examples |
|---|---|---|---|
| Runtime | django-typer management commands | Yes | `worktree provision`, `tasks work-next`, `followup sync` |
| Bootstrap | Typer CLI (`t3`) | No | `t3 startoverlay`, `t3 info`, `t3 ci cancel` |
| Overlay | Typer CLI delegating to `manage.py` via subprocess | Indirectly | `t3 <overlay> workspace ticket`, `t3 <overlay> worktree start` |

Internal utilities (`utils/`) are Python modules, not a CLI tier.

**Runtime commands** (`core/management/commands/`): `lifecycle`, `tasks`, `followup`, `workspace`, `worktree`, `db`, `env`, `run`, `pr`, `ticket`, `tool`, `e2e`, `overlay`, `standup`, `signals`, `mr_reminder`, `checking`, `health`, `retro`, `loop_tick`, `settings_compare`, `generate_*_docs`. Each is a django-typer command group with subcommands. `db query` and `db shell` enforce read-only at two layers (leading-keyword filter + transaction `READ ONLY` / `query_only=ON`).

**Health readings carry their own age.** A verdict rendered without a measurement time
is indistinguishable from one taken seconds ago, so a condition that has since been
fixed goes on being relayed as current — the failure mode is the reporting, not the
subsystem. `t3 <overlay> health show` therefore stamps its header with the measurement
time and ages every open `KnownIssue` from the `first_seen`/`last_seen` already on the
row (`--json` carries both). `deploy/secret-decryption-probe.sh` applies the same rule
outside Django: a host-side, sub-`t3` check that certifies container secret decryption
per container and per invocation shape, stamping every line with both the measurement
time and that container's creation time, so a result quoted after a container was
recreated is self-evidently void. Neither surface caches; both are cheap enough to
re-run instead of quote.

**Retro enforcement tooling** (`t3 <overlay> retro review-findings`, [#1573](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1573)): the scaffold behind invariant 6 / §17.6. Fingerprints a PR's review comments, records the **supplied** A/B/C verdict (never auto-guessed), and files one deduped enforcement issue per class-C finding. The untrusted comment text is a leak vector, so its bare refs are neutralized and the rendered body banned-term scanned before filing — the `gh api` stdin path bypasses the PreToolUse publish gate, so this is the only guard.

**Gate-failure feedback loop** (`t3 <overlay> retro gate-failures`, [#2024](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2024)): the review-findings sibling whose input is a **gate firing on the agent's own output**. A teatree gate BLOCK lands in the session transcript as a `hook_blocking_error` attachment whose text leads with a `TEATREE GATE — <phrase>` marker. `eval.gate_failures.extract_gate_failures` reads the single transcript chokepoint, keys on the attachment type + marker (never `exitCode`), and reduces the gate identity to a bounded slug; `classify_gate_failure` routes each as `environmental` (a `hook_non_blocking_error` — missing plugin dir, hook-runner traceback) or `preventable` (anything agent-output-shaped, and any unknown BLOCK — fail toward an eval), and `--escalate` files one deduped enforcement issue per *recurring preventable* failure. Privacy by construction: `GateFailure` stores only the slug + session id, never the message/`stderr`/`command`. It lives in `teatree.eval` (a `core → eval` import would be a backwards tach edge; `retro` calls in on a forward edge). Auto-invoking it from the loop tick is orchestrator-only ([#837](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/837)), a separate change.

**Retro finding lane** (`t3 <overlay> retro finding`): what a retrospective persists a lesson WITH. Retro emits no memory file — a rule re-read only when an agent happens to load it is the vigilance that already failed — so a confirmed lesson is recorded as a VERIFIED, core-gap `ConsolidatedMemory` row (`loops.dream.retro_finding`) and handed to the SAME drain dreaming Pass 2 uses: queued on the umbrella host's pending ledger for the backlog sweep to fold into an existing host, and retired by `reconcile_batches` once that host merges. The dedup is over the rule text, so re-emitting a lesson whose fix is still open rides the existing gap. Nothing is re-implemented, so the banned-term / bare-reference withhold and the send-proxy leak gate apply unchanged. The umbrella write sits behind the same default-ON `dream_memory_promote` setting Pass 2 does: with it off the finding is recorded and its promotion DEFERRED onto the drain queue, never quietly spending an intent the operator withheld.

**Intake landscape survey** (`t3 <overlay> workspace landscape`, [#2541](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2541)): the read-only, gather-once half of ticket intake. Before the planner designs a plan, `teatree.core.intake.landscape.survey_landscape` composes three deterministic signals through narrow consumer-defined code-host Protocols (`PrLister`, `MergedPrLister`) — the local worktree state (dirty / unpushed checkouts, both failing *open* so in-flight work is never silently hidden), the operator's open PRs, and a per-issue `done|partial|superseded|open` disposition with a `close|merge|supersede|keep` action. The classification is a mechanical floor (a *merged* PR naming the issue ⇒ done/close — the resolved-but-open path; an *open* PR ⇒ partial/merge; otherwise open/keep); the planner agent refines the verdict from the prose. The `run_landscape` command renders a JSON `LandscapeReport` with a capped `fix_first` listing (five oldest open fixes and five idle author tickets, plus remainder counts and unread probes); a configured forge read failure raises `LandscapeForgeReadError` rather than returning a partial survey, while an absent code host yields a local-only survey with a warning. Distinct from §9's checking report (a mid-loop catch-up) — this is the pre-plan landscape the `/t3:ticket` step surfaces.

**Checking report** (`t3 <overlay> checking show`, #1529): a terse, read-only "what did I miss" catch-up for when the user checks in mid-loop. By default aggregates ALL configured overlays into one `AllOverlaysReport`; `--this-overlay` restores single-overlay scope. Each overlay has its own `checking_checkpoint_<overlay>.json` marker (atomic `tmp.replace` write, tolerant read); each marker advances independently AFTER gathering so a second run sees an empty window. Five groups: Merged / In-flight / Needs you / Metered / Remaining, every reference clickable, capped at 5. Needs you reads all four live kinds from `core.waiting.gather_waiting` once, oldest first, then failed agent runs excluding operator cancellations. Question items carry their row id, subject link, age and owner-only decision flag. Internal escalations are a separate report count and trailing recap line, not owner waits. The owner-card walk-through reads the complete waiting list beyond the report cap, records session-known waits, triages every card, and leaves owner-kept decisions pending for an authenticated Slack reply. Remaining is a live snapshot of PR steps, active pre-PR work, queues, holds, and per-item and total median-to-p80 ETAs; Total and Queue are meta rows outside the work-item cap and count; it never changes the window recap hash or DM. A merge-safe pass uses the newest recorded review head, which cannot be compared to the PR live head because PullRequest stores no head SHA and this report makes no forge calls; a later push without a new verdict may omit review steps. Completed-task durations use admission to the last real attempt end; merge uses CLEAR to audit; deploy uses audit to the first delivered transition. Each range names its source, sample count, and 24h or 7d window; sparse steps use the slowest measured median and p80 sources, and fewer than five samples across every source report unknown without a numeric default. Overlay-scoped items carry an `[overlay]` inline tag. A window start at/after `now` falls back to the 24h lookback; advance is monotonic. `--since` and `--no-advance` inspect without advancing. The needs-you group is overlay-extensible via `OverlayBase.get_checking_sources()` (default `[]`); core makes no live forge calls.

**Global CLI** (`cli/`): `t3 startoverlay`, `t3 agent`, `t3 capabilities`, `t3 info {,artifacts}`, `t3 sessions`, `t3 cost`, `t3 docs`, `t3 ui`, `t3 admin`, `t3 peer {list,up,down,status,open}`, `t3 settings compare`, `t3 ci ...`, `t3 review ...`, `t3 review-request ...`, `t3 tool ...`, `t3 config ...`, `t3 doctor ...`, `t3 update`, `t3 setup ...`, `t3 assess`, `t3 loop {start,stop,status,tick,slack-answer,claim-next,pause,resume,disable,enable,loop-state}`, `t3 mcp serve`, `t3 recover`, `t3 push`, `t3 fast-push`, `t3 overlay {install,uninstall,status}`. `t3 push` (`core/forge_push.py`) is the one supported push path from the worker container: it hands the forge token to git as env only, never passes `--no-verify`, and counts a branch pushed only once `git ls-remote` reads it back at the local tip. The mechanics of `t3 push`, `t3 fast-push`, `t3 admin` (the Django admin and the `/dash/` dashboard), `t3 settings compare`, `t3 peer`, `t3 cost`, `t3 info artifacts` and `t3 overlay install` live in [docs/blueprint/global-cli.md](docs/blueprint/global-cli.md).

**Attachment ingestion** (`t3 tool to-markdown <file>`, #1479): converts binary spec attachments (PDF, XLSX, DOCX, PPTX) to Markdown so the agent can read them as structured text. Wraps `markitdown` (`teatree.backends.markdown_conversion.MarkdownConverter`) behind the **optional** `markdown` extra (`markitdown[pdf,docx,xlsx,pptx]` — never `[all]`); absent the extra the command exits non-zero with an install hint rather than crashing. Plugins are disabled and no LLM client is wired — converted output is treated as untrusted data and emitted verbatim.

**Child work items** (`t3 <overlay> ticket create-sub --parent <url> --title <…> [--type Task|Incident|Issue]`): the sibling of `ticket comment`, resolving the code host per-URL across overlays and delegating to `CodeHostBackend.create_sub_issue`. On GitLab this folds the three error-prone hops into one — REST create, `workItemConvert` to the child type (an Issue→Issue parent link is forbidden, so the default `Task` is the natural sub-item), then `workItemUpdate hierarchyWidget.parentId` to nest it — returning the child IID + URL for chaining. GitHub child work items use a different API and are unsupported.

**Structured-search MCP server** (`t3 mcp serve`, [#1023](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1023)): teatree exposes its sole allowed MCP server; Slack, GitLab, Notion and other services are tool groups on that server, not separate MCP servers. A stdio server built on the official `mcp` SDK's `MCPServer` (`mcp.server.mcpserver`), so an agent reads teatree's internal model as typed tool calls instead of shelling out to `t3 … list` and parsing text. The `teatree.mcp` package separates the three concerns: `serializers.py` (pure model→JSON-dict), `search.py` (synchronous ORM queries that **reuse the model managers** — `Ticket.objects.for_overlay`/`in_flight`/`resolve`, `Worktree.objects.active`, `IncomingEvent.objects.unprocessed` — never re-deriving query logic), and `server.py` (`build_server()` registering each query as an async tool that crosses the ORM boundary through `sync_to_async`). Fourteen always-registered **read-only** tools (`ToolAnnotations(read_only_hint=True)`, the `_READ_TOOLS` table in `server.py`): `command_search` (which `t3` leaf to run), `ticket_get`, `ticket_list`, `ticket_search` (overlay/state/kind/role/text/in-flight), `worktree_status` (by ticket ref or active overlay set), `pr_for_ticket`, `task_list`, `question_list` (pending owner-audience cards, oldest first, 50 per page with `offset`; includes decision and owner-only flag; an owner-audience read; the internal rows `question_answer` can settle stay on `questions list`), `loop_stats` (task counts by status + dead-letter total), `incoming_event_recent`, `config_setting_get`, `gate_status`, `factory_signals` (the derived-on-read factory quality/velocity report, §Factory signals below), and `factory_score` (the recipe-weighted score) — plus the per-service read groups (below), and the write suite. The read-only tools were the first version; the write tools below ([#3076](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3076)) add mutation without weakening the §17.4 guarantee, because each write tool routes through the exact gate-carrying seam the CLI calls rather than the transport directly.

**Owner review waits** (`core/review/owner_waits.py`, `loop/question_drain.py`): live review posts refused by the per-token gate record durable owner cards. A substrate-held reviewed pull request records no card: `ping_substrate_hold` sends one idempotent DM per reviewed head, and the pull request stays in the waiting digest as a merge authorization, because a substrate CLEAR with no human authorizer does not count as coverage (`waiting._has_covering_clear`). A unified-authorization refusal returns before that gate and is recorded during an interactive check-in when known to the session. A live-post card is recorded after an on-behalf rollback and retires after verified approval or when its recorded PR settles. The question list reads owner-audience cards oldest first; internal escalations remain factory work in checking reports.

**Per-service declared tool groups** ([#3076](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3076)): the MCP server also wraps *third-party services* — but only the ones a registered overlay declares. `Service(StrEnum)` (`backends/types.py`: github/gitlab/slack/notion/sentry/sharepoint) is the closed vocabulary; each overlay declares its needs on `OverlayConfig.required_third_party_services` (a `frozenset[Service]` Pydantic field — code default in the overlay's `overlay_settings.py`, DB-overridable via the `overlays` registry row, validated fail-loud against the enum). `build_server()` computes the UNION across all registered overlays (`_required_services()`) and registers a service's tool group iff its `Service` is in the union — no declaration ⇒ no tools (fail-closed), and the standing server instructions carry policy only (a short preamble, the mailbox block when the child is runner-bound, each admitted overlay group's own block): every built-in and per-service tool describes itself in its tool description, so an unregistered tool is advertised nowhere. Resolved once at server build; an overlay or override change needs an MCP restart. Six service groups ship (reads, plus the gated writes below on the forge, slack and notion groups): **github**/**gitlab** (`mcp/services_forge.py`, one parametrized registrar since both satisfy `CodeHostBackend` — `current_user`/`my_prs`/`review_requested`/`pr_author`/`pr_comments`/`issue`/`issue_comments`, plus the wave-1 reads `issue_search` (up to ten linked open-issue summaries with number, title, url, state and updated_at, plus `truncated` when more matches exist; narrow the query then)/`issue_list_assigned`/`my_merged_prs`/`pr_get` (a one-read PR snapshot composing open/merge/draft state, author, and approvals), all riding existing `CodeHostBackend` methods — no protocol change; the wave-2 reads `pr_list`/`pr_diff`/`pr_commits`/`repo_get`, which DO add four `CodeHostBackend` methods (`list_prs`/`get_pr_diff`/`list_pr_commits`/`get_repo`) implemented on both concrete hosts, an unknown repo/PR degrading to a caught structured error rather than crashing the server; and the wave-2 **issue WRITES** `issue_create`/`issue_note`/`issue_close`/`issue_update` on the pre-existing `create_issue`/`post_issue_comment`/`close_issue`/`update_issue` `CodeHostBackend` methods — `issue_note` wraps `core.issue_hygiene.record_issue_note`, so a requirement lands in the description and only `status`/`evidence` stay comments, and `issue_create` wraps `create_or_extend` as a TWO-phase call whose first leg writes nothing and hands back the closest 20 open tickets, the total open count and the compared count (§ Issue hygiene) — each routing the outbound title/body through the public-repo leak scrub (`gates/privacy_gate.scan_outbound_text` — a customer codename bound for a public forge is REFUSED before the backend call) + the #117 send-proxy chokepoint, so no leaking or non-allowlisted write reaches the forge), **slack** (`mcp/services_slack.py` — `mentions`/`channel_history`/`thread_replies`/`permalink`, plus the gated `react` reaction write routed through `OnBehalfSlackEgress` — a self-DM reaction is ungated, a colleague/channel reaction runs the #117 send-proxy + on-behalf approval gate + notify receipt and is refused without a recorded approval), **notion** (`mcp/services_notion.py` — `page_status`, the `discussions` read, and the approval-gated `replace`/`create`/`comment`/`property_set`/`archive` writes), the **sentry** pilot (`mcp/services_sentry.py`), and the read-only **sharepoint** document-library connector (`mcp/services_sharepoint.py` — `sharepoint_list`/`sharepoint_cat`/`sharepoint_verify_link`/`sharepoint_verify_read_only`; an rclone-backed OneDrive/SharePoint `documentLibrary` remote whose encrypted `rclone.conf` is unlocked non-interactively from `pass`, with read-only enforced at the OAuth-scope level — `access_scopes` limited to `Files.Read*`/`Sites.Read.All` so a write returns HTTP 403 — and `verify_read_only()` asserting that contract; calendar/mail integration is deferred pending the owner's scope/consent decision per #3084). Every group resolves its client through a `core/backend_factory` seam (`code_host_from_overlay`/`messaging_from_overlay`/`notion_client_from_overlay`/`sentry_client_from_overlay`/`sharepoint_client_from_overlay`), never a direct `backends.{github,gitlab,slack,sentry,sharepoint}` import — the transport-boundary fitness test (`tests/teatree_mcp/test_transport_boundary.py`) enforces that.

**Live agent mailbox** (`src/teatree/agents/live_mailbox.py`, `src/teatree/mcp/agent_mailbox.py`):
five tools appear in a Claude task's TeaTree MCP child; pydantic_ai mounts peers, send, inbox and wait natively beside its other toolsets. New Codex threads expose up to five dynamic mailbox tools by phase (App Server `dynamicTools`) through a thread-scoped worker handler; resumed Codex threads retain their creation-time tool catalog and receive pushes without a claimed inbox copy. The runner assigns an unguessable
identity and ticket metadata, passes only that task's token to its child, and revokes the identity
on exit. The plugin's `t3 mcp serve --interactive` joins as an operator-authenticated attended peer with no ticket metadata; its held socket connection revokes the identity on disconnect. With no live worker, the plugin keeps its other MCP tools and omits mailbox tools. Headless worker descendants cannot join: the check uses same-uid credentials and process ancestry, with the container as the boundary. Agents can discover live peers across tickets and same-host workers, send to one peer, and
read or briefly wait for their own inbox. Headless agents cannot register themselves or leave a message for a parked task. An idle
`claude_sdk` peer can start one new turn during its bounded window; a parked task
is never woken by a live message.
Live provider pairing proof is still owed and will be attached to the umbrella issue after a credentialed run.

Mailbox messaging is not a repository write. `agent_mailbox_self` and `agent_mailbox_peers`
are read tools for every phase that mounts this server. `agent_mailbox_send`,
`agent_mailbox_inbox` and `agent_mailbox_wait` stay annotated non-read-only because they
change delivery state. Planning, reviewing and critic_reviewing allow exactly those three
by name on their read-only server; other read-only phases retain only self and peers.
The broker queues peer text framed as information, never a tool call on the sender’s behalf.

The worker publishes one private Unix socket in `<control_db_dir>/live/`. Each live command carries
the target session's per-drive attempt token and worker generation; a stale fence is refused before
delivery. Worker-to-worker `relay.*`
calls verify the socket and owner record, kernel peer PID, process start time, and live worker
command before trusting discovery or forwarding text. A PID hidden by a namespace fails closed. The
receiver also verifies the sender through its source broker and per-message proof. Discovery removes
abandoned sockets before applying the 32-worker limit; peer listing skips other failed reads with a
warning, while a send fails if it cannot find a target after incomplete discovery. The same socket's
`live.*` operator verbs retain the caller checks described in §5 and implemented by
`src/teatree/agents/live_ingress.py`.

A new Codex task without an MCP child retains an inbox in the worker; the worker binds the existing mailbox identity to the Codex thread and handles `item/tool/call` without mounting a broker socket in the read-only executor. A resumed Codex thread keeps its original tool catalog, so its mailbox is push-only and its receipt never claims an inbox copy. Unknown threads are refused. A send waits at most 20 seconds for current-turn push (`accepted_current_turn`, §5),
new-turn acceptance by an idle Claude peer (`accepted_new_turn`), or an
acknowledged MCP inbox read (`accepted_inbox_read`). Push text is framed as peer information, not an
operator instruction. A refusal or uncertain push returns a typed receipt with the original code;
every receipt says whether the text remains in the inbox. The first acceptance wins, same-key
retries wait on the original message, and at most four sends per task wait concurrently. Acceptance
proves the session took the message, never that the model obeyed it. No mailbox table, reconnect
token, cross-host route, or external messaging transport exists. A participant registered without a delivery path returns `recipient_cannot_receive`; a runner-bound pydantic_ai task has a native binding and is receivable.

**Overlay-contributed tool groups**: a registered overlay adds its own tools through `overlay.connectors.mcp_tool_group()`, which returns an `McpToolGroup` (`core/mcp_tool_group.py`) — the `McpTool`s themselves, not a registrar callback, so the server can judge each one before it reaches an agent. `build_server()` refuses a group whose `requires` names a `Service` no overlay declared (the per-service fail-closed contract, applied to the overlay path) and a group carrying a non-read-only tool with no declared `seam` (an ungated mutation is the one thing this surface must never carry); both refusals are whole-group, so the instructions never advertise a tool that did not register. A `mcp_tool_group()` that RAISES costs only that overlay its tools — one contributor's missing credential does not take the server down for the others. Registration itself runs through `_NoShadowServer`, whose `add_tool` refuses a name already given out rather than letting mcp's `ToolManager` keep the incumbent and drop the newcomer with a log line.
