Its `call_tool` preserves the SDK's unexpected-crash type and traceback while returning the exception class and a message redacted with `redact_secrets` and capped at 500 characters; `ToolError`, `ResourceError` and `MCPError` keep their SDK behavior.

**Registration + consumption** ([#2863](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2863)): the server is registered as a **plugin-bundled** MCP server — a `.mcp.json` at the repo root (sibling of `.claude-plugin/`), the convention official Claude Code plugins use, so it starts automatically once `t3 setup` enables the plugin, with no separate `claude mcp add` step. `teatree.core.mcp_registration.verify_teatree_mcp_registration` is the single chokepoint that confirms `.mcp.json` still declares the server correctly — `t3 setup` runs it as a read-only, idempotent confirmation step, and `t3 doctor check` (`_check_teatree_mcp_registration`) re-verifies it, and `_check_teatree_mcp_liveness` exercises the registered `t3 mcp serve` with a real `initialize`. It is the only MCP server teatree depends on: every third-party integration is one of its service tool groups. `/t3:ship` § 4b step 5 is the first skill call-site consuming a tool (`ticket_search`) instead of shelling `t3 <overlay> ticket list` and parsing text.

**Factory signals** (`core/factory/`, `t3 <overlay> signals`, MCP `factory_signals`): derived-on-read quality/velocity signals over the merge/review/CI/repair ledgers already recorded — mirrors `core/standup.py`'s read-only-aggregator doctrine (no new models, no migrations, no LLM calls). Each signal is a **provider-shaped** function returning `SignalReading(value, sample_size, window_days, status)` with `status ∈ {ok, insufficient_data, instrumentation_gap}` — fail-loud, never fabricating a green (sample `< MIN_SAMPLE=5` → `insufficient_data`; a provably-silent upstream recorder → `instrumentation_gap`, never 100%). The signals: **S1 first_try_green** (`MergeAudit` merges with ZERO `RedMrFixAttempt` rows, joined via `core/merge/pr_slug_resolution.normalize_repo_slug` + `pr_ref_from_url`; a dead `my_prs` recorder → `instrumentation_gap`, plus the `re_ci_count` companion); **S2 defect_escape** (fix-kind tickets + `RedCardSignal` rows in the window over the PRECEDING window's merges — window-level, not per-PR); **S3 review_catch** (fraction of merged PRs whose `ReviewVerdict`/`for_pr` history holds or surfaces a blocker/major `Finding`; ≥5 merges at catch-rate 0 → hard RED "vacuous review lane" — the rubber-stamp detector); **S4 merge_latency** (median `MergeClear.issued_at`→`MergeAudit.merged_at` exact FK join + the stale-actionable-CLEAR age, RED above 48h even in a zero-merge window); **S5 repair_burn** (mean terminal `TaskAttempt.iteration` per succeeded `(ticket, phase)` + the failed-attempt fraction); **S6 pr_start_to_merged** (median earliest-current-admission-to-merge hours, the start never later than the PR's creation, RED at a 48h median, plus the age of the oldest open pull request, RED at 72h even without a merged sample). `compute_factory_signals(window_days=28)` composes the readings against the immediately-preceding window as a rolling baseline into a `FactorySignalsReport` (per-signal red-floor `tripped` + rolling-baseline `regressing` verdicts, one top-level verdict ∈ {ok, regressing, red}); starving a signal can only lower the score — an `instrumentation_gap` is RED, never a free pass. The providers are a star-shaped `SIGNALS` registry the recipe (`evals/recipe.yaml`, §Factory score below) names verbatim; the module produces signal only — no gate, no merge lockout. A parallel `VISIBILITY_SIGNALS` registry carries readings that are REPORTED but never graded — presently **net_hand_written_loc** (`quality/hand_written_loc.window_loc`: the hand-written lines the tree gained between the window's boundary commits, generated paths excluded by `git check-attr merge` so `.gitattributes` stays the single source). Its rows ride in `FactorySignalsReport.signals` but sit outside `_REGISTRY_IDS` (no recipe weight, no score contribution) and outside `_aggregate_verdict` (a git read can never redden the operator's alarm) — the owner ruled the LoC leg advisory, and a grading dashboard row would be the rejected gate under another name. The same module backs the advisory `net-loc-report` CI lane (`scripts/ci/loc_ratchet.py`), so the pipeline number and the dashboard number cannot disagree.

**External outcomes** (`core/factory/external_outcomes.py`, `ExternalOutcomeSnapshot`, the `external_output_vs_internal_success` + `merged_without_verdict` reconciliation checks — [#4506](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4506)): the counterweight to every signal above. §Factory signals and §Factory score are both derived from teatree's OWN ledgers, so every one can read healthy through a window in which nothing reached `main` — tasks reporting `completed`, phases visited and tickets advanced are bookkeeping, not evidence that anything shipped. `read_external_outcomes(Forge)` reads the one number the factory cannot write for itself: the merged-PR count the FORGE reports for the overlay's own repos (`core/overlay_repos.owned_repo_slugs` — the default issue-intake scope, which `issue_intake_repos` may narrow) over a trailing 7d window, through the `CodeHostBackend.list_merged_prs_since` Protocol method both hosts implement. Three states are kept apart on purpose: a completed read is a number; `NO_FORGE` (no host, no declared repos) is UNMEASURED; and a read that FAILS raises `ExternalOutcomeReadError` — downstream an empty list IS the alarm condition, so a rate-limited read degrading to "zero merges" would manufacture the very finding the measure exists to raise honestly. `refresh_if_stale` records each completed read as one `ExternalOutcomeSnapshot` and serves any snapshot younger than `EXTERNAL_OUTCOME_TTL` (6h) without touching the network, so the watchdog's many daily `t3 doctor` runs cost at most four forge reads. The two disagreement checks live on the §Reconciliation ledger — unflagged, running on every `t3 doctor`, surfacing-only, DM'd once per day — and each names BOTH numbers: `external_output_vs_internal_success` alarms on ≥`MIN_INTERNAL_SUCCESSES_FOR_OUTCOME` internal SUCCESS attempts against zero forge merges, and `merged_without_verdict` takes its DENOMINATOR from the forge (which PRs actually merged) against teatree's own numerator (which carry a `ReviewVerdict`), so no amount of internal success can satisfy it. An unmeasured or failed read is `degraded` — surfaced, never DM'd, and never scored healthy.

**Factory signals and score.** `core/factory/` computes a read-only recipe-weighted report over the `SIGNALS` seam (`compute_factory_signals` and `SignalReading`/`SignalRow`). The committed `evals/recipe.yaml` declares the signal weights and normalisation. `t3 <overlay> recipe score` computes it read-only; `--record` persists one `FactoryScoreSnapshot` and queues one approval question per unapproved recipe sha, and the MCP `factory_score` tool reads it. Nothing records a score per tick.

**Autoresearch outer loop** (`core/models/outer_loop_experiment.py` + `loops/outer_loop/`, `t3 outer status|propose|history|tick|resolve-keep|resolve-revert` — T4-PR-3): the self-improving runtime that turns the factory score into an optimisation target. It is a two-loop frame — the INNER maker pipeline builds one ticket into a merged PR; the OUTER loop advances at most one `OuterLoopExperiment` one FSM step per tick through **propose → ratify → implement → measure → keep-only-if-better**, consuming `factory_score.score()` through `loops/shared/score.py:read_score` as its measure. Implement anchors a synthetic-issue `Ticket` and routes it through `Ticket.schedule_implementing()`, so an experiment's PR merges through the SAME critic-gated keystone every ticket uses; the outer loop gains ZERO new merge authority. The keep rule is pure and anti-Goodhart (`loops/outer_loop/decide.py`): KEEP iff the target signal improved beyond its own `regress_band` AND nothing else regressed vs the admission baseline. `admit()` RAISES unless a consumed ratify `DeferredQuestion` exists, and a `KEEP_PENDING` experiment reaches `KEPT` only through a consumed keep question; both are internal rows the owner's standing answer settles by policy (`approve`, `kept`, each audited), while a `REVERT_PENDING` experiment needs the operator's git revert plus `t3 outer resolve-revert <id>`, which frees the single experiment slot. It runs wherever the active preset admits `outer_loop` (present and afk do), off the live tick: `off_live_tick=True` keeps it off the live fan-out and the worker's `drive_off_live_tick_loops` chain fires `t3 outer tick`. Its guards are code, shared with the directive loop (`loops/shared/guards.py`): signal trust refuses while any factory signal reports `instrumentation_gap`; the budget bounds the run (`precheck_budget`, one active experiment, one proposal per week, and a brake that parks after three consecutive non-kept decisions — code constants in `loops/outer_loop/guards.py`). The ledger row binds hypothesis → shas → both score snapshots → the human decisions, and `t3 outer status`/`history` name the refusal reason. **Known weakest link:** MEASURE is a time+merge-count window, NOT causal attribution, so a KEPT decision is "correlated better", not "proven caused"; the no-regression-anywhere rule and the human-ratified revert bound the risk.

**Overlay contract check** (`t3 overlay contract-check --compose <paths>`) reads every `${VAR}` reference in compose files and fails if any is neither defaulted nor declared by core (`_declared_core_keys()`) or the active overlay (`overlay.provisioning.declared_env_keys()`).

**Teatree source resolution in overlays.** `[tool.uv.sources] teatree = { path = "../../souliane/teatree", editable = true }` is the committed default — no SHA pinning, no mode switching. CI clones teatree at the same relative path before `uv sync`. Local dev uses whatever is checked out locally.

**Machine interface — `--json` / stable exit codes ([PR-30](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2565)).** `t3` is a machine interface a front-end (Pi, a CI runner, another agent) drives by shelling to `t3 … --json` and parsing stdout. The contract: under `--json` stdout is a **pure JSON channel** (valid `json.loads`, zero human bytes); the human view (tables, banners, diagnostics) always goes to **stderr**. `teatree.core.machine_output.emit(payload, *, json_output, out, err, human)` is the single seam every record/query command routes through — it serializes the typed return via `json.dumps` on stdout under `--json` and renders the human view on stderr otherwise. A converted command sets `print_result = False` so django-typer does not additionally repr the return onto stdout, while `call_command` consumers still receive the typed object (the same typed return PR-18's `print_table` renderer consumes — one seam, not two conversions). **Exit codes:** subcommands raise `SystemExit(N)` (never `typer.Exit`, swallowed under `call_command`); the overlay bridge (`cli/overlay.py` `managepy`/`managepy_core`) catches `CommandFailedError` and re-raises `SystemExit(returncode)`, so a bridged child's exit code propagates faithfully with no traceback instead of collapsing to a stack-trace exit 1. `t3 capabilities --json` publishes the machine-readable capability registry — each command's `--json` support and exit-code contract — so a front-end discovers the interface instead of scraping `--help` (the registry, not BLUEPRINT prose, is the SSOT for the converted-leaf set: `queue status`, `tasks list`/`create`, `followup sync`, `workspace emit`, `worktree status`/`diagnose`, `questions list`, plus the pre-existing JSON commands). **Every** command that declares `--json` routes through the `emit` seam — the earlier "returns a human string, both channels on stdout" precedent is retired, so a `--json` command's stdout is JSON or nothing at all and its human view is always stderr. `MachineOutputCommand` is the base a converted command subclasses: `BaseCommand.execute` swaps in Django's own `OutputWrapper` whenever a stream is passed as `call_command(…, stdout=buf)`, and that wrapper has no `disable` flag, so `print_result = False` would silently stop working and the typed return would land on stdout *after* `emit` wrote the JSON there; the base installs django-typer's disable-capable wrapper so the pin holds on every call path. The rollout is incomplete and ratcheted: `teatree.quality.machine_output_seam` statically detects the two bypass shapes (a `--json` flag not routed through `emit`; a structured return with no `print_result = False`) and `tests/quality/test_machine_output_seam.py` pins the remaining unconverted handlers in a shrink-only ledger, so a NEW bypass turns CI red.

**Golden-path lifecycle wrapper — `t3 <overlay> do <ticket-ref>` ([PR-31](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2565)).** One command walks a ticket through the lifecycle (intake → provision → plan → code → test → review → ship) so a front-end (or a human) never has to remember the sequence. It is a **thin** front-end-seam wrapper over the EXISTING chokepoints — it SEQUENCES and REPORTS, it adds no gate and never touches the FSM directly. The step order and the `Ticket.state`→status mapping live in `teatree.core.lifecycle_pipeline` (`PIPELINE` + `drive`, kept pure and side-effect-free against injected `DriveSeams`). Two step kinds split the walk: `auto` steps (intake=`workspace ticket`, provision=`workspace provision`, ship=`pr create`) are invoked in-process via `call_command` (child streams redirected to stderr so `do`'s stdout stays a pure JSON channel); `agent` steps (plan/code/test/review) are reported `pending` and the walk stops — dispatching the phase agent is the loop's job (§5.2), not this wrapper's. **Resumable + idempotent:** it re-reads `Ticket.state` each step and resumes at the live phase, never redoing a completed one; a gate-refused auto step surfaces the blocker and stops. `--json` emits the per-step typed status through the `emit` seam (steps array + `stopped_at`/`stopped_reason`); `--plan` prints the sequence with no side effects. **Exit codes:** `0` = progress/pending/done, `1` = a gate blocked an auto step (or the ticket is `ignored`).

---

## 9. Code Host Sync

`teatree.core.sync.sync_followup() → SyncResult` is platform-agnostic. Per-overlay it resolves the overlay's `CodeHostBackend`, fetches open PRs authored by the current user (incremental via cached `updated_after`), upserts tickets by `issue_url` (or PR URL if no issue linked), enriches non-draft PRs with pipeline + approvals + review threads, infers ticket state from PR data (`infer_state_from_prs()` advances forward only, never regresses — and a post-ship inferred state is routed through the shared DoD decision per § "Sync writers honor the same gate"), and detects merged PRs. It also re-checks the user's OWN open authored PRs for merge conflicts every sweep — a non-incremental fetch (always `updated_after=None`, so a re-conflict that did not bump the PR's timestamp as `main` advances is still seen) whose conflicted PRs land in `SyncResult.conflicted_mrs` (the overlay-agnostic `ConflictedMR` shape: iid, repo, web_url, title). The GitLab backend reads `has_conflicts` / `merge_status == cannot_be_merged` / `detailed_merge_status == conflict`; `followup sync` surfaces them in a loud WARNING block ([#78](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/78)). Detection only — never an auto-rebase or auto-push.

| PR data | Inferred state |
|---|---|
| Draft | `work_started` |
| Non-draft | `pr_opened` |
| Non-draft + review-requested or approvals > 0 | `review_requested` |

Review threads are classified `waiting_reviewer` / `needs_reply` / `addressed`. Draft notes (GitLab) / pending reviews (GitHub) surface as a statusline `review_draft` prompt to publish.

Posting discipline (#1207): `t3 review post-comment` defaults to creating a DRAFT and DMs the user the link; the colleague-visible `--live` path is gated on a single-use, MR-URL-scoped `LivePostApproval` minted by `t3 review approve-live-post <mr-url> --slack-ts <ts>` after the Slack DM at that timestamp is verified (from the user, recent within 15 min, contains an explicit approval phrase). The historical immediate-post default is retired; CLI enforces draft-by-default rather than relying on prose discipline.

One-step authorization (#126): `t3 review authorize <repo>!<mr> --approver <id>` collapses the former two-command on-behalf dance — it records the durable `OnBehalfApproval` AND mints the matching single-use `LivePostApproval` in one call. The `post-comment --live` path consults one consolidated read-only decision (`review_authorize.resolve_live_authorization`, returning a `LiveAuthorization` that carries BOTH whether the post may proceed and whether it must still burn the live token, so the two gates cannot declare a post authorized and refuse it one call later). The token waiver is scoped to the POSTURE, not to the verdict: only a posture that permits egress — the owner's global opt-in to autonomous posting, read via `on_behalf_gate_recorded.owner_voice_permitted` through the same fail-closed seam as the verdict — waives it. A PROCEED reached any other way still burns the token, so naming `post_comment` in the `on_behalf_auto_actions` self-documentation allowlist authorises the post without retiring the #1207 seal on a colleague-visible publish.; the two original commands remain for the Slack-ts verification path.

Batch posting (`review.batch_post`): a review is N findings, and the authorization ceremony is per-REVIEW. `ReviewService.post_comments` (MCP `review_post_comments`) runs the full pre-publish gate chain on every body independently, then opens ONE `publish_or_blocked` envelope around all of them — one recorded approval covers the review. A body a gate refuses refuses the whole batch naming its position; a comment that fails mid-publish stops and reports how many landed (forge posts are not transactional, so the approval consume rolls back and survives for a retry of the remainder).

Published-note removal: `t3 review delete-discussion <repo> <mr> <note_id>` removes a published MR note; `t3 review delete-issue-note <repo> <issue_iid> <note_id>` is the issue/work-item twin (`DELETE /issues/{iid}/notes/{id}`). Both are colleague-visible on-behalf mutations routed through the recorded-approval gate (#960) — the issue path scopes the approval to `<repo>#<issue>` (distinct from the MR `<repo>!<mr>` scope, via `issue_gate_target`) and verifies the delete took (#2081, `verify_issue_note_deleted`). These CLIs are the only sanctioned path: the `block-raw-review-post` hook (#1164) hard-denies a raw `glab api`/`gh api` DELETE to `…/issues/<iid>/notes/<id>`.

Note CREATION splits by surface, and the hook's refusal names the CLI that can address the one the caller hit. A merge-request note is `t3 review post-comment` (draft-default, dedup, on-behalf approval). An issue/work-item note is `t3 <overlay> ticket comment <issue-url> --purpose <purpose>`, which since #162 decides WHERE the text goes before it decides how to scrub it (§ Issue hygiene): the `t3 review` create seam is merge-request-shaped — `post-comment` is keyed on an integer MR IID, and its draft-default rests on a draft-notes API the forge exposes only under `merge_requests`, so there is no `issues/<iid>/draft_notes` to mirror. `ticket comment` reaches the same `POST /issues/{iid}/notes` endpoint — for a `status`/`evidence` purpose — through the shared scanned forge-write seam (`route_forge_write` — the public-repo leak gate + the #117 send-proxy audit/allowlist/redaction that `chokepoints.yaml`'s `forge-comment-write-seam` mandates for every `post_issue_comment`), which is what the hook's `issues` arm exists to keep a raw REST write from skipping. A normative purpose never reaches that endpoint at all.

Verified-delivery notify wrapper ([#1181](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1181)): `teatree.messaging.notify_with_fallback` tries `notify_user` and, on a transport `FAILED`, falls back to a round-trip-verified backend send (`BotPing.transport` records the path). Loop/CLI sites route through the wrapper; `teatree.core` callers stay on `notify_user` (no `core → messaging` edge). A `NOOP`/`FAILED` INFO `BotPing` is durably parked, and the always-on `undelivered_notify` scanner re-attempts parked rows each tick under their `idempotency_key`; the drain is bounded ([#2064](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2064)) — a row past `MAX_REDELIVERY_ATTEMPTS` or 72h is `EXPIRED`, so the backlog never grinds and stale DMs never surface late.

Named non-delivery + ambient egress resolution: `notify_user_outcome` is the egress and returns a `NotifyOutcome` carrying a `NotifyReason` (`internal_audience`, `routed_to_pull`, `already_sent`, `ledger_unavailable`, `no_messaging_backend`, `ambiguous_overlay`, `no_user_id`, `claimed_by_concurrent_tick`, `delivery_failed`); `notify_user` is its boolean face. A bare `False` is banned by construction — nobody watches the CLI turn a notification fires in, so an unexplained non-delivery reaches no one, and the two halves of the old conflated "no messaging backend or user_id" reason have different fixes. Every drop is logged at ERROR and recorded on its `BotPing` row. Resolution is ambient: the headless worker, the loop, and the MCP server export no `T3_OVERLAY_NAME`, so `messaging_from_overlay()` falls back to the SOLE registered overlay carrying a real messaging transport (`OwnerMessagingTransport.sole`, the credential-keyed peer of `notify_targets._sole_overlay_field`) instead of collapsing `Multiple overlays found` into "no backend". Two credentialed overlays stay ambiguous and resolve to `None`, which the egress names `ambiguous_overlay` — the box must never guess which workspace the owner reads. The Slack round-trip doctor gate probes that ambient seam directly and reads the `BotPing` ledger back, so a per-overlay config check can no longer read as proof that notifications deliver.

**Push vs pull — the owner DM carries decisions only** ([#4524](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4524)): a DM interrupts a person, so it is the right vehicle for one class of message — something needs the owner's decision and nothing proceeds without it. `NotifyAudience` (§ above) answers WHO a notification is for; `teatree.core.modelkit.dm_channel_policy` answers whether that person should be INTERRUPTED for it, and it is deny-by-default: an `OWNER_DELIVERY`/`OWNER_ESCALATION` notification reaches Slack only if one of its idempotency key's colon-delimited prefixes is in the closed `PUSH_SIGNALS` allowlist, so `watchdog:compose-up-failed` pages while `watchdog:red` does not. `OWNER_QUESTION`/`COLLEAGUE_ACTION` push on their audience alone. An owner question is only ever a `DeferredQuestion` recorded with one of the `OwnerDecision` kinds and the facts checked first; `notify send`/`dm` and the MCP `notify_user` refuse `kind=question` with that route. A new alarm therefore cannot reach the DM by being *written* — only by being *registered*, which is the conversation the gate exists to force. Enforced at the `notify_user_outcome` chokepoint beside the `INTERNAL` short-circuit, so every caller inherits it, including the shell ones arriving through `t3 <overlay> notify send`; `notify_with_fallback` stands down on the OUTCOME's `ROUTED_TO_PULL` reason rather than on the audit row, which would fall open exactly when the ledger write was lost. Nothing is discarded: the signal lands as a terminal `BotPing.Status.PULLED` row keeping its text, key and audience verbatim (excluded from `_RECOVERABLE`, so the drain never resurrects it onto the DM channel), and `t3 <overlay> notify digest` is the pulled surface — grouped by signal with counts. The `self-improve:still-open` repair notice follows this pull lane. `t3 <overlay> notify census --days 8` counts bot messages by UTC day and class from the owner DM channel history, including thread replies. `notify send` exits 0 on a withheld signal, because a non-zero exit reads as a transport failure to `deploy/watchdog.sh`, which parks such a page and re-sends it every pass.

Tabular DMs ([PR-18](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1777)): `teatree.backends.slack.table_format` is the pure formatter turning headers + rows + per-column alignment into a native Block Kit `table` block (`rich_text` cells, alignment via `column_settings`, capped 100 rows total incl. the header / 20 cols) plus a space-aligned monospace fence fallback (`slack_table_fence`; over-budget cells ellipsis-truncated widest-column-first, never wrapped, rows capped to match the block with an `… and N more` trailer, empty → `(no rows)`). `notify_user` takes an opaque `blocks` argument threaded to `post_message` so a bot→user DM posts the block with the fence as the `text` degradation; the blocks stay opaque JSON so `teatree.core` never imports the Slack backend (no `core → backends.slack` edge). The CLI twin is `teatree.core.table_output.print_table` (§ "Machine interface"), so both surfaces render tabular data through one seam each; the `slack-formatting` reference skill (an overlay companion) documents the formatter and the mrkdwn gotchas.

Line width ([#3809](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3809)): every outbound message body is wrapped to at most 90 characters per line, so it stays readable on a phone and in a narrow DM column. The rule is enforced at the TRANSPORT, not at a composition seam — `normalize_slack_message` is the documented formatting seam but most senders (`speak`, `slack_answer`, `self_improve`, `notify_with_fallback`, the on-behalf egress) post without it. Three wire calls carry a body and each wraps at its own egress: `SlackBotBackend._post` applies `teatree.slack_mrkdwn.wrap_slack_message` to every `chat.postMessage` body — the hook process's own question mirror is gone, its Slack delivery now goes out through this same transport via `notify_user` ([#4673](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4673)) — `audio_upload.upload_audio_dm` wraps the audio DM's `initial_comment` (the `speak.slack` path returns that body from `deliver_user_dm` and never reaches `chat.postMessage` at all), and `client.post_webhook_message` wraps its raw `httpx` body. A new sender therefore inherits the rule by construction. Structural carve-outs are never broken and never counted as violations: fenced code, inline code, mrkdwn links, bare URLs, table rows, and any single whitespace-free token wider than 90; Block Kit `blocks` are never rewritten because Block Kit owns its own layout. The transform only breaks at existing whitespace and never rejoins, which makes it idempotent and returns a body it need not break byte for byte. The one call-site escape is a `wrap_exempt_reason` string — reviewable in a diff, and `tests/quality/test_slack_wrap_chokepoint.py` refuses a blank or computed one and turns red if any new module reaches a text-bearing Slack method, or opens a raw `httpx` call inside the Slack backend, outside the wrap path. The publish surface itself (`publish` / `add_reaction` / their routed twins) lives in `teatree.backends.slack.egress`, keeping `bot.py` under the module-health LOC cap.

Review-shape audit (#1206): `t3 review run <MR_URL>` is the read-only entry point reviewer sub-agents call before scanning a diff. It fetches MR metadata, classifies complexity, counts existing-review state (open discussions + draft notes + approvals), and emits a structured JSON summary so every reviewer starts from the same shape rather than improvising. The command never publishes — it stays outside the on-behalf surface. A merged/closed MR (live `state` field from `/changes`) short-circuits to a `skipped_merged` / `skipped_closed` verdict (#2081) — a note can never land on one, so a mid-flight close aborts the review. Both forges are served: GitLab through `teatree.cli.review.run`, GitHub PRs through `teatree.cli.review.run_github` over the existing `GitHubCodeHost` (same payload shape, same classifier, same skip verdicts — an `UNKNOWN` live state is a failed read that surfaces `api_unavailable`, never an audit reported as open). A URL naming neither forge exits 2 with `bad_url`.

Structured-evidence gate (#1280): `t3 review post-comment` and `post-draft-note` refuse a finding whose body matches an "X is missing/wrong/broken/stale" pattern unless an accompanying `FindingEvidence` record (`--evidence-json '{...}'`) carries verified receipts — it passes only when `confidence='verified'` AND at least one verified-path field is non-empty (full schema in code). Implemented in `teatree.cli.review.evidence_gate`; runs alongside the on-behalf (#960), colleague-MR shape (#1114), comment-bloat (#2663), and TODO-anchor (#1186) sibling gates inside `ReviewService._run_pre_publish_gates`.

Guarded reads (#3509): every external read on the review CLI goes through `teatree.core.modelkit.gate_verdict` (the gate-verdict leaf, §17.6.5), which comes in two shapes because the right answer differs per site. `guarded_read(what, read, *, neutral) -> ReadOutcome[T]` still degrades to the documented neutral but LOGS the failure and carries `failed`, so "the read failed" and "there is nothing there" stop being the same answer — used by `shape_gate.fetch_mr_author`, `todo_gate._fetch_file_diff` and `inline_shape_gate.count_inline_drafts`, each of which keeps its fail-open DIRECTION deliberately (failing closed would refuse every review post whenever the forge is unreachable). `read_or_refuse(what, read) -> T` raises `EvidenceUnavailableError` instead, for a read whose neutral would be a guess with outbound consequences: `forge_target.resolve_base_url` REFUSES when no forge base URL is configured rather than falling back to `gitlab.com`, while an explicitly-set `$GITLAB_URL` is still honoured because that is an operator's stated choice, not a guess. This is `/t3:rules` § "External Read Failure Must Fail Loud" applied at the reader: the fail-open belongs to the caller as a conscious orchestration choice, and what is banned is the reader hiding the failure.

URL-derived review target (#3793/#3794): both coordinates a review post needs — the forge base URL and the API token — are resolved in `teatree.cli.review.forge_target` from the `owner/name` slug the command was given, via `get_overlay_for_url`. Resolving them from the AMBIENT overlay instead made the whole surface conditional on how many overlays are registered, because `get_overlay()` raises `Multiple overlays found` with more than one and no explicit pin; deriving them from the invocation makes resolution total, and routing BOTH through the same owning overlay stops a post being addressed to one forge with another's credential. `ReviewService` carries the target repo, and the MCP review seam (`review_post_seam(repo)`) is built per repo rather than once per process. `forge_target.read_token` returns a `ReadOutcome` so a FAILED read stays distinguishable from an absent one — `commands._require_token` reports the underlying cause for the first and keeps the login hint only for the second.

Comment-bloat gate (#2663): the same publishing path refuses a review note that drags in *project chatter* — a stakeholder `@handle`, a Slack thread timestamp, or a tracker id (`#1234`/`!42`) paired with a coordinate-with-people directive (`ping the author`, `sync with the team`, `in standup`). A review comment is about the diff, not the tracker. The note-LENGTH dimension of bloat stays owned by the colleague-MR shape gate (#1114, 3-paragraph/200-word cap), so this gate adds no parallel length cap; a *bare* `tracked at #1234` non-blocker pointer (the TODO-gate remediation form) is NOT refused. `--allow-bloat` is the #126 per-call escape for a genuinely load-bearing reference. Implemented in `teatree.cli.review.bloat_gate`.

Close-trailer scanner (#1398): `ban_close_trailers_on_namespaces` lists fnmatch patterns over `namespace/repo`. DB-home (#1775); set via `t3 <overlay> config_setting set ban_close_trailers_on_namespaces`; the TOML value is ignored on read. When the target PR/MR's repo matches and the body carries a `Closes|Fixes|Resolves` trailer (`part of` and full-URL variants too), `ShipExecutor.build_pr_spec` silently strips those lines before opening the PR (`teatree.core.intake.close_trailer_scanner`). Distinct from the overlay-scoped `forbid_close_keywords` gate (#1012), which refuses the publish; this scanner cleans the body and proceeds.

Open-questions warn (#1933): any open question (solved or not) and any non-explicit assumption must be listed in BOTH the commit body AND the PR description under an `Open questions & assumptions` section (per-item status `decided-by-user` / `assumed` / `open`). `teatree.core.gates.open_questions_gate` runs at both PR-creation chokepoints (`ShipExecutor.build_pr_spec`, orphan-branch `create_or_defer_pr`): a body lacking the heading warns and proceeds. Warn-only per the "a gate without a reliable heuristic warns" rule — the heading wording is not separable enough to block on. Canonical doctrine in `skills/ship/SKILL.md` § 5.

Required description sections (#312): the MR-description generator emits a standard body — `## What`, `## Why`, plus any section an overlay declares mandatory via `OverlayMetadata.get_required_description_sections()` (e.g. `["Configuration"]`). `teatree.core.review.mr_metadata.ensure_standard_body` appends every missing section (with the overlay's `get_description_section_defaults()` body when supplied, so a thin commit still ships a meaningful default) at every PR-creation chokepoint (`ShipExecutor.build_pr_spec`, `ship_preview`, orphan-branch `create_or_defer_pr`); never duplicating a present section and preserving the first line. The gate side — `OverlayMetadata.validate_pr` → `validate_mr_metadata(required_sections=…)` — flags a description missing a declared section, so an explicitly-authored body that bypasses the generator is still rejected. Core is overlay-agnostic: it renders only what the overlay declares, hard-coding no section names or wording.

The no-orphan pre-push hook (`t3 <overlay> pr ensure-pr`) is the seam that has no author-written body at all: it opens the PR from the branch's own commit alone, so every PR it created was born failing this same gate and had to be patched by hand afterwards. `mr_metadata.auto_created_description` renders that body — first line the conventional-commit subject, `## What` the commit message, and `## Why` a statement of PROVENANCE, never an invented rationale, since the hook has no source for the why. The gate itself is unchanged: it recognises no auto-creation marker and still rejects a raw commit-message body.

**That `## Why` states what the PR IS; it does not instruct an author who is not there ([#4424](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4424)).** It read `TODO — … Replace this line with the rationale before requesting review.`, addressed to the author whose absence is the whole reason the hook opened the PR — so nothing ever filled it in, and three cold reviews on one day each filed it separately. The `## Why` now carries the hook's provenance (`AUTO_CREATED_MARKER`, the stable phrase `lacks_rationale` keys on) and the two facts the hook genuinely holds: the branch it rescued, and the `issue_url` of the ticket owning that branch's `Worktree` row. The explicit "no author-written rationale exists" line is CONDITIONAL, not blanket: the hook nests the whole rescued commit message under a generated `## What`, so it is emitted only when that nested body has no `## Why` of its own — found live and self-fixed on the PR this change shipped through ([#4542](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/pull/4542)), where the unconditional version asserted "no rationale" directly beneath one this very commit message carries. The tracked issue carries no closing keyword: a rescue PR is not necessarily the whole fix, so auto-closing its ticket on merge would claim more than the hook knows.

**The ship step CLEARS that body when it adopts the hook's PR ([#3991](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3991)).** `create_pr` adopting an already-open PR took the URL only, so the hook's own body was what merged — a `## Why` a reviewer cannot distinguish from "the author had no rationale". `backends/github/pr_create._adopt_body` now writes the ship's own description onto the adopted PR, gated on the pure core predicate `mr_metadata.lacks_rationale` (blank body / the `AUTO_CREATED_MARKER` phrase / a `## Why` header with nothing under it) so the shapes the hook produces are replaced while an authored body — a redelivered ship re-finding its OWN PR, a human-opened one on the same branch — is never clobbered. A body that cannot be READ is left as-is with a warning, for the same reason. The write is confirmed under §17.1 invariant 13 (`verify_by_reread`), and what it confirms is that the write LANDED (the body is no longer the one that was read) — not that the replacement reads well, since `ensure_standard_body` appends a bare `## Why` to a ship description whose commit has none, and failing on that would red a ship whose write worked. A write that did not land fails the ship rather than reporting success on a PR still carrying the placeholder.

Egress-leak gate family — one doctrine, several entry points, each named here with its load-bearing invariant; detector lists, exit codes, thresholds, and exempt sets live in the code:

- **Generic forge-write detectors** ([#5000](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5000)): `teatree.hooks.privacy_generic_detectors` holds the push scanner's email, home-path, private-IP, API-key and internal-hostname patterns and its reserved-domain/SSH-remote exclusions. The in-process `scan_outbound_text` gate, the Bash banned-terms PreToolUse gate, and PR creation (`ship` scans the generated title and body before push; `ensure-pr` scans before deferral or create) apply them to forge payloads. Detection runs on raw text: an exact registry allow entry exempts only a finding it wholly covers, the push scanner's fixture marker applies only to its own lines, and forge writes exempt the Linux and macOS hosted CI runner workspace prefixes. In the hook the check runs whenever the banned-term branch does not block (including a downgraded term), only on forge writes (`teatree.hooks.forge_write_scope`) and local commits, never on Slack posts; its verdict is `leak_policy.decide(LEAK, …)` over the command's whole-segment visibility: public/unknown egress blocks, a local commit warns for the pre-push rescan, and a provably private target passes. The in-process gate only reaches public or unknown targets, so it always refuses. Refusals name `generic:<category>`, never the matched value. The high-confidence secret gate blocks API keys on private targets.
- **The ONE leak-policy decision function** ([#3532](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3532), `teatree.hooks.leak_policy`): `decide(term_class, visibility, surface) -> BLOCK | WARN | ALLOW`, TOTAL over (class × visibility × surface). The subsystem's enforcement points each used to compose their own tuple of term source × matcher × visibility resolver × carve-out set × fail direction, so the same decision was re-derived and drifted per site. This module owns the decision, the term TAXONOMY (`LEAK` / `PROSE_COLLIDER` / `TONE` / `ALLOW`) and the three-value `Visibility` enum; it carries no term data, no config reads and no I/O — the registry (`banned_term_registry`), the matcher (`term_match`) and the visibility resolver (`_repo_visibility`) stay where they are, and being a pure leaf keeps it importable from the cold PreToolUse hook and from the Django-side gates alike. **Polarity:** `UNKNOWN` visibility folds onto `PUBLIC` — a destination the gate cannot PROVE non-public is scanned and blocked, so detection failure never weakens the gate. **Surfaces:** `LOCAL_COMMIT` is the one surface that downgrades a blocking class to `WARN` (a commit reaches no remote until `git push`, and the #703 pre-push gate re-scans commit messages before it does); every other surface is an egress with no backstop behind it, so it BLOCKS. Class routing is derived here too — `banned_term_registry.GATE_CLASSES` is built from `classes_for_surface`, so "which classes does this gate scan" also has exactly one answer.
- **An unresolvable publish destination is SCANNED, not skipped** ([#3477](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3477)): `hooks.public_visibility.destination_visibility` classifies an EMPTY slug and one carrying an unexpanded `$VAR` as `UNKNOWN` → scan. `$OWNER` expands at run time and can expand to a public repo, and the sibling classifier `publish_destination.is_public_destination` has always treated both as PUBLIC — the two disagreed, and the disagreement was the fail-open half. The cost of this direction is a scan on a command whose target cannot be read; the cost of the other is an unscanned public egress. The offline `private_repos` allowlist remains the network-free way to keep an own-private post skip-eligible.

- **Public-repo diff privacy-scan** (#685, #730): pre-push hook `refuse-public-push-with-leak.sh` reads its committed gate files relative to its checkout and the pushed history from the work-tree top (including when prek runs inside a vendored subdirectory); it runs `scripts/privacy_scan.py` from the pushed checkout (stdlib only, run with `-S` and `PYTHONPATH` at the checkout's own `src`, so a push that fixes the gate is judged by the fixed gate; the hook reads no override variable of its own, and the PreToolUse guards refuse an inline `TEATREE_TERM_REGISTRY=`, `T3_CONFIG_DB=`, `T3_DATA_DIR=`, `XDG_DATA_HOME=`, `HOME=`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME=`, `GIT_CONFIG_*=` or `SKIP=` on a command that commits, pushes or runs prek, and a hand-written `refs/remotes/` ref) over each newly-public commit's own patch and message when `origin` is not provably private — the commits the remote does not already hold, read from the remote itself (`teatree.hooks.remote_holds`, which `t3 fast-push` shares through `core/push/push_range.py`: `git ls-remote --heads` at the URL git pushes to, plus the sha git reports for the ref, listed only when `git ls-remote --get-url` and `git remote get-url --push` both return that very URL, so a `url.<base>.insteadOf` aimed at a fake repository with an identity `pushInsteadOf` cannot redirect the listing) and never from this clone's remote-tracking refs, which anyone can write; a remote that cannot be listed widens the range to every commit the reported sha does not hold, never narrows it — — patches on stdin in diff mode (context and removed lines are already public), messages on stdin in `--text` mode (no temp file), because git puts no separator between one commit's last hunk and the next subject and a message may quote a hunk itself — a later commit never clears an earlier one's finding, and the refusal labels a finding that sits behind the pushed tip — and the pushed ref NAME (a branch or tag name is published the moment the push lands and survives deletion in `refs/pull/*`), blocking emails / home paths / private IPs / keys / internal hostnames / banned terms. An annotated tag's message and tagger line are read for registry terms and opaque ids. It fails CLOSED on a genuine finding (a dedicated exit code) and on every other non-zero too — a crashing scanner, no Python 3.13+, an unset registry: a gate that cannot look has not shown the push is clean. A registry term is reported as `class#index`, never by value. The environment can still be filled in ways no command-line guard sees (an exported variable, `printf -v`, `read`, `source`), so in this project's own checkout, before the hook trusts any verdict, a private one included, it holds the term lists it loaded (the `leak` and `overlay` classes) to the digests committed at the checkout's HEAD in `.github/term-source-fingerprint.json` (`privacy_scan.py --verify-registry`, exit `5`; `teatree.hooks.term_fingerprint`, held equal to `scripts/term_source_drift.py` by test) and refuses on a mismatch, on a fingerprint it cannot read, and on one that is missing, untracked or edited since; the `prose_collider` and `tone` classes have no committed fingerprint. The hook also runs only committed code: it reads `scripts/hooks/leak-gate-sources.txt` from HEAD (held to the import closure of the scanner, the visibility resolver, `remote_holds` and `commit_objects` by `tests/conformance/test_leak_gate_sources_list.py`) and refuses when a listed file hashes differently from its HEAD blob (`--assume-unchanged` included), naming the file and never its content, before any of that code runs, and runs an archived copy of them: the listed files are copied out of HEAD into a directory of the hook's own (removed on exit; a hook that cannot make one refuses) read as raw blobs (`git cat-file blob`, so no repo-local smudge filter and, with `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`, no replace ref rewrites them) and every Python it starts runs from that copy with `-P -S`, so no untracked `argparse.py` or `dataclasses.py` in the script directory, on `PYTHONPATH` or in the repo being pushed, no planted `__pycache__/*.pyc` and no site hook is ever imported; the history it scans is read unreplaced, with `--no-textconv --no-ext-diff --no-color` and every commit message both as git decodes it (UTF-8) and as the object stores it, plus each whole commit and tag object (extra headers, author, committer and tagger names), all read by one module, `teatree.hooks.commit_objects`, that the hook runs from the archived copy and `t3 fast-push` imports: for registry terms and opaque ids as it is, and for every detector with only the exact `@users.noreply.github.com` cut from the identity lines (the indented copies in a `mergetag` block too), so a secret or a home path in a header is found, and so is a token worn as a noreply login, while a noreply address is no email finding, so a textconv driver, a replace ref or a log encoding set in `.git`, and an `encoding` header that travels with the pushed commit (`i18n.commitEncoding=UTF-16LE` on plain bytes), cannot blank what the scanner reads (as `t3 fast-push` and the staged-diff reader do too, the PR-body paths scanning the text they publish); it also refuses an untracked `*.so` or `*.pyd` beside a listed module, and an untracked directory named like one, because Python imports those in the module's place. Visibility is resolved by `teatree.hooks.repo_visibility_cli --live` (the hook, started exactly as the scanner is, under `-P -S` from that archived copy, so no `.pth` file of the checkout's `.venv` and no `sitecustomize` runs first; and the identity gate of `t3 fast-push`), a thin seam onto the host-routed probe (`gh` for GitHub, `glab` for GitLab) that asks the forge now: the day cache and the `private_repos` allowlist are files a pusher can write, so neither establishes PRIVATE for a push and only the forge's own answer skips the scan (an unanswered probe is undetermined and is scanned); the FULL remote URL is passed, never a host-stripped slug, because the host segment is what selects the forge tool. Shelling `gh repo view` here hard-coded ONE forge, so a `gitlab.com` remote was permanently undetermined and the gate re-scanned a PRIVATE repo on every push. `_repo_visibility.slug_for_remote_url` is the normalizer both that CLI and the checkout-based `slug_for_cwd` share. **Named residuals** (none is closed from inside the hook; `banned-terms-tree`, `overlay-leak-tree` and `term-source-drift` are required checks on `main` and read the result after the push, and ruleset 14777841 carries only a `pull_request` rule with no bypass actor):
  - (a) A fake fingerprint committed together with a matching fake registry passes the hook's registry check. `term-source-drift` catches it (it compares the CI secret with the committed fingerprint and fails on a fake one); the leaked registry terms themselves are read in the final tree against the real secret by `banned-terms-tree` (leak class, plus every leak, overlay and allow value under the value-free roots) and `overlay-leak-tree` (overlay class and opaque ids).
  - (b) The `prose_collider` and `tone` classes have no committed fingerprint, so a registry copy that keeps the `leak`, `overlay` and `allow` lists can drop terms from the other classes and still verify. Tracked in #5041.
  - (c) An exported `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or `SKIP`, a hooks path set through `git -c` or `GIT_CONFIG_*`, a `gh`/`glab` shim first on `PATH`, or a `core.sshCommand` wrapper that serves the listing from a fake repository and forwards the push to the real remote (not cheap to close without refusing legitimate custom ssh setups) makes the local hook not run or answer for itself; a local hook can be skipped by a pusher who controls the environment. The backstop is the CI scan jobs `banned-terms-tree` and `overlay-leak-tree` (registry terms and opaque ids in the final tree, not generic secrets, paths, emails, commit messages, ref names or tag messages), and the follow-ups tracked in #5041 (a required `identity-literals-tree` check, a `t3 doctor` FAIL on a hookless clone and a daily canary); until then this is a named residual.
    - Repo-local git config or attributes can change what git feeds a local hook (e.g. a file marked `-diff`, or any file under `core.bigFileThreshold=1`, shows as `Binary files differ` and is not scanned; grafts empty the hook's range but the remote rejects such a push): tracked in #5041 as a binary-content scan; backstop = the required CI tree jobs.
    - Content deliberately encoded so the term is not visible as text (UTF-16 under a latin-1 header, base64) defeats any literal scanner: tracked in #5041.
  - (d) An edited hook script (`scripts/hooks/refuse-public-push-with-leak.sh` replaced by `exit 0`) or an edited `.pre-commit-config.yaml` (the hook unwired) lands the push: the running file cannot check itself and nothing inside the hook can see how it is wired, the deleted-hook class. Tracked in #5041 (the daily canary and the `t3 doctor` hookless-clone check), with the required CI jobs `banned-terms-tree` and `overlay-leak-tree` reading the result after the push.
  - (e) On a vendoring checkout the registry is held to no fingerprint, so an exported registry of the caller's choosing is accepted, and a commit plus a repo-local `core.worktree` can disguise the project's own checkout as vendored. The CI tree jobs and that host's own CI are the backstop.
- **Proactive public-repo identity gate** (#762, #785, #2655): the proactive sibling of the reactive backstop above. `teatree.core.public_identity.is_public_github_remote` drives the clone-local stamping of the canonical GitHub `users.noreply.github.com` author identity onto any worktree off a PUBLIC GitHub clone (the worktree provisioner and `workspace stamp-identity`), so a public-repo commit never carries the inherited real-domain email the leak backstop would later block. It is HOST-AWARE (#2655): the gate short-circuits any non-github host to `False` BEFORE the `gh repo view` visibility probe (`is_github_host`, recognising `github.com` and `github.com-<ssh-alias>`), so a non-github remote (e.g. a `gitlab.com` SSH URL) whose host-stripped `owner/repo` slug collides with a real public github.com repo is never mis-stamped with the github identity. Callers pass the full remote URL — host intact — never the bare slug.
- **Foreign-open-MR push guard** (#2211): pre-push hook `refuse-push-to-foreign-mr.sh` — a collaboration-safety sibling (not a leak gate). For each pushed ref it resolves the branch's backing OPEN PR via `gh pr list --head <branch> --state open` and BLOCKS, naming the author + PR number, when the PR's author login != our `gh api user` login — a teammate's open MR our changes would silently modify (our work belongs on our own branch; a worktree opened to INSPECT a colleague's MR is read-only). Our own open MR, a branch with no open MR, and a foreign CLOSED/merged MR (which `--state open` excludes) all pass; so does an MR authored by a login the operator declared for that host in the DB-home host-keyed `self_forge_identities` (a declared `COLD_SETTINGS` key, so `t3 <overlay> config_setting set self_forge_identities '{"gitlab.com": ["my-factory-bot"]}'` validates the table, stores it, and republishes the host projection this hook reads — the write seam a raw sqlite INSERT bypasses; it is a personal identifier, so it is withheld from a shared `config_setting export`) — a bot that authors our MRs so the owner stays eligible to approve them would otherwise read exactly like a teammate, since a forge CLI answers with ONE login. Declaring nothing leaves every verdict unchanged, and a login declared for another host never applies; an explicit `[push-to-foreign-mr-ok: <reason>]` commit-message token allows a genuine co-authoring push. Fails OPEN on a `gh` failure / gh-unavailable / unresolved login so a transient forge-API error never bricks a legitimate push.
- **Banned-terms posting gate** (#1415, `teatree.hooks.banned_terms_scanner`): the `PreToolUse` non-commit sibling. A publish is any non-read `gh`/`glab` verb carrying a free-text flag or file content, a `git commit` or annotated `git tag` message, a forge-bound `curl` write, and every literal field value of an `api` write; one flag table (`teatree.hooks._forge_write_detection`) drives both detection and extraction. The `curl` grammar in `teatree.hooks._curl_payload` uses `curl_options.json`, generated from curl `tool_getparam.c` aliases at `curl-8_5_0` and `curl-8_18_0`; it binds URLs and methods per `--next` group, reads clustered and long option values for both detection and extraction, and treats `--` as the end of options. An opaque curl segment (an unknown or abbreviated option, or `-K`/`--config`) is judged as one transfer group: any argv URL naming a forge fails closed with the opaque option's reason. An upload (`-T`) and a file body (`-d @file`, `-F name=@file`) fail closed on any publishing group, forge or Slack. Unresolvable clustered values fail closed on publishing gh/glab/git segments. A read (a read verb, a known GET/HEAD, a graphql query document) is not scanned. The `-K` rule holds only when argv names the forge URL: a config file that supplies the URL and the data, and default `~/.curlrc`, stay outside this argv gate as residuals. It and the #1213 quote-scanner share `extract_bash_payload` and a destination check: no override token is honoured: a public post is always scanned, and a local commit only warns because the pre-push gate re-scans every pushed message. A `> path <<EOF` heredoc is scanned only when its path is posted via a body-file flag; a stdin `--body-file -` heredoc always is. A `t3 review post-comment`/`post-draft-note` segment carries the published body in any of three sources — the positional `NOTE`, `-m`/`--body`, or `--body-file <path>` (#32) — all of which are scanned, while the `--file` argument (a diff ANCHOR, a SOURCE path, not a body-file) is deliberately skipped (`teatree.hooks._t3_review_post`). An inline `--body`/`-m`/positional-NOTE body whose `$(...)` command-substitution sits inside a SINGLE-quoted span is inert literal text bash passes verbatim, so `resolve_inline_body_value` reads the value token's source span (`raw`) and SCANS such a body rather than fail-closing on "publish body could not be read" — a body bash WOULD expand (unquoted / double-quoted `$(...)`) still fails closed toward a PUBLIC or UNKNOWN-visibility target since its real content is unknown before the command runs (#1415), while toward a LITERAL provably-private destination it skips (nothing to leak; only a substitution that DETECTABLY publishes, `$(gh … create …)`, keeps the scan — `public_visibility._construct_bearing_segment_verdict`) — though for a LOCAL `git commit` that unreadable-body sentinel then DOWNGRADES to a warn regardless of landing-repo visibility (`command_targets_local_commit`: a commit is local and the pre-push gate, `refuse-public-push-with-leak.sh` #703, re-scans every commit message before a public push), while a `gh`/`glab` POST keeps hard-blocking; a `git commit -F -` reads its message from STDIN, so a heredoc / `printf|git commit -F -` writer body is RESOLVED and scanned (a real term in a readable commit body still blocks) rather than fail-closed as an unreadable file named `-`. The raw-aware `command_has_opaque_forge_transport` mirrors this so a non-forge segment that merely mentions a single-quoted `$(...)` is not treated as a hidden forge transport. A forge post hidden behind an opaque, wrapper, or path-form transport — `sh -c "gh …"`, `eval "gh …"`, `xargs gh`, `env GH_PAGER= gh`, a `foo=1 gh …` env-prefix, or an absolute-path `/usr/bin/gh` / `./glab` — is now DETECTED as a publish: the leader is canonicalized by basename and wrapper-stripped (`canonical_leader` / `_WRAPPER_PROGRAMS`, mirroring `raw_merge_detect`), so publish DETECTION and payload EXTRACTION agree and the segment fails CLOSED at BOTH the banned-terms gate and the #1213 quote gate (the opaque-transport fail-closed sentinel is appended outside the body-file guard so the quote gate, which passes `fail_closed_body_file=False`, fails closed too) instead of passing unscanned. An unresolved `gh`/`glab` WRITE destination — e.g. `gh pr review <n> --body …`, whose target repo the hook cannot resolve — is now SCANNED, not skipped (`public_visibility` scans unless the destination is provably non-public). The gate delegates matching to `check-banned-terms.sh`, whose exit-code contract is 0=clean / 1=banned-term-found / 2=scanner-could-not-run; when the shell fallback resolves to an old system `python3` (below the >= 3.13 floor) the matcher import crashes, so the fallback probe-imports the module first and exits 2 (fail CLOSED, loud) rather than letting the crash exit 1 (which collides with "found") become ALLOW. `scan_text` mirrors this: a crash, unexpected exit, or exit-1-with-no-report returns `SCANNER_UNAVAILABLE_MARKER`, while a scan KILLED at its budget returns the separate `SCANNER_TIMEOUT_MARKER`. Both BLOCK on every surface — a security gate that fails open on a crash is the bug class (#1954) — but they are distinct markers because they call for opposite remedies: a timeout is a LOAD signal answered by the `T3_BANNED_TERMS_SCAN_TIMEOUT_S` budget seam (default 10s, kept under the harness's 30s hook ceiling so a killed hook can still report), not by repairing a healthy interpreter. The per-scan `uv run` is `--frozen` so unbounded dependency resolution is not charged against that budget; `--no-sync` is deliberately not used, since the hook's own `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT` is provisioned by nothing else and skipping the sync would fail closed permanently rather than intermittently. **Company-identifier carve-out** (#1415 over-block): the optional registry `allow` class names the org's OWN compound identifiers (`<org>-engineering`, `<org>-product`, internal-URL namespaces) that are never customer PII; `utils.exact_carve_out.mask_exempt` blanks each EXACT occurrence of an allow-listed identifier BEFORE matching (one extended by a letter, digit, `-` or `_`, or for an entry holding `.`/`@` by a `.`/`@`/`+` joined to a word, stays scanned), so a SHORTER single-token banned term (a bare org slug) can no longer surface inside a longer company-owned identifier or an internal-URL path — while a genuine customer codename NOT on the allow-list is still blocked, and a bare standalone org-slug token still fires. The carve-out is the one `mask_exempt` helper: the shared matcher applies it for the shell hook (`banned_terms_cli`), the posting gate (`banned_terms_scanner`, incl. report attribution) and the diff-only commit scan, and the forge-write seam calls it directly, so all honour it identically; empty (default) is a no-op preserving prior behaviour.
- **Pre-dispatch quote-scanner** (#1401, `handle_dispatch_prompt_quote_scanner`): the dispatch-boundary companion to #1213 — denies only a HIGH user-voice/PII match in an `Agent`/`Task` prompt, with no inline escape, so a verbatim quote cannot leak downstream.
- **Diff comment detectors** (added-lines-only): `code_comment_self_reference` (#1465) is a **blocking** `privacy_scan.py` diff detector (**fail-open per detector**, #1536) for bookkeeping self-references. `code_comment_density` (#1538) is the commit-side half of the comments-as-code rule (#1532; names + types are the docs, a long comment is a code smell → refactor); it is **content-aware** — beyond the line-count ratio / consecutive-run passes it flags a comment whose words merely **restate** the next code line and a docstring opening that merely **echoes the signature** (a single such line suffices), while leaving a genuine non-obvious-why comment and a justified multi-line docstring ALLOWED. It is **advisory** — the standalone check (#1369, `t3 tool comment-density`), the pre-push hook, and the `comment-density-warning` CI job warn and **exit 0**, and it is NOT a `privacy_scan.py` blocking detector (#1844 — no content-blind "overly long prose" heuristic that would flag legit long comments). A golden corpus (`tests/test_comment_density_gate.py`) + an eval pin must-DENY (restatement + signature-echo) symmetric with must-ALLOW (a docstring carrying a real why). The push-stage gates (`doc-update-gate`, `ensure-pr`, the leak gate) are `stages: [push]` — skipped by a bare `prek run --all-files` but re-run by CI — so `t3 tool verify-gates` runs three stages — commit, push, then the manual-stage CI-job hooks (`test-path-mirror`, `test-shape`) filtered to the ones the prek config prek itself loads declares (`prek.toml` or `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, nearest at or above `--repo`, never above the git toplevel) — and skips that third stage only when a readable config declares none, since prek exits 1 on a selector no hook matches. A missing, unparsable or non-mapping config selects every CI-job hook instead: an unread config is not one declaring none, and prek's own config error keeps the run red. It returns the combined exit code, so local green == CI green. It also DISCLOSES its venue ([#4720](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4720)): the command takes no target, so run from the main clone it graded `origin/main` while exiting 0, and a reviewer reporting that exit code handed back a green for a tree nobody asked about. Every run now prints the checkout, full HEAD SHA and branch it measured; `--expect-sha <head>` (env `T3_VERIFY_GATES_EXPECT_SHA`) makes the target a hard check; a clean primary clone on its default branch is REFUSED (exit 2, nothing graded) unless `--allow-main-clone`; and a green run names the CI jobs no local hook covers (`teatree.cli.verify_gates.UNCOVERED_CI_JOBS`, pinned against `ci.yml`) so exit 0 cannot be read as "CI will be green". Exit 2 is "nothing measured / wrong tree", distinct from exit 1 "a gate failed". The main-clone refusal reads the `targetBranch` key of git config's `teatree` section alongside `origin/HEAD`, so a fork's integration checkout is the same structural non-target, and "dirty" is untracked-INSENSITIVE (a scratch file nobody `git add`-ed changes nothing about what `--all-files` graded). The dispatch side matches: a reviewing brief carries a `GREEN-PROOF BINDING` block naming the dispatched head and its `--expect-sha` invocation (`agents.phase_blocks._green_proof_binding_lines`, sourced from `review_target_for_task`), so the reviewer is handed the bound command rather than expected to remember the caveat.
- **Gate subprocess database isolation**: `t3 tool verify-gates` reads the term registry in the invoking environment and, when available, passes it to every stage as `TEATREE_TERM_REGISTRY`; it snapshots the live control DB into a temporary `XDG_DATA_HOME` and `T3_CONTROL_DB_DIR` before running hooks, removes inherited `T3_CONFIG_DB` and `DATABASE_URL`, and keeps the gate receipt in the invoking environment. The snapshot preserves ticket brief constraints without letting hooks write the live DB. The pinned-regressions lane stays green under that exported environment, as pinned by `test_corpus_stays_green_under_the_environment_verify_gates_exports`.
- **Run scratch lifetime and worker reclaim ([#5837](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5837)).** `verify-gates` removes its private snapshot directory on completion, failure, SIGTERM or SIGHUP. `review merge-tree` keeps a successful default extract for the reviewer, removes a failed or interrupted one, and never owns `--into`. The existing worker artifact pass reclaims only the two generated directory families after three hours when tree-wide mtime and process cwd, command line and environment reads prove them unused; the plan records freed bytes or a refusal.
- **Full-tree banned-brand backstop** (#1570/#1591, `core.banned_terms_tree`, CLI `t3 banned-terms scan-tree`, CI job `banned-terms-tree`): catches what the diff/payload gates structurally cannot — a brand ALREADY committed, invisible to any post-landing diff — by scanning every tracked file's **committed blob** (`git show HEAD:<path>`, working-tree fallback only for an uncommitted file) with the shared whole-token matcher (`teatree.hooks.term_match`, pinned by the parity test). The registry's `leak` class is a curated high-confidence term list; an empty class is refused before scanning. **The same fail-loud rule holds on the ENUMERATION axis** ([#4354](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4354)): `git_tracked_files` RAISES `TreeEnumerationError` when git could not answer (a non-repo `--repo-root`, an absent `git`, a `safe.directory` refusal, a loaded-box `ls-files` timeout) or answered with no tracked files at all, so the CLI exits MISCONFIGURED (2) instead of reporting `clean (0 findings)` on a tree it never read — zero files scanned yields zero findings whatever the tree contains. A missing registry refuses the scan on every CI event, and an unreadable tree also refuses. Every scan requires a populated registry leak class and exits MISCONFIGURED (2, distinct from 1 = findings) when it is absent or empty, including on local and CI runs. `scan-tree --classes leak,overlay,allow` (the job's second step) instead reads the checked-out tracked files under `src`, `scripts`, `tests`, `docs` and `.github` for EVERY value of the named classes: the allow class is scanned there, so no allow entry exempts anything, and the only exemption is the exact repository-owner login read from `origin`.
- **Consolidated banned-term registry** (`hooks.banned_term_registry`): ONE class-tagged `banned_term_registry` row (or the `TEATREE_TERM_REGISTRY` secret) that `terms_for_gate(gate)` routes by class (`leak` → diff+tree+core, `prose_collider` → diff+core, `tone` → diff, `overlay` → the core-generic gate, `allow` → the allowlist, exempting only EXACT occurrences through `utils.exact_carve_out`, so an identifier that extends an entry is still scanned). The four earlier lists (`banned_terms`, `banned_brands`, `banned_terms_allowlist`, `overlay_leak_terms`) are no longer read. With the registry unset or a gate's scan classes empty, the diff, core and tree gates RAISE `BannedTermsUnsetError` (fail-closed); the `overlay` and `allow` gates may read empty. The `scan-tree` path requires a nonempty `leak` class; the `banned-terms-tree` + lint CI jobs read the `TEATREE_TERM_REGISTRY` secret.
- **Programmatic on-behalf egress privacy-scan** (#1295 capability J): the autonomous-events reply transport (`core.reply_transport`) wires the publication privacy gate (`core.gates.privacy_gate.scan_outbound_text`, the egress sibling of `scan_for_publication` sharing its match pass) into its single on-behalf chokepoint — `_BaseReplier._send` and `redeliver`, via `_enforce_privacy`, **scoped to code-host events** (`_FORGE_BY_SOURCE`: GitHub/GitLab only — a Slack channel ref is not a repo target and is out of scope here). A GitHub PR comment / GitLab MR note posted under the user's identity is body-scanned for the overlay's `privacy_redact_terms`/`privacy_block_patterns` plus the built-in quote anchors BEFORE the wire call — closing the gap the Bash-hook banned-terms/quote gates structurally miss (those scan raw `gh`/`glab` command text, not a programmatic API post). **Public-ness is derived from the SAME visibility axis the Bash gates use** (`hooks.publish_destination.is_public_destination` → `_target_is_public`), NOT a per-overlay `public_repos` list (which is empty by default and would make the gate inert): a repo is scanned unless provably private (`private_repos` / `private_repos` allowlists or a cached `gh`/`glab` probe), so it actually protects a real public repo like `souliane/teatree`. It fails CLOSED on a finding (`PublicationPrivacyBlockedError` → FAILED `ReplyDispatch`, never delivered; on `redeliver` it propagates to the retry sweep) and on a classification error (treats the target as public → scanned); a provably-private repo is a clean pass and a bot→user `post_dm` is never scanned. Overlay redact-rule resolution is a best-effort ADD on top of the always-on built-in quote anchors — when no overlay resolves, the built-in detectors are the floor, so the gate never goes inert.
- **Outbound send-proxy — one chokepoint + audit** ([#117](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/117)): `teatree.core.send_proxy` routes Slack colleague posts, replies and reactions, plus forge PR/MR/issue writes, through one allowlist, redaction, and audit seam. `read_posting_credential` remains the sole point-of-use reader for posting credentials. The per-overlay `send_proxy_allowlist` matches a raw destination or `<channel>:<destination>` with `fnmatch`; the bot-to-owner self-DM is always allowed for never-lockout. Every other non-allowlisted destination is refused before the wire call, while payload redaction uses the overlay privacy terms and every verdict records a `SendAudit` row. An unreadable allowlist denies non-self sends. The audit write is best-effort; an audit failure does not turn an allowed send into a refusal. Notion writes use their own configured-root guard rather than this proxy.

---

### Issue hygiene — the description is the specification

A lane reads an issue's **body**; nothing feeds it the comments. So a requirement,
change request, scope change or decision posted as a COMMENT is silently never
executed — the failure #162 records. `teatree.core.issue_hygiene` is the
single application-level issue-write facade that closes it, and
`chokepoints.yaml`'s `forge-comment-write-seam` lists it beside the transports so a
new issue writer lands behind it rather than on the allowlist.
Its guarded removal of a generated description section lives in
`teatree.core.issue_writes.section_removal`; the facade keeps the public write
entry point while the helper checks authorship, the expected body and the
post-write result.

- **Purpose routing.** `record_issue_note(host, issue_url, purpose, content)` takes a
  closed `NotePurpose`. The four NORMATIVE purposes (`requirement`,
  `change_request`, `scope_change`, `decision`) append a dated, marked section to the
  description; only `status` and `evidence` post a comment. There is no untyped note
  API — `t3 <overlay> ticket comment` requires `--purpose` and the MCP tool is
  `<forge>_issue_note`.
- **Self-authored only.** `core.self_forge_identities.require_self_authored_issue`
  re-reads the issue immediately before every mutation and returns the payload the
  write is then computed from, so authority and body come from one fetch. The
  self-set is the union of `user_identity_aliases`, the per-host
  `self_forge_identities` map, and `host.current_user()` — deliberately NOT
  `trusted_issue_authors`, which includes colleagues. An unknown, absent or
  unreadable author fails closed; external people's tickets stay theirs.
- **Compare-and-append, not blind replace.** Neither forge offers a precondition on
  an issue update, so `append_description_section` is optimistic: re-read, rebase the
  section onto the body just read, write, re-read and verify both the idempotency
  marker and the base text survived; a lost race retries onto the newest body,
  bounded by `MAX_APPEND_ATTEMPTS`, then raises `IssueWriteConflictError` rather than
  reporting success.
- **Dedupe before create.** `create_or_extend` reads every OPEN issue on the repo
  (`CodeHostBackend.list_repo_open_issues`, paginated), ranks them against the draft,
  and refuses to file until the closest 20 are judged. A fitting ticket in that set
  is extended; an open fit outside it is refused. A new one's body stays the supplied
  body. Every completed outcome records the judged candidates, total open count, decisions and reasons in a
  private `SendAudit` row with the `other` channel and an audit-only verdict; the
  retention pass removes these rows after 30 days. A fitting ticket somebody ELSE
  filed returns `external_conflict` — neither edited nor duplicated.
- **A deterministic filer judges mechanically, it does not skip the pass.**
  `create_or_extend_by_marker` fits a candidate iff its body carries the caller's
  stable marker, judged from the SAME single listing the commit uses — two
  independent reads would race into a spurious `unjudged` refusal. The retro
  enforcement filer (`core.review.review_findings.file_class_c_issue`, keyed on the
  finding fingerprint) and the dream binding-reconciliation filer
  (`loops.dream.promote_memory`, keyed on the conflict key) both file through it, so
  neither reaches a raw `create_issue` and both left `chokepoints.yaml`'s allowlist.
  The whole-backlog read also replaces a `search_open_issues` dedupe whose index lag
  let a back-to-back re-run refile once. `IssueDraft.action` carries the filer's own
  audit label, so one scrub serves the leak gate and a per-filer `SendAudit` row.
- **Close is split from the note it used to carry.** ``close_issue(comment=...)`` was a
  hidden comment seam: the reason a ticket was retired is exactly what a later reader
  needs, and a comment is where nothing looks. `close_with_rationale` writes the
  rationale as a dated description section and only THEN closes, carrying no comment.
  Order is load-bearing — an unconfirmed append leaves the ticket OPEN and retryable,
  because a ticket closed for a reason nobody can read is the invisibility this section
  exists to end. The dead-issue closer (`loop.mechanical.close_dead_issue`) files
  through it and left `chokepoints.yaml`'s allowlist; two consequences are intended and
  pinned — a ticket someone ELSE filed is refused rather than closed (Rule 5), and the
  handler still never raises out of a tick. The dream umbrella (`loops.dream.umbrella_ledger`)
  is a line-level upsert rather than an append, so it keeps its own `update_issue` and
  its allowlist row, but its read now goes through the Rule 5 guard: the body each
  upsert is computed from is the body it was authorised on, one fetch.
- **One read of `self_forge_identities`.**
  `core.self_forge_identities.declared_identities_for_url` is the URL-keyed face of
  `hooks.foreign_mr_cli.declared_self_identities_raw`, the single read of that setting —
  the foreign-MR push gate, the review-candidate self-author skip, this guard and
  `cli.review.own_mr.owner_authored_mr` use it. The review own-MR proof includes
  logins declared in `self_forge_identities` for the API host. The shared reader
  PRESERVES case: the push gate lowercases, review-candidate casefolds, and the
  review own-MR proof compares exactly. The primitive lives in the hooks layer
  because the cold PreToolUse subprocess reads it too and may import only stdlib
  plus `teatree.config`.
- **The MCP create dedupes in two calls, so the pass cannot be skipped.**
  `<forge>_issue_create` is the surface an agent actually files from, so it is where a
  skippable dedupe would be skipped. Called with no `dedupe` it WRITES NOTHING and
  returns `judgment_required`, the 20 closest open tickets by draft title and description,
  `open_count`, `compared`, and a `snapshot` of only those candidate URLs;
  the retry supplies `dedupe={"snapshot", "decisions"}` and gets `created_new`,
  `extended_existing`, `external_conflict` or `stale_snapshot` — never a bare URL,
  because which of those happened is what the caller has to report. The two travel as
  ONE argument: a judgment set without the candidates it was made against cannot be
  checked for completeness. A new ticket entering the closest set makes the snapshot
  stale; changing the title or body between calls may also change the selected set.
  Tickets outside that set are not judged and can still contain a duplicate.
- **A tracked source that files an issue is caught statically.** A PreToolUse hook only
  sees a live session — a CI workflow step, a checked-in script and a skill teaching the
  wrong command all run outside one, which is how `ci.yml`'s selection-audit filer ran a
  bare `gh issue create` and filed one duplicate per PR. `teatree.hooks.raw_issue_write_sources`
  asks the same question of the repository's own tracked text, and
  `tests/quality/test_raw_issue_write_sources.py` keeps the tree green on it. It reports
  an INVOCATION, not a mention: the shared `forge_subcommand` traversal plus a
  markdown-code-span strip, so the gate documentation naming the bypass is not itself one,
  and a `FORBIDDEN` line is a documented counter-example. Its verb set is the one that HAS
  a sanctioned replacement — `edit` and `close` are spared for the same reason the hook
  spares them. The one real finding it had, the CI filer, now files through
  `scripts/ci/file_selection_audit_issue.py` keyed on a stable audit marker.
- **The raw CLI spelling of a note is denied too.** `hooks/scripts/raw_issue_write_guard`
  (matcher `teatree.hooks.raw_issue_write_detect`, registered in the shared
  `hard_deny_registry` so Lane A and Lane B refuse alike) hard-denies an INVOKED
  `gh issue comment` / `glab issue note` and names the purpose-routed remedy. The
  sibling `raw_review_post_guard` already covered the REST spelling of the same
  surface. Scoped to the note on purpose: the create / body-edit / close verbs have
  no complete sanctioned CLI replacement yet — the sweep's own fold runs
  `gh issue edit --body-file`, and a label-only `--add-label` is not a body write —
  so denying them would wedge documented workflows with nowhere to go. Reads and a
  tool that merely QUOTES the spelling pass; the gate is a narrow
  targeted-command one and fails OPEN.
- **Sweeps post zero comments, and the count is measured.** A `sweep_run_id` makes
  the facade refuse every comment purpose, and each changed issue URL is recorded
  once on an append-only `TicketSweepRun` (`ticket sweep-begin` / `sweep-finish` /
  `sweep-trend`). A zero-change run still persists a row — without it, a healthy
  factory and a sweep that never ran are indistinguishable. `t3 doctor` surfaces the
  series, the zero streak and any unfinished run, threshold-free by design.
- **The count is evidence, not a claim.** `PHASE_REQUIRED_EVIDENCE["backlog_sweep"]`
  is `ticket_sweep`, and `agents.ticket_sweep_recorder` checks the named run against
  the store: an unknown id, a run still open, or a `changed_count` disagreeing with
  the recorded URLs is refused under `MALFORMED_TICKET_SWEEP_PREFIX` — an envelope
  refusal, so the sweep earns `transient_requeue`'s one-shot corrective retry rather
  than paging a human. Absent on any other phase is a no-op.

---

## 10. Configuration

Runtime setting bounds, write history, doctor drift reports and the owner digest are specified in [the configuration appendix](docs/blueprint/configuration.md#101-configuration-store).
