**One declaration per setting default ([#4994](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4994)).** `UserSettings` fields carry value, parser, help, category and storage metadata in `SettingSpec`; cold and hook registry entries carry the same metadata. The pydantic schema, overlay parser map, help text and generated `defaults.toml` consume those declarations. The generated file is an export, not a resolution tier. `declared_keys.txt` records the full key namespace and requires an explicit retirement or rename when a key disappears. Tier model, effort and phase defaults are accessed through the registry instead of module-level copies.

**Compare instances ([#4994](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4994)).** A version-2 settings snapshot carries each instance's own default, resolution source, setting family, group and credential-key stubs; saved version-1 snapshots remain readable. The dashboard and `t3 settings compare` share filters for family, group, text, scope and differing rows. The initial view shows global-scope differences, and no cap hides a differing row. A peer value can be applied only through this instance's existing settings or seed writer when the shared sync classifier returns IMPORT or CLEAR; CLEAR deletes the local settings row, and Revert restores the prior local value or removes a row created by Apply. Peers are read-only, and withheld credential values never become copy controls.

**Sealed credentials.** `SealedSecret` stores instance entry names and AES-GCM ciphertext in the canonical control DB, including from worktree-isolated processes. `T3_MASTER_KEY_SOURCE` selects one master-key source; its default is `file:<control-db-dir>/master.key`, and in a deployed container (`TEATREE_ROLE` set) a key path that resolves inside the control-DB directory is refused for any write that would create it and warned for loads. The deployment mounts the key directory read-only from the host into exactly the services that mount the password store, outside the volume, so a whole-volume backup or export does not carry it. The file is created with mode 0600 only for the first write to an empty sealed table, which logs one WARNING naming its key_id. Existing rows with a missing, invalid or wrong key, or a row that fails authentication (named), make factory health critical and fail `t3 doctor`; otherwise doctor's OK line carries the active key_id. `t3 <overlay> secrets status|ls` and `secrets import-pass` without `--apply` are read-only; `secrets status` exits 1 on any key verdict other than OK, and the default import plan lists names and destinations. `secrets set|rm|verify` act on sealed rows whatever the selector says (`set` reads stdin or a hidden prompt; `verify` prints `ok|absent|unreadable` per referenced name, never a value, and exits 1 only when a name is unreadable); `secrets get` and `secrets exec` read through the active store — `get` prints one line to a pipe (exit 1 absent, 2 unreadable, 3 on a terminal, which `deploy/t3` also refuses on the host) and `exec` places values in the child's environment only. The secret-print detector denies an unredirected `secrets get`. `secrets import-pass --apply` copies every `row` entry from `pass` into sealed rows — names and statuses (`copied|updated|unchanged|empty|expired|failed`) only, backups beyond the retention window `expired`, `external` and `drop` entries untouched, nothing deleted from `pass` — writes the `SecretCutover` marker only after a second full read-back, exits 1 with no marker on any failure, and refuses when `T3_SECRET_STORE=sealed` or the key sits inside the control-DB volume. `T3_SECRET_STORE` (`pass` by default, or `sealed`; environment-only) selects the Python facade's store at first use: `sealed` reads and writes the rows with no fallback to `pass`, an unknown value raises naming the variable, and a store installed in code (tests) always wins. `secrets status` prints the selected store. Doctor resolves the sealed model only after Django bootstrap, keeping cold CLI imports safe. Sealing protects DB backups and exports of the control-DB volume, not a same-uid process in a service that mounts the key.

**Boot-time credentials — no plaintext secrets on disk ([#3454](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3454), [#3433](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3433)).** The headless deploy reads runtime secrets through `deploy/secret-read.sh`, which selects `pass` by default or sealed rows when `T3_SECRET_STORE=sealed`. The sealed path uses a Django-free, read-only canonical-DB reader with confirmed absent versus unreadable exits; host-only deploy commands leave sealed values for the container, which mounts the DB and key. The compose `env_file` (`deploy/teatree.env`) contains no plaintext secret values. `deploy/entrypoint.sh` sources `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN` only when `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN_PASS_PATH` explicitly names the bootstrap entry; there is no guessed GitHub path that can shadow an overlay's DB-routed `github_token_pass_key`. `T3_ADMIN_PASSWORD` retains its `T3_ADMIN_PASSWORD_PASS_PATH` override plus the box-layout default. An existing env value always wins, so a rotated secret is picked up on the next boot without storing it literally in `teatree.env`. The deploy workflow no longer writes those two secrets to the env file; `deploy/README.md` documents provisioning and rotation. <!-- skill-symbol-ref: teatree.env is gitignored — the deploy workflow writes it on the box, so it is absent from the tree by design -->

**Honest GitHub-token permission preflight ([#3436](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3436), expanded [#3477](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3477)).** `teatree.core.gates.gh_token_preflight` (mirrored in `deploy/entrypoint.sh`, pinned equal by a test) probes whether `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN` carries the permissions the loop uses, split into two tiers under a never-lockout invariant: a REQUIRED tier (`metadata: read`, `issues: write`, `pull_requests: write`, `contents: write` — unchanged from #3405) that hard-FAILs (`exit 1` / a doctor FAIL) when missing, and a RECOMMENDED tier (`workflows: write`, `actions: write`, `actions: read`, `checks: read`, `statuses: read`, `projects: read`, `secrets: write`, `variables: write`) that only WARNs with remediation — a running deployment missing a recommended permission still boots, with the gated feature (CI trigger/status, auto-merge's required-checks rollup, board sync, the `t3 eval ci-account` OAuth-account switch) simply degraded. It is token-class aware: a **fine-grained** PAT is judged by the per-route `403 Resource not accessible` probe (both tiers, with `workflows: write` deliberately left unprobed — the route's 403-vs-404 ordering for a denied token could not be empirically confirmed, so it is always reported as unprobeable rather than risking a false "missing"), but a **classic** PAT does NOT return that 403 (the probe would fail OPEN), so a classic token is judged entirely by scope membership from the `X-OAuth-Scopes` response header (`repo` required; `workflow`/`read:project` recommended). `projects: read` is probed only when the overlay configures a Projects v2 board. The bash mirror distinguishes a genuine REQUIRED-tier DENIAL (block the deploy loudly) from a TRANSIENT/indeterminate probe failure (retry with backoff, then warn and continue) so a GitHub/network blip can never crash-loop `init`; GitHub exposes no API to widen an existing token, so `format_remediation` always proposes a recreate, never an auto-add.

**Stranded pre-commit patches.** `prek` / `pre-commit` stash every unstaged change in the checkout before running hooks and restore them afterwards; the restore can FAIL (the tree moved on in between, so `git apply` reports `patch does not apply`) and the working tree is then left holding NONE of those changes — the edits are gone from disk, `git status` reads clean, and the only notice is one line in a hook log. The patch file is written on every commit with unstaged changes and never deleted, so its EXISTENCE is the normal steady state and carries no signal; `checks_stranded_prek_patches` therefore keys on CONTENT — a file-hunk whose EVERY added line is absent from the working-tree copy of that same file — bounded to a 24-hour window, since an abandoned edit and a lost one are indistinguishable once they age. Advisory (never gates the exit code), and it names the patch that still holds the work. The root cause is two lanes committing in ONE checkout, which one checkout per lane removes and nothing inside a shared checkout can. Both this check and the untracked-debris check beside it take their bearing from `cli/doctor/checkout_root.doctor_checkout_root` — the declared invocation cwd's checkout, else the installed clone, never `Path.cwd()`, which inside the container is the image's WORKDIR and no checkout at all, so a cwd-anchored check resolves no repository there and reports nothing while returning success ([#4727](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4727)). Their tests run from a cwd that is NOT the checkout for the same reason: one that chdirs in first measures a condition the deployed venue never has, and scores a no-op as passing. The debris check reads `status_porcelain_z_strict` so a swallowed `git status` error WARNs UNVERIFIED rather than reading as a clean tree, and `-z` names a path holding a space raw instead of C-quoted.

**Git-hook install completeness.** `teatree.core.gates.git_hooks_preflight` probes whether a checkout's `pre-commit` and `pre-push` hooks are actually installed — a `.git/hooks` holding only `*.sample` files is a silently-broken install of the same class as the token preflight above: nothing errors, but every push runs with the whole local gate layer absent (`scripts/hooks/refuse-public-push-with-leak.sh`, the banned-terms gate, and `dev/push-gate.sh` via the push-stage `ci-critical-parity` hook). The failure is not that hooks are never installed but that they land in a DIFFERENT clone than the one work happens in: a containerized `t3 setup` installs into the container clone while every commit and push comes from the host checkout, so a verdict drawn from the installed clone alone reads green while another clone pushes ungated. `teatree.core.gates.git_checkouts.discover_checkouts` therefore enumerates the checkouts no other mechanism covers — the installed clone, every checkout under the auto-isolated worktrees root (including ad-hoc ones created by a bare `git worktree add`), and the clone owning each — and `probe_checkouts` returns one verdict per git hooks dir, so a worktree family collapses onto its clone. It is Django-free, so setup and doctor can call it before `ensure_django`; a teatree-provisioned worktree already gets its hooks from `worktree provision`. Both `t3 setup` (`teatree.cli.setup.git_hooks_installer.GitHooksInstaller`, delegating to the existing `teatree.core.prek_hook.install`, which is idempotent by overwrite) and the doctor check `checks_bootstrap._check_git_hooks_installed` consume that one discovery, so the repair and the verification can never disagree; the doctor hard-FAILs per unprotected checkout, naming its path, the missing hook names, the gates each carries, and the `t3 setup` remediation. An explicitly set `core.hooksPath` pointing anywhere other than the default hooks dir is the operator's deliberate intent — reported and left untouched, never stomped and never counted as a gap. A hook prek did NOT write is destroyed by the `prek install -f` this probe triggers, so `teatree.core.hook_quarantine` writes its bytes into `<git-common-dir>/t3-hook-quarantine/` BEFORE that call and REFUSES the call outright when it cannot — durability precedes destruction, and after the park no raise, return or SIGKILL can lose them because nothing is carrying them. What survives is the hook's BYTES, not its DISPATCH: a foreign-config shim dropped at a hook type the root config does not declare is never rewritten, so the operator's `<name>.legacy` beside it stays on disk with nothing left to run it, and only a carried channel — the class this design deletes — could report that. A checkout whose shared git dir `git rev-parse` cannot answer for FAILS the install rather than running `-f` with nowhere to park, which also fails a transient git failure it cannot tell from "not a checkout". What was destroyed is reported by LISTING that directory at one chokepoint (`git_hooks_installer._qualified`, reading `GitHooksProbe.parked` — `quarantined` folded to `None` when the checkout could not be inspected at all — through the single sentence `hook_quarantine.parked_finding`, which the doctor and worktree provisioning emit verbatim so the three surfaces cannot word one finding three ways), never by a value returned out of the install: no line may say `OK` while a hook is parked, on the failing run OR on the re-run it advises, and `_check_git_hooks_installed` FAILs on the same fact for free. A `<name>.legacy` with no `<name>` beside it is parked and NEVER re-activated — measured, prek 0.4.11 deletes it and a resurrected one really does execute on every push, and the filesystem cannot say whether a shim was removed or the operator broke the chain deliberately.

**Generated-docs merge driver ([#3582](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3582), [#4259](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4259)).** The generated docs — `docs/generated/cli-reference.md`, the antipattern catalog, the management-commands list — collide on nearly every CLI-touching PR: two PRs each adding a `t3` subcommand touch adjacent lines of the same generated list, and a merge-queue rebase re-triggers the same conflict, after which `check_cli_reference_sync` fails until regenerated. The right resolution is a re-run of the file's generator, and **a merge driver cannot be that re-run**: git decides every content merge before it writes any of the merge result to the working tree, so the tree a driver would regenerate from is still the local (ours) side — measured with a control on a merge whose source files did not even conflict. A generator run there reproduces ours byte-for-byte, exits 0, and git records the doc as a CLEAN merge holding ours, dropping the other side with no marker; the outcome then also depends on whether this clone happened to run provisioning, so one merge yields two results. `.gitattributes` still marks these paths `merge=generated`, but `scripts/hooks/git_merge_generated.py` now resolves them by git's own textual 3-way merge (`git merge-file` into the `%A` slot — byte-identical to an unregistered clone, conflicted when the text genuinely conflicts) and writes a stderr advisory naming the generator to re-run once the merge completes. The CI sync gates (`check_cli_reference_sync`, `tests/eval_replay/test_readme_sync.py`) stay the backstop; the advisory is what makes a stale doc loud at merge time rather than red in CI. The driver's *command* lives in per-clone `.git/config` and is never committed, so `teatree.core.git_merge_driver.install_merge_driver` registers `merge.generated.driver` wherever teatree commits from: `t3 setup` walks the same `discover_checkouts` set as the git-hooks installer above (via `teatree.cli.setup.merge_driver_installer.GitMergeDriverInstaller`), and worktree provisioning registers it per worktree. Django-free and idempotent — a clone without the driver merges the same bytes, it just gets no advisory.

Control-plane Git subprocesses through `teatree.utils.run` pin `core.fsmonitor` off, and pin `core.hooksPath` off for verbs that do not need installed gate hooks or the real setting. `commit`, `merge`, `pull`, and `push` use the checkout's default hooks directory unless a system or global config sets a custom path. A checkout-local or command-line custom path refuses the write, so it cannot silently replace installed gates. An unreadable common directory also refuses the command. Pins follow caller global options and therefore take precedence over a caller `-c` setting. Git still executes hooks planted in the effective hooks directory on these write verbs, including `reference-transaction` and `post-commit`; other executable Git config such as `core.sshCommand`, `credential.helper`, filters and merge drivers remains outside these two pins. Its audit is tracked in [#5466](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5466). `config` and `rev-parse` retain the real hooks path for preflight checks. The stdlib-only agent hook probes carry the two off-pins, and the deploy shell scripts load `deploy/git-pins.sh` so a checkout’s ambient Git config cannot run commands during a control-plane probe or fast-forward. The deploy fast-forward also pins `pull.rebase=false` to preserve unrelated tracked dirt when the operator config requests rebase.

The **DB/TOML hard partition** ([#1775](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1775)) — every `UserSettings` field has exactly ONE home (the `ConfigSetting` DB store for the ~100 DB-home fields, including the per-overlay `workspace_dir` whose TOML value is ignored on read and whose unset default regroups worktrees under `~/workspace/t3-workspaces/<overlay>/` — resolved by `config.worktree_root()`, distinct from the CLONE root `config.clone_root()` at `~/workspace` where main repo clones live; the `UserSettings` partition is now **FULLY DB-home** (config-unify is complete for settings — the carve-out is EMPTY, so NO `UserSettings` field reads a config file; the last NON-settings config — the definition registries `REGISTRY_SETTINGS` (`[overlays]`, `[e2e_repos]`, `[peer_instances]`) — is DB-home too: each is one JSON-dict `ConfigSetting` row (`config/registries.py`) that `loader._inject_db_registries` overrides into `config.raw` via the Django-free `cold_reader`, so `discover_overlays` / `load_e2e_repos` / `load_peer_instances` are untouched and teatree boots fully DB-configured with no config file present) — config-unify retains the pre-Django cold-reader seam for live settings; `orchestrator_bash_gate_enabled` (the last per-overlay-TOML-overridable field — per-overlay override now lives in a `ConfigSetting` overlay-scope row, `TOML_OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE_SETTINGS` is empty; the gate reader `teatree_gate._gate_key_is_enabled` is DB-first via `cold_reader` with a TOML self-rescue fallback; its fellow-travellers `timezone` and `privacy` made the same move and were later retired reader-less by [#4203](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4203)), `handover_mirror_path` (its pre-Django SessionStart reader reads the store via `cold_reader`, which fails open to the same default bootstrap path `write_mirror` uses when unset, so it needs no TOML even when Django is down), `statusline_chain` (the bash statusline hook reads it from the canonical sqlite via the `sqlite3` CLI + `json_each`, so it needs no importable teatree python and no TOML parse), and `autoload` (the #256 engagement flag; its cold pre-Django readers — `hooks/scripts/teatree_settings.autoload_enabled` via `_cold_db_bool` and `statusline.sh` via the `sqlite3` CLI — read the store DB-ONLY, so a `[teatree] autoload` TOML value is ignored on read and the SessionStart how-to advisory points at `config_setting set autoload true`), and finally the last two — the nested structured tables `speak` / `mr_reminder` (stored as JSON-dict `ConfigSetting` rows via `parse_speak_setting` / `parse_mr_reminder_setting`, rebuilt bespoke by the resolver since a dict cannot flat-replace the dataclass field — `resolution._BESPOKE_STRUCTURED_FIELDS`, with `speak` keeping its per-overlay MERGE; the stored `speak` row is parsed through `LocalPlayback.parse`) to the DB, env on top of either — Django settings, `OverlayConfig` methods, logging, data storage, and the state-placement rule (cache vs intent, #628) live in [docs/blueprint/configuration.md](docs/blueprint/configuration.md). A DB-home value is set with `t3 <overlay> config_setting set <key> <json> [--overlay <name>]` (migrate a pre-partition file once with `config_setting import`); a value left in `[teatree]` / `[overlays.<name>]` for a DB-home key is ignored on read. Its override table also documents the DB-home knobs: `mr_title_regex` (#1540, the Conventional-Commits title pattern the `pr create` gate enforces, no `--force` bypass), the `autonomy` switch (#1668, collapsing those gates into one value, set via `t3 <overlay> autonomy set`), and the `wip` throughput dial (`slow < medium < full < boost`, orthogonal to `mode`/`autonomy`). The `### 10.1 Configuration store` subsection cited from `commands/followup.py` is preserved there. **Toward DB-only runtime (config-unify PR1).** `teatree.config.cold_reader` is a Django-free, stdlib-only reader of the same `teatree_config_setting` store for the cold bash/statusline path that cannot afford a Django import — the deliberate inverse of `teatree.paths.resolve_data_dir`: it always targets the PRIMARY `~/.local/share/teatree/db.sqlite3` (a worktree checkout reads the installed `t3`'s config, not an isolated per-worktree copy), with a test pinning its duplicated path computation equal to `teatree.paths.TRUE_CANONICAL_DB`. Every read fails open to the caller's default. Every read goes through one WAL-aware sqlite path (`cold_db.fetch_one`; the Django-free sqlite plumbing — path resolution, read-only open, single-row fetch, `row_exists` — lives in `teatree.config.cold_db`, re-exported through `cold_reader`). Its cold callers are the `teatree_settings` gate readers AND the fast Stop/PreToolUse hooks, so no hook pays a per-call `t3` subprocess that cold-boots Django on the synchronous hot path. There is no posture read left to make: [#3826](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3826) deleted the mirror file the probe used to obey (a second source of truth that drifted a week out of date and silently muted the owner), and [#4202](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4202) deleted the posture booleans themselves along with the Django-free resolver that read them — a mode is a per-loop on/off table, so no hook asks the control DB what it MEANS. No teatree hook runs on `UserPromptSubmit`, so a prompt never waits on teatree or Django: the live turn and the owner's words are read from the session transcript, per-turn state resets at `Stop` (or at the first tool call after a newer owner prompt, since an interrupted turn fires no `Stop`), an answer to a session's own question, from any channel, is handed back at that session's next `SessionStart` or `Stop` (`teatree.answer_handback`), the question and Slack-DM backlogs are read on demand, and what a prompt hook used to carry — the cold-tier memory recall and the standing directives now due — rides the first tool call after the owner's prompt instead, written by `hooks/scripts/owner_turn_context.py`, a Django-free `PostToolUse` process bounded at 0.3 s that serves each owner prompt once. **Lossless cold-hook import (config-unify PR2).** `teatree.config.registries.COLD_HOOK_SETTINGS` registers the pre-Django, hook-only global `[teatree]` keys that have no `UserSettings` field — the `teatree_settings` gate kill-switches (`brief_anchor_gate_enabled`, …) plus the bespoke `hook_router` integer budgets (`orchestrator_turn_budget`, `orchestrator_turn_wall_clock_seconds`). `import_toml_to_db` now unions it with `OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE_SETTINGS` for the GLOBAL `[teatree]` table only, so a non-default gate or threshold survives the TOML→DB import instead of resetting to its in-code default when a later PR flips the cold readers onto the store. `config_setting` get/set/clear now handle these keys as part of the unified known-key set (each is folded into `ALL_KNOWN_CONFIG_SETTINGS` by its parser), and the cold readers are DB-only: `hooks/scripts/teatree_settings` resolves each flag from the `ConfigSetting` store via `cold_reader` and falls back to the compiled-in `ColdHookSetting.default` when no row exists, so the never-lockout fallback reads no file. A no-silent-drop fitness test mechanically enumerates the live cold-read sites, so a new hook gate flag added without a registry entry turns the suite red. **TOML round-trip export (config-unify PR6).** `teatree.core.config_interchange.migration.export_db_to_toml` is the precise inverse of `import_toml_to_db`: it serialises the `ConfigSetting` store back to TOML — GLOBAL rows under `[teatree]`, each overlay scope under `[overlays.<name>]`, each JSON-stored value rendered as its native TOML scalar — so `export → import → export` is a fixed point and TOML is a full round-trip interchange format (no longer import-only). The load side is `import_toml_to_db(text, dry_run)` + `t3 <overlay> config_setting import [--input <path>] [--dry-run]`: it REJECTS unknown and secret/personal-identifier keys (reusing the export withhold rule and refusing the WHOLE import so a bad key never leaves a partial store), validates every value through the same registry parser the resolver applies on read (`ALL_KNOWN_CONFIG_SETTINGS`), and writes NO row for a value equal to the shipped default — so a dump of `defaults.toml` imports to zero rows and `restore = delete row` holds. Export key-sorts every table/scope so the dump is a deterministic function of the store's CONTENT, which is what makes the round-trip byte-stable. The `t3 <overlay> config_setting export [--overlay <name>] [--output <path>]` subcommand dumps to stdout by default; `--overlay` scopes the dump to one overlay. Only `ConfigSetting` rows are read, so bootstrap-file-only and secret-ref keys never materialise in the dump. **Byte-identical `defaults.toml` round trip + nested file shape ([#3825](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3825)).** The store holds only DELTAS from the shipped floor, so the dump and `defaults.toml` were not inverses — importing the shipped file writes zero rows and exporting them emits no `[teatree]` table. Two INDEPENDENT filters make the inverse expressible: `default_keys_only` restricts what is ELIGIBLE to the `Category.DEFAULT` key set (dropping registries, secrets, personal identifiers and every overlay scope) and `include_defaults` widens WHICH eligible keys are emitted from divergent-only to all of them, filling a key with no DB row from `teatree.config.provenance.resolve_settings` restricted to the PERSISTED tiers (`env` and the active overlay's code defaults are machine/overlay state a shipped file must not bake in). Both default off, so an unfiltered dump emits exactly the rows it always has (the nesting below re-shapes how every surface RENDERS them, but adds and drops nothing); both on is the defaults shape, whose emitted key set is asserted EQUAL to the `Category.DEFAULT` set (equality, not a subset — a subset check still lets a key vanish) and which renders through `defaults_snapshot.render_toml`, the SAME emitter the owner-approved `snapshot_settings_defaults` writes with. `export(defaults-shape)` after `import(defaults.toml)` therefore reproduces the file byte for byte, header and seed tables included. The file renders the declaration hierarchy as real NESTED TOML tables while the KEY NAMESPACE stays flat — that namespace is the persisted contract every env override, `ConfigSetting` row and cold sqlite3 read depends on — and `config/cold_defaults.flatten_settings_table`, the single Django-free reader, flattens the group wrappers back on read (a sub-table named after a declared setting, `speak` / `mr_reminder`, is that setting's value; any other is a group). Import applies the same flattening, so a nested file and a flat one resolve identically. The seed-table half of both directions lives in `teatree.core.config_interchange.seed_tables`. **Export-leak guard — secrets MAY live in the DB (config-unify, PR1).** The earlier rule that banned-terms/brands carry **NO DB tier** is **superseded**: the DB store is private to the operator, so a customer/brand secret LIVING in the DB is fine — the leak surface is the **EXPORT** (the future "auto-configure a fresh teatree for a new user" path), not the storage. `export_db_to_toml` therefore applies a **secret guard** that withholds a row by DEFAULT under two complementary defenses (neither sufficient alone): (1) a per-setting `private` flag expressed as membership in `teatree.config.secret_settings.SECRET_SETTINGS` (`banned_term_registry`, `private_repos`, the token refs) — a static denylist; and (2) an active **banned-term content scan** over each exported `key + json(value)` (terms resolved from the live `banned_term_registry`; a store holding no ban-class term refuses the export and the snapshot, while the import proceeds unscanned with every value shown withheld and says so), which catches a NON-listed key whose value happens to embed a customer/brand term — the case a keylist can never enumerate. `export_db_to_toml` returns a `ConfigExport(toml, redacted)` carrying the withheld `RedactedRow(scope, key, reason)` set; `t3 <overlay> config_setting export` warns each withheld row + a summary on stderr, and `--include-private` selects a personal backup that still withholds each row carrying a banned term; `--include-banned-term-values` (with `--include-private` and `--output`) carries those too and needs no term; it uses the terms it finds only to keep a term-carrying name out of its omitted-row report, and names no omitted row on a box with none. A scope or entry name carrying a term withholds everything under it, reported without the name. Every report names the row and class, never the matched term. **A private backup is restorable, and says so ([#4156](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4156)).** `--include-private` emits exactly the rows the import refuses, so the file it wrote was one nothing could read back and nothing said so — the flag whose purpose is a COMPLETE backup produced an unrestorable one. The fix keeps the fail-closed row rule and adds the file-level half of the same guard in `config_interchange.secret_guard`: such a dump stamps itself with a reserved `[backup] include_private = true` table (`stamp_private_backup` / `is_private_backup`), the export names on stderr what it wrote and how to restore it, and `t3 <overlay> config_setting import --restore-private` accepts the private rows of THAT file only — `restore_private` grants nothing unless the document also declares itself a backup, so an ordinary shared dump's secret rows are refused under the flag exactly as without it, and every other caller (the dashboard included, which points at the CLI instead) keeps the default-False posture. The marker is an intent declaration, not a security boundary; what the guard rests on is the DEFAULT import refusing every private row whatever the file claims. Only the SHARED defaults shape stays byte-identical to `defaults.toml`; every private export, defaults shape included, carries the restore marker, and an unshipped loop, preset or schedule is omitted (reported as not shipped, by name only where a term scan clears the name) so the backup restores with `--restore-private`. This guard is the prerequisite that lets a later slice move the secret lists themselves into the DB (closing the last TOML carve-out) without a shared export ever leaking them. The pre-push scanner alignment still holds: `scripts/privacy_scan.py` resolves via the shared `teatree.hooks.banned_terms_cli.resolve_banned_terms` (env override → `[teatree].banned_terms` TOML, fail-CLOSED — a present-but-unset/unreadable config reports the detector INERT on stderr rather than degrading to a silent empty ban list), and the banned-terms GATE flag (`banned_terms_gate_enabled`) is already DB-first via the cold reader (PR3).

**A key whose reader resolves no overlay declares itself box-global.** `SettingMeta` carries a `box_global` marker beside its category and registry; `config.setting_registries.BOX_GLOBAL_SETTINGS` collects the per-setting declarations marked `box_global=True`. It names the loop-timer keys whose scanner is built with `overlay=""` or whose reader is `cadence_seconds()` — a loop timer is one clock per box, so a per-overlay row is either unreachable or decided by whichever overlay the process happens to be. It narrows the SCOPE only: the key stays a DB-home `UserSettings` field and a GLOBAL row resolves through the ordinary chain. The refusal is at the WRITE seam (`ConfigSetting.objects.set_value` / `set_values`, via `reject_unhonourable_overlay_scope`), which is the only way such a row comes to exist, so the resolver never has to decide what an unhonourable row means. A cadence read through `scanner_factories`' per-overlay fan-out (`architectural_review_cadence_hours`, `pull_main_clone_cadence_hours`) IS honourable per overlay and stays scoped.

**The settings dashboard renders one section per request, and names value PROVENANCE ([#3825](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3825)).** `/dash/settings/` rendered every schema key at once — 272 `<form>`, 1,060 `<input>` (812 hidden), 271 CSRF tokens, 260KB, 14,212px tall and wider than the viewport. Sections now sit on the LEFT and the selected section's rows on the RIGHT: `dash.settings_editor.build_settings_sections` derives the nav from the group tree over the key NAMES (no value resolution), `build_settings_group` resolves one section's rows, and the nav `hx-get`s `dash:settings_group` into the detail pane. The per-row `hx-target="closest tr"` swap is unchanged (an edit never re-renders the document), `key`/`scope` moved into the `hx-post` URL instead of hidden fields, and the body's `hx-headers` carries ONE CSRF token per page. The never-drop guarantee moved with the layout: the partition is total, so every schema key is reachable through exactly one section — asserted over the whole section list rather than within one page. The near-constant `category` column (the setting's KIND, `default` for hundreds of rows running, misread as "this value came from the default") is replaced by `teatree.config.provenance`, which walks the resolver's OWN layer readers (`read_setting_layers` / `env_setting_overrides` / `overlay_code_defaults`) instead of folding them, so a row's value and the tier that supplied it — env / DB overlay scope / DB global scope / overlay code default / shipped file / code default — can never disagree. Import is a file upload rather than a textarea; the dry-run preview and the safety-posture confirm phrase are unchanged, and both live on the `/dash/import-export/` page rather than here.

**The settings dashboard is a comparison GRID, and its help text is authored once ([#3880](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3880)).** A row is one SETTING with its shipped default and then one cell per scope — `global` first, then each overlay — so a value that diverges between scopes is one line rather than a per-scope hunt (the class of divergence that muted the owner for a week: a stale mirror holding one posture while the DB held another). A cell is green while it equals the default and brown once someone has changed it, with the icon and words kept beside the colour so the verdict survives greyscale; each section in the nav carries a brown count of the settings in it that DIFFER, counting a setting ONCE however many scopes override it. Provenance moved from a column to the cell's tooltip and *restore default* is gone — with click-to-edit, emptying a cell IS the restore, and the cell posts itself on change with no save button, swapping the whole `<tr>` back because a global write changes what every overlay column inherits. Anything the schema constrains to a listable set (`bool`, an enum, a `Literal`) renders as a SELECT derived from the field's own annotation (`schema.setting_choices`), so an invalid value is impossible to ENTER rather than rejected afterwards. Help text lives once in `config/setting_help.py` — Django- and pydantic-free, because the shipped file is written from the cold path — and renders both as the tooltip here and as the tail of the trailing comment beside that key in `config/defaults.toml`, with the byte-identical round trip still green and `tests/config/test_setting_help.py` pinning the table TOTAL over the schema. That comment LEADS with what the key ACCEPTS ([#4053](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4053)) — `config/setting_annotation.py` names the stored type off the field annotation and pairs it with the alternatives from the SAME `schema.setting_choices` the select above is built from, written out as one line there and nowhere else — so a dump reviewed away from this page answers "may this take any string?" without the reader opening the schema, and the two surfaces cannot come to disagree about what a key admits. The type naming sits beside the rendering rather than in `schema` for the reason `setting_choices` gives about its own values: how a stored form READS is a surface decision (a `dict` is a `table` in the file), and `tests/teatree_config/test_setting_annotation.py` pins it TOTAL over the schema so a shape it cannot name never ships silently. The page's query cost is now the number of SCOPES and still constant in the number of settings.

**Every dashboard read is bounded by the page, never by table size ([#3873](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3873)).** `/dash/health/` took 21.3s and `/dash/tickets/<id>/` answered 5,005,822 bytes, so a card read as one that would not open. Neither surface had changed — `TicketTransition` reached 2.96M rows and `TaskAttempt` 340k, and code that reads a whole table crosses the threshold silently. Four reads are bounded at the source. The cycle-to-date spend chip aggregates in SQL (`TaskAttemptQuerySet.usage_groups` groups on `(model, lane, phase, estimated, reported?)`, `CostBreakdown.from_groups` totals the buckets, and `from_usages` funnels through the SAME accumulator so the per-row and aggregate spellings cannot compute different numbers) behind the `taskattempt_cost_cover` covering index, so the scan reads an index rather than a table whose rows carry the error/result payloads. The drawer keeps the newest `TRANSITION_ROWS` / `TASK_ROWS` / `ATTEMPT_ROWS` of each history — the attempt cap a SQL window, since a prefetch that fetches thousands to render ten is the same unbounded read one level down — and each panel STATES the total it truncated from, because a silent truncation is the same defect wearing a different hat. `build_ticket_lifecycle_mermaid` draws DISTINCT edges: a state diagram is a set, so the worst ticket's 22,965 rows are 10 arrows. And a polled panel carries `hx-sync` while a board card carries a stable `id`, so a slow render cannot start a second poll over itself or re-morph the element under the cursor. `RequestScopedReadCacheMiddleware` + `teatree.request_cache` collapse the repeated settings / overlay-discovery / preset / loop reads one render issues into one each, memoized only inside an explicitly entered scope (the CLI and the loop tick are untouched) and dropped by ANY non-SELECT statement — keying invalidation on the SQL rather than on model signals is what keeps read-your-writes true for a bulk `update()`. Django 6.1's `QuerySet.fetch_mode()` with `FETCH_PEERS` batches lazy relation loads, and a manager can apply it per model, but every dashboard page already renders with zero lazy loads (verified under `FETCH_RAISE`), so the explicit tools do the work and `FETCH_PEERS` is applied only where a measured N+1 exists. The 5s-polled outcomes tail takes the newest `LIVE_OUTCOME_ROWS` finished attempts, which no index led on, so every poll scanned `TaskAttempt` whole and sorted it into a temp B-tree — 28.9ms at 13,749 rows, growing with the table; the partial `taskattempt_recent_ended` on `(-ended_at, -id)` over finished rows turns that into a `SEARCH` with no sort (0.13ms), and `build_recent_activity` reads the same way. Every page's plan is pinned by `assertNumQueries` at two populated sizes, so an absolute count AND its flatness both turn red; the index is pinned by its query PLAN instead, since a count cannot see a sort.

**Cycle time is measured continuously, not by hand ([#3847](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3847)).** [#3994](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3994) produced the per-transition median table once, from a hand-written query, and it named `planned -> coded` and `reviewed -> shipped` as 71% of a 210-minute cycle — a bottleneck visible only by hand is one nobody looks at until after something has gone wrong. `core/cycle_time/` answers it from recorded rows: `pull_request_flow.py` measures the earliest current admission stamp to merge and open pull request age in seconds, filters merges to the selected window, and feeds the factory signal and the pull request band on `/dash/cycle-time/`; `spans.py` walks each ticket's `TicketTransition` chain over `state_edges()` and measures every `from_state -> to_state` edge against its predecessor's timestamp; `distribution.py` reduces a window to median AND p90 per edge (a median alone hides the tail, and the tail is what blocks a delivery day) plus the same edge bucketed over time; `timeline.py` splits each span into the stretch an agent HELD the phase and the remainder it merely waited, since a phase starved of dispatches and a phase an agent ground on for an hour are different problems. **The cycle-time work split never subtracts `TaskAttempt.started_at`.** That column is `auto_now_add`, and every attempt row is INSERTED at agent completion, so `ended_at - started_at` is ~0 for every row the factory has ever written — a work-time built on it reports that no agent ever worked. The per-attempt figure on the live, ticket and sessions pages times from `Task.admitted_at` through `TaskAttempt.display_started_at`, never before the same task's previous attempt ended, and keeps the insert-to-end figure only where no admission can be measured (no stamp, or one overwritten after the attempt ended). Work time comes from `Task.admitted_at` (the runner-handoff stamp) paired with the attempt's completion stamp, merged across overlapping tasks so parallel dispatches into one phase are one elapsed stretch rather than double-counted; the span split added no new column, because those figures were already recorded for another purpose. The pull request clock uses the existing `Task.admitted_at` stamp, falling back to the first ticket transition and then the pull request creation stamp when no admitted task remains. `admitted_at` is stamped going forward only, so a span where an attempt demonstrably ENDED yet no task carries the stamp reports its split as UNMEASURED rather than as zero work — calling an unmeasurable stretch "queue wait" would be the same fiction reached by a different route, and it is the shape most historical rows have. Spend rides alongside through the shared `AttemptUsage` seam, carrying the estimated share separately so a price-table guess is never rendered as a reported figure. `/dash/cycle-time/` (nav item *Cycle time*) draws it: the aggregate table, a trend line per slowest edge, and one stacked bar per recently-active ticket whose segments carry the queue/work split, with that ticket's spend beside them marked estimated or reported. The charts are inline SVG whose geometry `dash/charts.py` computes — no charting library is introduced, every mark inherits the FSM rail's `--col-hue` through the same `[data-group]` attribute the board columns use, and every coordinate is assertable from a plain test rather than locked inside a canvas. All bars share ONE scale and all trend series share one vertical scale, because per-bar normalisation would draw a 3-minute edge and an 85-minute one identically and erase the comparison the page exists for. Its query plan is pinned flat like every other page.

**A merge's elapsed time is attributed to one cause per minute ([#5313](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5313)).** From the earliest recorded task admission to the first merge transition, `core/cycle_time/waits.py` unions agent engagements, then assigns the remaining time in precedence order to a pending human answer, a sibling task holding the checkout, a checkout fence refusal, a quota park, a task waiting for its eventual admission seat, a still-pending task not admitted for an unrecorded cause, review, or the no-next-task remainder that makes the parts sum to elapsed time. The longest wait is the longest uninterrupted block, with its start and duration, rather than the largest aggregate cause. An unbilled park is not agent work; a billed park is a real run. Work ends at the last real attempt, or at the first park when no real attempt follows admission; a cancel row never leaves work open-ended. A park wait ends at the task’s recorded terminal attempt, so a cancelled, failed or completed task cannot claim the remaining time through merge. A terminal task with park history but no recorded terminal attempt makes the split unmeasured. A terminal task without admission claims no not-admitted wait. Readmission overwrites `Task.admitted_at`, so a ticket whose earlier run is visible but whose first admission is lost reports UNMEASURED rather than a precise split. The checkout park result carries the complete holder identity; a holder on the same ticket marks a sibling wait, and the read model never parses a reason string. An owner checkout-attestation question remains a fence wait. The last 10 merges appear on Cycle time and a summary appears on the Board. Park rows are retained for seven days, so older histories are marked incomplete rather than presented as precise. The headline intervention figure counts recorded hand cancellations between the earliest surviving admission and merge even when the time split is unmeasured; a cancel before that admission is excluded. Hand-created root tasks on each ticket's first session, excluding its first planning task, are shown separately as an inference that may include false positives; other unrecorded actions keep the recorded count a lower bound.

**Agent transcripts have a home in the nav ([#3873](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3873)).** `/dash/transcript/<session_id>/` always answered 200 but was linked only from a ticket's drawer, so reading a transcript meant already knowing which ticket to open. `/dash/sessions/` (nav item *Sessions*) lists the most recent `SESSION_ROWS` agent sessions — one row per session, de-duplicated by a SQL window over `agent_session_id` — each linking to its redacted tail, and the transcript page's nav lands back on it. The index renders dispatch facts only and never an attempt's `error` body; the tail routes every line through `redact_for_local_display`, pinned by a Django-test-client test asserting a configured banned term never appears in either page's response bytes. Each row also carries the skill bundle its dispatch recorded, on the shared `dash/partials/_skills.html` rule.

**The dashboard answers "what is running right now" ([#3856](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3856), [#3886](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3886)).** The board renders ticket FSM state, which is a LAGGING view — a refused loop and a loop that swept and found nothing both leave it unchanged, so the single question an operator asks most often about an autonomous system had no surface. `/dash/live/` (nav item *Live*, read model `dash.live.build_live_view`) is that surface, four panels over state that is ALREADY recorded rather than re-derived at render time: **running** — `TaskAttempt` rows with no `ended_at`, carrying phase, execution target, lane, model, elapsed and the transcript link; **queue** — the PENDING/CLAIMED `Task` counts in ONE grouped read (two `count()` calls would report an instant that never existed) plus what is next; **loops** — every REGISTERED mini-loop with its cadence anchors and, when the live tick would refuse it, the tick's OWN reason, because `loops.loop_table.loop_block_reasons` is the read-only projection of the same `_admission_block` the fan-out gates on and can therefore never drift into a second vocabulary; and **outcomes** — a short tail of ENDED attempts with usage-window parks marked, so a park storm is visible while it happens. Polled with the board's `hx-sync` + `morph:innerHTML` pair and pinned by `assertNumQueries` at two populations like every other page. It renders dispatch facts ONLY and never an attempt's `error` body — the transcript click-through remains the one surface showing agent output — pinned by a test asserting a configured banned term never reaches the response bytes. Every surface listing a task also renders the skill bundle the dispatch RECORDED (`TaskAttempt.skills_loaded`), through one shared rule (`dash.skills.skill_bundle` + `dash/partials/_skills.html`): the bundle is assembled from sources that can each silently contribute nothing — phase frontmatter, cwd detection, the transitive `requires:` chain against a cached index, the overlay's companions — so an EMPTY bundle on a headless dispatch renders as a fault chip rather than as blank, while an interactive attempt (which resolves no bundle by construction) is exempt. Re-derivation at render time is deliberately not done: it would report today's answer for yesterday's dispatch, the exact bug class the panel exists to expose. A ticket's PR chips ([#3909](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3909)) render the row's human state label and carry that state as a class, so a merged, a review-waiting and an abandoned PR do not read as three identical chips; `PullRequest.State` gained the terminal `CLOSED` member that made the third of those expressible, and the teardown gate — which already re-reads every non-terminal row's live forge state — now RECORDS that verdict (`PullRequest.objects.settle_forge_state`, terminal verdicts only, never on the fail-open `UNKNOWN`) instead of discarding it, so an abandoned PR stops advertising live work and a settled row is not re-probed on every later pass.

**The default board answers who acts next on each open PR ([#5007](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5007)).** `/dash/board/` polls a PR-centric read model every four seconds. Each successful tick records a verified, per-source open-PR snapshot: `PrSweepScanner` covers watched follow-up repositories and `MyPrsScanner` covers the owner’s PRs across its allowed hosts; incomplete reads preserve the prior entries while marking freshness unknown. The board joins those observations with `PullRequest`, `ReviewRequestPost`, pending owner questions, and task/attempt rows, then groups cards by next actor and action. CI, approvals, threads, age, review re-asks, and factory activity show their recorded values or an explicit unknown. CI, approvals and threads come from the observing tick alone: `PrSweepScanner` takes the review decision from the forge listing plus, on GitHub, the standing approval count and batched unresolved-thread count (the required approval count stays unknown); GitLab sweep listings leave approval and thread counts unknown. `MyPrsScanner` reads approvals as count of required where the host reports them, and unresolved threads, from the host for green PRs. Unknown or stale freshness blocks a merge-ready claim. A verified sweep hides an absent recorded PR only when that sweep watched its repository. The board's next-action decision and the tick's stall recognition live together in `core.review.pr_next_action`. Each `t3 loops tick` report (human and `open_prs` JSON) lists every PR its scanners observed with age and next task, PRs with lifecycle rows first, then oldest first, and `loop.pr_stall` acts on each PR stalled on a cause the factory repairs by debugging (red CI, a conflict, requested changes; a draft park or a sweep fault is a blocker, never a stall) (`stall_repairs` JSON, `STALL` lines): a head-scoped `RedMrFixAttempt` claim permits at most one `debugging` task per PR, head, and repair kind, with none while any task of the ticket is in flight; otherwise it records an internal `DeferredQuestion` once per PR and cause naming why no repair is safe. A PR no author ticket owns is listed and never acted on. FSM-derived phase eligibility controls both the card and drawer buttons, and the task-action POST enforces the same rule. The ticket lifecycle board remains at `/dash/board/lifecycle/`, with every column counted in the database and paged independently at 50 cards (`page_<state>`), and its backlog and delivered columns collapsed initially; its in-column dwell is shown only to the minute.

**The shipped posture is autonomous by default, and every raise is a named authorization ([#3895](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3895)).** `config/defaults.toml` is the rendered EXPORT of the declarations, not a tier the resolver reads (`resolution.effective_default` answers from the declaration itself), so a dataclass-default edit and the shipped file move together or not at all. On the owner's explicit authorization the file ships `autonomy = full`, `mode = auto`, `wip = full`, and the directive loop available, with `issue_implementer` and `triage_assessor` admitted by the shipped preset: a fresh install intakes and delivers without an operator opt-in. Both tiers move together by construction: `[teatree]` is RENDERED from the in-code declarations (`config/declared_defaults.py`), so a shipped value cannot diverge from its dataclass default at all and there is no divergence gate left to satisfy. `autonomy` is therefore raised by moving BOTH tiers, and the raise is visible in exactly one place: `tests/config/test_defaults_file.py::TestSafetyAndDarkFlagsPinned` carries `_FAIL_CLOSED` (what safe MEANS per safety-posture key) beside `_PINNED` (what ships), and refuses any key whose shipped literal leaves its fail-closed value without an `_OWNER_RAISED` entry naming the authorization. The completeness assertion is untouched — `_PINNED` still covers `SAFETY_POSTURE_KEYS | dark_flags()` exactly, and every dark flag's literal must equal its own `off_value`. The raise is DECOUPLED from colleague egress: egress is decided by the active mode's `egress` posture, which no tier reaches, on the #3630 argument one gate over — "carry the work end to end" is a different decision from speaking to a colleague under the owner's own identity with no per-action approval. The posture is box-global rather than a settings key, so there is no per-overlay tier for a raise to collapse and none of this reasoning is a pin the resolver could rewrite. The shipped `present` preset carries `egress = "allow"`, so colleague egress is OPEN at every tier as shipped; closing it is selecting a posture that forbids it, per action rather than per loop. The shipped table declares `egress = "forbid"` on exactly two — `afk` and `maintenance`; `token-outage` and `off` both carry `allow`, so neither closes the owner's voice, and whether `off` should is [#4703](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4703). What did NOT move: `require_human_approval_to_merge` (#3630 — no tier collapses it, and neither does the raised default), colleague egress (#3895, above — the posture sits outside the tiers entirely), the DARK safety gates, and every always-on floor (independent cold review, CI-green-before-merge, the leak gate, the recorded-approver keystone).

**Setting taxonomy (B11).** `teatree.config.setting_taxonomy` classifies every live config key as a `feature-flag`, `gate`, `gate-switch`, `safety-posture`, `cold`, `registry`, or computed `plain` key. It reads the registries that own those classes and `ALL_KNOWN_CONFIG_SETTINGS`; a key can hold several classes at once. `tests/config/test_setting_taxonomy.py` pins each class to its registry, and `governance_trailer` renders the result beside `config_setting set`/`get` values.

**Feature flags vs. settings (T4-PR-1).** A *setting* is a durable knob the operator tunes and keeps; a *feature flag* is a TEMPORARY switch that gates in-flight code and is meant to DIE — born with the code it guards, removed once that code is trusted (or abandoned). A future flag uses a `UserSettings` bool or typed-mode field and resolve their VALUE identically through the untouched `env → ConfigSetting(overlay) → ConfigSetting(global) → dataclass-default` chain; the code-level registry `teatree.config.feature_flags.FEATURE_FLAGS` only DISCRIMINATES a flag from a setting, GOVERNS its lifecycle, and AUDITS dead toggles — it never becomes a second value tier (the value lives in the `ConfigSetting` store; the registry lives in code, because a flag is born and dies with the code it gates). Each `FeatureFlag` carries a `stage` (`FlagStage`: `DARK` → `SETTLING` → `REMOVE`), a `tracking_issue`, a `summary`, and an `off_value` (`False` for a positive-sense `*_enabled` flag, `True` for an inverted-sense `*_disabled` one). The **lifecycle** runs `DARK` (gated code in flight, ships OFF, enabled per-install for a trial) → `SETTLING` (trusted, default-enable imminent, flag survives as an escape hatch) → `REMOVE` (gated code permanent, the toggle is dead weight to delete). This is the typed-registry-plus-fitness-test idiom of `SETTING_HOMES` / `registries.COLD_HOOK_SETTINGS`: the conformance suite `tests/config/test_feature_flags.py` keeps every entry honest — each names a real bool-or-typed-mode `UserSettings` field registered in `OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE_SETTINGS` (registration-drift closed like `registries.COLD_HOOK_SETTINGS`), carries a non-empty `tracking_issue` and a valid `stage`, and — the load-bearing Goodhart guard — a `DARK` flag's dataclass default MUST equal its `off_value`, so a dark feature can never ship default-ON without a code-reviewed stage demotion. Surfaces: `t3 config show` partitions flags OUT of the user-facing `intent` dump into a stage-labelled `flags` section (a temporary switch never reads as a knob to keep); `t3 <overlay> config_setting set|get <key>` appends the §Setting taxonomy governance trailer, which names the flag's stage and tracking issue among every other class the key holds; and `t3 <overlay> config_setting flags` is the read-only dead-toggle audit report that surfaces a `REMOVE`-stage flag LOUD. The live registry is empty after the former gate flags became unconditional; stage discrimination is tested over a mixed fixture so the lifecycle checks remain non-vacuous. A future graduation is a real code change: move its stage, rewrite its summary, and pin the default-ON value and exit from `dark_flags()` in conformance checks. `dark_flags()` is the query hook a later self-catching critic uses to tie a dark flag to the done-means-merged status of the code it gates. **Classification is mandatory, not optional:** every `require_*` `UserSettings` toggle must appear exactly once in `FEATURE_FLAGS` or in `DURABLE_GATE_SETTINGS` (the sibling frozenset naming the gate toggles that are durable operator policy — each gates a complete capability whose satisfying artifact has a producer command, or is a standing doctrine knob). An unclassified toggle turns the conformance suite RED; that hole is how a gate ships default-off and reviewed by nothing while its ON state would block every delivery.

Local text-to-speech (#2060): `teatree.core.speak.deliver_user_dm()` handles on-behalf self-DMs, while `deliver_user_dm_sidecar()` enriches already-posted `notify_user` DMs, gated on macOS `say`, driven by the DB-home `speak` setting. `local` controls speaker playback (`off`/`dm`; legacy `all` resolves to `dm`) and the independent `slack` bool attaches audio to the DM. Playback is mutually exclusive via a bounded-wait cross-process `flock` so fan-out reads never overlap or backlog (#2152/#2156), and `clean_for_speech()` strips markdown/URLs/emoji and drops status/log lines so `say` reads prose, not "Info" preamble (#277). **Meeting-aware mute + question-mirror parity (#2171):** the playback call site silences local `say` while a configured presence backend (`[teatree.speak] presence_backend`, `teatree.core.presence` + `teatree.backends.msteams.presence` over MS Graph `/me/presence`) reports the user in a meeting — Slack arm and configured `local` untouched, unknown/unreachable never suppresses; and a loop-driven AskUserQuestion carries audio to the phone too, via `notify_user`'s own speak sidecar on the delivery drain ([#4673](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4673) retired the separate mirror enricher and its `speak-dm` worker). Config: `SpeakConfig`/`LocalPlayback`, appendix §10.1.1.

**Every empty renders as its own kind ([#4078](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4078)).** `core.config_display.render_value` is the ONE value-to-display rule behind the settings editor, the read-only config surface and the Django admin. `None` reads as the em-dash — it is the one empty that means "no value here" — while `""`, `[]` and `{}` each name their own shape, so an empty list can never read as an unset value. A key the shipped file carries no entry for renders `NO_SHIPPED_DEFAULT` rather than a bare `none` that reads as a value. A free-text cell shows `SettingCell.editable`, which is the JSON literal except that an unset value renders as an empty box rather than the four letters `null`; `SettingCell.selected` keeps the literal so a `<select>` still matches its own `null` option, and the emptied-cell restore gesture is unchanged.

**Cadence is a derived grid, not an invented floor ([#4079](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4079)).** `loops.loop_cadence_editing.CADENCE_STEP_SECONDS` IS `timer_chains.IDLE_POLL_FLOOR_SECONDS`, not a second constant restating it: every loop rides a self-rescheduling `loop_timer` chain whose successor is held at that floor on every path that does not complete a clean tick (a held/not-due loop, a faulted tick whose anchor never moved, a cadence-less loop), so a sub-floor or off-grid cadence is honoured only while nothing goes wrong. Intervals must be positive multiples of the step, and an off-grid write is **refused** with a message naming the admissible values either side — never silently rounded, because storing a number the operator did not type is the same untruth the derivation removes. Rows written before the grid are REPORTED (`off_grid_cadences`, surfaced on the loops table) and left exactly as they are: only the operator knows what a 45s row meant. The bounds explanation is the table's legend once; a per-row note survives only where that row's bounds genuinely differ — a `cadence_is_floor` loop's registry maximum.

**A stored row's DIVERGENCE from the shipped default is reported, not just its value ([#4074](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4074)).** `ConfigSetting` holds deltas from `config/defaults.toml`, and nothing said when the file's floor moved UNDER a row: `config_setting get` answers `[source: db, global]`, accurate and silent about the default having changed since the row was written. `teatree.cli.doctor.checks_config_drift` closes that — a `t3 doctor` listing of every stored row whose value differs from its shipped default, naming key, scope, stored value and shipped value. It is **advisory and never gates the exit code**: most divergent rows are deliberate operator intent, and the finding is precisely that a drifted row and an intended one are otherwise indistinguishable. Values pass through the shared `core.config_display` masking taxonomy, so a drifted secret is still NAMED without either value reaching the output. A key the shipped file carries no entry for is skipped — there is no shipped value to diverge from, and the Personal/Secret keys absent by construction would otherwise bury the real findings.

---

**Existing dashboard routes expose the recorded OTel state.** `/dash/live/` and `/dash/health/` (including their polled fragments) share `dash.telemetry.build_telemetry_view`: it reads only the fixed-size local admission, factory and lifecycle JSONL tails, then joins at most twelve observed issue digests to the indexed `SelfImproveFiring.dedup_key_digest`. An issue row names its allowlisted cause, observed age, latest observation and last *recorded* action; a linked repair ticket is offered only when a firing actually has one. A `boot/ready` observation clears earlier boot failures, and a durable `resolved_at` clears an older runtime observation. The feed is labelled current, stale after ten minutes, or unobserved in the last day; none of these labels alone proves worker health. GET views run no network or subprocess probe, and `/dash/cycle-time/` continues using its existing transition/attempt duration model rather than a second OTel duration store. `/dash/sessions/` and `/dash/skills/` project each persisted `TaskAttempt.result.skill_assurance` receipt into requested, found, injected, explicit-load, optional observed-load and evidence-*skill* names, capped to recent attempts and safe name shapes. They never render evidence bodies. `agent-declared application` means an agent-provided application receipt plus observed Skill-tool or `SKILL.md` reads for explicitly loaded skills, not independently proven application; inline skills still rely on declaration. Missing observed loads or an absent or incomplete receipt reads as unverified, never used.

The incident view takes the newest observation of each digest, then walks observations in bounded database-join batches until it has twelve *active* rows. Resolved observations do not consume those slots. If a newer observation reopens a resolved digest, it starts a new displayed generation: the old firing's age, action and ticket are not inherited as if the repair applied to the new incident.

**OTel incident confidence.** Admission, lifecycle, factory-issue, and skill-assurance spans pass through one allowlisted schema before local JSONL or optional OTLP export; prompt text, paths, identities, and token values are not span fields. Checked readers distinguish a complete empty window from missing, corrupt, truncated, or clipped evidence. They read at most 16 MiB per daily file (enough for a full day of five-second admission decisions with headroom), and an incomplete read cannot silently resolve a self-improvement firing. Task, message, and question incident detectors use durable lifecycle state; a claimed loop response is not a confirmed reply, and a missing-skill repair closes only on its linked terminal ticket transition. The dashboard exposes source degradation separately from the age of its last valid event.

**OTel repair evidence.** Exported telemetry rows carry W3C trace and span ids, and a finished attempt adds its iteration, error fingerprint, and hashed session reference. Hook PreToolUse gate decisions (each deny, and each deny the circuit breaker or `_fail_open_or_deny` lets through) go to a Django-free local ledger with the same id shape, allowlisted gate and rule markers, and a hashed session reference; hooks never export OTLP. A read window of up to seven days covers every daily file within one shared read budget, and a damaged day is logged and marks the read incomplete. The `LifecycleIncidentDetector` owns failure-burst repair tickets: a recurring fingerprint cites two distinct attempts, while a cause-bearing mixed burst names up to five distinct attempts with bounded first-line error heads. Gate denials are recorded to the ledger; they do not open repair tickets.

## 11. Skills & Plugin Architecture

Skills live in `skills/*/` — one `SKILL.md` + optional `references/` per skill. When installed as a plugin, skills are namespaced under `t3:` (e.g. `/t3:code`). The lifecycle skill set is `code`, `debug`, `test`, `review`, `review-request`, `ship`, `ticket`, `workspace`, `handover`, `next`, `retro`, `contribute`, `setup`, `platforms`, `rules`. The read-only auxiliary skill `checking` sits alongside it — the what-did-I-miss report, the pending deferred questions, the daily follow-up routine, and the session's task list (via `tasks list --session`) plus the in-session harness-TODO maintenance discipline — the agent reconciles its live `TaskList`/`TaskUpdate`/`TaskCreate` list against the conversation each turn, aided by the read-only `tasks reconcile-checklist` emitter; teatree cannot write the live harness list from a hook/subprocess, so this maintenance is an in-session discipline, not a background job).

Skills declare dependencies via a single YAML frontmatter `requires:` field (transitive, topo-sorted, cycle-checked). The closure resolves against one index over every skill root (`skill_support.index`: this checkout's `skills/`, then `~/.agents`, `~/.claude` and `~/.codex` skills, first root wins — the same precedence as the embed), so an apm-installed skill expands like an in-repo one; a member no root holds a `SKILL.md` for warns, read from the filesystem, and passes through. Third-party skill frameworks (e.g. superpowers) are absorbed into the `rules` skill rather than delegated, to avoid context duplication.

The managed Claude settings template caps skill listing descriptions at one character, so each session sees skill names while a selected skill still loads its full body. The host drift check and container seed enforce the same cap.

Raw Agent-tool briefs use `t3 <overlay> skill-preamble` for only the skills named in the brief: aliases render once, framework stack skills render as required load directives, and other requested skills embed their bodies. An unresolved skill still fails the command.

**Skill supply control.** The dashboard's Skills inventory separates teatree-required prompt skills from user-managed Claude/Codex installs: required entries and their transitive references are read-only, while harness-local extras may be removed or excluded. The four configurable loop selectors (`scanning_news_skill`, `eval_local_skill`, `backlog_sweep_skill`, `dogfood_smoke_skill`) therefore live under `Agents / mode & harness`, not their individual `Loops` groups — both the owning loop and the cross-cutting supply inventory consume them.

**Sub-agents (`agents/`).** Phase agents wrap skill bundles (`orchestrator`, `coder`, `tester`, `e2e`, `e2e-review`, `reviewer`, `review-request`, `shipper`, `debugger`, `followup`, `planner`, `answerer`, `scanning-news`, `bughunter`, `triage-assessor`) — each a YAML+description wrapper referencing skills via `skills:` frontmatter, no content duplication. `e2e-review` ([#2298](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2298)) is the reviewer leg of the EXTERNAL review loop (dispatched for the `e2e_reviewing` phase), making the skill↔agent pairing 1:1 for `e2e`/`e2e-review`. `review-request` (`(author, requesting_review) → t3:review-request` in `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE`) is the spawnable agent for the review-request phase — read-only on the codebase, it discovers the ready PR and posts the request under the on-behalf policy. They are invoked by lifecycle skills, by the headless executor (§5.2) on a claimed phase task, and by the loop tick (§5.6) when a scanner signal calls for agent judgment. Interactive-only skills (no agent): `retro`, `next`, `contribute`, `setup`.

**Worktree-scoped dispatch resolution ([PR-12](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2564)).** A dispatched task runs in its OWN worktree, so the overlay and framework-skill detection root are resolved from the task's ticket — its `overlay` field (via `get_overlay_for_ticket`) and `clone_paths.dispatch_detection_root(ticket)` — never the orchestrator's ambient cwd, which is the loop's clone (or the drain process's directory) rather than the ticket's checkout. The root is the first materialised worktree whose recorded path still exists on disk, else the clone of a `ticket.repos` name, else the clone the issue/PR URL names, else, for a ticket naming no repo, its named overlay package checkout (`OverlayEntry.project_path`) when `extra.source` is `self_improve`, or the overlay's workspace-repo clones otherwise. A missing package checkout for that `self_improve` case yields no root; a named repo with no clone here also yields no root rather than another repo's. Planning is queued beside provisioning, so the clone rung is the normal path, not an edge ([#5073](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5073)). `resolve_skill_bundle(..., detection_root=…)` threads it into `SkillLoadingPolicy.select_for_runtime_phase`'s detection `cwd` for the headless SDK run and `loop_dispatch claim-next`; with no root it falls back to the ambient cwd and logs a WARNING naming it. The same seam (`agents.dispatch_preflight`) injects a **target head-state** block into a coding/maker brief — the worktree's HEAD commit (sha, subject, committed-at) classified against the task's triggering timestamp — so the maker builds on any commit already on the branch instead of restarting; robust by construction (any missing worktree / non-git path / git failure yields no block, never a crash on the dispatch path). Every dispatched spawn carries a type-prefixed **display name** `t3-<type>-<id>` (`dispatch_gates.spawn_display_name`) the `claim-next` payload carries as the Agent tool `description`, so every spawn is attributable at a glance; an untyped task has no registered sub-agent and is already excluded upstream by the dispatchable claim filter. Long-running maker briefs auto-inject a heartbeat-DM cue; verification (review) briefs carry the anti-rubber-stamp block (reproduce the proof first, grade every quality dimension). The same seam injects a **branch-currency preflight** into a TESTING brief (#2663): `t3 tool verify-gates` judges the worktree against ITSELF, so a branch can pass every local gate and still not merge — the block fetches the target once (`branch_currency.fetch_target_head`), reads the behind-count, and names the paths `git merge-tree --write-tree` predicts would conflict (`branch_currency.predict_merge_conflicts`, the public form of that probe), prescribing merge-never-rebase + re-gating the MERGED tree for a predicted conflict only; a branch that is behind but merges clean is told no merge is needed, because a merge only moves the head and restarts CI. The target is `resolve_target_branch`'s, so a ticket `target_branch` override is honoured. It **fails LOUD, never silent-empty**: a missing worktree, a failed fetch or an unreadable count each render `UNVERIFIED` plus the commands to run by hand — `branch_behind_target` is deliberately NOT the probe, since it collapses a failed fetch and an already-current branch into one `None`. The existing ship-side (`_ship/gates`) and CLEAR-side (`runners/ship`) currency gates are unchanged; this is the same invariant moved upstream to where the conflict is cheapest to resolve.

**Distribution.** Two install paths, one source of truth:

- **APM**: `apm install souliane/teatree`
- **CLI-first**: `git clone … && uv tool install --editable . --overrides uv-overrides.txt && t3 setup` (`--overrides` carries no entry today but stays wired: `uv tool install` does not read `[tool.uv] override-dependencies`, so a later entry would be invisible to the global install without it) — also registers the plugin in `~/.claude/plugins/installed_plugins.json` with `installPath` pointing at the main clone (no `~/.claude/plugins/t3` symlink; always live)

On every `t3 setup` run, `dep_drift` checks `[project].dependencies` against the editable install and reinstalls + `execv`-restarts if a declared dep is missing. The same run re-syncs runtime skill links and **prunes stale ones** — a teatree-managed link whose skill was removed or renamed upstream is removed so the dropped skill stops resolving, while contribute-mode workspace links and a user's own real skill directories are left untouched. Because `t3 update` re-runs `t3 setup`, updating teatree auto-cleans skills dropped upstream.

**§11.4 Bash Permissions.** The plugin's `settings.json` ships a **broad allow, narrow deny** `permissions` list — every tool family the workflow touches is allowed, with load-bearing denies (push to default branches, `--force`, `--no-verify`, root `rm -rf`, `curl|bash`, `gh repo delete`) taking precedence; the `t3` CLI is the workflow's safety wrapper, so blocking inside the CLI is the wrong layer. Plugin config is **not self-modifiable** — edits to the allow-list are rejected by Claude Code's autonomy guardrail, so a mid-workflow classifier denial means the agent stops and asks via `AskUserQuestion` (`skills/rules/SKILL.md` § "Classifier Denial Protocol"). `t3 doctor authorizations` is read-only — it reports which generic recommended auto-mode authorizations are absent from `~/.claude/settings.json`; teatree ships **no** classifier whitelist of its own.

**§11.5 Explicit skill loading.** Skill loading is fully explicit — slash commands (`/t3:code`), phase mapping (`t3 agent --phase coding`), ticket status, the transitive `requires:` chain, and cwd/overlay context. There is no free-text keyword scan of the prompt or a task description: `SkillLoadingPolicy` resolves from those explicit signals only, and the `SessionStart` hook surfaces only the framework / overlay / companion skills a session's cwd context implies (`PreToolUse` blocks Python code edits until those load). Session-end retro fires from the `SessionEnd` hook keyed on the lifecycle skills loaded during the session; ticket-URL intake resolves through `t3 <overlay> workspace ticket <url>` (overlay-resolved, provider-agnostic), not a frontmatter URL pattern. The `SKILL.md` schema (`skill_support/schema.py`) rejects a stale `triggers:` / `search_hints:` key loud, so a skill file never silently carries a removed mechanism; `companions:` is the recognised soft counterpart of `requires:`. Headless dispatch, `t3 agent`, the `SessionStart` cache (`core/skill_cache`) and the hook closure all build the `requires` index through `skill_support.index.build_skill_index`; a caller passing no index gets that live one, never an empty list.

Headless work briefs name every requested skill and ask for one `skill_application` entry per skill with one line of evidence. The bounded receipt keeps only the presence of accepted evidence. A stopped attempt keeps observed requested skill loads; a resumed run credits earlier loads only from the same task and agent session. The skill-gap detector excludes usage-window parks, completed-row no-ops, and zero-turn unverified attempts before limiting the recent attempt window.

Headless dispatch persists a bounded skill-assurance receipt on each attempt. Mandatory phase skills must exist and be fully embedded or explicitly loadable before a billable turn; a missing file or truncated body without a load path stops dispatch. The agent's short application references are declarations, not proof. An explicitly loaded skill reaches `declared` only when a Skill-tool call or `SKILL.md` read is also observed; absent receipts remain `unverified`. The cheap self-improvement tier groups recent gaps by overlay so owners can repair missing installation or delivery without exposing skill bodies in telemetry. This is not a live bridge from interactive skills to headless agents.

**§11.6 Progressive disclosure in skills.** A long SKILL.md carries its **decision-relevant spine** — the trigger, the do-X-never-Y verdict, and any always-gated safety list — and moves the mechanics behind each rule (step-by-step procedure, config precedence chain, per-flag rationale, worked recipes) into `skills/<skill>/references/*.md`, split largest-section-first. A spine entry names its reference by repo-relative path, and every dispatch can follow it. The embedded skill block opens with one reach line (`skill_injection` → `skill_files.reach_line`) naming the absolute directory `skills/<skill>/…` paths live in; it leads every embed, so a budget cut sheds lifecycle tail sections before it could reach the line. Lane A keeps `Read` on every phase allowed `read_file` (the headless dispatch denies only the external-contact built-ins, `src/teatree/agents/_runner_options.py` `_EXTERNAL_CONTACT_BUILTINS`).
