On Lane B, `Read` falls back to an exact registered skill file when the worktree jail refuses or misses the path — `agents.skill_files.SkillFileIndex` registers `<root>/<skill>/SKILL.md`, `<root>/<skill>/references/*.md`, and `<root>/rules/RULES.md` as regular, non-symlinked files over `harness_skills_dirs()`, and answers only exact key or registered-path matches, so `..`, look-alikes and any other file under a skills root resolve to nothing. The worktree copy wins over the registry, so a ticket editing a skill reads its own copy. A phase allowed `read_file` with no worktree gets a `Read`-only toolset over those files (`lane_b.filesystem.build_skill_file_toolset`); Grep/Write/Edit stay jailed, and the `_NONE` phase (`short_describe`) stays toolless. Content **moves**; removing a rule is a separate reviewed decision. Progressive disclosure composes with — and does not replace — the two bundle-level levers: **phase-scoped loading** (only the skills a phase needs are embedded) and **summary demotion** (a non-primary skill in a dispatched bundle gets a one-line companion entry naming its absolute `SKILL.md` to `Read` when it applies, or saying no body resolves on this host, rather than its full body). This is the design rule the `/t3:rules` § "Split Long Skills With Progressive Disclosure" prose enforces on authors. `skills/rules/SKILL.md` is the canonical instance: because `rules` is embedded in full on every lifecycle dispatch, it is an always-embedded core (≤ 19,456 B) holding every Non-Negotiable rule's trigger and verdict as a stub naming its `skills/rules/references/<file>.md`, and every original section lives verbatim in exactly one reference file (`tests/conformance/test_rules_skill_split.py` pins the ceiling, section survival, and that every `§ "<title>"` citation of the rules skill resolves). Its sibling `skills/rules/RULES.md` holds the seven always-on rules, ranked by importance; the core's preamble, `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` link it before their first section (`tests/conformance/test_always_on_rules.py`). The read-only executor embeds that document before the rules core, resolving it beside the selected `rules/SKILL.md`; a missing document refuses the dispatch before the harness opens. Rules eval scenarios grade the reference that holds their section, derived by the corpus generator from the headings on disk.

The dispatch budget keeps at least 2048 bytes beyond its existing 4096-byte append allowance for every phase. The MCP serve import graph keeps at least 25 modules below its ceiling: pure machine JSON parsing sits below Django management, and agent SDK and rich Markdown modules load only at their call sites.

**§11.7 Configuration review cadence.** `CLAUDE.md`, `skills/*/SKILL.md`, and the hook/permission config (`hooks/hooks.json`, plugin `settings.json`) get a deliberate, deletion-first review every 3–6 months — the accumulation of gates, rules, and skill prose is pruned back to what still earns its place, rather than only ever growing. The dreaming pipeline (`/t3:dreaming`, `t3 dream run`) is the executor: its memory-consolidation + core-gap pass is the standing cadence that surfaces stale config and drives each removal to a merged change.

**§11.8 A skill's worked examples resolve against the tree.** An agent skimming a skill cannot tell an illustration from a work item, so an example naming a plausible-but-absent teatree symbol reads as a bug to go fix — and unlike a stale import or a stale patch target, nothing mechanical catches it. `quality/skill_symbol_refs` resolves every teatree-shaped reference in `skills/**/*.md` — a `src/teatree/...` path, a repo path under any other top-level directory, a dotted `teatree.<...>` name, a bare module-local `<module>.<SYMBOL>` name, a `from teatree.<mod> import …` clause — against the live tree, and `tests/teatree_quality/test_skill_symbol_refs.py` turns red on the rest. In charter mode it also resolves backtick-quoted package-relative paths through indexed package roots, suffixless Python paths through matching `.py` files, and `src/teatree/` paths through an app's `templates` or `static` directory. Findings are per-reference, so a resolving citation never vouches for a broken one beside it on the same line. A dotted name resolves only through source, never through a bare directory: Python reads any leftover directory as an implicit namespace package, so a retired module's `__pycache__` remains would otherwise vouch for the citation that outlived it — silently, in the permissive direction, and differently in every clone. The bare shape resolves through an index of the modules the tree ships (`src/teatree/**`, `hooks/**`) and is bounded at both ends, because an extractor keyed on shape alone cannot tell a module-local symbol from ordinary attribute access: the head must name one of those modules and the tail must be module-symbol-shaped (`UPPER_SNAKE` or `_private`), which is what keeps `typer.Exit`, `permissions.allow` and `overlay.config` out. Outside `src/teatree/` a path is a citation only when it names a file under a directory that exists — the tree's own top-level names recur in prose (`dev/staging`) and in stand-in namespaces, so that precondition is what lets the guard reach a stale `tests/…` citation without making every illustration red. An illustration that needs a stand-in target uses a namespace the tree has no claim on (`src/acme/...`, `src/<pkg>/...`); a token that is genuinely not an importable name — an entry-point group, a config-section header, a legacy artifact named so it is never re-created — carries a `skill-symbol-ref: <reason>` marker, scoped to its own line or to the fenced block it introduces.

The same resolver reaches two surfaces the markdown walk cannot read. The charter documents an agent loads before any skill (`BLUEPRINT.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/blueprint/*.md`) are scanned by `scan_file`; Python docstrings and `#:` attribute comments under `src/teatree/**` and `hooks/**` are scanned by `quality/python_prose_refs`, which decides which lines are prose and masks the rest before handing the source to the same `scan_source`. That second surface is where a `:func:` citation of a function that MOVED modules shipped to `main` and survived a cold review: `--doctest-modules` collects nothing from a module carrying no `>>>`, the comment-density gate exempts docstrings outright, and a reader following the citation finds nothing and cannot tell renamed from moved from deleted. An ordinary `#` comment and a non-docstring string literal stay unscanned — a prompt template names symbols it does not cite. Both surfaces carry a seeded known-set so the walk lands without a tree-wide cleanup first, and each is asserted in BOTH directions: a NEW unresolved reference reds, and a pinned one the scanner no longer reports reds until its entry is deleted, so a stale allowance cannot outlive the citation it covered. Both known-sets live in `quality/known_unresolved_refs.yaml`, read by `quality/ref_baseline` — data rather than a test literal, because the repair has to be mechanical: a pin goes stale for reasons OUTSIDE its own PR. [#4451](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4451) is the shape — two PRs off a shared base, one seeding a pin while the other rewrote the citation it named; neither `refs/pull/N/merge` contained the other, so both were green alone and `main` was red on six required contexts the moment the second landed, with every open PR inheriting it. `t3 tool ratchet-prune` reports both halves and repairs exactly one of them. The asymmetry is the guard: deleting a pin the scanner no longer reports makes the ratchet strictly TIGHTER, so it is safe to automate, while banking an unresolved reference LOOSENS it — that half is reported and never written, so no repair path can widen the guard it is repairing. `load_baseline` fails loud on a missing or malformed file rather than degrading to an empty set, which would read as "nothing pinned" and satisfy the staleness half vacuously.

---

## 12. Testing

**>90% branch coverage, non-negotiable** (`fail_under = 93, branch = true`). Omits only migrations.

- In-memory SQLite (`:memory:`) for isolation and speed; `django.tasks.backends.immediate` for synchronous task execution
- `conftest.py` monkeypatches `HOME`/`XDG_*` to `tmp_path`, strips `GIT_*`, isolates overlay env, resets backend + overlay caches between tests
- Tests mirror `src/` paths under `tests/teatree_core/`, `tests/teatree_agents/`, `tests/teatree_backends/`, `tests/teatree_loop/`, plus top-level cross-cutting suites
- **A string patch target must RESOLVE and must reach the binding the subject READS.** `quality/patch_targets` resolves every constant `patch("a.b.c")` / `patch.object(<module alias>, "attr")` against the live tree the way `unittest.mock` does, so a stale name — invisible to an import-based rename sweep, and silent rather than an error — turns `tests/quality/test_patch_targets_resolve.py` red. Resolving is not sufficient: `quality/patch_bindings` additionally asks whether the patch reaches the module under test. A MODULE-LEVEL `from M import attr` in the subject is a SECOND binding a patch on `M` never replaces, so the real callable runs while the test reads green; a call-time import re-fetches from `M` and IS reached. The subject is the module the test's own mirrored path names, which is the only decidable answer — every first-party module a test file imports yields 81 candidate findings on this tree and the spot checks are false. Coverage is therefore partial by construction, and a deliberate defining-module patch declares itself with a `# patch-binding: defining-module` pragma on the call.
- New tests lean integration / E2E / functional (Django test client, `call_command`, real `git` under `tmp_path`); unit tests are reserved for pure logic, and only unstoppable externals are mocked
- **Eval tiers.** The merge-blocking eval proof is model-free: `tests/eval_replay` replays every scenario against its committed pass and fail fixtures, resolves each pass-fixture `t3` command against the live CLI registry, and runs the real-code regression pins strict (a skipped pin is not green). Live-model runs (`--backend anthropic_api`) are trend signals: `T3_EVAL_SUITE_BUDGET_USD` caps a nightly run at $5 and a weekly run (a rotating 1/8 of the catalog) at $20; `eval/cost_observation.ConservativeSuiteBudget` reserves each request's worst-case cost before transport and settles it to the billed usage once answered, judge calls included (`eval/judge.ModelSeamJudge`), and a run whose budget runs out exits 75 as incomplete coverage.
- The external E2E runner's `stack` target reads generic `stack_frontend_port` / `stack_start_command` keys from `get_e2e_config()`, probes the port through `host_published_port_host()`, and skips worktree discovery. Its execution/evidence target remains `stack` while the spec mode is `local`, because an overlay's remote stack may be a restored local stack reached through a tunnel.
- Core has no Playwright suite (no UI). Overlays declare their own via `get_e2e_config()`; `t3 <overlay> e2e {run,external,project,in-tree}` runs them. The three sources are disjoint: `project` is the repo's own in-tree pytest suite, `external` a cloned specs repo run against a live stack, and `in-tree` a Playwright config that lives in the INVOKING checkout — resolved from the invocation cwd plus the overlay's declared `e2e_dir`, with the ambient environment inherited and nothing injected, so a browserless CI lane (a unit or static-analysis lane needing no stack, tenant or credential) reproduces byte-for-byte in a contributor's own worktree rather than a cache-repo clone. Its `-c <config>` comes from the same `overlay.e2e.playwright_args(spec_path)` hook the `external` runner uses, or an explicit `--config`; resolving neither REFUSES (exit 2) rather than let Playwright fall back to a default config whose global setup logs in. Every specs source is a declared repo cloned at a ref, so the `external` runner resolves its Playwright working dir in two arms ([#2620](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2620)): an explicit `--repo` (a `[e2e_repos.<name>]` clone) wins; else, when `get_e2e_config()` supplies BOTH a `url` and a `ref` (`overlay_e2e_repo` lifts them into an `E2ERepo`, `e2e_dir` default `e2e`), the overlay's own repo is cloned at that ref (a `--branch`/`--ref` override wins) — so a migrated suite on an unmerged branch runs without a separate checkout. Neither declared is `E2eSpecsResolutionError.no_specs_source` (exit 1). A green/red `e2e run` records run provenance on the durable recipe (`Ticket.extra['e2e_recipe'].last_run`, `core/intake/e2e_workitem.record_run`): result + per-repo SHAs + env, plus (for vanilla-spec suites whose specs live in another repo, [#272](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/272)) the exact `spec_path` and the overlay-resolved `manifest_entry` id (its CI lane) via the `overlay.e2e.run_provenance(spec_path)` overlay hook — so a run is reproducible from the DB alone. Core stays overlay-agnostic (default hook returns `""`; empty provenance fields are omitted). For a multi-config Playwright suite (one config per lane — api-flow vs contrib vs portal), the overlay maps a spec to its `-c <config>` (and any other per-spec Playwright flags) via the `overlay.e2e.playwright_args(spec_path)` overlay hook; the `external` runner threads those into the `npx playwright test` command ahead of any explicit `--playwright-args` (default `[]` — no flags, prior command shape preserved). The per-feature **acceptance scenarios** a templated test plan renders from come through the sibling `overlay.e2e.scenarios(spec_path)` overlay hook (default `()`): each element is an overlay-defined frozen `Scenario` (`surface`/`title`/`preconditions`/`steps`/`expected`/`modality` `ui|api`/`captures` of `Capture{slot,caption}`), so the overlay owns the scenario manifest and core just threads the tuple to the renderer. `t3 <overlay> e2e write-test-plan --manifest` ([#1409](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1409)) maintains ONE structured test-plan note per **ticket** (never the MR), idempotent on a hidden marker carrying a JSON state blob (the merge SOT); each run merges its manifest's env(s) over the prior state, freezing the side it omits. The note renders a test plan — a multi-repo header with clickable commit-provenance and a `Dev ± Local` reconciliation, then bodies in one of four templates ([#2304](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2304)): the three workflow-shaped ones (`capture-matrix` default side-by-side table, `browser-click-first` screenshots, `link-api` links + code) and `scenario-plan`, which renders ordered acceptance scenarios — each a `### Scenario N — <surface>` block with Preconditions / numbered Steps / Expected / Actual (a ✅ pass marker or a blocked-or-omitted note) and either captioned inline screenshots (`ui` modality) or a contract-check marker (`api` modality) — separated by `---` rules with an `**Environment:**` footer. `--body-file` posts a pre-authored body verbatim (mutually exclusive with `--manifest`). Pure render/merge lives in `_test_plan/render`; the `scenario-plan` shape in `_test_plan/scenario`; the two non-default workflow renderers in `_test_plan/workflow_templates`; host orchestration in the rest of the `_test_plan` package.
  - **Upload integrity.** Artifacts embed the relative `/uploads/<secret>/<file>` GitLab claims on save and upload to the project that owns the note's issue URL; a post-upload `verify_upload` gate re-fetches each via the token-authed API and magic-byte-checks it, so "posted" means "verified renderable" (`TestPlanMediaError` refuses otherwise).
  - **Image preflight** (`core/evidence/test_plan_validation`, before any upload): every image must carry a `highlightAndShoot` red box, none may be byte-identical (md5 dedup), and a manifest with no media is refused (`TestPlanValidationError`); `--skip-validation` is the user-only bypass.
  - **Video preflight** (`core/evidence/video_evidence`, the video counterpart to the image preflight): an evidence video is refused when its opening frames are blank (the dead-pre-roll defect that shipped a 40s-blank walkthrough) or the clip is blank throughout — `scripts/analyze_video.py` yields the verdict and the `e2e write-test-plan` path plus the `/t3:e2e` / `/t3:e2e-review` skills reject on it. Pinned by the `e2e_review` eval scenario (HOLD on a blank-pre-roll video).
  - **FINAL BDD source** (`core/evidence/bdd_scenario_source`): the manifest's mandatory `scenario_source` (`prd_page`, `bdd_revision`, `status: final`, `scenario_ids`) renders a hidden `<!-- t3-bdd-source {…} -->` comment (sorted JSON, beside `t3-e2e-data`), never reader-visible; a `--body-file` author writes that same comment line, and publishing strips it. A re-run inherits the source from the prior state, `--embed-captures` included, and the source is resolved before any capture is migrated or copied. `e2e write-test-plan` (both `--manifest` and `--body-file`) plus `e2e verify-plan-captures` refuse a plan whose declaration is missing, repeated, not valid JSON, not `final` or incomplete, that shows a scenario-source field in reader-visible text, or whose `<!-- t3-bdd-trace [...] -->` (or legacy `<!-- covers ... -->`) comment names an ID outside the declared set (`BddSourceError`, surfaced as `TestPlanValidationError`). `verify-plan-captures` checks every plan that owns an evidence directory — `<name>.md` and `<name>-<lang>.md` — and refuses captures no plan owns. Pinned by the `e2e_review` eval scenario (HOLD on a preflight BDD source) and the `e2e` author scenario that stops and asks on a draft BDD.

---

## 13. Quality Gates

The `review-evidence` worker command writes a receipt in git admin state for one clean committed head. It records duplicate definitions in changed Python files and a mutation kill row for each changed test node; coder and merge-critic briefs read the same head-bound receipt. The push gate also reads plain-word limits from the ratified plan and coding task's execution reason, then refuses a diff that adds a migration, setting or public name, or edits a file declared unchanged; failed source reads cannot certify a clean diff.
**One per-action escape ([#5021](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5021)).** Hook denies share `emit_pretooluse_deny` and the `gate-escape` control-plane command. An inline `[gate-escape:<gate-id>: <reason>]` is valid only for its declared gate policy. Audited agent exceptions write a `GateEscape` row before allowance; human-only exceptions require a verified owner grant bound to the same actor, session, subject, HEAD and action digest, then consume it once; privacy/leak gates never accept an escape. A missing audit write, expired grant or repeated action denies. Legacy per-gate tokens have no authority. The four Stop gates use the same record-before-allow path. The canonical ID catalog is in [hooks/CLAUDE.md](hooks/CLAUDE.md) § "Escape markers & kill-switches".

| Tool | What it checks |
|---|---|
| `pytest` + `pytest-cov` | >90% branch coverage |
| `ruff` | All rules enabled, specific ignores justified (`# noqa` requires approval) |
| `ty` | Static type checker with `error-on-warning = true` |
| `import-linter` | Wildcard sibling-independence tach cannot express (substrate → `contrib`; mini-loops independent). Acyclicity + backend/agent-independence are tach-enforced (`forbid_circular_dependencies`, #1922). Config: `pyproject.toml` |
| `ast-grep` | Deterministic AST anti-pattern rules under `.ast-grep/blocking/` (run by the push gate's Engine B `TestBlockingSetIsGreen` scan + the CI `test-shard`) — e.g. `except-swallow-to-empty`, `cas-stamp-before-side-effect`, and `lenient-git-run-dead-except` (an `except CommandFailedError` around the lenient `git.run`, which never raises, is a dead handler — use `git.run_strict`) |
| `codespell` | Spell check |
| `prek` | Runs all above on commit |

**Workflow contract** (`tests/test_ci_workflow_hardening.py`): every workflow defaults its token to read-only and grants write scopes per job; every action is pinned to a full commit SHA with one version label; every checkout states whether it persists credentials; pull requests reuse the content-hash CI image tags while every other event builds its own under `main-` tags and consumes the pushed digest; jobs that deploy or push with a credential bind the `production` environment; a daily workflow reports force pushes to `main`.

**A gate verdict needs executed evidence ([#4929](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4929)).** `teatree.core.modelkit.gate_verdict.evaluate_gate` collects a gate's evidence, then judges it into `Pass` / `Refuse` / `Unknown`. Any exception on the way (a missing executable, a failed git read, a crashing judge) is `Unknown`, rendered `[gate:<id>] DID NOT RUN: <cause>` and never a pass. Visual QA, the ship and CLEAR mandatory-E2E gate, and debt-delta run through it. Detail: [§17.6.5](docs/blueprint/factory-architecture.md).

Key ruff decisions: ALL rules selected then specific ignores; D1xx disabled (no docstrings — self-documenting code); `from __future__ import annotations` banned (use native 3.13 syntax).

---

## 14. Django Project Workflows

Reference DB architecture, the import fallback chain (`DjangoDbImportConfig` strategy + selective fake-migration retry + renumber-record reconcile + post-import steps), DSLR integration, the worktree provisioning workflow (`worktree provision`), the server startup workflow (`worktree start`), and the state reconciler (`t3 <overlay> workspace doctor`) are implementation details — they live in code under `teatree.core.runners`, `teatree.utils.db`, `teatree.utils.django_db`, `teatree.utils.django_db.reconcile`, `teatree.core.worktree.reconcile`, and the `lifecycle` / `worktree` / `workspace` management commands. The architectural contract:

- **One reference DB per overlay** (canonical control DB at `teatree.paths.CANONICAL_DB`; worktree-aware code auto-isolates onto a per-worktree DB under `teatree.paths.auto_isolated_worktrees_dir()` = `~/.local/share/teatree-worktrees/<slug>/`, the `<slug>` deterministic from the checkout path via `teatree.paths.isolated_slug`). `t3 <overlay> workspace clean-all` reaps the orphan dirs under that root — those no LIVE CHECKOUT owns — while keeping any dir holding a `.git` checkout (the #706/#835 data-loss guard). Ownership is one answer fed by three evidence sources (#3852), with the **filesystem** as the primary one: every directory carrying a `.git` under the scanned roots, UNION every checkout `git worktree list` reports across the discoverable clones, UNION the `Worktree` rows — plus each dir's own `owner-checkout.path` stamp, which inverts the one-way slug hash so liveness is proven rather than inferred. The slug is `sha256(checkout_path)`, so "does this path exist on disk" is the question itself and a registry-derived answer is only a proxy for it: asking registries first made coverage depend on which clones happened to be discoverable, and a clone that is never a candidate produces no gap, so its checkouts' absence was indistinguishable from death. That only holds while the scan reports everything it did NOT cover, so anything it skips is a gap ([#3872](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3872)): it walks THROUGH symlinked dirs (deduplicating recursion on the resolved path, so a loop terminates) and reports rather than truncates at its runaway depth cap — skipping both silently left `complete` true while live checkouts were missing from the keep-set, which is exactly the evidence a deletion rests on. Every source contributes gaps and a gap fails CLOSED for every dir whose own stamp cannot prove it dead — it is kept and the gap reported, never a deletion on partial evidence. A stamp proves death only where this venue sees the owner's physical place: `IsolatedEnvDir.stamp_owner` also records `owner-checkout.location` (`teatree.mount_identity.physical_location` — the mount table's device plus the path inside that filesystem, boot-scoped for anonymous devices), and an owner counts as gone only when it is absent here AND this venue resolves its path to that same location ([#4923](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4923)). That is what lets a gap elsewhere stop vetoing such a dir: the container's own home otherwise reads a host-only checkout as absent. A stamp predating the location record, or naming another venue's location, is missing evidence and kept; each pass upgrades the stamps of the live owners it discovers. A reclaimable dir is released rather than removed wholesale: its `unshipped-work/` salvage and stamps survive, stamps are deleted last so a failure leaves the dir judgeable, teatree's own unlistable handoff dirs are opened first, a symlinked entry is never followed, and one failing dir is reported without aborting the pass. Besides `clean-all`, the artifact sweep runs this reaper unattended. A dir modified at or after the instant the keep-set was computed is likewise kept: the box provisions continuously, so a snapshot-then-delete loop would otherwise reap a dir minted mid-pass. `retention prune --apply` finishes with a `VACUUM`, since deleting rows on SQLite reclaims no disk and this DB is the seed each env dir is copied from
- **Remote-state freshness is a precondition of the #706 guard.** `commits_absent_from_all_remotes` is a purely local graph query over `refs/remotes/*`, and a tracking ref goes stale when its branch is deleted upstream by anything other than this clone (a forge's auto-delete-on-merge, or a sibling clone) — against a stale ref it reports unpushed work as pushed, authorising the reap of the last copy. Legacy destructive consumers refresh those refs with `git.fetch_all_prune` and keep the checkout if refresh fails. The shared removal proof reads remote heads live without fetching; an unreadable remote also keeps the checkout.
- **Shared checkout-removal proof.** Every removal path checks unregenerable ignored files, commits against live remote heads or the landed default branch, open merge requests, and other in-flight rows before removing a checkout. Unknown remote or forge state keeps the checkout. Teardown preserves a compose project, database, pass entry, and external resources while another row still uses them. `force=True` does not bypass this proof; `discard_unshipped_work=True` does. The operator's `--force` teardown and the proven-dead-checkout row release set that explicit discard flag.
- **Analyze-then-wipe done-worktree reaper** (`teatree.core.worktree.worktree_done`, surfaced by `workspace clean-all` / `clean-merged`) — a worktree is auto-wiped ONLY when its ticket is FSM-done (`MERGED`/`DELIVERED`/`IGNORED`, or a forge squash-merge) AND every unpushed commit and uncommitted change is PROVEN content-redundant against `origin/main` (`teatree.core.worktree.branch_classification.branch_redundancy` — a FIVE-rung ladder, first match wins: patch-id `git cherry` zero, synthetic-squash `commit-tree` tree-delta capture, `branch --merged`, the path-independent blob probe `branch_content_landed_on_base` (the refactor case, where the base took the branch's exact bytes at another path), and above them the one non-git rung — the forge's merge record at EXACTLY the current tip (`forge_merged_tip_captured`), the only instrument that survives a squash whose content the base later evolved past. A landed verdict is VETOED while the forge reports an open PR (`open-pr-veto`). A bare forge merged-PR signal alone never authorizes deletion (post-merge work is kept). There is **no recovery snapshot** (#1770 removed): unproven work is kept, never destroyed. A PURE GHOST row — checkout dir absent, `refs/heads/<slug>` proven absent (not merely unreadable) in the row's stored clone or the single clone a name scan finds, and no surviving `git worktree` registration — is RELEASED by `workspace doctor --fix` rather than kept: it holds no bytes, so every probe below fails closed and the row would otherwise pin its `db_name` forever, shielding a leaked per-worktree database from the orphan-DB reaper. `broken_checkout.is_pure_ghost` is that predicate; it refuses a name scan that matches several clones and answers False on any git error. The automatic `clean-all` sweep never releases a ghost row. Done-detection judges the CHECKED-OUT branch, not the DB slug the two can drift apart on, so a landed slug can no longer report a live in-flight checkout as done. The ladder runs ONCE per row and its forge probes are memoised per pass. Every per-row call is exception-isolated, so one raising row yields an `error` outcome instead of aborting the sweep. Two fail-safe pre-gates run ahead of done-detection — a **liveness guard** (`cleanup_liveness`: live `Session` / active `Task` / process CWD / git `index.lock` / recent HEAD commit ⇒ skip-and-report) and an **ownership guard** (`cleanup_ownership`: data-driven via `colleague_repo_url_pattern` + `user_identity_aliases`, a colleague-authored branch on a shared product repo is excluded up front; a no-op for the owner's solo repos). Everything NOT auto-deleted is surfaced as a machine-readable **structured EMIT** record (`workspace emit` → JSON array, schema in `cleanup_emit`: path/branch/kind/`unique_commit_shas`/`uncommitted_paths`/`merged_with_post_merge_work`/`content_verified`/`verdict_source`/`banned_terms_status`/liveness/`last_commit_date`/owner) for a separate judgment skill to route. That array is the UNION of two source sets: the `Worktree` ledger, plus the linked checkouts no row tracks (a dispatched agent's bare `git worktree add`), which reach it as `kind: orphan-worktree` (`teatree.core.worktree.orphan_emit`, over the discovery and unique-work test the raw-orphan reaper shares in `teatree.core.cleanup.orphan_checkouts`) — so the surface that REPORTS stranded work cannot name a different population from the pass that refuses to reap it. Only WORK-BEARING orphans are emitted; a clean one is recoverable from its remote, so reporting it would drown the signal. The record carries the verdict's PROVENANCE, not just its answer: an empty `unique_commit_shas` used to mean both "proven to hold nothing unique" and "no probe could run", so an unresolvable clone emitted the same bytes as a shipped branch and the skill routed both to DELETE. `content_verified` is `false` for every verdict nothing proved and `verdict_source` names the deciding layer (`cherry-zero-unique`/`synthetic-squash`/`branch-merged`/`content-landed`/`forge-merged-tip`/`not-redundant`/`open-pr-veto`) or the reason none could (`inconclusive`/`clone-unresolvable`/`uncommitted-work`); the skill routes an unverified record to KEEP. The record also carries the WORKING TREE's delta (`uncommitted_paths`, from `working_tree_dirt`), because a checkout whose entire work is staged-but-uncommitted has no commits for any probe to speak for while holding content on no ref: `CleanupEmitRecord.to_dict` forces such a record `content_verified: false` with `verdict_source: uncommitted-work`, so the KEEP branch that produced it and the record it hands the skill can no longer disagree. Its upstream cause is closed too — `clone_paths.resolve_clone_path` returns a stored `extra['clone_path']` only while `probe_checkout` confirms it is still a checkout, otherwise falling through to a fresh `find_clone_path` scan, and `workspace doctor` reports the `StaleClonePath` drift that `--fix` repairs. The **salvage primitive** (`workspace salvage`, `cleanup_salvage`) captures an emitted item's banned-terms-clean unique content onto a fresh branch + PR, verifies the forge has it, then deletes the source ONLY after a verified landing — a failed push/open/verify leaves the source intact. Every decision-bearing git probe in the reaper/salvage/stash paths runs `git.run_strict` (raises on failure) and routes an inconclusive probe to the conservative KEEP outcome: the orphaned-stash reaper (`drop_orphaned_stashes`) keeps a stash whenever the upstream-capture probe (`_workspace.stash._branch_captured_upstream`) fails or its `git cherry` produced no comparison lines (a stash ref is a merge commit `git cherry` omits — an empty result is "nothing compared", never "captured"), and `cleanup_salvage._unique_content_texts` refuses to salvage a `require_banned_clean` item whose content is unreadable, so an unscanned blob is never pushed to a public PR
- **One canonical answer to "is this branch landed?"** ([#4070](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4070)) — `t3 <overlay> workspace branch-verdict <branch>…` (`teatree.core.worktree.branch_verdict.branch_verdict_report`) is the read-only serialization of `branch_redundancy` for an ARBITRARY local branch, PR-backed or not. It closes the gap that `landscape` cannot fill — its `has_unpushed` comes from `commits_absent_from_all_remotes`, which is sha-based and, per its own docstring, not empty for a squash-merge (deliberately fail-OPEN, because intake asks "might something be in flight?", not "is it landed?") — and that `emit` fills only by ABSENCE. The payload carries `redundant` + `source` alongside `forge_merged`, `merged_with_post_merge_work` and `unique_shas` TOGETHER, so the forge signal is never readable on its own as "safe to delete post-merge work". A fourth field, `content_present_on_target`, is the one layer the module adds of its own: `git cherry` reads a patch's PRIOR appearance on the target and a REVERT there does not erase it, so `git merge-tree --write-tree` asks the present-tense question instead. `ShipExecutor`'s [#776](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/776) duplicate-PR refusal consumes the AND of both (`branch_is_landed`) instead of the ancestor-only `git.branch_merged`, which a squash-merge defeats — so a squash-merged branch is refused, a reverted one ships, and presence runs first as a pure-git short-circuit so the ordinary unlanded branch pays no forge probe. Two enforcement halves keep the primitives from being re-derived: the WARN-only PreToolUse advisory `handle_warn_merged_detection_probe` (a hand-rolled `git cherry` / `branch --merged` / `merge-base --is-ancestor` / `log --not` against a DEFAULT-branch-shaped target earns one stderr line naming this command; never a deny, because the same command is a legitimate mid-rebase read — advisory even when an escape marker appears), and the AST ratchet `tests/conformance/test_merged_detection_single_home.py`, which pins every `src/` call site to the canonical home or an allow-listed non-landed-ness use.
- **Configurable import strategy per overlay** (`overlay.provisioning.db_import_strategy(worktree) → DbImportStrategy | None`) — overlays declare *what* to import; core runs the engine
- **Migration retry with selective faking** for known-stuck migrations, declared per overlay
- **Renumber-record reconcile** ([#1038](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1038)) — when a snapshot DB carries OLD migration numbers and the base branch has since renumbered (a migration inserted earlier bumped later numbers), `django_db.reconcile` detects Django's `InconsistentMigrationHistory` and renames the stale `django_migrations` record to the new name so `check_consistent_history` passes and the forward migrate proceeds. It fires ONLY on a *provable* pure renumber (identical descriptive suffix, exactly one lower-numbered stale record, unapplied readable on-disk dependency); a genuine divergence is left untouched so real schema drift surfaces as a failure, never masked
- **Post-DB steps** run after import (password reset, fixtures, …) — declared per overlay
- **State reconciler** (`t3 <overlay> workspace doctor`) reconciles DB ↔ disk ↔ docker drift on demand. Alongside the infra stores it surfaces the **work-tracking-truth** drift classes (SELFCATCH-1) — `UnpushedWork` (commits absent from all remotes), `DoneButUnmerged` (a done-claiming ticket with no `MergeAudit` and a non-upstream branch), `DuplicateScope` (>1 worktree dir per issue) — each built from the existing `commits_absent_from_all_remotes` / `branch_redundancy` / `find_foreign_issue_worktrees` probes with fail-closed semantics (an inconclusive probe is a finding, never a silent pass). The global `work_state` loop scanner runs the same probes each tick and emits `workstate.drift` into the action-needed statusline zone, so committed-but-unpushed / done-but-unmerged / duplicate-scope work surfaces autonomously instead of when a human asks. Read-only — it surfaces drift; destructive remediation stays gated
- **Redis is a normal per-stack compose service** — there is no teatree-owned shared Redis container or DB-index pool. An overlay whose app needs Redis runs it as an ordinary `docker-compose` service in the worktree's own stack (its own container, isolated network), reached by the app at the in-stack hostname. One-stack-at-a-time provisioning makes a shared pool pointless; the per-stack container IS the isolation. `worktree start` heals a `provisioned` worktree whose DB was never created (interrupted provision), and `ServiceLauncher` is single-flight per `(worktree, service)` so concurrent frontend rebuilds never race the output dir

---

## 15. Dependencies

`pyproject.toml` is the only dependency list and the SSOT for every version; this section does not restate it — it names the architecturally load-bearing runtime pins so a reader knows WHY each is there.

Load-bearing pins: `claude-agent-sdk` is an EXACT pin (not a floor) because `tests/test_claude_cli_pin.py` derives the eval/test image's `claude` CLI generation from that exact version — the generation the wheel BUNDLES and actually executes — and reds on a bump until it is re-derived from the new wheel; both tiers are additionally held at or above `_CATALOG_CLI_FLOOR`, the highest `MODEL_MINIMUM_CLI_VERSIONS` entry across `tier_models()` — the oldest CLI the API accepts for every tier model — because every other assertion there compares the pins only to EACH OTHER ([#4704](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4704)). A second guard in that file holds the wheel's BUNDLED CLI — the binary the SDK’s bundled CLI locator actually execs — at or above that same floor, the gap that once took planning down factory-wide while every string-comparing lane stayed green. It resolves against `mcp>=2,<3` with no override — the pinned release declares `mcp>=1.23.0,<3.0.0`, which admits that bound, so the `[tool.uv] override-dependencies` entry that once narrowed `mcp<2.0.0` is now empty (the file and every install site's `--overrides` stay wired for the next entry). The wide bound is safe because the SDK reaches only the low-level `mcp.server.Server` / `mcp.types` surface, never the `mcp.server.mcpserver.MCPServer` surface `teatree.mcp.server` uses; `tests/test_claude_agent_sdk_pin.py::TestTheSdkResolvesUnderTheLockedMcp` re-derives that import set from the installed wheel, reds if any of it stops resolving, and reds if a release re-narrows the bound so the override has to come back. It carries NO Dependabot `ignore` entry: the quarantine that once froze it (a bundled CLI at/after 2.1.204 renders an `AskUserQuestion` call as a markdown chip rather than a `tool_use` block, reddening every scenario matching that tool call) was replaced by the `surface` axis below, which makes those scenarios advisory ([#3855](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3855)); `pydantic-ai-slim[anthropic,openai]` is a RUNTIME dep (not dev-only) because `agent_harness=pydantic_ai` is a live headless-dispatch path (the `[anthropic]` extra backs the CLI-free Anthropic Messages-API binding, the `[openai]` extra the OrcaRouter client); `click` is DECLARED (not left transitive through typer/django-typer) and held `<8.5` because 8.5.0's own `Argument(help=...)` overwrites the value `TyperArgument` sets pre-`super()`, blanking every typer-declared positional-argument description in `t3 --help` and in the regenerated CLI reference, and `.github/dependabot.yml` carries the matching version-scoped `>=8.5` ignore because Dependabot's uv updater rewrites the bound itself; `croniter` parses the `[teatree.availability].windows` cron expressions (§17.1 invariant 9); `tomlkit` renders the `config_setting export` DB→TOML interchange dump; `django-linear-migrations` records the per-app `max_migration.txt` fork sentinel (§4 migration-fork probe); `gunicorn`/`whitenoise` serve the dashboard; `mcp` backs the MCP server; `pillow` handles image attachments; `coverage`/`asgiref`/`pyyaml` are transitive-critical direct pins; `pydantic-settings` backs the typed settings schema (`teatree.config.schema`); `opentelemetry-sdk` + `opentelemetry-exporter-otlp-proto-http` carry the admission and factory spans (`teatree.core.telemetry.admission`), local sink always on, OTLP optional; `httpx2` is declared because openai 3 and pydantic-ai build their clients on it and `teatree.llm.usage_tee` hooks its response types; `cryptography` supplies the AES-GCM primitives behind `teatree.utils.sealing` (the sealed-credential store); `packaging` supplies the specifier semantics `t3 doctor`'s installed-version skew check needs (`teatree.utils.dep_skew`).

Optional extras (`markdown`, `notion`, `slack`) install on demand; the dev tooling lives in `[dependency-groups]`. Security auditing is `uv-audit` in CI (there is no `safety` dependency).

**Keeping dependencies current, and knowing when that failed ([#4346](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4346)).** Three surfaces, each covering a gap the others cannot see. Dependabot's `uv` ecosystem watches `pyproject.toml` **and** `uv.lock`, so a security release the declared constraint already permits — needing only a lockfile move — is still proposed; the `pip` ecosystem could not propose one, which is how Django 6.0.8 sat unoffered for a week under `django>=6,<6.1`. A deliberate hold (`click<8.5`, `ruff==0.15.1`) survives the uv updater — which rewrites a pyproject bound itself — only as a version-scoped `ignore` starting exactly where the bound stops, so every release the bound admits, security fixes included, is still proposed; `tests/test_dependabot_ecosystem.py::TestHoldsSurviveDependabot` derives each range from the pyproject bound and reds on a whole-dependency ignore, a range reaching into the admitted versions, or ruff drifting from the `ruff-pre-commit` hook rev ([#4900](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4900)). The weekly `uv-lock-upgrade` workflow re-resolves the whole graph, which per-package PRs never do. And the `uv-audit` job opens with a **canary** (`scripts/ci/audit_canary.py`): a green audit is indistinguishable from a blind one, because nothing reds when the tool reports *nothing* — an empty OSV response, a parser regression, an over-broad allowlist and a renamed flag all look exactly like "no findings". The canary feeds the same tool a pin with permanent advisories and demands one back (`SEEING` / `BLIND` / `UNREADABLE`, exit 0 only on `SEEING`), so the gate's green means the gate ran. It bounds that green to *"as current as the advisory databases"*, and no further: measured on 2026-08-15, neither the `osv` nor the `pypi` backend reports any advisory for `django==6.0.7`, eleven days after 6.0.8 shipped four CVEs — so no audit backend could have caught that miss, and the canary would have been green throughout. Catching a release the databases have published no range for is version-currency's job, which is why the dependabot half above is the one that closes it. When the gate does find something, a failure-only step runs `t3 tool dependency-audit` (`teatree.quality.dependency_audit`), which annotates each advisory with whether the affected component is reachable from `src/` — 6.0.8's High-severity raster CVE could not be reached here while its Moderate admin one could, so severity alone ranked them the wrong way round. The verdict is three-valued: `NOT_IMPORTED` is the reassuring answer, so it is reported only when the distribution→import mapping came from installed metadata; a guessed mapping that finds nothing reports `UNKNOWN`. Reachability is a static lower bound over `import` statements, never a proof of deadness.

**What that currency may move on its own ([#4437](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4437)).** The weekly refresh above opens its PR with a PAT so CI fires and then calls `gh pr merge --auto --squash`, so whatever it resolved lands on green with no human step. The gap that made dangerous was a ceiling nothing asserted: widening `django>=6,<6.1` to `<6.2` resolves to the SAME version in isolation — `uv` preserves locked versions — so the diff reads as a no-op, and what it actually authorises is the *next* automated resolve (measured: 6.0.8, the in-series patch, under `<6.1`; 6.1, a new feature series, under `<6.2`). Two surfaces close it. `tests/test_django_version_ceiling.py` pins the floor and the ceiling as constants and additionally refuses a window spanning more than one feature series, naming [#4809](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4809) — which owns the Django window — as the only place the pin is decided. And `scripts/ci/lock_delta.py` classifies the resolve itself: it diffs the pre-upgrade lockfile against the produced one, ranks each move `downgrade` / `major` / `minor` / `unknown` / `patch`, renders the PR body from what it measured (a body written once at creation described a resolve later reruns replaced), and returns the auto-merge verdict. That verdict is patch-only and fail-closed — a move to an older release, a minor, a major, and a version string it cannot parse all withhold auto-merge and route the PR to the factory's cold review of the diff and tests, which then merges it (a bump is never refused), an unreadable lockfile reds the job rather than opening a PR of unknown provenance, and an empty verdict (a skipped or failed classify step) reads as unsafe by shape. Added and removed transitive packages are reported and never block: the whole-graph resolve churns those routinely and neither is a version boundary. Because a withheld PR waits for that review and the same boundary is still there next Sunday, the refresh reuses an already-open refresh branch instead of stacking one more identical PR every week.

**A seven-day wait before a new release is locked ([#5163](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5163)).** `[tool.uv] exclude-newer = "7 days"` makes every resolve — `uv lock`, the weekly refresh, a Dependabot update — lock only releases at least seven days old; `uv.lock` records the window as `exclude-newer-span = "P7D"` under `[options]`. uv reads that value from 0.9.17 on; an older uv drops the whole `[tool.uv]` table and rewrites `uv.lock`, so both `uv-pre-commit` pins (the repo's and the overlay template's) run a uv at least that new, the `uv-lock` hook fails a lock an older uv rewrote, and `tests/test_dependency_cooldown.py` holds the window, the lock's span and the pins together. Dependabot's security updates skip Dependabot's own cooldown, but uv's window still filters the version such an update would lock, so a fix published inside the window is taken through a dated escape. A resolve that moves a package to an older release is `downgrade` in `lock_delta.py` and never self-merges. **Taking a release inside the window.** Trigger: a security advisory or a reviewed routine refresh needs a release younger than seven days; for an advisory, the required `uv-audit` check reds on a fixed release younger than seven days (Dependabot's update cannot resolve it: uv reports the version filtered by `exclude-newer`). Whoever picks up that red check — the factory's red-check lane or the owner — adds, in one PR reviewed like any other, `[tool.uv] exclude-newer-package = { <package> = "<cutoff>" }`, plus one entry for each co-released dependency the fix needs (mcp 2.3.0 also needs mcp-types 2.3.0), then runs `uv lock --upgrade-package <package>` once per escaped package and regenerates the SBOM. The cutoff is the newest upload time among ALL files PyPI lists for the escaped release (the sdist and every wheel), rounded UP to the next whole second in UTC `Z` form: uv's cutoff is exclusive, so an exact upload time drops that file from the lock. `python scripts/ci/cooldown_escapes.py --verify-lock` checks each entry against PyPI before the push — every file of the escaped version that the lock's Python can run is in `uv.lock`, and the cutoff is exactly that rounded-up time — and refuses a `false` (it never expires) or a fractional value. The `uv-audit` CI job runs that check on every event (it reads PyPI only while an escape exists) and prints a pointer to this routine whenever pip-audit reds. Once the cutoff plus seven days has passed, the window admits the release by itself: the job's scheduled run then fails on the entry (`--fail-on-expired`, a warning on every other event), and whoever handles it removes it from `pyproject.toml` and relocks, which moves no version.

**What a version verdict cannot see ([#4569](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4569)).** A refresh PR's diff is whatever has been pushed onto its branch, not just the `uv.lock` the workflow committed: #4490 self-merged 108 `src/` and 80 `tests/` files pushed there to make a `ty 0.0.20 → 0.0.73` resolve green, because a patch-level VERSION verdict is silent about PATHS. `scripts/ci/refresh_scope.py` asks the other half — is every changed path `uv.lock`, `dist/sbom.json` or under `docs/generated/` (renames counted as both paths; an unreadable or empty diff never vouches) — and arming needs both verdicts. Arming happens at creation, so `lock-refresh-scope.yml` re-asks on every push to a `chore/uv-lock-upgrade*` PR, judged by the base's workflow and script (`pull_request_target`, nothing from the head executed). An escape disarms auto-merge, verified by re-reading the PR, and the PR goes to the factory's cold review, then merges; the check stays green because a bump is never refused, and reds only when it cannot read the diff or the disarm does not land.

**Keeping the SBOM in step with a Dependabot bump ([#4907](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4907)).** `dist/sbom.json` is generated from `uv.lock`, and the required `sbom` job regenerates it and diffs, so a Dependabot uv bump — which moves the lockfile and never the SBOM — goes red on that gate by construction; the weekly refresh above regenerates the SBOM itself and is unaffected. The `dependabot-sbom-sync.yml` follow-up closes it: a `workflow_run` on the CI workflow, gated to failed `pull_request` runs Dependabot started on a same-repo `dependabot/uv/**` branch, downloads the `sbom` artifact that job uploaded (kept by its `if: always()`) and commits those bytes verbatim onto the exact head CI tested, non-force, with `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN` so CI re-fires. `workflow_run` is what makes this possible at all: a Dependabot `pull_request` run gets no Actions secrets, and a `GITHUB_TOKEN` push never re-triggers CI. It copies rather than regenerates because regenerating means `uv sync` from a PR-chosen lockfile inside a job holding a write credential; the job installs nothing and never checks out the PR's tree. `scripts/ci/sbom_drift.py` decides what may land: `version-only` — the two documents equal once each component's own version is masked in `version`, `purl` and `description` — is synced, while an added, removed, renamed or re-referenced component, a project-version or a dependency-graph change reads `component-set-changed` and stays red for a human. It also skips a head that moved since the run and a base that changed an SBOM input after the merge-base, because CI built the merge ref. The alternatives were rejected: routing these PRs to the red-PR fixer spends an agent on a byte copy and depends on the box, and a version-blind `sbom` check narrows a supply-chain gate while an SBOM without versions is useless. The cost is that Dependabot stops rebasing a PR carrying a foreign commit; `@dependabot recreate` drops it and the next red run re-applies the sync.

---

## 16. Key Conventions

- Python 3.13+. Use `X | Y` union syntax. Never `Optional`.
- `from __future__ import annotations` is banned.
- No docstrings on classes/methods by policy. Self-documenting code (names + types are the documentation).
- Management commands use `django-typer`, not `BaseCommand`.
- `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` is stripped from env when running `managepy()` so the overlay's own settings win.
- **Port allocation is ephemeral (Non-Negotiable).** Host ports are auto-mapped by Docker at `worktree start`; never written to `.t3-cache/.t3-env.cache`, the DB, or any other persistent store. Inter-service traffic uses compose service DNS.
- **GitHub access is HTTPS + `gh auth git-credential` (Non-Negotiable).** Every GitHub remote is `https://github.com/<slug>.git` and git delegates auth to the `gh` credential helper `deploy/entrypoint.sh` installs via `gh auth setup-git`; auth is token-based, so there is no key to distribute, mount or rotate. An ssh-form GitHub remote, a `url` … `insteadOf` rewrite naming github.com, and a `~/.ssh` made load-bearing by either are all forbidden — while the GitLab credential helper and the ssh-tolerant remote *parsers* (`utils/git_remote.py`, `core/public_identity.py`) stay separate, legitimate paths. `tests/conformance/test_github_access_is_https.py` asserts it over the tracked tree; `t3 doctor`'s `_check_github_remotes_are_https` asserts it over the live gitconfig of every discovered checkout, the untracked half a static scan cannot see ([#4447](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4447)).
- Coverage omits only migrations.
- The headless executor's SDK run is non-persistent (exits with the task). The `t3 worker` is the persistent process; an attended session is optional.
- Statusline state is rendered to a file (`${XDG_DATA_HOME:-$HOME/.local/share}/teatree/statusline.txt`, override `TEATREE_STATUSLINE_FILE`) by the loop and `cat`-ed by the hook, which does no DB or network I/O.
- **Overlay-specific names must not appear in the scanned roots.** The gate (`scripts/hooks/check_no_overlay_leak.py`) enforces this — `BLUEPRINT § 1` — across `src/teatree`, `docs`, `skills`, `agents`, `tests`, `scripts` and top-level `README.md`/`BLUEPRINT.md`/`AGENTS.md`, run full-tree by the `overlay-leak-tree` CI job. Forbidden terms are loaded at runtime from the `overlay` class of `$TEATREE_TERM_REGISTRY` or the DB `banned_term_registry` setting so the public repo never holds tenant names; an empty class or missing registry refuses the scan.
- E2E tests use file-based SQLite (not `:memory:`) because Playwright spawns a separate server process.

---

## 17. The Self-Improving Factory Architecture

Teatree is a durable self-healing **and** self-improving development factory. This section is the lasting architectural reference — the umbrella under [#836](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/836); each component below is a separately tracked ticket implemented as deterministic teatree code, not skill prose.

Why this architecture exists, observed repeatedly: durability comes from **enforcement encoded in code/structure**, not prose that decays. A rule kept in memory/skills and relied on by vigilance recurs anyway; the same rule encoded as a gate/test/hook does not. The invariants below are the structural form of that lesson — load-bearing, binding every change to teatree itself.

### 17.1 Invariants

1. **Two layers, never conflated.** *Self-healing* (independent review, draft-locks, recovery, gates) is the substrate. *Self-improvement* runs on top: each caught failure-class becomes the smallest enforcement artifact that makes the class structurally impossible. Self-improvement is **gated by** self-healing — the system is never changed in a way the healing layer cannot catch or roll back.

2. **The flywheel.** A defect (from diff review, OR the code-health loop on un-changed code, OR an orchestrator-noticed near-miss) → the orchestrator synthesises → output is the *smallest enforcement* (gate/test/hook), never a prose rule → the failure-class is extinct. A repeat failure whose only output is memory/prose is a flywheel failure.

3. **Topology.** The orchestrator is the synthesis brain (retro synthesis, code-health triage, enforcement escalation, merge/clear decisions). Sub-agents are sensors/hands emitting structured signal into durable state, never self-judging. Skills carry judgment/methodology; teatree code carries the deterministic loops, gates, and intake. Corollary: mechanics → code, judgment → skill.

4. **Blast-radius rule.** Substrate changes are draft-locked and need a recorded human sign-off, satisfied by EITHER a per-PR `MergeClear.human_authorizer` OR the overlay carrying a STANDING merge-approval grant — `autonomy = full`, OR an explicit `require_human_approval_to_merge = false` on a non-collaborative tier ([#2666](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2666): `mode = auto` + `require_human_approval_to_merge = false` is the same owner statement as `autonomy = full`; the collaborative `notify` tier is excluded since it merges only after a colleague approval). Either standing grant lets the overlay merge substrate without a per-PR sign-off. Either way the agent runs the `t3 <overlay> ticket merge` keystone (§17.4.3); the human never performs the merge. The carve-out removes ONLY the per-PR sign-off — the floor (cold-review `reviewer != maker`, SHA-bind, CI-green, not-draft, never-lockout, privacy/leak scan) holds on every substrate merge; with the merge-approval gate ON on a below-full tier the `human_authorizer` stays mandatory. The path detector `diff_paths_are_substrate` is what holds this when `blast_class` is left at its `logic` default, and it matches each changed path BOTH as the forge reported it and with the `vendor/teatree/` prefix (`paths.VENDORED_CORE_PREFIX`) stripped — a forge reports paths relative to the WORKING-TREE root, so in a fork that vendors core every `src/teatree/…` literal would otherwise match nothing and the whole detector would be dead code, while such a fork's own root-level `hooks/` and `docs/blueprint/` must keep matching.

   `skills/rules/RULES.md` is substrate by exact path. This hold requires the owner's per-PR authorization or standing delegation; it does not prove the owner read the diff.

5. **Durability discipline is load-bearing.** Durable task/state plus pre-compaction snapshots let the orchestrator brain survive compaction/restart; keep them.

6. **Enforcement over prose, as a standing audit.** Invariant 2 makes the flywheel's *output* a gate; this invariant makes the *standing posture* explicit. Every user behavioural directive MUST be (a) codified in teatree and (b) enforced by deterministic code/gates wherever mechanizable, so skill prose shrinks to the judgment that genuinely cannot be mechanized and skills get **lighter** over time. This is a recurring retro/review responsibility: the enforcement gate (§17.6 / [#850](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/850)) turns a mechanizable rule into a gate, and the recurring audit keeps reclassifying prose rules → code gates.

7. **Consolidation over drift.** Behavior encoded outside the teatree framework — personal `settings.json` hooks, dotfiles automation, overlay-local ad-hoc config, personal memory guardrails — must be considered for promotion into teatree on every retro/review pass. Genuine per-instance variance must be modelled as a documented teatree setting or config knob; undocumented divergence silently drifts and violates invariant 2.

8. **All FSM state transitions go through the `t3` CLI.** The pre-condition and pre/post transition hooks are the coherence mechanism (ledger update, attestation-binding to the HEAD/workstream the phase was earned against, privacy/AI-signature scan, `mark_merged()`). Out-of-band state mutation — raw `gh pr merge` / `glab mr merge`, or hand-editing the phase ledger / FSM state — is prohibited and **mechanically guarded** (`hook_router._BLOCKED_COMMANDS`, the same hook layer as the draft-lock and structured-question gates). The keystone REVIEW_REQUESTED → MERGED transition is §17.4; the non-bypassable gate placement is §17.6.3. The two sanctioned escapes for stale state — `lifecycle clear-ledger` and the hardened `lifecycle visit-phase … reviewing --agent-id` — are themselves `t3` commands, never manual edits.

9. **Every user-directed question is captured — sync or durable — and reaches Slack.** A user-directed question must either (a) call `AskUserQuestion` with the user reachable this turn, or (b) be recorded as a `DeferredQuestion` row and posted to the owner's Slack DM. Mode resolution is a single deterministic precedence (exposed by `t3 loop preset show`): the manual `ModeOverride` (it never expires; only a person lifts it) → the active schedule's governing slot → the configured `default_mode` (`present`). Live presence routes questions, never the mode. A `Mode` row is a pure per-loop on/off table ([#61](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/61) merged availability into the mode; [#3826](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3826) deleted its last serialization, the fast-hook mirror file; [#4202](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4202) deleted the three posture booleans that survived), so no mode buffers a question: questions are asked immediately ([#4045](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4045)) and only a LOOP-DRIVEN call is denied, because a suspended autonomous session cannot wait for a Slack reply in-band. One reader walks that one chain over the one control DB — `mode_resolution.resolve_active_mode` — and every unreadable input fails toward ASKING, never toward silence. The deferral path is a *sanctioned destination* for the same `AskUserQuestion` call (converted at the `PreToolUse` layer, never bypassing the §807 structured-question gate); a LOOP-DRIVEN capture reaches the owner's DM through the one `drain_unmirrored_deferred_questions` → `notify_user` egress, kicked at capture time so it does not wait for a tick ([#4673](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4673)). An ATTENDED question is checked before it renders: one decision per question-tool call, plain words and the shared card length limits; a Stop check bounces final-reply decision lists back to the question tool. An ATTENDED question is never duplicated to Slack: the owner is at the terminal it renders in, so it is neither posted nor recorded — an un-mirrored row is what that drain selects, so recording one would re-post it a cadence later. A live user turn (the owner typed, or answered in-client, in this session's transcript within a 90 s freshness window) renders in-client so `/checking` works without a mode flip, and every capture unifies on one generation-stamped, mirror-linked `DeferredQuestion` — a Slack reply binds the live generation, and the answer is readable on demand (`t3 <overlay> questions list --all`); an answer to a question the asking session recorded is also handed back to that session at its next `SessionStart` or `Stop` (`teatree.answer_handback`), and nothing is injected into any other session. The live-turn mechanics live in `docs/blueprint/loop-topology.md`. Component: §17.3 C3.

   Only an attended session may answer a card addressed to the owner in his place under this section and `skills/interactive/SKILL.md`; a headless run never does so under that section.

10. **Orchestrator never executes work directly — every implementation, review, test, debug, and ship action is dispatched to a sub-agent.** The orchestrator's role is synthesis, classification, dispatch, and CLEAR issuance (invariant 3, §17.4.1, §17.8 clause 3); the *hands* are sub-agents (`t3:code`, `t3:review`, `t3:test`, `t3:debug`, `t3:shipper`, `/t3:wip`'s delivery sub-agent) and the durable loop. Inlining implementation work — even a "trivial" typo Edit, a Bash run redoing a sub-agent's job, a local test cycle dodging `t3:test` — conflates judgment with execution, denies maker≠checker independence, and concentrates the compaction risk the topology spreads across durable handoffs. **Narrow exceptions:** read-only orientation in the orchestrator's own session (`Read`/`Grep`, `gh pr view`, `git status`, `AskUserQuestion`, sanctioned messaging), the `t3 …` invocations it owns (`MergeClear`, attestation, next-dispatch), and conversational replies with no repo mutation. Mechanized as gate 2 in §17.6.4 (`handle_enforce_orchestrator_boundary`): a `PreToolUse` deny on a main-agent LONG/HEAVY foreground `Bash` command, with `run_in_background: true` the escape hatch and a payload `agent_id` distinguishing sub-agent from main ([#115](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/115)). It shares one `_fail_open_or_deny` chokepoint (with always-available `t3 <overlay> gate … disable` kill-switches and the `self_rescue` allowlist) with the sibling over-deny gates; the PUBLIC-egress leak gate is excluded and stays fail-CLOSED. Detail in §17.6.4. The one audited per-action exception path in §13 applies to this gate family as well: `orchestrator_bash`, `orchestrator_foreground_dispatch` and `orchestrator_delegation_gate` each require their own gate ID and durable record. The action digest, actor, session, subject and HEAD bind a record to one call; old inline markers do not relax the refusal.

11. **The reactive Slack-answer loop is the one consumer of the `PendingChatInjection` queue.** The inbound-Slack bridge (#1014) records each user DM as a `PendingChatInjection` row; the loop reads its work through `PendingChatInjection.loop_unreplied()` (`loop_replied_at IS NULL`, or an unconfirmed session or question claim older than two minutes), narrowed to the rows the overlay's configured Slack owner sent (`owner_identity.owner_rows`). No session hook drains or marks the rows, so nothing in a session's prompt path depends on the control DB. The `(overlay, slack_ts)` `UniqueConstraint` lets the scanner over-poll safely.

12. **An outcome-claiming completion carries a resolvable artifact pointer, fail-closed.** The out-of-band completion surface (`tasks complete --note`) records externally-landed work. `teatree.core.review.completion_evidence` makes two separate judgments: a note *asserts an outcome* when an `OUTCOME_CLAIM_KINDS` verb (`merged`/`posted`/`shipped`/`deployed` + synonyms) co-occurs with a context cue (internal idioms like `merge conflict` are stripped first), and an asserting note must carry a *resolvable pointer* an auditor can follow — a URL, a cued git SHA, an MR/PR/issue ref, a note id, a Slack ts, or a real/dotted-module path — else `check_completion_evidence` refuses (`CompletionEvidenceError`). A completion with no note, or asserting no outcome, is never gated (the structural form of invariant 6's "done claims require evidence"); the exact matchers live in `completion_evidence.py`. A `failed → completed` resolution requires a mandatory `--note` ([#1949](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1949)); `claimed → completed` stays note-optional.

13. **Post-write side effects are confirmed by re-reading, not assumed from the write's own response** ([#1192](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1192)). A write's own success response (`ok: true`, a harness tool call returning without error) is not proof its effect is actually visible — eventual consistency, a stale replica, or a race can make a claimed write unobservable moments later. `teatree.core.verify_by_reread.verify_by_reread` is the small core contract: given a `reread` callable that independently re-observes the target system, it returns a `RereadOutcome` (`confirmed` / `not_confirmed(reason)`) and never raises — a verification failure is a signal to log or retry, never a reason to crash the write path. Call sites: Slack `reactions.add` (`teatree.backends.slack.reactions.add_reaction_verified`, wired into `add_reactions_for_transition` / `add_approval_reaction`) re-reads via `reactions.get` before trusting the write; and `create_pr` ([#1194](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1194), `teatree.core.merge.pr_create_verify.verify_pr_exists`) re-reads a just-created PR's open-state on the ship / `ensure-pr` path before recording the URL, so a phantom PR (a 404 re-read) is reported failed and never advances the FSM. Inbound-event ingestion applies the sibling shape — a failed drain records the error and retries with backoff, then dead-letters (`IncomingEvent.record_failure`, [#673](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/673)) so a poison event never silently drops nor blocks the queue. Every tool on a foreign MCP server, reads included, is refused at the `PreToolUse` boundary by `handle_block_foreign_mcp_tool`; agents use teatree MCP tools or the `t3` CLI. Direct MCP Slack writes are refused at the `PreToolUse` boundary (`handle_block_mcp_slack_write`, [#1196](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1196), kill-switch `[teatree] mcp_slack_write_gate_enabled`) so every Slack write routes through the `t3` CLI egress chokepoint under this contract.

14. **teatree resolves all required state from its own stores; assistant memory is never a functional input** ([#3277](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3277)). Every piece of state teatree needs to *function* — config, gate enablement, publishing/merge doctrine, factory settings, loop state, trusted identities, credential routing — resolves from teatree's OWN stores: the DB-home `ConfigSetting` / `LoopState` tables (via the Django-free `teatree.config.cold_reader` / `cold_db`, or the ORM), `pass`, and repo config. An assistant's `MEMORY.md` / `~/.claude/**/memory` / per-project memory file is a convenience for the *assistant*, NEVER a source of truth for the factory — otherwise the factory would behave differently on another machine, another agent, or a fresh session, violating the portability requirement (§17 clause 5). The only teatree paths that read the memory dir treat it as *product data teatree maintains or surfaces*, each degrading to a clean no-op when the dir is absent: the nightly `teatree.loops.dream` consolidation pass (cross-link / merge / reindex / decay) that MAINTAINS the corpus. Pinned behaviorally by `tests/test_no_agent_memory_dependency.py`: a representative slice of the runtime state resolvers is byte-identical whether the assistant-memory dir is absent or populated with contradicting config-shaped bait, and a poisoned `Path.home()` proves no state resolver reaches the home tree.

### 17.2 The flywheel — 17.8 Orchestrator-as-keystone contract

The flywheel diagram, components (C1 Retro / C2 Code-health loop / C3 Availability), §17.4 Orchestrator-decides / loop-executes topology, §17.5 TODO-consolidation triage, §17.6 Enforcement gate (anti-relaxation, sound tach boundaries, the shipped gate family), §17.7 Enforcement-over-prose, and §17.8 Orchestrator-as-keystone contract — all live in [docs/blueprint/factory-architecture.md](docs/blueprint/factory-architecture.md), where the section headings (`### 17.2`–`### 17.8`) are preserved for cross-references.

**Anti-pattern catalog ([#166](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/166)).** `src/teatree/quality/antipatterns.yaml` is the SSOT for recurring architectural anti-patterns; `teatree.quality.catalog` loads it and a hook renders [docs/generated/antipattern-catalog.md](docs/generated/antipattern-catalog.md). The `docs-drift` CI job regenerates the catalog (with `ANTIPATTERN_CATALOG_NO_STAGE=1` so it cannot self-stage and hide drift) before the `git diff --exit-code docs/generated` gate, so a yaml→catalog skew reds CI unconditionally. Each entry's `detection` tier (`greppable` vs `judgement`) feeds the three review tiers: design-time (`architecture-design`), per-PR deterministic (`check_antipatterns.py`), periodic holistic (`architectural-review`). The periodic tier's own output is gated the way its subject is ([#4215](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4215)): a finding ships only when a **distinct** reader positively confirms it — default refuted, severity set by the verifier — and every subsystem verdict states what was read, so a skipped subsystem cannot render as a clean one. Its judgement-entry list is a derived cache of this catalog, kept honest by `tests/teatree_skill_support/test_architectural_review_method.py` rather than by a second store. The sibling `t3 tool test-shape` flags near-identical unparametrized tests and test:source ratio regressions past a baseline — advisory `warn` by default, opt-in `block`.

**Worktree-FSM diagram generator ([#12](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/12)).** `src/teatree/core/diagrams.py` renders the `Worktree` django-fsm transition graph to a byte-stable Mermaid `stateDiagram-v2` (pure introspection, no I/O); `scripts/hooks/generate_fsm_diagrams.py` writes the canonical [docs/generated/diagrams/worktree-lifecycle.md](docs/generated/diagrams/worktree-lifecycle.md) and splices the identical block between worktree-fsm markers in `README.md` + `skills/workspace/SKILL.md`, with `check_fsm_diagrams_sync.py` as the `docs-drift` gate — the same generate→drift-gate shape as the cli-reference pair, so the lifecycle diagram can never drift from the model.

**Admin-dashboard HTML snapshot ([#12](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/12)).** `src/teatree/core/factory/dashboard_snapshot.py` renders the teatree Django admin index — frozen to a dedicated `AdminSite` carrying only `teatree.core`'s registered model admins, with output-affecting settings (`ROOT_URLCONF`, `STATIC_URL`, `LANGUAGE_CODE`, `TIME_ZONE`, `DEBUG`) pinned and the CSRF token stripped — to a byte-stable HTML string via Django's test client; `scripts/hooks/generate_dashboard_snapshot.py` stands up a throwaway sqlite test DB and writes the canonical [docs/generated/dashboard/admin-index.html](docs/generated/dashboard/admin-index.html) (which carries the two token `<link>`s the `templates/admin/base_site.html` re-skin injects; the hook's `files:` glob re-fires on that override + `admin-theme.css`). The `docs-drift` job regenerates it (NO_STAGE) before the same `git diff --exit-code docs/generated` gate the diagrams use, and `tests/teatree_core/factory/test_dashboard_snapshot.py` is the loud in-suite gate (it owns a DB, so it plays the `check_fsm_diagrams_sync.py` role) — so the dashboard "screenshot" can never drift from the registered models. `core/admin.py` registers every core model: one without a hand-written admin gets the list-only `ReadOnlyAdmin` (403 on writes and per-row pages; Text/JSON and secret-looking columns never selected), and `tests/conformance/test_every_model_has_an_admin.py` holds the writable-admin allowlist, so the snapshot lists all of them.

**Representative CLI-output snapshot ([#12](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/12)).** `src/teatree/cli/cli_output_snapshot.py` renders the front-door `t3` command output (`t3 --help`, `t3 loop --help`) to byte-stable markdown via `teatree.cli.command_tree.render_help_blocks` — the same #2599 determinism seam (pinned width, no env-derived sizing, home-rooted defaults folded to `~`, overlay surface pinned to `t3-teatree`) the CLI reference uses, so no timestamp/duration/PID/path can leak; `scripts/hooks/generate_cli_output_snapshot.py` writes the canonical [docs/generated/cli/representative-output.md](docs/generated/cli/representative-output.md). The `docs-drift` job regenerates it (NO_STAGE) before the same `git diff --exit-code docs/generated` gate, and `tests/teatree_cli/test_cli_output_snapshot.py` is the loud in-suite gate — the curated, always-fresh complement to the exhaustive CLI reference (#67).

**Scoped mutation testing ([#131](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/131)).** `t3 mutation run` runs mutmut over only diff-touched modules in the NARROW `[tool.teatree.mutation]` safety registry; `tests/quality/test_mutation*.py` carry the ledger + kill-proof. `BaselineRatchet` ([#1926](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1926)) makes the recorded `baseline_surviving` count a ratchet — a run above it fails in BOTH `warn` and `block` mode (count may only shrink), the prerequisite to flipping `mode` to `block`.

**A verdict is a claim about a set that was ENUMERATED ([#4135](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4135)).** `t3 tool triage-issues` printed a clear backlog while both of its `gh` calls had failed unauthenticated, and a hand triage of the same repo at the same moment found three resolved-but-open issues. Each enumerator was individually correct — it wrote the failure to stderr — and each verdict was individually correct given its input; the defect is the seam, because `return []` on a failed read makes "the forge could not be reached" and "the backlog is clear" the same bytes. Every `gh` read in `teatree/triage.py` now goes through one chokepoint that raises `ForgeEnumerationError`, and the three `t3 tool` verdicts report UNKNOWN and exit non-zero rather than a verdict they never established — so `--close-resolved` cannot act on a scan that did not run. `tests/conformance/test_failed_enumeration_reports_unknown.py` holds the family as an AST walk plus behavioural lanes, and it is SCOPED to the triage tool surface on purpose: `src/teatree` has ~34 sites returning a neutral value on a non-zero `returncode` and most are legitimate probes whose empty answer honestly means absent, so a tree-wide property would need an allowlist longer than itself — a change detector wearing an invariant's clothes. The general doctrine it instantiates is `skills/rules/SKILL.md` § "External Read Failure Must Fail Loud, Never Silent-Empty".

**Test-path-mirror forward-guard.** `t3 tool test-path-mirror` enforces *tests mirror production code*: a test file's first-party imports (parsed by AST, never executed) must place it under the mirror dir `tests/teatree_<pkg>/...` of at least one imported module; a top-level src *module* maps to the `tests` root as an EXACT-only expectation. The verdict is a per-path **ledger**, not a scalar count: `[tool.teatree.test_path_mirror] baseline_file` points at a committed sorted grandfathered-path list (`tests/test_path_mirror_grandfathered.txt`). The gate is RED when any LIVE violation is NOT in the ledger (a NEW mis-pathed file, named) AND RED when any ledger entry no longer violates (forced banking — closing the old count-baseline's headroom hole where unbanked reductions let new mis-pathed files ride in green). `--update-baseline` rewrites the ledger to the exact live set but refuses to ADD an entry without `--allow-regression`. A path list merges as a git **set-union**, so two disjoint PRs never collide — the semantic non-mergeability of a single integer (git cannot union two +1s) is gone. A small reviewed exemption set (`tests/integration/`, `tests/e2e*`, `conftest.py`, a `# test-path: cross-cutting` pragma, …) keeps legitimate multi-package tests out of the count.

**Skill-CLI-migration forward-guard ([#3076](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3076)).** `teatree.quality.skill_cli_ratchet` enforces the MCP-serves-everything migration on the *skill* side: skills route their forge/monitoring reach through the teatree MCP tools, not raw `gh`/`glab`/`sentry-cli` shell-outs. It shares the test-path-mirror ledger shape — `[tool.teatree.skill_cli_ratchet] baseline_file` points at a committed `<skill file>::<command signature>` list (`tests/skill_cli_ratchet_grandfathered.txt`) grandfathering the current raw-call surface (the documented `skills/platforms/references/` CLI fallback lane, bootstrap exceptions, and call sites with no MCP tool yet).
