RED on a NEW raw call (a skill regressing to a shell-out, named) and RED on a stale ledger key (forced banking). **Prohibition examples** ("never `gh pr merge`", detected by surrounding never/FORBIDDEN/mechanically-refused markers) and per-line `mcp-ratchet: allow` pragmas are excluded, so rewriting a prohibition into an MCP call — which would invert its meaning — is never demanded. Detection is textual and code-context-aware (fenced blocks + inline backticks only, so prose mentions of a tool are not flagged). The gate runs as a pytest fitness test in the CI `test (3.13)` lane (`tests/teatree_quality/test_skill_cli_ratchet.py`); regenerate the ledger with `python -m teatree.quality.skill_cli_ratchet --update-baseline`. Scoped to the 3rd-party CLIs (`gh`/`glab`/`sentry-cli`); a `t3`-direct ratchet awaits the ratified long-running-CLI exception class. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Merge-safe ratchet ledgers + CI parity.** The two intra-package deferred-import ratchets follow the same per-item ledger shape: `tests/quality/deferred_import_pegs.toml` (`[intra_core]` / `[intra_loop]`) pegs each source file's function-scoped `teatree.core` / `teatree.loop` imports (unlisted files peg at 0), counted by the shared `tests/quality/_deferred_imports.py` walker. Over-peg blocks (names the file), under-peg banks (lower the entry); disjoint per-file bumps merge as set-union, same-file contention surfaces as a git textual conflict. The local/CI predicate gap is closed two ways without violating the no-full-suite-on-push invariant (`tests/test_no_full_suite_on_pre_push.py`): a push-stage `ci-critical-parity` prek hook runs `dev/push-gate.sh` (the fast early signal — the never-lockout contract + the incremental push gate; the broad `tests/quality` dir is CI-only, ~420s locally, covered whole-tree by the `test (3.13)` shard), and `dev/ci-parity.sh` chains the EXACT full CI predicate (prek all-files, `makemigrations --check`, `t3 tool test-path-mirror`, `check_module_health.py --from-ref`, `dev/test-cov.sh`, `t3 ci coverage`) as the one documented pre-push command for src-touching PRs — opt-in by workflow, never a push hook, because the 93% whole-tree coverage floor is a whole-tree property no diff-scoped subset can prove.

The push gate's machine lock covers only its incremental sweep (stage 3); its serial never-lockout and conformance checks run before lock acquisition, while a failed lock acquisition still refuses stage 3. `verify-gates` gives each stage 600 seconds scaled by box load (up to 3×), and adds the push gate's 1500-second lock wait to the pre-push stage deadline so valid queueing is reported as work in progress.

**Incompleteness-marker ratchet.** `teatree.quality.incompleteness_markers` scans every shipped `.py` under `src/teatree`, `hooks/`, and `scripts/`, plus `BLUEPRINT.md` and `docs/blueprint/`, for prose in which the code declares itself unfinished — an author marker, a component described as disconnected from what would make it take effect, a carve-out kept with nothing in it, a field held against work that has not happened. Scope is code plus the architecture spec because those are the surfaces where a false claim actively misleads; dated one-shot design docs are pinned OUT (`ONE_SHOT_DESIGN_DOCS`), so widening the doc scope cannot pull one in unnoticed. Only comments and docstrings are read (`teatree.quality.prose`) — a phrase inside a string literal is a runtime value, not the module describing itself. Matching is case-insensitive and spans one line wrap, because the tree emphasises with capitals and docstrings break sentences mid-phrase. Phrase families are SHAPES in `incompleteness_markers.yaml`, data rather than literals in the scanner, which is what lets the detector scan itself; each carries trigger literals that decide what is worth parsing, with a test proving no file the pre-stage rejected could have matched. The ledger `tests/quality/incompleteness_marker_pegs.toml` takes the same per-file shape as the deferred-import pegs above: over-peg blocks naming file, line, phrase and remedy; under-peg blocks too, so resolving a statement is banked instead of leaving budget for the next one. Raising an entry means shipping an admission that the implementation is incomplete, which is inadmissible by default — finish it, or do not ship the phase. A deterministic sub-gate additionally blocks a marker whose adjacent issue reference resolves to a CLOSED issue (done-but-uncleaned, or a promise nobody tracks), reading the committed `tests/quality/closed_issues.toml` snapshot and never the network: a gate that flakes on an expired token is worse than no gate, and an issue absent from the snapshot reads as open, so staleness costs recall and never a wrong block. Refresh it with `scripts/refresh_closed_issue_manifest.py`. What the ratchet structurally CANNOT catch is the inverse — prose claiming shipped-ness it lacks, which by construction contains no marker. The same registry serves a second, stricter gate over the verification trees `tests/`, `evals/` and `e2e/` (stated in `tests/quality/test_deferred_marker_ban.py`): a marker there records work a test did not do, so it is banned outright with no ledger to bank a count in. Two narrowings make a zero-tolerance gate safe where a guard's own tests must name the strings the guard detects. Only the families the registry marks `form: marker` apply, so adding a family with that form extends the ban and there is no second list; and the marker must OPEN a comment, in the registry's own marker form (the tag carrying a colon, a paren, or an issue ref — a bare word is the harness's domain noun). A docstring is the object documenting itself, a string literal is a runtime value, a YAML block scalar is verbatim carried text and markdown's fenced or indented code is quoted rather than said, JSON and JSONL have no comment syntax at all, and the same word mid-sentence is the file talking ABOUT markers. Ruff's `FIX`/`TD` families are the commit-time half over `.py`, drawing the same comment-versus-mention line; `tests/quality/test_ruff_antislop_caps.py` pins them out of `per-file-ignores` too, since ruff resolves an exemption by path. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Safety-biased incremental push gate ([#122](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/122)).** `dev/push-gate.sh` scopes the two whole-tree costs that dominated every push — the `--doctest-modules src/teatree` sweep (Engine A) and the ast-grep `TestBlockingSetIsGreen` scan (Engine B, relocated to `push_heavy` and CI's `test-shard` like #3032's jscpd/mutmut) — to the changed files. `teatree.quality.changed_set` is the single changed-files + FULL-trigger classifier both the push gate and `scripts/ci/changed_lanes.py` consume (one SSOT, no drift — the `CONFIG_*` sets are hoisted here and imported back). `teatree.quality.push_gate.plan_push_gate` decides scoped vs whole-tree; `scan_findings(paths=…)` scopes Engine B (`paths=None` = the CI-identical whole-tree path). The doctrine is fail-safe-to-FULL: every uncertainty (a `.ast-grep`/manifest/config/conftest/migration change, any non-`.py` under a code dir, any delete/rename, an unclassifiable path, a missing merge-base) runs the whole sweep; a missing ast-grep engine DEFERS Engine B to CI loudly, never wedging the push. The scoped gate runs by default after the CI `selection-audit` soak; uncertainty in changed-file classification falls back to the full sweep. The CI whole-tree backstop (`TestBlockingSetIsGreen` + whole-tree `--doctest-modules` in the required `test (3.13)` check) is NEVER removed; the push gate is a fast early signal, CI is the hard gate. `dev/ci-parity-fast.sh` is the inner-loop parity (scoped prek + `makemigrations --check` + the scoped gate, NO coverage floor — run the full `dev/ci-parity.sh` once before push). Engine A runs the sweep in a child whose `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` is STRIPPED (`push_gate.sweep_env`): an inherited setting may remain in `os.environ`, and pytest-django ranks it above the ini — so an inherited environment silently swaps the settings module out from under the sweep and collects the tree under settings CI never uses ([#3808](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3808)). And a failing sweep is REPORTED, never reduced to an exit code: `_run_doctests` returns a `DoctestOutcome` carrying the exit code and the run's own output, `run_push_gate` appends a note naming the targets, the code and the output tail, and `--emit-cmd` prints `sweep_command` — `sweep_argv` plus the visible `env -u…` strip — so the operator's hand-run is the run that failed. A gate that exits non-zero with no diagnostic is indistinguishable from an environmental flake and teaches its users to bypass it.

**Scope-dependent evidence.** `tests/conformance/test_scope_dependent_callers.py` checks that contextvar readers have tests under their owning scope; a bare call alone cannot prove a scoped read. Playwright evidence is read from its live report path, without a separate epilogue parser.

**One UNSCOPED lane survives on the push path: `tests/conformance`.** Everything else the push gate runs is diff-scoped, because an import graph can decide it. A conformance assertion cannot be scoped even in principle — its input is the whole tree, so a new `ScanSignal` kind with no dispatch/statusline route breaks `tests/conformance/test_signal_route_totality.py` whichever module the diff names. `tests/quality`'s always-run cousin was relocated to CI for cost (~420s); `tests/conformance` is 34s at `-n auto` and does not grow with the diff, so it stays. `dev/test-affected.sh` already force-keeps the dir (`FLOOR_DIRS`), but that lane is opt-in and the hand-picked directory lists agents substitute for it (`pytest tests/teatree_core tests/teatree_loop …`) exclude the dir — which is how the class reached CI twice ([#3787](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/pull/3787), [#3788](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/pull/3788)) behind green local runs. On the push path it is unconditional. Pinned by `tests/test_no_full_suite_on_pre_push.py::TestCiCriticalParityHook` in both directions: `tests/conformance` must be there, `tests/quality` must not.

**The order-dependence lane cannot report a false green.** CI's `test-shuffle` job and its local twin `dev/test-shuffle.sh` both run the curated order-safe set serially under `pytest-randomly`, which lives in the non-default `shuffle` dependency group (installing it into `dev` would shuffle every `uv run pytest` and destabilise the coverage lane). A hand-rolled `uv run pytest -p randomly … | tail` on a plain `dev` env therefore raises `ImportError: Error importing plugin "randomly"` while the PIPELINE exits 0 — a shell pipeline reports its LAST stage's status, so a lane that collected nothing reads green to anything gating on the exit code. The runner closes that with three layered guards: `set -euo pipefail` and no pipes; an `import pytest_randomly` preflight that fails loud with the fix command before pytest is asked to load the plugin; and `-o required_plugins=pytest-randomly`, pytest's own in-session assertion, so a degraded environment cannot yield an UNSHUFFLED green either. `tests/test_ci_shuffle_lane_scope.py` pins each guard against its own removal and pins byte-level directory parity between the local runner and the CI job, so the twin can neither drift narrower (a false local green) nor wider (asserting order-safety CI never verified).

**Docs are CLASSIFIED, not escalated ([#3645](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3645)).** `affected_tests.classify_selection` decided FULL *before* the tach graph was consulted for any changed path outside the modelled src/test roots — and the blueprint-sync gate REQUIRES a `BLUEPRINT.md` edit on exactly that class of commit, so every well-behaved implementer's diff forced the whole tree (measured: 59m32s / 30182 tests for a one-module import fix, plus 30 shared-box artifact failures that then had to be disproved). `teatree.quality.doc_impact` is a pure leaf (stdlib + filesystem) answering the two questions the selector could not ask — `is_doc_path`, `reference_tokens`, and a `disk_reference_reader_lookup` mapping those tokens to the tests that READ them. Docs are not assumed inert: `classify_selection` partitions doc paths out of the changed set *before* delegating to the shared `changed_set.classify` so a doc never reaches the out-of-root escalation, and `build_force_keep` then force-keeps the tests whose source names the changed doc (full path, basename, or any containing directory) as a `reference-read` reason — the textual analogue of the import scan that maps src modules to their tests. The classified set is markdown, the `docs/` tree and the mkdocs config, all outside `src/`/`tests/`, so a `.md` fixture under a code root is untouched and still forces FULL; everything executable keeps the old bias (conftest, `factories.py`, test settings, migrations, `.ast-grep` rules, config/toolchain, python outside the modelled roots, and any delete/rename of code — including one sitting beside a doc edit). The merge gate is untouched — the sharded CI run plus the combined coverage floor stays the authority, so a narrowed LOCAL classifier cannot create a false green. On this repo a `BLUEPRINT.md`-only diff now selects 17 reference-reader test files + 3 floor dirs instead of the whole tree.

**`dev/` lane runners are classified the same way; the lane's OWN machinery forces FULL ([#3817](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3817)).** `changed_set.CONFIG_PREFIXES` calls the whole `dev/` tree un-modellable toolchain. That is right for its two other consumers — `scripts/ci/changed_lanes.py` (CI lane routing) and `quality.push_gate` (the doctest + ast-grep sweep), both of which keep a whole-tree CI backstop — but for TEST SELECTION it made a one-line edit to `dev/push-gate.sh` select the whole tree (33952 collected tests on this repo), so the change that most needs a local run was the one change that could not get one. A shell / compose / Dockerfile asset under `dev/` is imported by nothing and executed by no test, so the only test that can observe an edit to it is one whose source NAMES it: `affected_tests.is_lane_runner` puts it in the same non-imported class as a doc and `is_reference_mapped` partitions both out before the shared classifier, mapping them to their readers. Python under `dev/` is excluded (importable ⇒ the out-of-root FULL still owns it), and `changed_set` itself is untouched, so CI-lane routing and the push gate stay exactly as conservative as before.

The safety this narrowing appears to remove never existed where it mattered, and is now explicit and strictly stronger. The `dev/` blanket covered only the shell runner; the selection logic proper — `changed_set.py`, `affected_tests.py`, `doc_impact.py`, `force_keep_plugin.py`, `test_path_mirror.py`, `cli/affected_tests_tools.py` — is `src/teatree/**/*.py`, which the shared classifier SCOPED like any other module, so the selector was already using its own edited logic to decide what to run. `affected_tests.SELECTION_DEFINING_PATHS` names all seven (the six modules plus `dev/test-affected.sh`) and `classify_selection` returns FULL for any of them BEFORE the doc/lane-runner partition and before the shared classifier — a selection cannot validate a change to the code that computes it. Membership is by exact path, so `test_every_pinned_path_exists_on_disk` fails on a stale entry a rename would otherwise drop silently. Measured on a one-line `dev/push-gate.sh` diff: FULL was 33904 tests in 890s at `-n auto`, peaking at 20.4 GB of system memory; SCOPED is 3825 tests in 205s (the 3 floor dirs plus the 65 test files that name `dev/`) — the same cost any other scoped diff pays. A FULL verdict is therefore long rather than impossible on a quiet box, but its ~3.7 GB of worker RSS is what OOM-kills a box already carrying several agents; `PYTEST_XDIST_AUTO_NUM_WORKERS=<n>` bounds `-n auto` so the run finishes slowly instead, and CI's sharded `test (3.13)` lane remains the authority.

**tach plugin is authoritative; force-keep preserves our escalations ([#3672](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3672)).** The impact engine for a SCOPED selection is tach's own **pytest plugin** (`--tach --tach-base origin/main`), which walks the import graph natively inside one normal pytest session and deselects the tests a diff cannot reach. `teatree.quality.affected_tests` owns only the ESCALATION policy the plugin cannot infer. `classify_selection` decides FULL-vs-SCOPED — a changed conftest / `factories.py` / test-settings / migration / any unclassifiable executable path forces FULL, a migration additionally requires `--create-db` — and a FULL verdict runs the whole suite with the plugin OFF, so nothing is deselected. On a SCOPED verdict `build_force_keep` computes the set the plugin's module graph cannot know — the floor dirs, the reference-reader tests (those naming a changed doc or `dev/` lane runner), the test-path-mirror path, and the changed test files themselves — and `teatree.quality.force_keep_plugin` applies it as a FORCE-KEEP layer over the plugin's deselection: a `pytest_collection_modifyitems` hook wrapper that hides the force-kept items before tach runs and re-inserts them after, in the SAME session, so no test runs twice. The force-keep layer reads the plugin's own `--tach-base` so both diff the identical ref, and fails SAFE — if the escalation set cannot be computed mid-run it protects the whole collected set rather than let tach deselect on an unproven scope. `cli/affected_tests_tools.py` emits the plugin invocation for a scoped run and reports the force-keep layer; `dev/test-affected.sh` runs it. The merge gate is untouched — the sharded CI run plus the combined coverage floor stays the authority, so this diff-scoped LOCAL selector never creates a false green. Direct local pytest refuses a directory selection above 1,200 recorded test seconds, or one with no recorded tests when the durations file exists, before collection. A missing durations file keeps the existing path-only guard for mutation copies. Agents use the affected lane or specific test files/node ids while CI proves the whole tree.

**Behavioral eval harness ([#1160](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1160)).** `src/teatree/eval/` grades agent behaviour across free deterministic lanes (real-code-path regression corpus, skill-coverage, catalog-discovery, negative-control, transcript-replay, corpus-grade, skill-command-validity) and a metered AI lane (in-process `claude-agent-sdk`, weekly standalone `eval.yml`, authed on the credential `agent_harness_provider` selects — DEFAULT the subscription OAuth token, with the metered `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` selectable per run via `t3 eval run --credential api_key`). Bare `t3 eval` runs the whole suite in one table; subcommands target one lane. The architecture:

- **#550 3-tier skill model.** Tier-1 `skill-command-validity` (free) fails on a backticked `t3 …` that no longer resolves against the live CLI registry, over every human-authored prose doc — the skills tree, `agents/*.md`, `BLUEPRINT.md` and `docs/` (minus `docs/generated/`, which is rendered FROM that registry); Tier-2 is the metered behavioral scenarios; Tier-3 `skill-prose-judge` is **advisory** (scores SKILL.md prose, never fails the suite).
- **Content-keyed passing verdicts ([#5015](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5015)).** Single-trial fresh evals may read `--verdict-cache <ledger>`: `eval.verdict_cache` keys each resolved scenario by its graded prompt, typed matchers and judge rubric, declared fixture skills, exposed SDK plugin assets (overlay `src/` and manifest-referenced paths included), runner code, backend package versions, the model the backend actually runs after tier overrides, and effective run settings (API endpoint and request/token caps). A spawning scenario also keys the committed checkout tree; if that tree is unavailable, only spawning scenarios run fresh. Only clean PASS rows with measured fresh execution enter the 30-day ledger through `t3 eval verdict-cache record`; failures, skips, fallback-model runs, flaky escalations and multi-trial runs never enter it. CI merges the latest main and PR Actions caches by verdict timestamp, runs only changed or expired keys in the usual Docker isolation, refreshes main before saving, and saves new passes after the run. The Docker staging mount receives a copy of the ledger and copies out only requested redacted reports. JSON keeps ran rows in `scenarios[]`, lists reused passes in `cached[]`, and reports `totals.cached`; Markdown names each cached verdict, key and linked source run separately. Cached-only selections make no runner call, while every fresh row still passes the existing execution, metering and verdict gates.
- **Under-load behavioural-drift lane ([#2346](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2346)).** `EvalSpec.lane` selects the harness mode: `clean_room` (default) loads one skill into an empty context; `under_load` reproduces real-session instruction drift — the skill bundle as system prompt plus an 8k–20k-token polluted `context_preamble` folded into the user prompt. The bundle is capped at a deterministic char budget (the scenario's own `agent_path` skill, the `rules` skill, and every small canonical-source skill always kept; only the largest peripheral skills shed) so a full bundle + a realistic preamble + a multi-tool scenario fits the model's input window instead of overflowing with "Prompt too long" before the model runs. A `--lane` filter slices the one catalog so the cheap PR-path anti-vacuity gate and the weekly metered lane run the right subset. There is **no known-red allowance and no shrink-only ratchet**: every `under_load` scenario must be GREEN, and a failing one reds the run **outright**, exactly like a failing `clean_room` scenario (`t3 eval run --trials k` exits non-zero on any red scenario, in either lane). The behavioural fix lever is the **skill prose** — a drifting rule must be made unmissable in the loaded `skills/*/SKILL.md` the lane sends the model, never added to a tolerated-red list. The known-red quarantine ([#4173](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4173), above) is not an exception to this and never reaches a verdict: it decides only which scenarios the bounded selective-PR lane SELECTS, so a quarantined scenario that runs still reds its run wherever it runs. Each scenario's matchers are proven anti-vacuous on every PR by `tests/eval_replay/test_scenarios_anti_vacuous.py` (its failing fixture grades RED, its passing fixture grades GREEN, and a no-op transcript cannot satisfy it) before the metered lane (weekly `eval.yml` workflow + on-demand dispatch, off the per-PR path) ever runs.
- **Question-surface axis — the Slack round trip gates, the tool-call rendering does not ([#3855](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3855)).** `EvalSpec.surface` is ORTHOGONAL to `lane`: `lane` selects the harness mode, `surface` selects which question/answer surface the scenario grades. `headless` (the default) BLOCKS — it grades the contract teatree owns, that a question REACHES the user over Slack with a `DeferredQuestion` as the durable record and the answer comes back (`headless_question_contract.yaml` outbound, `askuserquestion_slack_resolution.yaml` inbound). `interactive` is ADVISORY — the scenario grades the Claude-interactive `AskUserQuestion` TOOL-CALL rendering, whose wire shape belongs to a bundled `claude` CLI generation, so it is still run and still reported (`N advisory failed` in the lane detail) but never flips a verdict. The verdicts it must reach are NAMED, never counted — `teatree.eval.surface.ADVISORY_EXEMPT_VERDICT_POINTS` is the canonical list and `tests/conformance/test_advisory_verdict_points.py` resolves every entry: `all._ai_lane_result` (the full-suite AI lane), `multi_trial.run_pass_at_k_lane`, `multi_trial.run_model_matrix_lane`, and both exits of the DEFAULT single-trial lane every metered CI leg actually drives — `run_modes.finalize_single_run` and `escalate.EscalationOutcome.is_hard_red` (`--escalate-on-fail`) — plus `green_proof.evaluate_green_proof`, the SECOND gate `eval-ci-heal` applies over the merged summary JSON after the shards finish. Counting these went stale twice ("three", then "four", both wrong and both omitting a real gate), which is why the list is a resolved-symbol enumeration rather than a number. The merged artifact carries each row's `surface` and the `advisory` flag derived from it, so the combine job and `loop.ci_eval_heal_advance.red_scenario_names` (the fixer-DISPATCH set) honour the exemption without re-deriving it; a row missing the flag reads as GATING. `t3 eval run --surface <headless|interactive>` slices the catalog outright, and `--surface` is forwarded across the `--docker` re-invocation like `--lane`/`--shard` (dropping it would silently run the full catalog in-container on metered spend). Absent means `headless`, so advisory status is always an explicit opt-in, and `tests/eval_replay/test_question_surface.py` reds when any scenario hard-requires the tool call while still gating — that gate is what REPLACED the `claude-agent-sdk` Dependabot quarantine, so Claude Code interactive stays graded without a rendering change ever reddening the headless lane. Generated scenarios DERIVE the label from their matcher shape rather than declaring it.
- **Production-hooks lane — measure the model+hook SYSTEM ([#107](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/107) cluster 5).** `EvalSpec.production_hooks: true` registers the SHIPPED teatree plugin (`hooks/hooks.json`; the repo root IS the plugin root, so a plugin-carried `hooks.json` fires despite the empty user-level `settings`) into the SDK child and sets `include_hook_events=True`, so a scenario whose behaviour is enforced by a Claude-Code hook (the #807 structured-question Stop gate, the #2665 completion-claim Stop gate) grades the model+hook system that ships instead of the raw hooks-stripped model. `hooked_env` additionally redirects the loop/hook state roots (`XDG_DATA_HOME`, `T3_HOOK_STATE_DIR`, `T3_LOOP_REGISTRY_DIR`, `TEATREE_CLAUDE_STATUSLINE_STATE_DIR`) into the sandbox home so `_session_drives_loop` sees a fresh owner-less registry and the Stop gate actually fires (else a developer's live loop-owner registry silently skips it). **Honesty is structural, never a spurious green:** gate-firing is a REPORT ANNOTATION (`pass (gate-assisted)` in `render_text`; `gate_assisted`/`gate_events` in the JSON report), NOT a per-scenario pass condition (a required "gate fired" matcher would wrongly RED a first-try-compliant model); a hooked run capturing ZERO hook events is a fail-loud `hooks_not_registered` error (the plugin failed to register → the lane would degrade to raw-model measurement); and the `harness_canary_stop_gate_fires` canary (a prose-only decision that can pass ONLY via the #807 bounce) REPORTS the wiring end-to-end each metered run. **What the canary no longer does is gate.** It can pass only via a captured `AskUserQuestion` call, so it is `surface: interactive` and therefore advisory — as are five of the other six `production_hooks` scenarios. The one whose behavioural VERDICT still gates on the shipped hook system is `done_only_on_deployed_dev_evidence` (the #2665 completion-claim Stop gate).
- **Harness-failure axis — a run that measured NOTHING is not a verdict, so no surface exempts it ([#3922](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3922)).** `hooks_not_registered` used to ride a terminal `EvalRun` — a failing VERDICT — so the advisory exemption swallowed it: 6 of the 7 hooked scenarios are `surface: interactive`, the only gating one is `lane: under_load`, and the nightly runs `--lane clean_room`, so on that shard set the fail-loud could never gate at all. `teatree.eval.harness_failure` is now its own axis beside `teatree.eval.surface`: the surface weighs a verdict, and a run whose harness failed has none to weigh. The reason is read by `RunGuards.hooks_registered`, an unconditional guard that takes NO surface argument and runs BESIDE each lane's verdict (after the lane's artifacts are written, so a red run still drops its transcript + summary), which is what makes the exemption structurally unreachable rather than merely un-applied. The lanes that must call it are NAMED, never counted — `HARNESS_FAILURE_GUARD_POINTS`: `single_trial.run_single_trial`, `multi_trial.run_pass_at_k_lane`, `multi_trial.run_model_matrix_lane`, `all.run_full_suite`, `ladder.ladder`, `benchmark.benchmark` — and the two producers of the serialized `advisory` flag (`HARNESS_FAILURE_ADVISORY_CARVE_OUTS`: `summary_json._ScenarioRow.as_json`, `escalate.escalate_failures`) never set it for a row that measured nothing, so the `eval-ci-heal` combine job's merged-artifact gate cannot bless a shard the lane guard failed. A `--trials k` matrix cell carries the signal on `MatrixRow.harness_failed` (its `terminal_reason` holds only the CAP reason, which would drop it silently), folded from `PassAtKResult.harness_failed` — every executed trial, the way `is_error` folds. Calling the guard is not sufficient on its own, because the guard reads the ROW's flag: a fold that omits it hands over a clean row and that lane's call is vacuous, so every `PassAtKResult` → `MatrixRow` fold is NAMED too (`HARNESS_FAILURE_FOLD_POINTS`: `multi_trial._matrix_trial`, `ladder._row_from`) and must carry the field. `tests/conformance/test_advisory_verdict_points.py` resolves every name and asserts each lane actually calls the guard and each fold actually carries the flag, so a NEW lane that forgets reds there rather than in a metered CI leg. `done_only_on_deployed_dev_evidence` is `lane: under_load`, so a leg that does not run it — the nightly's `--lane clean_room` shards (whose four hooked scenarios are all advisory), the changed-scenarios PR lane, `--surface interactive`, a `--name` run — still loses that scenario's behavioural verdict, but no leg loses the harness fail-loud any more. Registration is run-wide, so the signal is never lost: read the run output rather than inferring registration from a green exit code. Where production enforcement is the `t3` CLI rather than a Claude hook, fidelity is a gate-aware stub instead (`cli_stubs: [t3@on_behalf_forbidden]` refuses colleague-surface posts with exit 1, parity-tested against the production block message). Additive: a scenario declaring none is byte-identical.
- **Model resolution by abstract tier ([eval redesign]).** Eval scenarios reference models by abstract TIER, not a concrete id: `EvalSpec.tier` (`frontier`/`balanced`/`cheap`) or `EvalSpec.phase` (an FSM phase name) resolve through the single `src/teatree/agents/model_tiering.tier_models()` constant (`eval/model_resolution.resolve_eval_model`). The resolution precedence is `model:` (an explicit concrete-id escape hatch, `model@effort` preserved) > `tier` → `resolve_tier` > `phase` → `phase_models()[phase]` → `resolve_tier` > `DEFAULT_TIER` (`balanced`). Adopting a new model is one edit to `tier_models()` (or one `[agent.tier_models]` line) — every scenario and test follows, no edit. NO concrete model-id string literal appears in any scenario or test.
- **Non-Claude execution backend (`t3 eval run --backend pydantic_ai`).** A third `EvalRunner` (`eval/pydantic_ai_runner.py`, alongside the `api` SDK runner and the `transcript` replay runner) drives a NON-Claude model through the provider-agnostic `PydanticAiHarnessSession` (OrcaRouter BYOK, `x-lane: eval`), so a GPT/open-source swap is verifiable by the same scenarios — a config change (`agent_harness` + tier-model/router rows), not a code change. Grading stays runtime-neutral: the session adapts the model's streamed output into the shared `claude_agent_sdk` message vocabulary and `eval/message_mapping.eval_run_from_messages` folds it into an `EvalRun` unchanged — folding event dicts DIRECTLY via `StreamJsonEvent.from_obj` (no typed→JSON→dict round-trip). The scenario's declared tools become inert `pydantic_ai` stubs so tool-call behaviour is captured in the same `ToolUseBlock` vocabulary the SDK lane yields; the OrcaRouter credential resolves lazily inside `run`, so building the runner needs no live BYOK key.
- **CLI-free Claude execution backend (`t3 eval run --backend anthropic_api`, [#3222](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3222)).** A fourth `EvalRunner` (`eval/anthropic_api_runner.py`) runs the SAME Claude model as the `api` backend but through the Anthropic Messages API DIRECTLY — a `pydantic_ai` `AnthropicModel` over the `anthropic` package (the `[anthropic]` extra) → `api.anthropic.com`, spawning NO `claude` CLI child. So a downstream harness that forbids the Claude Code CLI can adopt teatree's `--backend api` eval scenarios while teatree's own default lane keeps the CLI-backed SDK transport. It shares the agentic loop + message-vocabulary mapping with the `pydantic_ai` backend (it resolves the `AnthropicModel` and delegates the drive to `PydanticAiRunner`, whose `model` is injectable), so grading is byte-identical; the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` credential (`AnthropicApiKeyCredential`) resolves LAZILY inside `run` (a missing key skips, or fails loud under `--require-executed` via `AnthropicApiKeyMissingError` — symmetric with the `api` runner's missing-`claude` skip). `backends.FRESH_CLAUDE_BACKENDS` = `{api, anthropic_api}` is the shared set both fresh-Claude lanes key on (require-executed arming, `--trials`/`--models` acceptance, docker credential forwarding). `eval.yml` gains a `backend` dispatch input (default `api`, so the weekly run is unchanged) that skips the Claude-CLI install step for the CLI-free lane; pair it with `credential: api_key` (the `anthropic_api` lane authenticates on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`).
- **Selective-PR eval + weekly benchmark publish ([#2704](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2704)).** A PR runs the metered suite ONLY for the scenarios its diff touched: `scripts/eval/scenarios_for_changed.py` maps changed paths (STDIN) to the names of every spec whose `source_path` matches, and the PR workflow loops `t3 eval run <name>` per changed scenario. **A scenario is built from its YAML AND from the skill prose it grades** ([#3944](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3944)): a clean-room scenario's system prompt is `agent_path` narrowed to `agent_sections`, so keying selection on the YAML alone let an edit to the exact section a scenario grades select ZERO scenarios while the lane reported PASS — vacuous-green by construction. The selector therefore also matches a spec whose `agent_path` is in the changed set, and the optional `--diff-file` (the same range's `git diff --unified=0`, parsed by `teatree.eval.changed_sections`) supplies the section granularity a path list cannot carry: a spec declaring `agent_sections` is selected only when one of ITS sections moved, while a spec with no `agent_sections` grades the whole file and any edit selects it. Every uncertainty (no diff, unreadable post-image, a preamble-only edit, a heading-less file) degrades to selecting MORE, never fewer. Section matches are ranked ahead of whole-file ones so the cap below can never truncate away the tightest evidence (the positional NAME filter; the multi-name `--only` flag is removed). `.github/workflows/eval-pr.yml` (`on: pull_request`) detects-then-runs the changed scenarios against the BASELINE preset (`--preset baseline`, single trial) — the same cheapest-passing per-scenario tier the weekly cron pins, so a PR's eval verdict matches the weekly's (a brand-new scenario absent from `baseline.yaml` falls through to its own tier). Because that lane runs the selection SEQUENTIALLY in ONE job, the selector caps it at `MAX_SELECTIVE_PR_SCENARIOS` (= `lane_shard.MAX_SCENARIOS_PER_SHARD`): a corpus-wide mechanical edit (a `model:`→`tier:` backfill, a mass rename) that touches every scenario file is truncated to a deterministic sorted-name prefix so it stays inside the 80-min step cap. The deferred tail is not dropped: `ScenarioSelection.deferred_names` carries it, `scripts/eval/scenarios_for_changed.py --deferred-out` writes it, and the PR lane records it as owed (next bullet). When the cap bites, `changed_scenarios.ScenarioSelection.truncation_note()` emits a `selected N … capped to K … deferred M to the scheduled lanes` line to stderr from both `t3 eval changed-scenarios` and `scripts/eval/scenarios_for_changed.py` ([#2737](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2737)), so a corpus-wide PR's truncated coverage is visible in the CI log instead of silently dropped. **Known-red quarantine ([#4173](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4173)).** Section-scoping is precise, and that precision has a cost: while a scenario is red, every PR touching the doctrine section it grades reds its eval lane on a failure it did not cause — and the `_BROAD` fail-safe widens that blast radius exactly when the diff is hardest to classify. `evals/quarantine.yaml` (`teatree.eval.quarantine`) is the registry that bounds it: each entry names a currently-failing scenario, the issue that will fix it, an ISO expiry date and a one-line reason, and `suppressed_scenario_names()` is what the selector drops AFTER banding — so the fail-safe `_BROAD` band cannot smuggle a tracked red back in. The suppression is SELECTION scope and nothing else: the scenario is still run, still graded, and still reds the weekly/heal lane and the green proof, so the no-known-red-allowance rule below is untouched and there is no tolerated-red list anywhere. Three staleness channels keep the list from rotting into decoration. **Expiry is self-enforcing** — past `until` the entry simply stops suppressing, the scenario re-arms and blocks again exactly as it did before quarantine, so the degraded state is the status quo rather than a silent pass and no permanent skip list is reachable. `t3 eval quarantine check` reds on an expired entry or one naming a scenario the catalog no longer defines; `t3 eval quarantine audit <merged-summary.json>` — the step `eval-ci-heal.yml` runs beside `green-proof`, `if: always()` — NAMES each quarantined scenario's outcome in the merged run (`STILL RED` / `NOT RUN`) and reds on an `ESCAPED` one, a quarantined scenario that PASSED and whose entry is now a lie. Every suppression is named on the selector's stderr (`ScenarioSelection.quarantine_note()`), so a shrunken PR lane is visible in the same CI log as a truncated one. The registry sits beside its scenarios dir (`evals/scenarios` → `evals/quarantine.yaml`, `quarantine_path_for`), so a consumer passing its own `--scenarios-dir` reaches its own registry with no extra flag. A missing registry is an EMPTY quarantine (an overlay with none of its own selects exactly as before), while a PRESENT but malformed one fails loud rather than degrading to "suppress nothing". Every metered run (PR + weekly) can emit two artifacts from THIS run's results: the PRIVATE per-trial `--transcript-html` (reasoning + tool calls), and the PUBLISH-SAFE `--summary-md` (`summary_markdown.render_summary_markdown` — total counts + total cost + model + a `scenario | lane | verdict | trials | cost` table built ONLY from spec identity + verdict + pass/trial counts + per-scenario `cost_usd`, NEVER a transcript; an anti-disclosure test pins a transcript sentinel absent). The weekly `eval.yml` uploads each shard's sanitized summary (`eval-summary-*`), and a `publish` job merges them (`scripts/eval/merge_summaries.py` — summing per-scenario cost into a dashboard total) into `docs/evals/index.md`, pushing to `main` so `docs.yml` serves the dashboard on Pages. A red weekly run is surfaced by GitHub's native failed-run email (no extra secret). The single-trial execution shape lives in `cli/eval/single_trial.py`, the multi-trial/matrix shapes in `cli/eval/multi_trial.py`.
- **Full-suite baseline report ([#4933](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4933)).** A green one-shard nightly smoke says nothing about the rest of the catalog, so `teatree.eval.green_proof.evaluate_baseline` judges the newest measured merged `eval-heal-<sha>` summary (parked or empty summaries measured nothing and are skipped) and `t3 eval baseline-status` prints the verdict: `MISSING` (no full-suite `eval-ci-heal` run on record), `STALE` (the proof does not cover the CURRENT core catalog's exact `(name, version)` identity set (the catalog CI measures, whatever overlays the host also installs), or is older than `BASELINE_MAX_AGE`, 7 days, or is stamped in the future; the verdict names any usage-limit-skipped rows), `NOT_GREEN` (current but carrying a gating red) or `GREEN`; the exit code is 0 only for `GREEN`. The runs come from `GhCiEvalClient` (the same seam as `ci-status`) and are filtered on `status == completed` only: a red full-suite run concludes `failure` yet still uploads its summary, so judging by conclusion would let an older green proof stand in for it; it adds no setting and no migration. It is an operator command, not a health-chip signal: `teatree.eval` depends on `teatree.core`, so the domain-layer health collectors cannot import the proof, and the baseline lives in CI artifacts rather than the control DB.
- **Tool results reach the grader on the CLI lane.** The Claude CLI lane delivers every tool result as a `UserMessage` of `ToolResultBlock` rows; `message_mapping` renders them to `user` events (a sub-agent's keep their `parent_tool_use_id`, so they never attach to a main-agent call). A `tool_call_succeeded` matcher counts a call as proved when its paired result has `is_error=False` and no non-zero exit marker (the non-CLI sandbox prints `exit=N`; the CLI reports success by the flag alone) AND its result regex matches.
- **Evals never drain the factory's subscription window ([#5014](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/5014)).** An eval spends a subscription account only while it keeps a reserve free for the factory: `teatree.account_headroom.WindowReserve` (default 40% of the 5h window and 30% of the 7d window free, overridable via `EVAL_RESERVE_5H_FREE` / `EVAL_RESERVE_7D_FREE`; a malformed value fails loud) is fail-closed — a missing 5h or 7d reading is unknown and never eligible. The CI selector (`scripts/eval/select_oauth.py` over `teatree.eval.oauth_selection.select_freshest`) picks only an account that meets it; with none it exits 75 (`MEASURED_NOTHING_EXIT_CODE`) and writes `parked=true`, and only the PR lane may instead flip to the metered key (the opt-in `EVAL_API_KEY_FALLBACK`); the nightly, weekly and heal lanes never do. A subscription 5h or 7d limit is never retried or waited out: the runner turns it into a `usage_limit_reached` skip and latches the suite (`teatree.eval.parallel`, and `UsageLimitLatch` on `--escalate-on-fail`), so no later scenario or escalation trial calls the model, and `t3 eval run` exits 75 unless something it measured failed. Every workflow maps 75 to a green park and never reruns into the limit: a limit or red marker makes a retry attempt invoke nothing and replay the verdict, so a retry never turns a measured red green. The `eval-gate` reports PARKED — green, naming the owed scenarios — when the eval was parked with nothing failed; a confirmed red still fails it. **Owed ledger** (`teatree.eval.owed`, `t3 eval owed record|outputs|collect|next|settle`): the PR lane uploads what it could not run (parked, limit, deferred) as the `eval-owed` artifact; the `prepare` jobs of the nightly and of the full benchmark merge the previous `eval-owed-ledger` artifact with the `eval-owed` artifact of each PR merged since (only the run on the PR's final head; a GitHub API error fails the job) and with each weekly or heal leg's `eval-owed-<leg>` artifact (a default-branch leg's uploads since the last harvest; a leg on a PR branch only at the final head of a PR merged since) and re-upload it on every scheduled run so it never expires, the nightly `eval` job and the benchmark `publish` job re-upload it after settling what they covered (the newest upload wins), and the nightly runs while anything is owed. The nightly runs up to 14 owed scenarios before its shard and clears an entry only when it passes; the benchmark settles only scenario names that passed every recorded matrix cell. The weekly sweep (`eval.yml`) is checked daily but runs only when an account meets the reserve AND resets its 7-day window within 24h (`EVAL_SWEEP_WITHIN_HOURS`), one leg at a time under a concurrency group, with each leg running its selected scenarios sequentially and recording a limit-cut tail as owed, so it spends quota that would expire unused; the publisher refuses a dashboard unless every planned leg wrote a summary and a complete receipt. On the box, `EvalLocalScanner` and dream eval promotion check the selected OAuth account; a stale cached row is probed live and persisted without changing the sticky routing pick (`token_report.local_eval_reserve_decision`). The heal loop checks only its `eval_ci_account_allowlist` pool, probes stale rows until an eligible account is found (`ci_eval_reserve_decision`), and holds a pending dispatch until the reserve returns. A stale probe failure has a visible scanner signal, dream summary, or heal note. Any CI run with parked legs requeues twice, then halts and escalates on its third consecutive park without spending a fix attempt. A scenario the usage limit skipped is never a heal red: the fixer gets only measured reds, the run counts as cut short (a fix is followed by a full-suite re-run), and `t3 eval green-proof` reports it unmeasured and exits 75 instead of failing. The CI OAuth pool publishes only `eval_ci_account_allowlist` (a safety-posture key, so setting it is the owner's act through the CLI, never the MCP write tool); an empty allowlist refuses before any GitHub write, and `t3 doctor check` fails while OAuth accounts exist and the allowlist is empty. Before enabling CI eval or heal, the operator must explicitly set that allowlist to the intended CI OAuth paths; the migration does not grant accounts by default.
- **Reusable eval-CI primitives + `workflow_call` workflows.** The selection / scheduled-guard / dashboard-merge logic is extracted into pure modules (`teatree.eval.{changed_scenarios,merged_prs,summaries}`) exposed both as reusable CLI subcommands (`t3 eval changed-scenarios` selects a PR's scenarios from a STDIN diff and writes its over-cap tail through `--deferred-out`; `t3 eval merged-prs-since` is the weekly no-PR-merged cron guard; `t3 eval merge-summaries` builds the public dashboard) and consumed by the host's `scripts/eval/*.py` (now thin shims over the same core). The two reusable workflows `.github/workflows/{eval-pr-reusable,eval-weekly-reusable}.yml` (`on: workflow_call`) own the selective-PR and weekly metered flows once — with overlay host-checkout + scenario-assertion inputs and the no-silent-green enforcement — so a downstream overlay's eval CI becomes a thin caller of teatree's primitives instead of a duplicate. The reusable PR workflow records that deferred tail in the owed ledger; it does not mark a bounded measured pass as parked. The anti-leak invariant is unchanged: the summary merger and the publish job read only the sanitized verdict rows, never transcripts. **Unmetered-shard publish guard.** The benchmark rides a subscription OAuth window; once exhausted mid-run the remaining shards still "complete" with every scenario force-FAILed at `$0.0000`, so publishing them would commit fake all-red matrices. `t3 eval verify-benchmark-publish <dir> --expected-shards <n>` (`teatree.eval.benchmark_publish_guard` over `teatree.eval.matrix_html_tally`, the reader counterpart to `render_matrix_html`) runs in the publish job BEFORE the dashboard is written and refuses the WHOLE publish when ANY single model in ANY shard records graded verdicts against zero metered cost — the partial case (one model metered, the rest zeroed) is the one an all-zero filter misses. A shard with no graded verdicts owed no spend and is legitimately empty. `--expected-shards` is REQUIRED, and carries the second refusal: it is the matrix leg count the run planned, so a shard whose leg timed out and uploaded nothing is a refusal rather than a smaller dashboard that looks complete (an empty directory is refused outright).
- **CI-eval heal primitives (workflow + machine-readable artifact + `gh` wrappers).** The workflow_dispatch-only `.github/workflows/eval-ci-heal.yml` runs the behavioral eval against a PR branch (a `scenarios` red-subset input — empty = full suite — plus `credential` and `pr_ref`), reusing the in-Docker `t3 eval run --backend api --docker --require-executed` body and uploading a PUBLISH-SAFE per-scenario JSON (`eval-heal-<sha>`) plus the PRIVATE transcript. That JSON is the `t3 eval run --summary-json <path>` artifact (`teatree.eval.summary_json.render_summary_json`, sibling of `--summary-md`): `generated_at`/`model`/`head_sha`/`totals` + a `scenarios[]` of spec identity + verdict + the triage discriminators (`is_error`/`terminal_reason`/`matcher_failed`/`judge_failed`) and a derived `triage_class` — publish-safe by construction (no transcript/tool-input/judge-rationale). The pure `teatree.eval.triage.classify_red` derives that class ONCE from the real `CAP_TERMINAL_REASONS` (`eval/models.py`) + throttle prefix (`eval/api_errors.py`) — `behavioral`/`judge` (fix), `infra_transport`/`infra_throttle`/`infra_cap` (retry), `no_coverage` (a skip under `--require-executed`, never green). The `t3 eval ci-trigger` / `ci-status` (non-blocking) commands wrap `gh workflow run` / `run list|view|download` through `teatree.backends.github.ci_eval_client.GhCiEvalClient` on the existing `_run_gh` seam (`GH_TOKEN` via env only, never argv/logs) — an operator dispatches + reads a run now; the heal loop drives them later.
- **CI-eval healing loop ([#3201](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3201)).** The `ci_eval_heal` mini-loop (`loops/ci_eval_heal/loop.py`) advances only operator-opened sessions; its `Loop.enabled` value is a tri-state manual override, and active presets determine whether the loop and its fixer run. The scanner (`loop/scanners/ci_eval_heal.py`) flags open sessions and the mechanical handler (`loop/mechanical_ci_eval_heal.py`) advances each one FSM step: `pending` dispatches only with reserve headroom measured within five minutes, refreshing stale allowlisted rows before deciding; `awaiting_ci` reads a completed run's `eval-heal-<sha>` payload; a readable, unparked success with no red becomes `green`; a measured behavioral red reaches the bounded fixer and a full-suite re-run; an unreadable or unconfirmable result halts. A run with only parked coverage returns to `pending` without spending a fix attempt, then halts and escalates after three consecutive parks regardless of the CI conclusion. Every halt raises a deduped `DeferredQuestion`. The anti-cheat gate rejects fixes that edit tests, and `CiEvalHealSession._no_reds` prevents a red session from self-certifying green.
- **`--benchmark` (the 3-tier matrix) + `--model` (force one model).** `t3 eval run --benchmark` runs every (filtered) scenario against ALL THREE tier models (`frontier`/`balanced`/`cheap`, each resolved through the single `tier_models()` constant via `app_helpers._benchmark_models`) on the metered matrix lane and renders the comparison matrix + a self-contained HTML dashboard (`eval/matrix.render_matrix_html`) — the MANUAL benchmark entry (`eval-benchmark.yml` → `eval-weekly-reusable.yml`, workflow_dispatch); adopting a new model needs no flag edit. The scheduled WEEKLY cron (`eval.yml`) is NOT this fan — it runs the cheap `--preset baseline` leg (each scenario once at its baseline-pinned tier, single trial, no effort fan), so the shared OAuth window is never drained. `t3 eval run --model <m>` forces the whole suite onto one model (single-trial metered). `--benchmark`/`--model`/`--models` are mutually exclusive. The DEFAULT `t3 eval run` is per-scenario tier/phase, one trial, no pass@k, no escalation. **Escalate-on-fail (`t3 eval run --escalate-on-fail --escalate-trials N`, default N=3).** Still available off the default path for the PR lane: a single noisy LLM trial is not yet a hard red — `teatree.cli.eval.escalate.escalate_failures` re-runs ONLY the failed scenarios at N higher trials and classifies each `flaky` (recovered) vs `confirmed` (the lane reds). Single-trial only. **Effort matrix (`scripts/eval/lane_matrix.py --efforts low,medium,high`).** An optional axis that fans each `{lane, shard}` leg across the three reasoning-effort tiers, carried through `t3 eval run --effort <tier>`. The `t3 eval run` lane dispatch (matrix / `--model` force / pass@k / single-trial) lives in `cli/eval/run_dispatch.py`.
- **Ground-truth corpus + audit layer ([#2192](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2192), [#1861](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1861)).** `eval/corpus/` pairs captured sessions with independently-authored labels under an independent-oracle guard (a matcher label whose author is the rule author is refused). The `SessionAuditRecord` ledger (parallel to `EvalScenarioResult`, ids/slugs only) is populated by the conversation-audit pass and rendered by a confusion-matrix renderer; the corpus/audit curation CLI (`t3 eval corpus|audit|label`) is the thin surface over these engines.
- **Cost + benchmark.** A per-scenario cost ledger (`EvalScenarioResult.cost_usd`) powers two cost gates and `t3 eval benchmark`. `--gate-cost-regression` is *relative* drift vs a mutable DB baseline run; `--gate-cost-bounds` is the *absolute* ceiling — each scenario's metered cost is checked against the CHECKED-IN `evals/cost_bounds.yaml` (calibrated `bound_usd` x `(1 + margin)`), a scenario over its ceiling or a configured-but-uncosted scenario fails loud (never skip-as-pass), and a ceiling set pinning NO scenario is RED rather than an inert flag reporting an unearned green, and the engine is the pure `teatree.eval.cost_bounds`. Both gates read the durable ledger, so they run on the persisted weekly/manual metered path, off the per-PR path; `--gate-cost-bounds` additionally requires a backend whose TRANSPORT reports its own bill, and `t3 eval run` exits 2 rather than pairing it with one of `UNMETERED_FRESH_BACKENDS`, whose `cost_usd` is DERIVED from the run's token usage at list price (`teatree.eval.cost_observation`) — a real figure, but not the billed quantity those ceilings are calibrated against. That same observer is what keeps a spending lane from reporting `$0.00`: `EvalRun.cost_source` records whether the figure was transport-`reported`, usage-`derived`, `not_metered` (a replay ran no model) or `unknown`, and `report._cost_summary` renders an `unknown` as *unknown* rather than as a measured zero. `t3 eval benchmark` runs the suite per `model@effort` variant and reports pass-rate, cost-per-pass, and honest cache-aware token metrics (billed cost is the headline).
- **Cheapest-green baseline + escalation ladder.** The file-backed `baseline` preset (`evals/presets/baseline.yaml`, `teatree.eval.presets`) records, per scenario, the cheapest model tier that PASSES it; `t3 eval set-baseline --from <matrix.json>` derives that map from a matrix run (a scenario failing every tier gets no entry and is surfaced, never tiered to frontier). `t3 eval ladder` (`teatree.eval.ladder`) is the CHEAP producer of that matrix: it escalates each scenario cheapest-first (`cheap → balanced → frontier`) and stops at the first tier it passes, so balanced runs only on `cheap`'s failures and frontier only on the scenarios that failed both (tiers sharing one model id are one rung, and `--benchmark` runs each distinct model once) — loss-free for the baseline question but a fraction of the full-matrix spend. It emits the same matrix JSON `set-baseline` consumes; a never-reached tier is an absent cell. Escalation is per-scenario and in-process, so a metered CI shard (`--shard i/n`) escalates its own subset inside one account's usage window with no cross-pass orchestration. `t3 eval benchmark` stays a full matrix (it exists to MEASURE per-model cost, so it runs every model on every scenario).

A skill's evals live in the central catalog `evals/scenarios/<skill>.yaml` (each spec carrying an explicit `agent_path: skills/<name>/SKILL.md`), never inline in the `skills/` tree (`tests/eval_replay/test_no_inline_skill_evals.py` enforces it); a skill with no eval declares `eval_exempt: <reason>`; `t3 eval coverage` is the per-skill coverage gate and EXITS NON-ZERO on a gap — the same verdict the `skill-coverage` lane and the `test_no_shipped_skill_is_an_uncovered_gap` pytest gate return for the same corpus. Every run ends with a plain-language closing verdict, and a setup-skipped lane reads as `NOT RUN`, never as validated. [evals/README.md](evals/README.md) is the SOT for all eval architecture detail.

---

The hourly CI OAuth pool reconciler never writes to a repository this deployment
does not own. An absent pool target is an explicitly disabled, non-actionable
state; a configured but unowned target and a failed reconciliation are both
explicit `action_needed` signals. A successful reconciliation is an explicitly
dropped housekeeping observation. None may fall through the generic in-flight
route silently.

## Maintenance

The BLUEPRINT stays architectural — not a prose mirror of the code — by **review-time judgment**, not a raw byte gate. There is no hard size cap and no per-PR byte-delta budget: a single hand-edited KB constant that every BLUEPRINT-touching PR had to bump made concurrent PRs re-break each other's CI endlessly, with no quality signal to show for it. Keeping the file tight is a reviewer responsibility — flag bloat, prose that restates code instead of capturing architecture, and stale or duplicated sections — captured in `skills/review/SKILL.md` § "Keep BLUEPRINT Tight" and in the periodic holistic review (`architectural-review`). When a section grows past appendix-class detail, split it into a linked appendix under [docs/blueprint/](docs/blueprint/); the top-level file holds the architecture, the appendices hold the depth.

Two structural pre-commit gates remain (these are sync/structure checks, not raw-size enforcement): `scripts/hooks/check_blueprint_sync.py` (BLUEPRINT updated alongside source changes) and `scripts/hooks/check_blueprint_invariant_numbering.py` ([#836](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/836), the §17.1 invariants form a gapless `1..N`, with the CI `blueprint-cross-pr` job catching the concurrent-PR case).

Both gates read `src/` and `BLUEPRINT.md` as **teatree-root-relative**, and git reports staged paths relative to the WORKING-TREE root — the same root only in a plain clone. `check_blueprint_sync` therefore maps the staged set back through `_vendoring_prefix()` (`""` here, `vendor/teatree/` in a fork that vendors core), so in either layout a change to teatree's own `src/` is gated and teatree's own BLUEPRINT satisfies it. A path outside that tree — a vendoring fork's own overlay source — is neither, which is also what keeps a concurrent agent's staged file in a shared clone from moving an unrelated commit's verdict.

The auto-generated tach dependency graph lives in [docs/dependency-graph.md](docs/dependency-graph.md), outside the corpus, so structural growth never bloats this file.

## Module Dependency Graph

See [docs/dependency-graph.md](docs/dependency-graph.md) for the auto-generated graph.
